# Ibn Ezra on Leviticus

אבן עזרא על ויקרא

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus)

פירוש פשט המשלב ביאורים המבוססים על דקדוק ובלשנות; חובר במאה ה-12.

12th-century commentary focusing on the simple meaning of the text and incorporating grammar and linguistics.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Ibn Ezra/Torah

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): On Your Way (Segments: 1023, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): Ibn Ezra's commentary on the Pentateuch, tran. and annot. by H. Norman Strickman and Arthur M. Silver. Menorah Pub., 1988-2004 (Segments: 1023)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:1:1)


**ויקרא.** מצאנו ברית יחיד בעבור שני דברים גם יתכן להיות מצוה אחת בעבור דברים רבים כמצות העולה והקרבן כי בתת כל חלק בעתו ימלט החלק שיש לו חלק לעולם הבא על כן פירוש לכפר לתת כופר והעד מתחלת כי תשא על כן כתיב פן יפגענו בדבר גם יש בעולות סודות לעתידות גם יתבונן מכל קרבן סוד התולדות והחטאות והמצות להחיות מורי התורה. וטעם **ויקרא אל משה** אחר ולא יכול משה שהכבוד קראו מאהל מועד שיבוא שם ושם ידבר עמו והכבוד לפנים מהפרכת ושם היה משה נכנס כי כן כתוב וזה טעם ותמונת ה׳‎ יביט. וטעם להזכיר הקרבנות קודם המצות כי השכינה תשוב אל מקומה אם לא ישמרו תורת העולה וכן היה וחלילה חלילה להצטרך לעולה וכן כתוב אם ארעב לא אומר לך רק יש לו סוד:


In the name of God who commandsthat a fire offering be brought before him.I begin to explain the book *And The Lord Called Unto Moses*.¹. 38. This poem is omitted in the printed editions of I.E.
AND THE LORD CALLED. We find two reasons for the covenant made with an individual.². The reference is to the covenant of circumcision that God made with Abraham (Gen. 17:9). The commentaries on I.E. differ as to what these two reasons are. Scripture tells us that circumcision is a sign that the Lord will be God to Abraham and his seed after him (Gen. 17:7). I.E. believes that circumcision is also a sign not to act promiscuously. I.E. writes, “The excision of the foreskin of the flesh is a sign of the covenant between man and his creator not to defile one’s soul by engaging in an act of sexual intercourse that is not in accordance with the way of truth” (*Yesod Morah*, (Strickman translation, p. 102). It is also possible for one commandment to have many reasons. The commandment regarding the burnt offering and the sacrifice³ are examples of the latter, for when the officiant offers each part⁴ in its proper time, then the part⁵ which has a part⁶ in the world to come is saved.⁷ Therefore the meaning of *le-khapper* (to make atonement) (v. 4) is to give ransom. The opening of the Torah portion *Ki Tissa*⁸ is proof.⁹ means to offer a ransom, for we read there that each male Israelite was to give a half shekel as a ransom for his soul (*kofer nefesh*) (Ex. 30:12). Scripture goes on to say, *that there be no plague among them*. Scripture therefore reads, *lest He*¹⁰ *fall upon us with pestilence*¹¹ (Ex. 5:3). There is also a secret¹² regarding the future in the burnt offering.¹³ One can also learn from each sacrifice the secret of nature.¹⁴ The purpose of the sin offering¹⁵. and the unleavened bread¹⁶. 38 and some editions read *veha-manot* (and the portions), i.e., the portions of the sacrifices which are to be eaten by the *kohanim*. is to sustain the teachers of the Torah.¹⁷, who teach Torah to the Jewish people. See Lev. 33:10; Mal. 2:7. The reason *And the Lord called unto Moses* follows *And Moses was not able to enter*¹⁸ (Ex. 40:35) is that the Glory¹⁹ called to Moses from the tent of meeting and told him to come there, and there He would speak with him.²⁰; that is, God invited Moses to the tabernacle. The Glory was on the other side of the curtain²¹ which separated the Holy of Holies from the holy. The Glory was in the Holy of Holies. and Moses entered there.²² The latter is clearly stated in Scripture.²³ This is the meaning of *and the similitude of the Lord doth he behold* (Num. 12:8). The reason Scripture mentions the sacrifices before the commandments²⁴ is that God’s presence will return to its place²⁵ (God) *will go and return to My place*. if Israel does not keep the law of the burnt offering.²⁶. The latter came to pass.²⁷ Far be it from God to require a burnt offering.²⁸ Scripture clearly states, *If I were hungry, I would not tell thee…*(Ps. 50:12).²⁹ These two verses completely dispel the idea that the sacrifices were food for God. On the contrary, it³⁰ has a secret meaning.³¹

footnotes:

² Hebrew, berit yachid
³ The other sacrifices.
⁴ Of the sacrifice.
⁵ The person. The individual Jew is a part of the people of Israel and of the human species. Hence I.E. refers to the individual as a part.
⁶ A portion.
⁷ According to I.E. the sacrificial animal serves as a substitute for the celebrant. “All these acts are performed in order that when they are done, a person should realize that he has sinned against his God…and that his blood should really be spilled and his body burned, were it not for the loving-kindness of the Creator, Who took from him a substitute and a ransom, namely, this offering, so that its blood should be in place of his blood, its life in place of his life, and that the chief limbs of the offering should be in place of the chief parts of his body” (Nachmanides, Chavel translation, Lev. 1:9, p. 21).
⁸ Ex. 30:11-16.
⁹ That le-khapper

¹¹ If we do not sacrifice to God.

¹⁵ Whose flesh was eaten by the kohanim

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:2:1)


**מכם.** מאוחר וכן הוא אדם מכם כי יקריב קרבן וכמוהו רבים או יהי׳‎ מכם מממונכם או יהיה מכם רמז להוציא את הגזל כי כן כתוב שונא גזל בעול׳‎:


OF YOU. The word *mikkem* (of you) is placed at the end of the clause.³². The latter literally reads, when any man bringeth an offering of you. Hence I.E.’s comment. The clause should be read as if written, *adam mikkem ki yakriv* (when any man of you bringeth an offering). There are many such examples.³³ On the other hand, the word *mikkem* might mean from your property.³⁴ (of you) means of your property. In this case the word *mikkem* does not have to be moved, for the meaning of *the clause* is, when any man bringeth an offering of your (that is, his) property. The term *mikkem* might also hint that one is not permitted to bring an offering that is stolen,³⁵ for Scripture states, *I hate robbery with iniquity* (Is. 61:8).³⁶ may be rendered iniquity or burnt offering. I.E. translates it as burnt offering.

footnotes:
³² Our clause reads, adam ki yakriv mikkem
³³ Where a word placed at the end of clause should be read as if placed earlier.
³⁴ In other words, mikkem
³⁵ In this case our verse is to be read as follows: When any man bringeth an offering of you, that is, it shall be of yours. It shall belong to you.
³⁶ Hebrew, be-olah. Be-elah


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:2:2)


**מן הבהמה.** יהיה הקרבן כלל ואחר כן מן הבקר ומן הצאן שהם מין כבש ועז פרט גם מלת קרבנכם כלל:


OF THE CATTLE. Shall the sacrifice be. This³⁷. is a general term. Scripture then gives the particulars and states, *even of the herd*,³⁸. *or of the flock*.³⁹. The flock refers to the species of the sheep and the goat. *Your offering*⁴⁰ is also a general term.

footnotes:
³⁷ Cattle. Hebrew, behemah
³⁸ Hebrew, bakar
³⁹ Hebrew, tzon
⁴⁰ Which concludes our verse.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:1)


**אם עלה קרבנו.** פרט ועול׳‎ מפורשת:


IF HIS OFFERING BE A BURNT–OFFERING. This⁴¹. is a particular. The term *olah* (burnt offering) is self-explanatory.⁴², *heh*, which means to go up. The term *olah* is thus connected in meaning to the word *oleh* (going up).

footnotes:
⁴¹ The term burnt offering
⁴² It comes from the root ayin, lamed


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:2)


**מן הבקר.** גדול או קטן אחר שמונת ימים ובעבור היות העולה קרבה כלה לגבוה היא מהנבחרת והזכר נבחר מהנקבה על כן אין בעולה נקבה:


OF THE HERD. Fully grown or young but more than eight days.⁴³ The burnt offering is brought from the choicest animals because it is entirely for God. The male is chosen above the female. Therefore a female animal is not brought for a burnt offering.

footnotes:
⁴³ An animal younger than eight days is unfit to be brought as a sacrifice. See Lev. 22:27.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:3)


**תמים.** בלא מום ואחר שאמר יקריבנו פירש אל המקום אשר יקריב אותו כי יכנס בחצר אהל מועד:


WITHOUT BLEMISH. *Tammim* means without blemish.⁴⁴ literally means whole. This might be taken to mean not missing an organ. Hence I.E. comments that whole means without blemish. After stating *he shall offer it*, Scripture goes on to explain the place where the celebrant is to bring it. It states that he is to enter into the courtyard of the tent of meeting.⁴⁵ was located in the courtyard of the tent of meeting.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ The word tammim
⁴⁵ The door of the tent of meeting


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:4)


**לפני ה׳‎.** דבק בטעם עם אל פתח אוהל מועד יקריב אותו:


BEFORE THE LORD. This is to be understood as connected to *he shall bring it to the door of the tent of meeting*.⁴⁶ THAT HE MAY BE ACCEPTED⁴⁷. I.E. renders this as, of his own free will. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Our verse is to be understood as follows: he shall bring it to the door of the tent of meeting before the Lord.
⁴⁷ Hebrew, li-retozno


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:3:5)


**לרצונו לפני ה׳‎.** שיקריבנו ברצונו ולא באונס:


BEFORE THE LORD. He shall offer it willingly, not by coercion.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:1)


**וסמך ידו.** היה נראה מהפשט שבידו האחת יסמוך כי דרך שעיר המשתלח איננו כדרך כל קרבן על כן שנה הכ׳‎ רק כאשר מצאנו המעתיקים שהעתיקו שכל סמיכה בשתי ידים סמכנו עליהם והגוף הקרב לכפר על העולה על הרוח יקרא עולה גם כן הקרב בעבור חטאת ואשם יקרא חטאת ואשם:


AND HE SHALL LAY HIS HAND. It would appear from the plain meaning of the text that the celebrant lays one hand⁴⁸ (his hand). on the animal about to be sacrificed, for the manner of the scapegoat⁴⁹ is unlike all sacrifices.⁵⁰ Scripture therefore changed [the manner of the laying of the hands with regard to it].⁵¹ However, we find that the sages who transmitted the laws to us stated that all “laying” is done with two hands.⁵² We rely on them. The body⁵³ which is offered to atone for that which comes (*ha-olah*) into the mind⁵⁴ 7:3. is called an *olah* (burnt offering). The sacrifices brought for sins and guilt are also called *chattat* (sin offering) and *asham* (guilt offering).⁵⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁸ For Scripture reads yado
⁴⁹ Literally, the goat which is sent, the scapegoat which is sent into the wilderness on Yom Kippur. Scripture explicitly tells us that two hands are to be laid on the scapegoat. See Lev. 16:21.
⁵⁰ The scapegoat is not treated as all other sacrifices. Its blood is not sprinkled on the altar, and none of its flesh is offered on the altar. It is sent away into the wilderness. Hence we cannot generalize from it.
⁵¹ Unlike other sacrifices, it requires the laying of two hands.
⁵² Whenever Scripture speaks of laying the hand it refers to the laying of two hands.
⁵³ The body of the animal which serves as a sacrifice.
⁵⁴ According to the sage, Rabbi Simeon bar Yochai, the burnt offering is brought to atone for improper thoughts. See Va-Yikrah Rabba
⁵⁵ Their names also explain the reason for these sacrifices.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:2)


**ונרצה לו.** יפיק רצון מהשם:


AND IT SHALL BE ACCEPTED⁵⁶ (literally, he will be favored). Hence I.E.’s comment. FOR HIM. He will obtain favor from God.

footnotes:
⁵⁶ Hebrew, ve-nirtzeh


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:4:3)


**לכפר עליו.** להיות כופר עונש שיש עליו:


TO MAKE ATONEMENT FOR HIM.⁵⁷. To serve as a ransom for the punishment due him.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ Hebrew, le-khapper alav



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:1)


**לפני ה׳‎.** כמו עולת הצאן **על ירך המזבח צפונה.** כנגד השלחן:


BEFORE THE LORD. Like the burnt offering of the flock, on the side of the altar northward, opposite the table.⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁸ The table of showbread. The latter was placed in the northern part of the tabernacle.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:2)


וטעם **ושחט.** הכהן שישחטנו אחד **ויזרקו** רבים את הדם וכן כתוב וימציאו בני אהרן:


The meaning of *and he shall kill* is that the *kohen* shall kill. One slaughters⁵⁹ (and he shall kill) is in the singular. and many dash the blood.⁶⁰ (and dash) is in the plural and thus refers to Aaron’s sons. Scripture clearly states this i.e., *and Aaron’s sons delivered unto him the blood* (Lev. 9:12).

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Ve-shachat
⁶⁰ Ve-zareku


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:5:3)


וטעם **אשר פתח אוהל מועד.** להוציא מזבח הקטורת:


Scripture states, *that is at the door of the tent of meeting* because it wants to exclude the altar of incense.⁶¹

footnotes:
⁶¹ Which was inside the tent of meeting.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:6:1)


**והפשיט.** כהן או הלוי נלוה אליו:


AND HE SHALL FLAY. A *kohen* or a Levite accompanies him.⁶²

footnotes:
⁶² In order to assist him.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:7:1)


**ונתנו בני אהרן.** שלא יגשו הלוים וזאת מצוה על הכהנים ולא פחותים משנים:


AND THE SONS OF AARON THE PRIEST. Levites shall not approach.⁶³ This commandment was given to the *kohanim*. Not less than two⁶⁴. are required.⁶⁵

footnotes:
⁶³ To perform the service described in our verse.
⁶⁴ Kohanim
⁶⁵ For the verse speaks of sons of Aaron.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:8:1)


**הפדר.** רבים חכמי הדור פירשוהו הגוף והנכון בעיני שהיה הפדר החלבים וכן הוא וערכו בני אהרן הכהנים את הנתחים שהם נתחי הגוף את הראש ואת הפדר שהם החלבים ויחסר אות וי״ו שהוא כמנהג הלשון אודם פטדה וברקת ונתח אותו לנתחיו ולנתחי הגוף את ראשו ואת פדרו או יהיה פירוש את הראש עם הראש וכמוהו רבים והעד הנאמן ויקטר משה את הראש ואת הנתחים ואת הפדר:


AND THE SUET. Many wise men of the generation explain that the word *pader* (suet) refers to the carcass. However, in my opinion the *pader* refers to the fat. Our verse is to be understood as follows: *And Aaron’s sons, the priests, shall lay the pieces*, that is, the pieces of the carcass;⁶⁶ refers to the head and the suet. Hence I.E.’s comment. *and the head, and the suet*, that is, the fat. A connective *vav* is missing from the word *ha-rosh* (and the head).⁶⁷ (literally, the head) is to be read as if written *ve-ha-rosh* (and the head). In other words, our verse is to be read as follows: And Aaron’s sons, the priests, shall lay the pieces, and the head, and the suet. This is in keeping with the style of the language.⁶⁸. However, the text is to be understood as if it were present. Compare, *odem pitidah*⁶⁹ is to be read as if written *u-pitidah* (and carnelian). See I.E. on Ex. 1:4 (Vol. 2, p. 6). *u-vareket* (carnelian, topaz, and smargd) (Ex. 28:17). *And he shall cut it into its pieces* (v. 12)⁷⁰ (the burnt offering) into its pieces. I.E. points out that a parallel verse literally reads, *And he shall cut it into its pieces and its head* (ve-et rosho) *and its suet* (v. 12). We thus see that *its pieces* refers *to the* carcass *of* the animal, not to its head and suet. and along with the pieces, its head and its suet. On the other hand, *et ha-rosh* (and the head) might mean with the head.⁷¹ need not be placed before *ha-rosh*, for the meaning of our verse is: And Aaron’s sons, the priests, shall lay the pieces, with the head and the suet. There are many such instances.⁷² has the meaning of with. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 27). The proof of this⁷³ do not include the head and the suet. is: *Moses made the head, and the pieces, and the suet smoke* (Lev. 8:20).

footnotes:
⁶⁶ It is possible to read our verse as follows: And Aaron’s sons, the priests, shall lay the pieces, the head, and the suet. In other words, the pieces
⁶⁷ Ha-rosh
⁶⁸ Scripture occasionally omits the connective vav
⁶⁹ Pitidah
⁷⁰ And cut it
⁷¹ In this case a connective vav
⁷² Where et
⁷³ That the pieces


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 1:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/1:8:2)


**וערכו.** על בן הבקר כי הוא גדול ועל הכבש וערך:


SHALL LAY THE PIECES. Scripture reads *ve-arekhu* (shall lay)⁷⁴ (shall lay) is a plural. It literally means, and they shall lay. because it speaks of a bullock, a large animal.⁷⁵ Regarding a sheep,⁷⁶ Scripture states, *and the priest shall lay*⁷⁷, which is in the singular. *them* (v. 12).

## Chapter 2


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:1:1)


**ונפש כי תקריב.** נפש אדם והזכיר הנפש שהמנחה נדבה גם הנפש תקרא נדיבה ורוח נדיבה תסמכני:


AND WHEN ANY ONE BRINGETH. The word *nefesh* (anyone) means a person. Scripture mentions *nefesh*¹. However, in chapter 1, it refers to a person by the term *adam* (man). See Lev. 1:2. Hence I.E.’s comment. because the meal offering is a free will offering and *nefesh* is called “willing.”² also means spirit. Compare, *And let a willing spirit uphold me* (Ps. 51:14).³ and not *adam* see Rashi on verse 1.

footnotes:
¹ Scripture here refers to a person by the term nefesh
² Nefesh
³ Thus the deeper meaning of our verse is, when a willing spirit brings a free will offering unto the Lord. For another interpretation of why Scripture here employs the term nefesh


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:1:2)


**סלת.** קמח חטה נקיה והיא הנקראת בלשון קדר סמי״ד והנה אין ראוי להיות מנחה לגבוה כי אם מהמין שאין למעלה הימנו:


FINE FLOUR. The meaning of *solet* (fine flour) is pure wheat flour. It is the same as that which is called *samid* in Arabic. Look, it is not fitting that a meal offering brought to the Most High come from anything than which nothing better exists.⁴ What is the value of this work today? From an historical and intellectual view, Ibn Ezra’s commentary on the Pentateuch is most important, as explained previously in the preface to Genesis. His influence can be seen in all subsequent Hebrew Biblical commentaries, particularly that of Nahmanides. His influence is equally apparent on Maimonides both in his philosophical work, the *Guide of the Perplexed*, and his *halakhic* work, his *Mishneh Torah*, extending also to many later Biblical scholars and philosophers. All this is well documented in Dr. Strickman’s forewords to the five books of this set. In spite of all of this, however, the question of the English translation’s value still remains. In his introduction to his commentary on the Torah, Ibn Ezra writes, “With regard to verses which deal with laws, statutes and regulations, if we find two possible interpretations for a verse and one of them in keeping with the interpretation of the transmitters of tradition, all of whom were righteous men, then without reservation and with all our might we will rely on the truth of their words. Heaven forbid that we should join the Sadducees who claim that the traditions of the Rabbinic sages contradict the literal meaning of Scripture and the rules of grammar. The fact of the matter is that our ancient sages are true and all their words are true” (Vol. 1, pp. 18,19). However, it must be noted that the rabbis who posited post-Mosaic changes in the Torah were men of stellar reputation. Abarbanel (Num. 21:1) claimed, though his opinion is very questionable, that Nahmanides held such views regarding Num. 21:1-3. Furthermore, despite the fact that Maimonides’ thirteen principles of Judaism ultimately became part of the liturgy and thus were accepted at face value by all pious Jews, in practice not all of Maimonides’ pronouncements on what constitutes heresy were accepted by post-Maimonidian authorities. There was room for difference. For example, according to Maimonides one who directs his or her prayer to anyone but God is guilty of idol worship. Nevertheless, there are prayers that are directed to angels or to God’s attributes. Thus there had to be another reason for normative Judaism’s rejection of the notion of any post-Mosaic changes in the Torah.

footnotes:
⁴ In other words, it is unfit that a meal offering brought to the Most High come from anything but the best.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:1)


**וקמץ.** מגזרת לקמצים וקדמונינו פירשו קמץ מלא ודבריהם אמת:


AND HE SHALL TAKE. *Ve-kamatz* (and he shall take) is related to the word *li-kematzim* (in heaps) (Gen. 41:47).⁵ (and he shall take) means and he shall gather. Our sages explained that *melo kumtzo* means a handful.⁶ 9a, 18b. Their words are true.

footnotes:
⁵ According to this interpretation, ve-kamatz
⁶ See Menachot


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:2)


**מסלתה.** מעט ממנה וכן מהשמן רק כל הלבונה יקטיר:


OF THE FINE FLOUR THEREOF. A little of it, and so too of the oil. However, he shall burn all of the frankincense.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:2:3)


**אזכרתה.** האל״ף נוסף והטעם מה שהיה לו לזכרון לפני השם בעבור מנחתו ורבים פירשוהו כריח וקטורת כמו זכרו כיין לבנון:


THE MEMORIAL-PART THEREOF. The *alef* of *azkaratah* (the memorial part thereof) is superfluous.⁷ comes from the root *zayin, kaf, resh*. The *alef* serves no purpose. *Azkaratah* is so called because the meal offering by the celebrant serves as a memorial before God.⁸ means *the memorial part thereof*. God recalls the celebrant because of his meal offering. Others say that *azkaratah* means its scent. It is like scent of the incense.⁹ Compare, *The scent thereof* (zikhro) *shall be as the wine of Lebanon* (Hos. 14:8).

footnotes:
⁷ According to this opinion azkaratah
⁸ In other words, azkaratah
⁹ The portion of the meal offering offered on the altar is similar to the incense offered on the altar.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:5:1)

**קרבן ראשית תקריבו אותם לה׳‎.** כטעם שתים שני עשרונים המונפים לפני השם בחג השבועות וזה פירוש לה׳‎ אף על פי שהם קדש לה׳‎ הם למאכל הכהן:

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 2:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/2:14:1)


**ואם תקריב מנחת בכורים.** רבים אמרו שמלת אם חיוב ולפי דעתי כי אין צורך כי החיוב הוא ראשית בכורים ולא הבכורים והרוצה להביא מנחה מבכורים נדבה יביא:


AND IF THOU BRING A MEAL-OFFERING OF FIRSTFRUITS.²³. To be understood here as the first grain. Many say that the word *im* (if) indicates an obligation.²⁴ as when you bring a meal offering of the first grain. See Rashi: “*Im* here has the meaning of *ki* (when), for this offering is not optional.” Rashi believes that our verse deals with the *omer* (measure) of barley offered on the second day of Passover. This offering was obligatory. It was offered from the very first of the harvest. See Lev. 23:9-14. I believe that there is no need for this explanation, for there is an obligation to bring the first²⁵ of the grain²⁶ and not the first grain. One who wishes to bring a meal offering from the first grain²⁷ as a free will offering may do so.²⁸

²⁵ The very first.

²⁸ However, he is not obligated to do so.

THE FAT THEREOF, THE FAT TAIL ENTIRE. **נפש כי תחטא.** לעשות בשגגה אחת ממצות לא תעשה שיש עליהם כרת או מלקות. ומלת נפש כלל לישראל ולגר כי כן כתוב. ואחר כן פרט והחל מהכהן הגדול והוא הכהן המשיח:


IF ANY ONE SHALL SIN. By inadvertently transgressing one of the negative commandments, the penalty for which is excision¹ 1:1. or flogging [for a deliberate act].² The word *nefesh*³ (any one) takes in Israelites and strangers,⁴ for it is so stated.⁵ is used in reference to a stranger in Ex. 23:9. It is widely used in Scripture to refer to Israelites. See Ex. 12:15. Scripture then goes into detail.⁶ It begins with the *kohen gadol*, the anointed *kohen*.

TO BRING GUILT ON THE PEOPLE. The *kohen gadol* taught incorrectly and the people were guilty, all of them having acted inadvertently.⁷*hen gadol*. FOR HIS SIN. *Al chattato* (for his sin) means because of his sin.¹² (for his sin) literally means on his sin. Hence I.E.’s comment. Since the *kohen gadol* is great he must offer a bullock, which is the largest animal of all that are offered on the altar.

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:14:1)


**ונודעה החטאת.** וכן משפט הכהן הגדול ואם איננו נזכר כי אם לא הודע אליו חטאתו לא יקריב פר החטאת. ויש אומרים כי בכל שנה יקריב כן שמא חטא. והזכיר עם כל עדת ישראל ונודעה החטאת שהכהן יתכן שיודיעם ואין מי שיודיע לכהן רק מעצמו. וחטאת הקהל כחטאת הכהן הגדול בכל משפטיה. והנה הכהן הגדול שקול כנגד כל ישראל:


WHEN THE SIN WHEREIN THEY HAVE SINNED IS KNOWN. The same law,²¹. even though it is not mentioned,²². applies to the *kohen gadol*, for if the sin not be known to the *kohen gadol*, then he does not bring a sin offering. Others say that the *kohen gadol* shall bring this sacrifice²³ every year because he may have sinned.²⁴. Scripture mentions *the sin…is known* with regard to the whole congregation of Israel, for it is possible that the *kohen* will inform them. However, there is no one to inform the *kohen;* he informs himself.²⁵ realizes by himself that he has sinned. The sin offering of the congregation is the same as that of the *kohen gadol* in all details. The *kohen gadol* is thus equivalent to all of Israel.²⁶

²³ A sin offering.
²⁴ Hence Scripture does not state “when the sin wherein he sinned is known” with regard to the kohen gadol
²⁵ The kohen gadol
²⁶ Hence their sacrifices are equal.

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:21:1)


**חטאת הקהל הוא.** הפר ואם ישגו ישראל ולא יעשו אחת ממצות עשה יקריב פר לעולה ושעיר לחטאת:


IT IS THE SIN-OFFERING. The bullock.²⁹ is in the masculine. Hence it refers to the bullock, not to the sin offering. Thus our text is to be understood as follows: The bullock is the sin offering of the assembly. If Israel errs and they do not do one of the positive commandments, then they shall bring a bullock as a burnt offering and a he-goat as a sin offering.³⁰ while Num. 15:22 reads, *and not observe all these commandments*.

footnotes:
²⁹ The verse reads, chattat ha-kahal hu. Hu
³⁰ Our text tells us that if the congregation sins in error they then bring a bullock as a sin offering. However, Num. 15:22-24 tells us that if the congregation sins in error they shall bring a bullock as a burnt offering and a he-goat as a sin offering. Hence I.E. points out that our text deals with the violation of a negative precept while Numbers deals with the violation of a positive precept, for the text reads, and do any of the things which the Lord hath commanded not to be done



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:22:1)


**אשר נשיא יחטא.** הפוך וכן הוא אשר יחטא הנשיא והוא דבק באשר למעלה ואם כל עדת ישראל כאילו אמר ואם אשר יחטא הוא נשיא שבט או נשיא בית אב:


WHEN A RULER SINNETH. The words are inverted.³¹ (when a ruler sinneth). Our verse should be read as if written, when the sinner is the ruler.³². I.E. interprets thus because he believes that *asher* (when) is connected to the verb (*yechete*), not to the noun (*nasi*). It is connected to that which is written above,³³ (and if) but with *asher* (the one who). that is, *And if the whole congregation of Israel…* (v. 13). Our verse, as it were, states, and if the one who sins is the ruler of a tribe or the ruler of a father’s house.

³¹ Our verse reads, asher nasi yechete
³² In other words, our text should be interpreted as if written, asher yechete nasi

### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:23:1)


**או הודע אליו.** אחז הכתוב דרך קצרה כמו לכהן והטעם שידע הנשיא מעצמו שחטא או הודיעו אדם אחר שראהו. דקדוק הודע אליו פועל עבר כמו והצר לך ויחסר המודיע כמו אשר ילדה אותה ללוי ויאמר רבי משה הכהן כי הוא מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו כי החולם והשורק יתחלפו והוא על משקל ויוסף הורד מצרימה והנשיא יקריב שעיר מטעם זרזיר מתנים או תיש כפירוש הגאון בספר משלי והוא זכר למעלת הנשיא רק לא יובא את דמה לפנים מהקדש והכהנים יאכלו חטאת הנשיא לכפר בעדו כי כן כתוב ולא יאכל הכהן הגדול חטאתו:


IF HIS SIN…BE KNOWN TO HIM.³⁴ (literally, or if someone made known to him his sin). Scripture employs an abridged style.³⁵ It is similar to the verse dealing with the *kohen*.³⁶. There too Scripture employs an abridged style. See I.E.’s first comment on verse 10. Its meaning is, if the ruler knows on his own that he has sinned or someone else who has seen him³⁷ makes it known to him.³⁸ The following is the grammatical explanation of *hoda elav* (be known to him): *Hoda* (be known) is a verb³⁹. in the perfect.⁴⁰ (he made known). Compare, *ve-hetzar*⁴¹ is a *hifil*. It is a variant of *hetzir*. The same is true of *hoda, hodi’a*. *lekha* (and he shall besiege thee) (Deut. 28:52). The one who informs is omitted.⁴² is omitted. Scripture does not read, *o* *hoda ish elav chattato*. Compare, *whom she bore*⁴³ (Num. 26:59).⁴⁴ Rabbi Moses the Kohen says that *hoda* (be known) is a *hofal*,⁴⁵ should be rendered, be known. for the *cholam* and the *shuruk* interchange.⁴⁶ for this word is *huda*. Hence I.E. notes that *hoda* is a variant of *huda*. *Hoda* follows the paradigm of *hurad*⁴⁷. (was brought down) in *And Joseph was brought down to Egypt* (Gen. 39:1). The ruler offers a he-goat as a sacrifice in keeping with *The greyhound*; *the he-goat also* (Prov. 30:31).⁴⁸. The verse speaks of those who walk haughtily. The he-goat walks with its head held high, as does the king. Hence a goat was chosen as the animal to be offered by the ruler (the king) as a sin offering. The latter is in accordance with the interpretation of the Gaon in the Book of Proverbs. The offering consists of a male, in keeping with the status of the ruler. However, its blood is not brought inside of the sanctuary.⁴⁹ and the congregation. The *kohanim*, as is stated in Scripture,⁵⁰ eat of the ruler’s sin offering to atone for the ruler. The *kohen gadol* does not eat of his sin offering.⁵¹

³⁷ Sin.
³⁸ Our verse is to be interpreted as if written: The ruler realizes that he has sinned or if someone makes his sin known to him.
³⁹ A hifil
⁴⁰ It is a variant of hodi’a
⁴¹ Hetzar
⁴² The subject governing hoda
⁴³ Translated according to I.E.
⁴⁴ Scripture does not identify who did the bearing.
⁴⁵ A passive. In other words, hoda
⁴⁶ The usual form of the hofal
⁴⁷ Which is a hofal
⁴⁸ The complete verse reads: The greyhound; the he-goat also; And the king, against whom there is no rising up
⁴⁹ In contrast to the blood of the bullock brought by the kohen gadol
⁵⁰ See Lev. 6:19.
⁵¹ See I.E. on Lev. 6:16.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:27:1

**מעם הארץ.** והם כל ישראל והכהנים ההדיוטים וכל הלוים:


OF THE COMMON PEOPLE. The reference is to all of Israel, the ordinary *kohanim* and the Levites.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/4:27:2)


**ואשם.** פעל עבר כמו כי זקן יצחק:


AND BE GUILTY. *Ashem* (guilty) is a verb in the perfect. It is like *zaken* (was old) in *when Isaac was old* (Gen. 27:1).

A GOAT, A FEMALE. For his status is lower than that of the ruler.⁵²

### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:32:1

AND IF HE BRING A LAMB. If he brings a sin offering from the lamb species then he shall bring a female, as in the case of the she-goat.⁵⁴

### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 4:35:1

## Chapter 5


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:1:1)


**ונפש כי תחטא ושמעה קול אלה.** הוא החרם וכן ואת אלית והכתוב אחז דרך קצרה והזהיר שחייב העד להגיד כי אם לא יגיד יש עליו עונש מהשם שישא עונו אם שכח ולא הגיד ואחר כן נזכר לו:


AND IF ANY ONE SIN, IN THAT HE HEARETH THE VOICE OF ADJURATION. *Alah* (adjuration) refers to a curse. Similarly *alit* (thou didst utter a curse)¹ is the verbal form of *alah*. in *about which thou didst utter a curse* (Jud. 17:2). Scripture employs an abridged style.² Scripture warns that the witness is obligated to testify, for if he does not testify then he is liable for punishment from God, for he bears his iniquity. If he forgets and does not testify and then reminds himself [then he brings a guilt offering].

footnotes:
¹ Alit
² Before laying down the law regarding one who refuses to testify, it first should have stated that there is an obligation to bear witness (Filwarg).



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:2:1)


**או נפש אשר תגע.** בנבלת הארבעה הנזכרות והם חזיר וחביריו:


OR IF ANY ONE TOUCH ANY UNCLEAN THING. [WHETHER IT BE THE CARCASS OF AN UNCLEAN BEAST.] The reference is to the carcass of any of the four mentioned,³ namely, the swine⁴ and his compatriots.⁵

footnotes:
³ The four beasts explicitly mentioned in Lev. 11:4-7.
⁴ Lev. 11:7.
⁵ The camel (Lev. 11:4), the rock-badger (Lev. 11:5), and the hare (Lev. 11:6).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:2:2

OR THE CARCASS OF UNCLEAN CATTLE. Unclean for food such as horses or asses.⁶*The carcass of unclean swarming things* refers to the carcass of the eight swarming things mentioned in Scripture.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ These are not explicitly mentioned in the Torah.
⁷ In Lev. 11:29,30.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:2:3)


**ונעלם ממנו.** ואחר כך יודע או נגע בזב וטמא מת וזבה ונדה:


IT BEING HIDDEN FROM HIM. He learned of this⁸ afterwards.⁹ [3. OR IF HE TOUCH THE UNCLEANNESS OF MAN.] He touched a man that suffered a flux,¹⁰. See Lev. 15:2. or someone who was unclean because he touched a dead body,¹¹ or a woman who suffered from a flux,¹² or a menstruant.¹³

footnotes:
⁸ That he was unclean.
⁹ After coming into contact with what is sacred.
¹⁰ Hebrew, zav
¹¹ See Num. 19:11.
¹² See Lev. 15: 25.
¹³ See Lev. 15:19.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:4:1)


**לבטא בשפתים.** דבור שפה:


UTTER CLEARLY WITH AN OATH. *Le-vatte visefatayim* (utter clearly) means to utter with the lips.¹⁴ means lips. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Sefatayim


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:4:2)


**להרע.** למי שהוא חייב או לפירוש הגאון להתענות. וטעם להזכיר אלה שהוא חייב להודות ועל האחרות שהן מצות לא תעשה לא יתודה לפני הכהן והקרבן אחד:


TO DO EVIL. To a guilty person,¹⁵ or, as the Gaon¹⁶ explains, to fast.¹⁷ Scripture mentions these sins¹⁸ because the violator is obligated to confess these sins. He is not obligated to confess before the *kohen* for other sins which consist of the violation of negative commandments.¹⁹ The sacrifice is the same.²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁵ To a person who is guilty of a capital crime or guilty of an offence that is punished by flogging. So Krinsky and Sarim. I.E. comments thus because a person would not be held to be violating an oath to harm an innocent man.
¹⁶ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
¹⁷ In other words, to do evil means to do evil to himself. See Rashi.
¹⁸ The point is: Scripture stated earlier that if one sins, he has to bring a sin offering (Lev. 4:27-35). It now lists a number of sins, i.e., refusing to testify, breaking an oath, coming into contact with that which is sacred when one is unclean. It then goes on to say that one who violates the latter brings the same sacrifice as those mentioned earlier in Lev: 4:27-35. The question thus arises: Why list these sins when they are apparently dealt with in Lev. 4:27-35?
¹⁹ In other words, the sins alluded to in Lev. 4:27-35.
²⁰ For the sins alluded to in Lev. 4:27-35 and for those mentioned in verses 1-4.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:7:1)


**ואם לא תגיע ידו.** כי היד היא העושה מלאכה והיא הקונה רמז למי שאין לו:


AND IF HIS MEANS²¹ (literally, his hand). SUFFICE NOT.²² For all manner of work is done by the hand. It is the means by which one acquires. The clause²³. alludes to one who does not have the means.²⁴ means: And if his means do not suffice.

footnotes:
²¹ Hebrew, yado
²² Literally, and if his hand does not acquire. Hence I.E.’s comment.
²³ And if his hand does not acquire
²⁴ To buy a lamb or a goat. In other words, And if his hand does not acquire


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:7:2)


**אחד לחטאת ואחד לעולה.** ויאמר רב יצחק כי טעם אחד עולה בעבור שלא תגיע ידו אולי עלה על רוחו מחשבה והקרוב אלי שהאחד כנגד האמורים והשני חטאת כמשפט:


ONE FOR A SIN-OFFERING, AND THE OTHER FOR A BURNT-OFFERING. Rabbi Isaac²⁵ says the reason that one bird is brought for a burnt offering is that an unseemly thought may have entered his mind because his means do not suffice.²⁶, a burnt offering, is offered in atonement for improper thoughts. See I.E. on Lev. 1:4. However, I believe that the bird brought as an offering corresponds to the organs of the sacrifice offered on the altar.²⁷ The second bird is a sin offering, as the law requires.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁵ According to Weiser the reference is to Rabbi Isaac Gaon of Sura.
²⁶ He might have been angry at God for his poverty. According to I.E. an olah
²⁷ See Lev. 3:9,10; 14:5. The bird brought as a burnt offering takes the place of the organs of the lamb or goat brought to the altar.
²⁸ For those who sin and repent.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:11:1)


**עשירת האיפה.** מאכל לאיש אחד ביום אחד:


THE TENTH PART OF AN EPHAH. The amount of food a person eats in one day.²⁹

²⁹ See Ex. 16:36.



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:15:1)


**תמעל מעל.** דבר שנתכסה עליו מגזרת מעיל והטעם כי חטאת הוא בקדשי השם יביא איל שהוא שוה כסף שקלים ומעוט רבים שנים:


COMMIT A TRESPASS. The reference is to something which covered the offender.³⁰ *Timol ma’al* (commit a trespass) is related to the word *me’il* (garment).³¹ literally means to garment a garment that is, to cover oneself with a garment. The reason why he shall bring a ram³² worth silver shekels³³. is that he sinned against that which is sanctified to God. The minimum of the plural *shekalim* (shekels) is two.

footnotes:
³⁰ The offender is compared to a person who covers himself with a garment which does not belong to him.
³¹ Thus, timol ma’al
³² And not a lamb or goat, which is a less expensive offering.
³³ Hebrew, shekalim



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:16:1)


**ישלם.** וישלם אשר חטא מן הקדש עם תוס׳‎ חמישתו וזה הוא אשם בשגגה בתחלה ואחר כך ידע:


HE SHALL MAKE RESTITUTION. He shall make restitution for sinning by using a holy object and add a fifth.³⁴ This is a guilt offering brought by one who errs at first and then becomes cognizant of his sin.

footnotes:
³⁴ A fifth of its value.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:17:1)


**ואם נפש כי תחטא.** ועשה אחת ממצות לא תעשה ולא ידע אם הוא אסור יביא איל ודעת רבים שזה אשם תלוי לא ידע אם עשה אם לא עשה והחטאת שבתחלה לא ידע ואחר כן ידע והנה החטאת שלא ידע שהוא אסור עד שהודע והאשם ידע שהוא אסור ושכח ואחר כן נזכר או אשם תלוי:


AND IF ANY ONE SIN.³⁵ And violates one of the negative commandments and does not know that what he did is prohibited,³⁶ he shall bring a ram.³⁷ Many believe³⁸ 23a. that the ram is brought as a “doubt guilt offering.” He brings a ram if he does not know whether he violated the law or not.³⁹) and permitted fat (*shuman*) lay before him, and he believed that both were permissible fat and he ate one of them. Afterwards, however he was informed that one of these was forbidden fat, and he does not know whether he has eaten the forbidden fat. Such a person has to bring a guilt-offering in doubt” (Rashi). A sin offering is brought if he did not know that he sinned and then became aware of it. Observe,⁴⁰ a sin offering is brought if he did not know that a certain act is prohibited until he was informed of it. A guilt offering is brought if he knew that it is prohibited but forgot, and then reminded himself. On the other hand, our verse might be speaking of a “doubt guilt offering.”⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁵ According to Lev. 4:27,28,32 when a person sins he brings a female lamb or a she-goat. However, according to our verse one who sins brings a ram. Hence the interpretations which follow.
³⁶ And then learns that what he did was prohibited. However, if he knows that the act is prohibited but forgets the law and then reminds himself he brings a sin offering.
³⁷ As a guilt offering.
³⁸ This is the opinion of the rabbis. See Keritot
³⁹ “This speaks of a person who is in doubt whether he has inadvertently committed an act of such a character as to be punishable with excision. For example, forbidden fat (chelev
⁴⁰ According to the latter opinion (Krinsky).
⁴¹ In other words, if we accept the opinion that a sin offering is brought for ignorance of the law, then we still have two options regarding the guilt offering spoken of in our verse.

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:22:1)


**ונשבע על שקר.** פירושו או נשבע על ממון שיבקש אדם ממנו והעד או מכל אשר ישבע עליו לשקר:


AND SWEAR TO A LIE. The meaning of *ve-nishba al shaker* (and swear to a lie) is, or swear to a lie⁴⁹ prefaced to *va-nishba* means or, rather than and. regarding money that one seeks from him. The fact that Scripture states, *or any thing about which he has sworn falsely* (v. 24)⁵⁰ is proof of this. The *heh* of *va-hennah* (therein) is superfluous.⁵¹ is a variant of *hen*.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:24:1)


**בראשו.** בעצמו או מה שהוא שוה:


IN FULL. *Be-rosho* (in full)⁵² literally means a head. Thus *be-rosho* literally means for its head. means in itself,⁵³ or its worth.

⁵² The word rosh
⁵³ Head is a metaphor for the entire body. It thus can be taken to mean the entire thing.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:24:2)


**וחמשתיו.** מיעוט רבים שנים והנה הם שני חמישיות ועוד אפרשנו:


THE FIFTH PART. The minimum of a plural is two. Thus *chamishitav*⁵⁴ is a plural. (the fifth part) means two fifths. I will explain it later.⁵⁵

⁵⁴ Chamishitav
⁵⁵ See I.E. on Num. 5:7 (Vol. 4, p. 35): “If he confesses on his own (then he adds a fifth part). However, if there are witnesses then he shall add two fifths. Those who transmitted the law say a fifth of a fifth. Their minds are greater than ours.”


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:24:3

**ביום אשמתו.** טעמו ביום שובו מאשמתו יביא איל:


IN THE DAY OF HIS BEING GUILTY. Its meaning is, in the day that he turns away from his guilt⁵⁶ he shall bring a ram.

footnotes:
⁵⁶ When he repents.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 5:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/5:25:1)

ACCORDING TO THY VALUATION. As the first one.⁵⁷. See I.E. on verse 15. Scripture adds fifths⁵⁸ because this guilt offering is brought for a deliberate sin. There is an individual opinion that this guilt offering is also⁵⁹ a “doubt-offering.”

TO BE GUILTY THEREBY. *Ashmah* (guilty) is a noun in the *kal*. Scripture employs it in place of the infinite.⁶⁰.

## Chapter 6


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:2:1)


**היא העולה.** נקראת כן בעבור שהיא עולה כלה על המזבח ובכאן רמז שלא יעלה עולה בלילה רק היא תהיה על מוקדה כל הלילה. וה״א מוקדה נוסף או מוקד ומוקדה הם שני שמות:


IT IS THAT WHICH GOETH UP. The burnt offering is called an *olah*¹ means goes up. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. because it is entirely consumed (*olah*) on the altar.², see I.E. on Lev 1:4. According to Meijler this interpretation complements the one on Lev. 1:4. That is, the *olah* goes up on the altar to atone for that which enters (goes up on) one’s mind. Scripture here hints that the *olah* should not be offered at night but rather that it shall be on its firewood until the morning.³ shall remain on the firewood all night, not that it should be brought upon the firewood at night. The *heh* of *mokedah* (its firewood)⁴ is a variant of *moked* (fire). Hence I.E.’s interpretation. It should be noted that I.E. renders *mokedah* “fire” (firewood), not its fire (or firewood), for there is no *mappik* in the *heh*. is superfluous. On the other hand, *moked* and *mokedah* may be two nouns.⁵

footnotes:
¹ The word olah
² For another interpretation of the term olah
³ For Scripture states that the olah
⁴ Mokedah
⁵ Both meaning firewood.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:2:2)


**ואש המזבח תוקד בו.** ולא חוץ ממנו:


AND THE FIRE OF THE ALTAR SHALL BE KEPT BURNING THEREBY.⁶ (on it). According to I.E. our clause should be rendered: And the fire of the altar shall be kept burning on it (the altar). Hence his interpretation. Not outside.

⁶ Hebrew, bo



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:3:1)


**מדו.** חלוק וחכמים אמרו שהוא חנוק כנגד מדתו:


HIS LINEN GARMENT. The word *middo* means a garment.⁷ of *middo* is not a pronominal suffix. It is rather superfluous. See the word *beno* (son of) (Num. 24:3). The wise men⁸ said that it is a garment in keeping with his size.⁹ 23:2. The word *middah* means a measure. According to the rabbis *middo* means his measure. Also see Rashi: “This is what is elsewhere termed the *ketonet*; and why then is it here called *middo*? To intimate that it (the *ketonet*) must be made to his measure.”

The meaning of *ba-boker ba-boker* is, every morning.¹³ The *kohen* shall first offer the burnt offering and then the organs¹⁴ of the peace offering.¹⁵, and afterwards goes on to say, *and shall make smoke thereon the fat of the peace-offerings*.

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:7:1)


**הקרב אותה.** שם הפועל וטעם הקרב יקריבו אותה בני אהרן וטעמו אחד מבני אהרן על כן והרים ממנו. והוספו זאת הפרשה על הכתובה בפרשת ויקרא:


SHALL OFFER IT. *Hakrev* (shall offer) is an infinitive.¹⁷ might be taken to be an imperative. The meaning of *hakrev otah bene aharon* is, the sons of Aaron shall offer it. This means that one of the sons of Aaron shall offer it. Scripture therefore reads, *And he shall take up there-from* (v. 8).¹⁸. This section¹⁹ adds to that which is written in the portion *Va-Yikra*.²⁰

¹⁷ Hakrev
¹⁸ Since the reference is to one of the sons of Aaron, Scripture employs the singular in verse 8, And he shall take up there-from

IT SHALL BE EATEN WITHOUT LEAVEN IN A HOLY PLACE. Note, Scripture adds two commandments.²¹ *Every male* (v. 11)²² is a third.²³

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:10:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:13:2)


**ביום המשח אותו.** אשר יוצק על ראשו שמן המשחה ורבים אמרו כי זה בי״ת תחת מ״ם והטעם כי מיום המשח אותו הנה זה חייב להקריב תמיד מנחתו:


IN THE DAY WHEN…ANOINTED. When the anointing oil is poured upon his head. Some say that the *bet* of *be-yom* (in the day) is in place of a *mem*.²⁹ (in the day) should be interpreted as from the day. Its meaning is, from the day that he is anointed he has to regularly bring his meal offering.³⁰

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:14:1)


**מרבכת.** אין לו אח ויש מי שאומרים רכה ויש מי שאומרים במהרה. ותי״ו תביאנה סימן לאהרן וכן תי״ו תקריב. ועל דעתי כי כן הוא תי״ו תפיני וטעמו תקון ואין ריע לו והקדמונים אמרו שהיא שתי מלות תאפה נא. ויאמר רבי יונה המדקדק שהוא על משקל דוכיפת וזה המשקל מעקל:


WHEN IT IS SOAKED. *Murbekhet* (when it is soaked) has no brother.³¹ Some say it means soft.³² being a variant of *murkhekhet*. Others say that *murbekhet* means quickly. The *tav*³³ serves as a second person masculine imperfect prefix. of *tevi’ennah* (thou shalt bring it) is directed at Aaron.³⁴ Similarly the *tav* of *takriv* (thou shalt offer).³⁵ is directed to Aaron. I believe that this is also true of the *tav* of *tufine* (in broken pieces).³⁶ It means to fix.³⁷ means you shall bake. See Rashi. It has no neighbor.³⁸ The ancients³⁹ on this verse. said that *tufine* is a combination of the two words, *te’afeh* and *na* (it shall be baked raw).⁴⁰ Rabbi Jonah the grammarian⁴¹ says that *tufine* follows the paradigm of *dukhifat* (hoopoe) (Lev. 11:19).⁴² comes from the root *tav, peh, nun*. See *Sefer Ha-Rikmah*, Vol. 1, p. 137, ed. by M. Wilinsky, Jerusalem, 1964. This is a distorted form.⁴³) are vocalized with a *cholem*. See *Sefer Ha-Rikmah, Ibid*.

³³ The tav

³⁶ It is an imperfect prefix directed at Aaron.
³⁷ That is, bake. According to I.E. tufine
³⁸ The word is not found again in Scripture.
³⁹ See Sifra
⁴⁰ It shall not be fully baked.
⁴¹ Rabbi Jonah ibn Janach.
⁴² According to Rabbi Jonah tufine
⁴³ Not all the words in this form (po’il

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:16:1)

IT SHALL NOT BE EATEN. As all the meal offerings are, for how can a *kohen* eat his meal or sin offering.⁴⁴ The place of the slaughter of all the sin offerings was in the north.⁴⁵

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:18:1)

IT IS MOST HOLY. It is like one of the other holy things.⁴⁶ (it is most holy) does not mean it is most holy but is to be interpreted as if written, *kodesh ke-ha-kodashim* (holy like the holy; that is, it is one of the holy things).

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:19:1)


**הכהן המחטא.** טעמו הזורק דם כאילו אמר המסיר חטא החוטא ורבי המפרשים אמרו כי טעם המחטא רוחץ או מטהר וכן תחטאני באזוב ואטהר:


THE PRIEST THAT OFFERETH IT FOR SIN. *Ha-kohen ha-mechatte* (the priest that offereth it for sin) means the *kohen* who sprinkles the blood. *Ha-kohen ha-mechatte* is another way of saying the *kohen* that removes the sin of the sinner.⁴⁷ literally means “removes sins.” Many of the commentaries say that *ha-mechatte* means who washes or cleans. The word *techatteni* (purge me) in *Purge me with hyssop, and I shall be clean* (Ps. 51:9) is similar.

footnotes:
⁴⁷ The word mechatte



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:1)


**כל אשר יגע.** בבשר החטאת יהיה קדש לשם והכהן יאכלנו לכן חלק הזורק כחלק כל אחיו:


WHATSOEVER SHALL TOUCH. Whatsoever shall touch the flesh of the sin offering will be holy unto God, and the *kohen* shall eat it. The portion of the *kohen* who sprinkles the blood of the sin offering is therefore⁴⁸ who sprinkles the blood removes the sin from the sinner…the portion of the *kohen* who sprinkles the blood is therefore equal to the portion of all the other *kohanim*. (Filwarg). like⁴⁹ the portion of all his brothers.⁵⁰. According to I.E. the *kohen* who sprinkles the blood of the sin offering receives half of its flesh. The remainder is shared by the other *kohanim* (Filwarg).

footnotes:
⁴⁸ For the sprinkling “removes” the sin from the sinner. This comment relates to the previous verse. It is to be understood as follows: The kohen
⁴⁹ Is equal to.
⁵⁰ The other kohanim


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:2)


**יזה.** א״ר משה הכהן שהוא כמו יטה מבנין קל ובפתחו׳‎ היו״ד מהכבד והאות המובלע נו״ן וכן ויז מדמ׳‎ אל הקיר בחיר״ק עם ויז בפתח רק במלת נטה מראה על הקיר בחיר״ק עם ויז הוא בצר״י בבנין הקל וכן מצאנו ויז נצחם מבנין הקל ויפה דקדק.


IS SPRINKLED. Rabbi Mosheh Ha-Kohen says that *yizzeh* (is sprinkled)⁵¹. is similar to the word *yitteh*⁵². (shall…bend) (Job 15:29).⁵³ in the imperfect and is similarly vocalized. It is a *kal*. When the *yod*⁵⁴ prefixed to the word in the imperfect. is vocalized with a *pattach* it is a *hifil*.⁵⁵ or *yatteh*. The swallowed letter⁵⁶. is a *nun*.⁵⁷ of the root *nun, zayin, heh* is swallowed by the *dagesh*. Hence the word *yizzeh*. We similarly find the word *va-yiz* (and…was sprinkled) in *and some of her blood was sprinkled* (II Kings 9:33) vocalized with a *chirik*, and *vayaz* with a *pattach*.⁵⁸ and the *hifil* in shortened forms. *Va-yiz* in place of *va-yizzeh* and *va-yaz* in place of *va-yazzeh*. However, the word *natah* (turned) is vocalized with a *tzereh* in the *kal*.⁵⁹ imperfect. Compare, *yet* (stretched) in *va-yet moshe et yado* (and Moses stretched forth his hand) (Ex. 10:22). We similarly find *ve-yez nitzcham* (and their lifeblood is dashed) (Is. 63:3).⁶⁰ can be vocalized with a *chirik* or a *tzereh* in the *kal* imperfect. He explained it very well.⁶¹. 38. The reading in the *Mikra’ot Gedolot* is very difficult to explain and is apparently corrupt (see Filwarg, Weiser, Meijler, Krinsky). It reads, “However, the word *natah* (turned) in *natah mareh el ha-kir* is vocalized with a *chirik*. We find it vocalized with a *tzereh* in the *kal*. Compare, *ve-yez* in *ve-yez nitzcham* (and their lifeblood is dashed) (Is. 63:3), wherein *ve-yez* is in the *kal*. He [Rabbi Mosheh HaKohan] explained it very well.” There is no verse that reads, *natah marehah el ha-kir*. The above can only be explained by emending the text. For a proposed emendation, see Meijler.

footnotes:
⁵¹ From the root nun, zayin, heh
⁵² From the root nun, tet, heh
⁵³ It drops the nun
⁵⁴ The yod
⁵⁵ Literally, from the heavy [conjugation] yazzeh
⁵⁶ The letter swallowed by the dagesh
⁵⁷ The first root letter. In other words, the nun
⁵⁸ The word appears both in the kal
⁵⁹ In the kal
⁶⁰ I.E.’s point is that the root nun, zayin, heh
⁶¹ We have followed the reading Vat. Ebr


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:3)


ובעבור שהחטאת קדש צוה השם לכבס המקום שנפל מדמה על הבגד **במקום קדוש** הוא חצר אהל מועד כי יש הפרש בין קדוש וטהור:


The Lord commanded that the place where some of the blood of the sin offering fell on the garment be washed because the sin offering is holy. [IN A HOLY PLACE.] The reference is to the courtyard of the tent of meeting, for there is a difference between holy and clean.⁶²

footnotes:
⁶² A clean place is a place that is ritually clean. Thus if Scripture had read “in a clean place,” it could have referred to any place that is ritually clean. It would not necessarily have meant the courtyard of the tent of meeting.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:20:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:20:4)


**אשר יזה עליה.** יחסר מקום או. והטעם או אם יזה אחר עליה שהוא הבשר יכבס הבשר הכהן ואחר כך יאכלנו או פירושו המקום אשר יזה מהדם תכבס ויהיה בגד לשון נקבה וזכר וכמוהו אשר תשיג ידו ותי״ו תכבס לכהן:


THAT WHEREON IT WAS SPRINKLED.⁶³, literally (that is sprinkled upon it). The word “place” or the word “or” is missing.⁶⁴) there is sprinkled of the blood thereof upon any garment, that (*asher*) it is sprinkled upon it, you shall wash in a holy place. The aforementioned is repetitious and ambiguous. I.E. suggests two possible interpretations. One, our verse is to be interpreted: And when (*asher*) there is sprinkled of the blood thereof upon any garment, or if one (*asher*) sprinkles upon it (the flesh of the sin offering), you shall wash it in a holy place. Two: And when (*asher*) there is sprinkled of the blood thereof upon any garment, the place where (*makom asher*) something is sprinkled upon it (the garment), you shall wash in a holy place. It means: Or if someone shall sprinkle upon it, that is, upon the flesh, then the *kohen* shall wash the flesh and then eat it. On the other hand, its meaning might be, the place where the blood is sprinkled shall be washed. In this case the word *beged* (garment) is both masculine and feminine.⁶⁵ (garment) is usually masculine. The word *aleha* (it) is feminine. Thus if “it” refers to *beged* (garment) then *beged* is also a feminine noun. The word *yado* (his hand) in *even as his hand can afford* (Lev. 14:31)⁶⁶ is similar.⁶⁷ is treated as both masculine and feminine. It is treated as feminine in Lev. 13:21, for the noun governing it (*tasig*) is in the feminine. However, it is treated as masculine in Ex. 17:12, where Scripture employs a masculine adjective to describe the hands of Moses. The *tav*⁶⁸ of *tekhabbes* is a second person imperfect prefix. of *tekhabbes* (thou shalt wash) is directed to the *kohen*.⁶⁹ in *thou shalt wash* is directed to the *kohen*.

⁶³ Hebrew, asher yizzeh alehah
⁶⁴ So Krinsky. The verse literally reads: And when (asher
⁶⁵ The word beged
⁶⁶ Translated according to I.E.
⁶⁷ The word yad
⁶⁸ The tav
⁶⁹ In other words, the term thou



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:21:1)


**תבשל בו.** גם בשלה ומורק ושוטף מבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו ובא ומורק בחול״ם בעבור הרי״ש שלא תדגש כי אם במקומות מועטים במשקל לא זורק עליו מגזרת מרקו הרמחים כי המ״ם שרש:


WHEREIN IT IS SODDEN. *Bushalah* (sodden), *morak* (scoured), and *shuttaf* (rinsed) are all *pu’als. Morak* is vocalized with a *cholam*⁷⁰, as is the case in the *pu’al*. because of the *resh* which, except in a few places,⁷¹ records seven instances in Scripture when a *dagesh* is placed in the *resh* (Krinsky). does not receive a *dagesh*.⁷² is placed in the middle root letter in the *pu’al*. However, the *resh* does not receive a *dagesh*. Hence the vowel changes from a *kubbutz to* a *cholam*. *Morak* follows the paradigm of *zorak* (dashed)⁷³. in *was not dashed against him* (Num. 19:13). *Morak* is related to the word *mirku* (furbish) in *Furbish the spears* (*Jer*. 46:4), for the *mem* of *morak* is a root letter.

footnotes:
⁷⁰ Rather than with a kubbutz
⁷¹ The massorah
⁷² A dagesh
⁷³ A pu’al



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:22:1)


וטעם **קדש קדשים הוא.** עם כל זכר שאין ראוי לאכול החטאת שהקריבו ממנה והובא לכפר כי אם התמימים והזכר תמים מהנקבה ואפילו קטן יקרא זכר והזכר שהוא בן שלש עשרה שנה כאשר העתיקו אבותינו:


[IT IS MOST HOLY.] Scripture states *it is most holy* with regard to every male⁷⁴ because only the perfect may eat from a sin offering whose organs are offered and is brought for atonement for sins.⁷⁵ with regard to all male *kohanim* in order to teach us that only the “perfect” may eat from the sin offering because it is most holy. Males are more perfect⁷⁶ than females.⁷⁷ Even a small child is called a male.⁷⁸ A male who is thirteen years old [is considered an adult] as our fathers have transmitted.

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Scripture states that the sin offering is most holy in verse 18. The question thus arises, Why repeat it again in our verse?
⁷⁵ Scripture repeats it is most holy
⁷⁶ I.E. believed that males are superior to females.
⁷⁷ No female may eat from the sin offering.
⁷⁸ In other words, a male under the age of 13 may eat of the flesh of a burnt offering.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 6:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/6:23:1)


**וכל חטאת אשר יובא מדמה וגו**׳‎ **לכפר בקדש.** והנה מקום הפרכת יקרא קדש כי החצר ואם היא קדש כנגד אהל מועד חול והנה גם יקרא לפנים הפרכת קדש כנגד אהל מועד כמו בזאת יבא אהרן אל הקדש וזאת החטאת היא חטאת כהן הגדול או הקהל:


AND NO SIN-OFFERING, WHEREOF ANY OF THE BLOOD IS BROUGHT INTO…THE HOLY PLACE. Note, the place of the “veil”⁷⁹) separating the “holy” from the “most holy.” See Ex. 26:33. is called holy,⁸⁰ for the courtyard,⁸¹ even though it is holy, is not holy in comparison to the tent of meeting.⁸² Observe, the inside of the curtain is called holy in comparison to the tent of meeting. Compare, *Herewith shall Aaron come into the holy place* (Lev. 16:3).⁸³ The sin offering spoken of in our verse is the sin offering of the *kohen gadol* or of the congregation.⁸⁴ or of the congregation is brought into the tent of meeting. See Lev. 4:3-5; 13-16.

**וזאת תורת האשם.** כבר הודעתיך ההפרש בין חטאת ואשם אף על פי שהכתוב יאמר בחטאת אשם ובאשם חטאת. וטעם להזכיר זאת הפרשה להזכיר החלבים שאין להם זכר בתורת החטאת:


AND THIS IS THE LAW OF THE GUILT-OFFERING. I have previously¹ explained to you the difference between a sin offering². and a guilt offering.³. [There is a difference between the two] even though Scripture employs the term guilt when referring to a sin offering⁴ (ashamo)…*for his sin*. Also Lev. 4:13, 14, *And are guilty* (ve-ashemu)…*then the assembly shall offer…a sin-offering*. and the word sin when referring to a guilt offering.⁵. Scripture records this section⁶ because it wants to make mention of the fat,⁷ to which no reference is made when Scripture earlier records the law of the sin offering.⁸. There the Torah deals with sin and guilt offerings.

ANY MAN’S BURNT-OFFERING. The word *olat* (burnt offering) is penultimately accented⁹ in accordance with the rules of Hebrew grammar.¹⁰ It is so accented because the word that follows¹¹ (man). has the accent on the first part of the word.

AND ALL THAT IS DRESSED. The word *na’asah* (dressed) is an adjective¹³ in the feminine. It is like the word *nirah* (seen) in *there was no stone seen* (I Kings 6:18). The masculine form of the word is found in *Now that which was prepared* (na’aseh) (Neh. 5:18).

UNLEAVENED CAKE.¹⁶ (literally cakes). As many as he desires.¹⁷ However, he cannot bring fewer than two.¹⁸ The same applies to the unleavened wafers.¹⁹.

SOAKED. I believe that the best interpretation of *solet murbekhet* (fine flour soaked) is chosen flour. The unleavened cakes²⁰. and the wafers²¹. were baked. The cakes made from chosen flour were not so treated.²² 76b.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:16:3)

ON THE DAY THAT HE OFFERETH HIS SACRIFICE. The word *hakrivo* is an infinitive²⁸ in the *hifil*. [AND ON THE MORROW.] On the morrow of that day.²⁹

²⁸ With a pronominal suffix.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:16:4)


וי״ו **והנותר ממנו.** כפ״א רפ״ה בלשון ישמעאל וכמוהו ויעזב את עבדו גם והארץ היתה תהו ובהו:


[THAT WHICH REMAINETH.] The *vav* of *ve-ha-notar* (that which remaineth)³⁰ has the same meaning as an unaspirated *fa* in Arabic.³¹ is to be rendered, *that which remaineth*, not and that which remaineth. So too Rashi. The same applies to the *vav* in *va-ya’azov* (left) in *left his servants* (Ex. 9:21)³² is to be rendered left, not and he left. and the *vav* in *ve-ha-aretz* (now the earth) in *Now the earth was unformed and void* (Gen. 1:2).³³

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:17:1

SHALL BE BURNT WITH FIRE. Like the parts of a sacrifice which Scripture designates for burning. However, it should not be burned on the altar.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:1)


וטעם **המקריב אותו לא יחשב.** אחר שיהיו האימורים קרבים לגבוה הנה כל הנשאר קדש וכן אבני המזבח שאחר שהם קדושים אין ראוי שישאר ממנו לחול על כן אבנים שלימות:


[NEITHER SHALL IT BE IMPUTED UNTO HIM THAT OFFERETH IT.] Its meaning is, since Scripture designates that specific parts of the sacrifice be offered to God, all that remains of the animal is holy. Similarly the stones of the altar.³⁴ Since they are holy, it is unfit that anything that remains from them be used in a non-sacred way. Scripture therefore reads, *of unhewn stones* (Deut. 27:6).³⁵

footnotes:
³⁴ The stones used in constructing the altar.
³⁵ The stones used for building the altar were not to be hewn, because when stones are hewn the chips that fall might be employed for non-sacred purposes or might be cast into a filthy place. See I.E. on Ex. 20:22 (Vol. 2, pp. 443-445).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:2)


**פגול.** כדברי המתרגם ארמית וכן ומרק פגולים כליהם. ויש שואלים אחר שעלה ריח ניחוח איך לא יחשב והטעם על המחשבה כי משפט השלמים להיות קדש והפגול איננו קדש ועוד בעבור שלא שמר את המצוה יצא שכרו בהפסדו אם הוא שלמים ואם נדר תתחדש עליו עון כנגד הראשון אם לא ישלם נדרו:


ABHORRED. The word *piggul* (abhorred) is to be rendered as the Aramaic translator does.³⁶ by *merachak* (removed). Similarly the word *piggulim* (abominable things) in *And broth of abominable things is in their vessels* (Is. 65:4). Some ask, “Since the sweet savor ascended,³⁷ how can it not be accepted?”³⁸ The answer is that it depends on his intention. According to the law, the peace offerings must be holy. However that which is abhorred is not holy.³⁹ Furthermore,⁴⁰ the celebrant’s gain⁴¹ disappears in his loss⁴² with regard to a peace offering. In the case of a vow offering, there is a new sin⁴³ corresponding to the first⁴⁴ if he does not fulfil his vow.⁴⁵

footnotes:

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:18:3)


**עונה תשא.** עונש ולא הזכירו הכתוב וכרת על טמא שיאכל בשר קדש:


SHALL BEAR HIS INIQUITY. His punishment.⁴⁶ (iniquity) at times has the meaning of punishment. See I.E. on Gen 4:18 (Vol. 1, p. 84). Scripture does not mention the punishment.⁴⁷ One who is unclean and eats sacred flesh is punished with *karet* (excision).⁴⁸

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:20:1)


וטעם **וטומאתו.** להיותו טמא מעצמו כזב ומצורע גם בעבור מקרה לילה כי כן כתוב כי אמר מקרה הוא או שיהיה טמא בעבור אחר כטומאת אדם מת או חי והוא זב ומצורע או בבהמה טמאה שאינה נאכלת ושקץ טמא מהעוף והשרץ. ופעם אחת בא אלי צדוקי אחד ושאלני אם האליה אסורה מן התורה ואען ואומר אמת כי האליה תקרא חלב כי כן כתוב חלבו האליה תמימה רק קדמונינו התירוה ואסרו כל חלב אז ענה הלא כל חלב אסור מן התורה כי כן כתוב כל חלב וכל דם לא תאכלו ובתחלה כתוב חקת עולם לדורותיכם גם אני עניתיו כי זה הפסוק דבק עם זבח השלמים ואין מלת חקת עולם לדורותיכם בכל מושבותיכם ראיה גמורה כי הנה כתוב ולחם וקלי וכרמל לא תאכלו עד עצם היום הזה עד הביאכם את קרבן אלהיכם ושם כתוב חקת עולם ואם כן לא נאכל לחם בגלות כי לא הקרבנו קרבן העומר וגם הוא השיב כל חלב שור וכשב וען לא תאכלו גם אני השיבותי כי גם זה הפסוק דבק עם זבח השלמים והעד כי כל אוכל חלב מן הבהמה אשר יקריבו ממנה להוציא חלב כל בשר שאיננו קרב לשלמים והכלל בשר חול על כן הזכיר בפרשה הזאת וחלב נבלה וחלב טרפה יעשה לכל מלאכה ואכול לא תאכלוהו וידוע כי בשר הנבלה והטרפה אסורות והאסור הוא הבשר ובעבור שאין החלב קרב לגבי המזבח שמא יחשוב אדם שהוא מותר על כן הזהיר ואכול לא תאכלוהו ובעבור זה לא הזכיר הדם ובאה זאת הפרשה לבאר עונש האוכל חלב בשר קדש וכן כל דם הוסיף לעוף על כן חלב העוף מותר והראיה הגמורה שאמר בספר אלה הדברים בבשר תאוה שהוא חול שיאכלנו כלו ולא הוציא רק את דמו לבדו בשלשה מקומות ואין זכר לחלב כלל אז פקח הצדוקי עיניו ופצה בשפתיו שבועה שלא יסמוך על דעתו בפירוש המצות רק ישען על העתקת הפירושים:


[HAVING HIS UNCLEANLINESS.] This means if his uncleanliness comes from his body, as in the case of a flux⁴⁹. or a leper.⁵⁰. Also because of uncleanliness caused by a nocturnal emission, as Scripture states, *for he thought: Something*⁵¹ *hath befallen him*⁵² (I Sam. 20:26). It also applies to uncleanliness that comes from contact with an outside source⁵³ such as a dead body, or a living person who experienced a flux or is a leper, or an unclean beast which may not be eaten, or a detestable thing from among the fowl and that which swarms.⁵⁴ A Sadducee⁵⁵ once came to me and asked, “Does the Torah prohibit the fat tail?”⁵⁶). I answered that it is true that the fat tail is called “fat” (*chelev*),⁵⁷ for the Torah states, *the fat thereof, the fat tail entire* (Lev. 3:9). However, while our sages prohibited all fat, they permitted the fat tail.⁵⁸. Nahmanides charged that Ibn Ezra’s position is more heretical than that of the Karaites. Luzzato claimed that I.E. did not really believe that fat (*chelev*) is permitted. He was merely playing devil’s advocate. However, Nahmanides correctly interpreted Ibn Ezra. I.E. held that biblically speaking only the fat of an animal actually offered on the altar is prohibited. Other fat is prohibited by the rabbis. See I.E. on Deut. 12:15. The importance of I.E.’s comment on this verse for understanding Ibn Ezra’s approach to the *halakhah* is discussed in the introduction to this volume. The Sadducee then replied, “Does not the Torah prohibit all fat? Indeed it is written, *that ye shall eat neither fat nor blood* (Lev. 3:17). The latter is preceded by Scripture’s statement, *It shall be a perpetual statute throughout your generations* (*Ibid.*).”I once again answered him that this verse⁵⁹ relates to the peace offering. Also, the clause *It shall be a perpetual statute throughout your generations*⁶⁰ implies even in a time when the sanctuary no longer exists. does not provide complete proof, for we see that Scripture states, *And ye shall eat neither bread, nor parched corn, nor fresh ears, until this selfsame day, until ye have brought the offering of your God* (Lev. 23:14).⁶¹. Now Scripture there writes, *it is a statute for ever* (*Ibid.*). Shall we then not eat bread in the Diaspora? For we have not brought the *omer* offering.⁶² means as long as the sanctuary exits. Similarly *a perpetual statute*. He, on his part, replied, “*Ye shall eat no fat, of ox, or sheep, or goat* (v. 23).”⁶³ I again answered that this verse too refers to a peace offering. *For whosoever eateth the fat of the beast, of which men present an offering* (v. 25) is proof, for the latter excludes the fat of all flesh which is not offered as a peace offering, that is, all secular meat.⁶⁴ Scripture therefore states in our section, *And the fat of that which dieth of itself, and the fat of that which is torn of beasts, may be used for any other service; but ye shall in no wise eat of it* (v. 24). It is known⁶⁵ that the flesh of that which dies of itself and that which is torn of beasts is prohibited. It is the flesh that is prohibited.⁶⁶ Now since the fat⁶⁷ is not offered on the altar, one might think that it is permitted. Hence Scripture warns,⁶⁸ *but ye shall in no wise eat of it*.⁶⁹) of an animal that dies or is torn by another animal. It is for this reason that Scripture does not mention the blood.⁷⁰ This section⁷¹ was written in order to explain the punishment incurred by one who eats the fat of sacred flesh. The verse relating to *any blood* (v. 27) was similarly written to add that the blood of fowl is prohibited.⁷² The fat of fowl is therefore permitted.⁷³ Scripture’s statement in the Book of Deuteronomy (Deut. 1:1)⁷⁴ with regard to meat eaten out of desire, that is, non-sacred meat (Deut. 12:15-25), is total proof of what I say,⁷⁵ for Scripture states that non-sacred meat may be eaten in its entirety.⁷⁶ (Deut. 12:15). The fat of the latter is permitted. Only its blood is excluded in three places.⁷⁷ Fat is totally unmentioned.⁷⁸ The Sadducee’s eyes opened and he uttered an oath with his lips to the effect that he will never rely on his judgment when it comes to explaining the commandments. He will rely only on the tradition transmitted by the Pharisees.⁷⁹

THIS IS THE CONSECRATED PORTION. This is the reward of the consecration of Aaron or of the consecration of his sons. The word *mishchat* (consecrated portion) means leadership.⁸⁰.

### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:36:1)


**ביום משחו.** מיום וכן בבשר ובלחם:


IN THE DAY THAT THEY WERE ANOINTED. *Be-yom* (in the day) is to be rendered, from the day.⁸¹ of *be-yom* has the meaning of a *mem*. The priestly gifts mentioned in verse 34 were given to *kohanim* from the day that they commenced to minister. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. Similarly *ba-basar*⁸² has the meaning of “from” (of), not “in.” *u-va-lachem* (of the flesh and of the bread) (Lev. 8:32).

### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 7:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/7:38:1)


**להקריב את קרבניהם לה׳‎ במדבר סיני.** כי לא הקריבו קרבן עד שבאו אל הר סיני וכבר הראיתיך שהמזבח שבנה משה על חלושת עמלק הוא בחורב והוא הר סיני ושם ישבו ישראל שנה פחות עשרה ימים כי כן כתוב ובמדבר לא הקריבו עולות כי כן אמר הנביא הזבחים ומנח׳‎ הגשתם לי במדבר ארבעים שנה בית ישראל גם לא עשו פסח כי אם במצרים ושנית בהר סיני כי במדבר לא היו להם מקנה ואחר שנסעו מהר סיני לא מלו בדרך והנה רובם ערלים ובעבור אכילת פסח מל אותם יהושע:


TO PRESENT THEIR OFFERINGS UNTO THE LORD, IN THE WILDERNESS OF SINAI. For Israel did not offer sacrifices until they came to Mount Sinai. I have previously shown you that the altar which Moses built after defeating Amalek was built in Horeb, which is Mount Sinai.⁸³ Israel camped there for a year minus ten days.⁸⁴ The latter is clearly stated in Scripture. They did not offer sacrifices in the wilderness, for the prophet clearly states, *Did ye bring unto Me sacrifices and offerings in the wilderness forty years, O house of Israel*? (Amos 5:25). Israel similarly only offered the Passover sacrifice in Egypt, and for a second time at Mount Sinai,⁸⁵ for they had no cattle in the wilderness. They did not circumcise on the way after they journeyed from Mount Sinai. Most of them were thus uncircumcised.⁸⁶ Joshua circumcised them⁸⁷ so that they could partake of the Passover sacrifice.⁸⁸
