⁸⁵ Num. 9:1-5.
⁸⁶ And were proscribed from offering the Passover sacrifice (Ex. 12:48).
⁸⁷ After entering the Land of Israel.

AND THE BULLOCK OF THE SIN-OFFERING. The word *chattat* (sin offering) has a *heh*¹ prefixed to it because the sin offering² is mentioned earlier.³ The same applies to the *heh* prefixed to *elim* (rams) and *matzot* (unleavened bread).⁴ (rams) and *matzot* (unleavened bread) have a *heh* prefixed to them because they refer to the rams and *matzot* mentioned in Ex. 29:1,2.

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:7:1)


**ויאפד.** כמו ויחגור וכאשר ציץ הזהב איננו המצנפת כן האורים והתומים אינם אבני החושן וכן הלוחות בארון על כן כתוב ויתן אל החשן:


AND BOUND IT UNTO HIM.⁵ The word *va-yepod* means and girded. Just as the golden plate (*tzitz*) (v. 9) is not the mitre (*mitznefet*) (*Ibid*.),⁶) and the mitre (*mitznefet*) are not one and the same, for the verse states, *and upon the mitre…did he set the golden plate*. so *the Urim and the Thummim* (v. 8) are not the stones of the breastplate (Ex. 28:15-21).⁷. See I.E. on Ex. 28:5, Vol 2, pp. 591-598. Similarly, the Tablets were placed in the ark.⁸ and the *Thummim* was like that of the ark to the tablets; that is, both served as receptacles. Scripture therefore reads, *And in the breastplate he put in the Urim and the Thummim* (v. 8).

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:10:1)


**ויקח משה את שמן המשחה.** טעמו וכבר לקח קודם משיחת אהרן:


AND MOSES TOOK THE ANOINTING OIL. It means that Moses had taken the oil¹¹ before the anointing of Aaron.¹²

footnotes:
¹¹ And anointed the tabernacle and its furniture.
¹² Scripture tells of the dressing of Aaron in the priestly vestments in verses 7-9. I.E. assumes that Aaron was anointed as soon as the garments were placed upon him (see Ex. 40:15). The Torah then goes on to tell of the anointing of the tabernacle and its furniture in verses 10-12. It then tells of the dressing of Aaron’s sons (v. 13). However, Ex. 40:9-12 tells us that the anointing of the tabernacle took place before the anointing of Aaron. Hence I.E.’s interpretation.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:13:1)


**ויקרב משה את בני אהרן.** פעם שנית בעבור שארכו הדברים או טעמו כאשר הקריב משה את בני אהרן הלבישם כתנות. ומלת וילבישם יוצאה לשנים פועלים:


AND MOSES BROUGHT AARON’S SONS. Scripture notes this a second time¹³ because it speaks at length.¹⁴ On the other hand, this means that when Moses brought Aaron’s sons he dressed them in tunics.¹⁵ here does not mean and brought. Rather it means when he brought. In this case there is no repetition in our verse. The word *va-yalbishem* (and clothed them) is a verb that governs two actions.¹⁶. Moses caused them to be dressed, and they were dressed.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:16:1)


**ויקח את כל החלב אשר על הקרב.** וכתוב בתחלה המכסה את הקרב וכתוב אחר אומר המכסה את הקרב ואת כל החלב אשר על הקרב דע כי החלב המכסה הוא רב מאד ואת כל החלב אשר על הקרב הוא מעט מפה ומפה גם המכסה הוא על הקרב לכן הכתוב אחז דרך קצרה:


AND HE TOOK ALL THE FAT THAT WAS UPON THE INWARDS. Scripture earlier stated, *the fat that covereth the inwards* (Lev. 7:3) Another verse states, *the fat that covereth the inwards, and all the fat that is upon the inwards* (Lev. 3:3). Note, the *fat that covereth* [*the inwards* ] is substantial. However, the *fat that is upon the inwards* consists of a little from here and there.¹⁹ That which *covereth* [*the inwards* ] is also *upon the inwards*. Hence Scripture employed abridged terminology.²⁰. The latter can be taken to refer to itself and also to *the fat that covereth the inwards*, for they are located in the same place.

footnotes:
¹⁹ It is not part of the layer of fat that covers the inwards.
²⁰ Our verse only speaks of the fat that was upon the inwards

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:17:1

**שרף באש.** יש אומרים הוא בעצמו ויש אומרים בצווי וראייתם כאשר צוה ה׳‎ את משה ואיננה ראיה כי כן דרך לשון הקדש:


WERE BURNT²¹ (literally, he burnt). WITH FIRE. Some say by Moses himself.²² Others say by the command of Moses. They offer as proof, *as the Lord commanded Moses* (v. 21).²³ referred to Moses, the verse would have ended with “as the Lord commanded him,” rather than with *as the Lord commanded Moses*. However, this is no proof, for that is the style of the Hebrew language.²⁴

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:22:1

**ויקרב את האיל השני איל המלואים ויסמכו אהרן ובניו.** ובכתוב הראשון וסמך אהרן ובניו והנה אין הפרש בין וסמך אהרן ובניו ובין ויסמכו אהרן ובניו כי מדקדק גדול טעה שאמר כי טעם וסמך הוא לבדו ואחר כן בניו וסמכו כלם יחד:


AND THE OTHER RAM WAS PRESENTED, THE RAM OF CONSECRATION, AND AARON AND HIS SONS LAID (VA-YISMEKHU) THEIR HANDS. The first verse reads, *and Aaron and his sons laid*²⁵) Aaron and his sons. (ve-samakh) (v. 14).²⁶ Note, contra a great grammarian who erred, there is no difference between *ve-samakh*²⁷, for he is referring to verse 14. *Va-samakh* is the reading in Ex. 29:10; 19 where the laws of the consecration were first given. *aron u-vanav* (and Aaron and his sons laid) (v. 14) and *va-yismekhu aron u-vanav* (and Aaron and his sons laid). The grammarian said that *ve-samakh* indicates that Aaron alone shall lay hands, and then his sons.²⁸ *Vesamekhu*²⁹ (and they laid). means, and they shall all lay together.

**הימנית.** כמו הפנימית. וטעם על תנוך בהן. בפרשה זאת תהיה והנה הדם יכפר על נפש אהרן וכן כי הדם הוא בנפש יכפר וטעמו בנפש שיש בו לכפר והנה נפש תחת נפש וקרוב מזה הטעם ותגע לרגליו:


RIGHT EAR. *Ha-yemanit* (right) is similar to *ha-penimit* (inner).³⁰ rather than in a *heh*. ### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:26:1)


**חלת מצה אחת.** היא ככר לחם אחת ויתכן היות ככר משקל ואיננו כמשקל הכסף והזהב:


ONE UNLEAVENED CAKE. The reference is to *the one loaf* (kikkar) *of bread* (Ex. 29:2s).³⁸ usually refers to leavened bread. Hence I.E. explains that Ex 29:3 is to be understood as the one loaf of unleavened bread. I.E. comments thus so that there be no contradiction between the description of the consecration service in our chapter and the one in Exodus. It is possible that the term *kikkar* (loaf) refers to weight.³⁹ of (un)leavened bread. This interpretation assumes that the term loaf would not be used in reference to unleavened bread. It is not the same weight as that of a *kikkar* (talent) of gold or silver.⁴⁰

**וישם על החלבים.** ולא הזכיר יותרת הכבד והכליות כי היותרת היא קטנה ופירוש המלה כמו מה יתרון לאדם והכליות הם על חלביהן כי לא יתכן שיקראו הכליות חלב רק האליה היא חלב:


AND PLACED THEM ON THE FAT. Scripture does not mention the lobe⁴¹. of the liver and the kidneys, for the lobe was small. The meaning of *yoteret* (lobe) is similar to the meaning of the word *yitron* (profit)⁴² in *What profit hath man* (Eccles. 1:3).⁴³ means the addition [to the liver]. The kidneys⁴⁴ are offered with their fat,⁴⁵ takes in the fat that is upon the kidneys. The kidneys were offered along with their fat because they are attached to it. for Scripture would not refer to the kidneys as fat.⁴⁶ Only the fat tail is fat.⁴⁷

### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:33:1)


**ימלא את ידכם.** תחסר מלת קץ כאילו אמר כי קץ שבעת ימים או זמן. יש אומרים לא תצאו שבעת ימים ובלילה היו יוצאים לצרכם והנכון בעיני שיצאו בשעת הצורך ביום ובלילה וחכם גדול אמר כי חפרו מקום בחצר אהל מועד וזהו רחוק והכתוב אמר ויבכו בני ישראל את משה שלשים יום כאילו אין שם רגע שלא היו בוכים רק הכתוב דבר שישבו פתח אהל מועד יום ולילה והטעם שלא יתעסקו בדבר ולא ילכו אל מקום אחר וכן ומן המקדש לא יצא כאשר אפרש:


HE SHALL CONSECRATE YOU. The word *ketz* (end) was omitted.⁵³ (for he shall consecrate you seven days). The verse may be taken to read, for seven days shall consecrate you. Hence I.E.’s interpretation (Meijler). Our verse should be read as if written, at the end of seven days.⁵⁴ means at the end of seven days you will be consecrated. On the other hand, its meaning might be, for a period of seven days.⁵⁵ has been omitted from the last clause in our verse. In this case our verse reads, for you shall be consecrated for a period of seven days. Some say that *And ye shall not go out…seven days*⁵⁶ indicates that they went out at night⁵⁷ for their needs.⁵⁸ However, I believe that they went out during the day and night for their needs.⁵⁹ A great scholar⁶⁰ said that they dug a pit⁶¹ in the courtyard of the tent of meeting.⁶² However, this is farfetched.⁶³ Scripture states, *And the children of Israel wept for Moses…thirty days* (Deut. 34:8), as if there was no moment that they did not weep.⁶⁴ Scripture clearly states that they shall sit at the door of the tent of meeting day and night (v. 35). Its meaning is that they shall not occupy themselves with any matter and they should not go to another place.⁶⁵ Similarly *neither shall he go out of the sanctuary* (Lev. 21:12), as I will explain.⁶⁶

### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 8:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/8:34:1)


**כאשר עשה ביום הזה.** פירוש וכאשר עשה ביום הזה משה כן צוה ה׳‎ לעשות שבעת ימי המלואים והיה ראוי להיות כאשר עשיתי אלא כי כן דרך לשון הקדש כמו והי׳‎ יחזקאל לכם ואת יפתח ואת שמואל והעד שפירוש צוה ה׳‎ לעשות כן ועשית לאהרן ולבניו ככה ככל אשר צויתי אותכם שבעת ימים ועוד ופר חטאת תעשה ליום כמו שנים ליום עולה תמיד וכתיב שם וחטאת על המזבח ומשחת אותו בכל יום עד סוף המלואים ועוד שבעת ימים תכפר על המזבח ולא הזכיר את שני האילים כי אחז דרך קצרה ודעת רבים כי פר חטאת לבדו יעשה בכל יום ולפי דעתי שטעם להזכיר הפר בעבור שינתן מדם איל העולה על אהרן ובניו והאיל השני יאכל בשרו רק פר חטאת הוא לכפר על המזבח וכן כתוב על הכפורים וטעמו בעבור כפורים:


AS HATH BEEN DONE THIS DAY.⁶⁷ This means, as Moses did on this day, so God commanded to be done during the seven days of consecration. Scripture should have read, as I⁶⁸ did.⁶⁹ However, such is the style of the holy tongue.⁷⁰ Compare, *Thus shall Ezekiel he unto you*⁷¹ (Ezek. 24:24); *and Jephthah, and Samuel*⁷² (I Sam. 12:11). The fact that Scripture states, *So the Lord hath commanded to do* (v. 34); *And thus shall you do unto Aaron, and to his sons, according to all that I have commanded thee; seven days* (Ex. 29:35)⁷³. is proof of this.⁷⁴ (Moses) *consecrate them*. Furthermore, Scripture states, *And every day*⁷⁵ (literally, to the day). *shalt thou*⁷⁶ *offer the bullock of sin-offering* (Ex. 29:36).⁷⁷ The latter is similar to *two day by day*,⁷⁸. *for a continual burnt-offering*⁷⁹ indicates that the word *la-yom*, which precedes it, means daily. Thus the term *la-yom* in Ex. 29:36 is to be rendered every day.(Num. 28:3).⁸⁰ in Ex. 29:35 is daily. We thus see that Moses offered the bullock of sin offering during the seven days of consecration. Scripture there writes, *and thou*⁸¹ *shalt do the purification*⁸², i.e. you will offer the sin-offering. *upon the altar…and thou shalt anoint it* (*Ibid.*) every day until the end of the days of consecration.⁸³. Furthermore, Scripture states, *Seven days thou*⁸⁴ *shalt make atonement for the altar* (Ex. 29:37).⁸⁵ It does not mention⁸⁶. It omits the two rams. Hence it appears that Moses did not perform the entire consecration service. the two rams⁸⁷ because Scripture employed an abridged style.⁸⁸ Many are of the opinion that Moses was to offer a bullock of sin offering alone every day.⁸⁹ (Ex. 29:36). It does not mention the rams. This is the opinion of the Karaite Yefet (Krinsky). I believe that the reason Scripture mentions the bullock⁹⁰ is that the blood of the burnt offering ram was to be placed on Aaron and his sons,⁹¹ and the second ram’s flesh was to be eaten.⁹² However, the sin offering bullock was to consecrate the altar.⁹³ Scripture clearly states, *al hakippurim* (beside the other offerings of atonement) (Ex. 29:36), the meaning of which is, for atonement.⁹⁴

## Chapter 9


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:1:1)


**ויהי ביום השמיני.** היה נראה לנו כי ביום השמיני שמיני לניסן כי המשכן הוקם באחד לחדש רק המעתיקים אמרו שהיה ראש חדש ניסן ובשבעת ימי המלואים היה משה מקים את המשכן בכל יום וסותרו כדי להרגיל בו וללמד:


AND IT CAME TO PASS ON THE EIGHTH DAY. It would have appeared to us that the *eighth day* is the eighth day of Nisan, for the tabernacle was erected on the first day of the month.¹ However, the transmitters of tradition² said that the reference is to the first of Nisan.³ 7:1. Moses erected and took apart the tabernacle on each one of the seven days of consecration⁴ to train and teach.⁵ how to do it.

¹ See Ex. 40:2.
² The Rabbinic sages.
³ See Seder Olam

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:1:2)


**קרא משה לאהרן ולבניו.** ויצאו מפתח אהל מועד או העדה נכנסה אל החצר:


THAT MOSES CALLED AARON AND HIS SONS. And they went out from the opening of the tent of meeting.⁶ left the opening of the tent of meeting where they had to stay during the seven days of consecration (Lev. 8:35) and went into the camp of Israel to meet the elders (Filwarg). On the other hand, the congregation entered the courtyard.⁷. During the seven days of consecration the *kohanim* and the elders were separated. On the eighth day Moses spoke to them as a group. Hence the two possibilities offered by I.E. as to how they met.

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:2:1)


**עגל בן בקר.** בשבעת ימי המלואים היה פר חטאת גם איל לעולה ולא הזכיר עם עגל בן בקר אם הוא בן שנה והקרוב אלי שפר כמו עגל בלא זכר שנה:


A BULL-CALF.⁸. During the seven days of consecration a bullock and also a ram were offered for a burnt offering.⁹ Scripture does not mention whether the bull-calf was “of the year.”¹⁰, which means less than a year old. Scripture reads, *egel ben bakar*. It does not read, *egel ben shanah* (a calf of the year). It appears to me that a bullock¹¹. and a bull-calf¹². are one and the same when the term “year”¹³ is unmentioned.¹⁴ and *par* refer to a bull over the age of one. Thus the bull offered during the seven days of consecration and the one offered on the eighth day were similar. They were both over a year old.

footnotes:
⁸ Hebrew, egel ben bakar
⁹ See Lev. 8:2,14,18.
¹⁰ Hebrew, ben shanah
¹¹ Hebrew, par
¹² Hebrew, egel
¹³ That is, “of the year.”
¹⁴ In other words, the terms egel


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:2:2)


**תמימים.** על העגל ועל האיל ופרי המלואים לחטא את המזבח וזה העגל לכפר על אהרן וכבר פירשתי זאת המלה:


WITHOUT BLEMISH. This refers to the bull-calf and the ram.¹⁵ (without blemish). The bullocks of consecration¹⁶ were to purify the altar. This bull-calf was to atone (*le-khapper*) for Aaron.¹⁷, “Let this calf come and atone for the making of the calf.” However, I.E. explicitly rejects this interpretation in Ex. 31:18 (Vol. 2, p. 656). I have already explained this word.¹⁸. See I.E. on Lev. 1:4, *“Le-khapper alav*: To serve as a ransom for the punishment due to him.”

footnotes:
¹⁵ Hence Scripture employs the plural temimim
¹⁶ See Ex. 29:36.
¹⁷ For any sin or sins that Aaron may possibly have committed. Some interpret for making the golden calf (Weiser). See Sifra
¹⁸ The meaning of le-khapper

A HE-GOAT FOR A SIN-OFFERING. For the congregation. Also [for the congregation] a calf that has not reached the age of a complete year,¹⁹. and a lamb for a burnt offering.

### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:6:1)


וטעם **ויאמר משה.** כאשר הראיתיך. ואשאל אותה וטעמו כבר אמר להם משה זה הדבר עשו שיתנו שעיר ועגל וכבש ושור ואיל אז יראה לכם כבוד ה׳‎ והטעם על האש שיצא:


[6. AND MOSES SAID.] *Va-yomer mosheh* (and Moses said) is to be understood in a manner similar to that of *va-eshal otah* (and I asked her) (Gen. 24:47). I have pointed out to you how the latter is to be interpreted.²² (and I asked her) is, I had already asked. See I.E. on Gen. 24:14 (Vol. 1, p. 236). The meaning of our clause is that Moses had already said to them, *This is the thing which the Lord commanded that ye should do*, namely, that you offer a he-goat (v. 3), a calf (v. 3), a lamb (v. 3), and an ox and a ram (v. 4);²³ before Israel *brought that which Moses commanded before the tent of meeting*. then the glory of God will then appear to them. The latter refers to the fire that came forth from the Lord.²⁴

footnotes:
²² According to I.E. the meaning of va-eshal otah
²³ What is recorded in verse 6 occurred before that which is recorded in verse 5. In other words, Moses told the Israelites, This is the thing that the Lord commanded…
²⁴ See verses 23 and 24.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:7:1)


**וכפר בעדך ובעד העם.** שהיא מצוה שתכפר על נפשך ועל כל הקהל ובפר החטאת תכפר בעדך ואחר כך תעשה קרבן העם וכפר בעדם כי לא יוכל אדם לכפר על אחד עד היותו טהור מכל חטא:


AND MAKE ATONEMENT FOR THYSELF, AND FOR THE PEOPLE. There is a divine command that you make atonement for yourself and for all of the people. You will atone for yourself with a sin offering bullock. Afterwards you will atone for the congregation by offering the people’s sacrifice, for a person cannot atone for another unless he has been purified from all sin.

AND…DELIVERED UNTO HIM The word *va-yamtzi’u* (and…delivered unto him) is related to the word *matza* (found). Aaron found the blood when it was needed.²⁷ is a *hifil*, meaning, and they caused it to be found.

In this case I.E. reads, “to the altar.” In other words, Scripture first tells us that the people’s offering was brought to the altar and then goes on to tell us how Aaron and his sons dealt with it

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:21:1)

AND THE BREASTS. The breast of the ox and the breast of the ram.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:21:2)


**ואת שוק הימין.** מזה ומזה ושוק סמוך אל הפאה או הצד:


AND THE RIGHT THIGH.³³. From this one and that one.³⁴ The word *shok* (thigh) is connected to the word *ha-pe’ah* (corner), or *ha-tazad* (side).³⁵ or *shok ha-pe’ah ha-yamin*. In other words, *shok ha-yamin* is not to be rendered the *right thigh*, for *ha-yamin* (the right) is not an adjective describing *shok* (thigh). If *ha-yamin* (the right) were an adjective describing *shok* (thigh), our phrase would have read, *ha-shok ha-yamin*, rather than *shok ha-yamin*.

footnotes:
³³ Hebrew, shok ha-yamin
³⁴ From the ox and the ram.
³⁵ Our verse should be interpreted as if it read, shok ha-tzad ha-yamin



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:22:1)


**וישא אהרן את ידיו אל העם.** אל נכחם וקדמוננו העתיקו מכאן צורת נשיאות כפים:


AND AARON LIFTED UP HIS HANDS TOWARD³⁶ (literally, to). THE PEOPLE. Toward them.³⁷ means towards the people. We have followed the reading in *Vat. Ebr*. 38. The *Mikra’ot Gedolot* editions of I.E. read, *ra’a nikhecham*. The word *ra’a* has not been explained. It appears to be a copyist’s error. See Filwarg and Weiser. From here our ancients transmitted to us the form taken by the “raising of the hands.”³⁸ lift their hands when bestowing the priestly benediction. See *Sotah* 38a.

footnotes:
³⁶ Hebrew, el
³⁷ In other words, el ha-am
³⁸ According to the sages we learn here that the kohanim

**וירד.** כאשר הראיתיך רבים וכבר ירד מעשות חטאת העם ועולתם והשלמים:


AND HE CAME DOWN. *Va-yered* (and he came down) is to be understood in accordance with what I have previously pointed out to you many times.³⁹ often introduces the pluperfect. See I.E. on verse 6. It means that Aaron had already come down⁴⁰ from offering the people’s sin offering, burnt offering, and peace offering.

Scripture reads came down because the altar was three cubits in height.⁴¹ After this,⁴² Moses and Aaron came to the tent of meeting. It is likely that they came to pray that a fire would come forth. When they came out⁴³ both of them blessed the people.

### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:24:1)


**את העולה.** עולת אהרן ועולת העם ועולת תמיד כי כן כ׳‎ מלבד עולת הבוקר:


THE BURNT-OFFERING. The burnt offering of Aaron, the burnt offering of the people, and the continual burnt offering,⁴⁴. for Scripture states, *beside the burnt-offering of the morning* (Num. 28:23).⁴⁵ (the festival offerings) *beside the burnt-offering of the morning, which is for a continual burnt-offering*. Hence the burnt offering is to be offered every day. Thus the burnt offering in our verse also includes the continual burnt offering.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ Hebrew, olat tamid
⁴⁵ The full verse reads, Ye shall offer these

AND THE FAT. From the bull-calf and the ram of Aaron, and the he-goat, the ox, and the ram


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 9:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/9:24:3)

THEY SHOUTED. *Va-yaronnu* (they shouted) means they raised their voices. Compare, *rinnah* (cry) in *And there went a cry throughout the host* (I Kings 22:36).



## Chapter 10


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:1)


**איש מחתתו.** כל איש לקח מחתתו ולפי דעתי שזה הדבר היה גם ביום השמיני והעד הן היום הקריבו את חטאתם:


EACH OF THEM HIS CENSER.¹ (literally, a man his censer). Each man took his firepan.² is to be interpreted as if written *kol ish machtato* (each man his censer). Our verse literally reads, And…the sons of Aaron took a man his censer. Hence I.E.’s comment that our verse is to be interpreted, And…the sons of Aaron took each man his firepan. I believe that this event also took place on the eighth day.³ *Behold, this day have they offered their sin-offering* (v. 19)⁴ is proof of this.

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, ish machtato
² In other words, ish machtato
³ See I.E. on Lev. 9:1.
⁴ The reference being to the sin offering mentioned in Lev. 9:2. See I.E. on verse 19.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:2)


**ויתנו בהן אש.** לא מהאש שיצאה וזה טעם אש זרה:


AND PUT FIRE THEREIN. Not from the fire that came forth from the Lord.⁵ This is the meaning of *strange fire*.

footnotes:
⁵ See Lev. 9:24.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:1:3)


וטעם **אשר לא צוה אותם.** שמדעתם עשו ולא בצווי להקטיר קטורת גם באש זרה:


[WHICH HE HAD NOT COMMANDED THEM.] They acted on their own and were not commanded to burn incense and certainly not to employ a strange fire.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:2:1)


**וימותו לפני ה׳‎.** כי חשבו שעשו דבר רצוי לפניו:


AND THEY DIED BEFORE THE LORD. They thought that they were doing something favorable before Him.⁶ means serving God. Their intention was to serve God.

footnotes:
⁶ Before the Lord



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:3:1)


**הוא אשר דבר ה׳‎.** כבר אמר לי השם שהוא יראה קדושתו בקרובים אליו כטעם רק אתכם ידעתי וכאשר אראה בם קדושתי אז אהיה נכבד ועל פני כל העם אכבד וייראו ממני:


THIS IS THAT THE LORD SPOKE. God has previously told me that He will demonstrate His holiness through those who are close to Him.⁷ Our verse is similar in meaning to *You only have I known*⁸. (Amos 3:2). I will be exalted when I show My sanctity through those who are close to Me. I will then be glorified before all the people, and they will fear me.

footnotes:
⁷ However, Moses did not record this communication (Krinsky). See Rashi for another interpretation.
⁸ The complete verse reads, You only have I known of all the families of the earth; Therefore I will visit upon you all your iniquities



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:4:1)


**שאו את אחיכם מאת פני הקדש.** יש אומרים כי הקטורת היתה לפני מזבח העולה והלוים נכנסים שם ויש אומרים שהיתה על מזבח הקטורת ומשה הוציאם מאהל מועד:


CARRY YOUR BRETHREN FROM BEFORE THE SANCTUARY. Some say⁹ on this verse. that the incense was before the altar of the burnt offering¹⁰ and the Levites entered there.¹¹ Others say¹² on this verse. that the incense was on the altar of incense¹³ and Moses took them out of the tent of meeting.¹⁴ and *Vat. Ebr*. 38. Krinsky reads *u-misham* (and from there) in place of *u-moshah* (and Moses). According to this reading the Levites removed the bodies of Nadab and Abihu from the tabernacle. See Rabbi Akiba in the *Sifra*, “They cast a spear of iron [from the courtyard, and speared them.] They [then] dragged them and took them outside.”

footnotes:
⁹ This is the opinion of Rabbi Eliezer. See the Sifra
¹⁰ Which stood in the courtyard of sanctuary.
¹¹ The Levites were permitted to enter there. They were not permitted to enter the sanctuary itself.
¹² This is the opinion of Rabbi Akiba. See the Sifra
¹³ Which was in the sanctuary.
¹⁴ This is the reading of the Mikra’ot Gedolot


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:4:2)


**פני הקדש.** היא החצר כנגד המחנה כאשר פירשתי:


FROM BEFORE THE SANCTUARY. The reference is to the courtyard. The latter, as I have explained, is referred to as the sanctuary, in contrast to the camp.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ See I.E. on Lev. 6:23.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:1)


**ראשיכם אל תפרעו.** יש אומרים לגדל פרע:


LET NOT THE HAIR OF YOUR HEADS GO LOOSE. Some say that *tifra’u* (go loose) means to let the hair grow long.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:2)


**תפרומו.** תקרעו והנכון שהוא מן בגדיו יהיו פרומים וראשו יהיה פרוע ורבים אמרו מכוסה ואחרים אמרו מגולה והאמת דברי הקבלה:


REND. The word *tifromu* means rend. Actually the word *tifra’u* (go loose) is related to the word *faru’a* (go loose)¹⁶ in *his clothes shall be rent, and the hair of his head shall go loose* (Lev. 13:45).¹⁷ Many say that it means¹⁸ and by extension *tifra’u*. covered.¹⁹ on Lev. 13:45. Others say that it means uncovered.²⁰ on Lev. 13:45. The words of tradition are true.²¹ is to grow the hair long. I.E.’s point is that there is no connection between *tifra’u* in our verse and *faru’a* in Lev. 13:45. Hence the difference of opinion regarding the latter has no bearing on the definition of *tifra’u* (Meijler).

¹⁸ The meaning of faru’a
¹⁹ See the Sifra
²⁰ This is the opinion of Rabbi Akiba. See the Sifra
²¹ That the meaning of tifra’u


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:6:3)


**ולא תמותו.** מלת ולא מושכת עצמה כמו ולא למדתי חכמה וכן הוא ולא על כל העדה יקצוף כי אתם תכפרו בעדם:


THAT YE DIE NOT. The word *ve-lo* (that…not) is to be read as if written twice. Compare, *And I have not learned wisdom* (Prov. 30:3).²²) learned wisdom, I have no (*lo*) knowledge of the Holy One. Our verse is to be understood as follows: and that He be not wroth with all the congregation,²³. The latter literally reads, and that He be wroth with all the congregation. That makes no sense in this context. Hence I.E. suggests that the word *lo* be inserted into the clause and that it be read as if written: and that He be not (*lo*) wroth with all the congregation. for you will atone for them.

**וידבר ה׳‎ אל אהרן.** כי נביא היה ויש אומרים על ידי משה כמו דבר אל אחז והזהירו שישמר הוא ובניו שלא ימותו כאשר מתו בניו הגדולים:


AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO AARON. For Aaron was a prophet.²⁵ Others say through Moses,²⁶ as in *And the Lord spoke again unto Ahaz* (Is. 7:10).²⁷ God cautioned Aaron and his sons to keep the law²⁸ so that they do not die as Aaron’s oldest sons died.

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:9:1)


**ושכר.** העשוי ממין חטה או דבש או תמרים כי היין משחית הדעת לשותיו ויתערבו לו הדברים. על כן ולהבדיל כי אתה כהן גדול ותבדיל בין מקום הקדש ובין החול והוא מלשון חלול גם יתכן בין יום קודש לחול:


NOR STRONG DRINK. Made from any kind of wheat, honey, or dates, for wine destroys the reason of the one who imbibes it and he confuses things. Hence Scripture states, *And that ye may put difference* (v. 10).²⁹ Since you are a *kohen gadol* you will differentiate³⁰, which is an infinitive that literally means and to put a difference, is to be rendered, and you shall differentiate (*utavdil*) in this context. between a holy place and a common place. The word *chol* (common) is related to the word *chillul* (profaned).³¹ means profane. Thus our clause is to be rendered, between the holy and the profane. It is possible that *between the holy and the common* means between a holy day and a common day.

**ובין הטמא ובין הטהור.** בבהמה ובשרץ המים ובעוף על כן נכתבה אחרי זאת הפרשה פרשת זאת החיה ואחר כן טומאת היולדת וצרעת אדם ובגד ובית ובעל קרי וזב וזבה ונדה וזה טעם ובין הטמא ובין הטהור:


AND BETWEEN THE UNCLEAN AND THE CLEAN. Among cattle and things that swarm in the water and that fly. Therefore the section opening with the words *These are the living things* (Lev. 10:11), and then the sections dealing with the woman who gives birth (Lev. 12:1-8) and the leprosy that is found in humans (Lev. 13:1-46), on garments (Lev. 13:47-59) and in houses (Lev. 14:33-53); one who experiences a seminal emission (Lev. 15:16-18); a male or female who experiences a flux (Lev. 15:1-15, 25-33); and a menstruant (Lev. 15:19-24) follow this section. This is the meaning of *and between the unclean and the clean*.

**קחו את המנחה.** אחר שהקטירו ממנה לגבוה אין ראוי לאכול הנשאר כי אם מקודשים ועתה תחלו לאכול החוק שנתן לכם השם שתקחו תמיד מאשי ה׳‎ ופירושו כל מנחה שהקטירו ממנה לשם:


TAKE THE MEAL-OFFERING. No one aside from those who are holy are fit to eat that which remains from the meal offering after part of it has been burned in honor of God.³² You shall begin to eat the portion which God has given you, to always take of God’s fire-offerings;³³ this means³⁴ all meal offerings, part of which was burned in honor of God.

**במקום טהור** ואם הוא חוץ החצר והחזה והשוק תאכלו זכרים ונקבות כי כן כתיב כל טהור בביתך יאכל אותו וכן קנין כסף ויליד בית ואלמנה ששבה וכאשר תאכלו עתה השוק והחזה יהיה לכם החק לקחתו מכל זבחי השלמים:


[IN A CLEAN PLACE.] Even though it is outside the courtyard. The breast and the thigh shall be eaten by males and females, for Scripture clearly states, *every one that is clean in thy house may eat thereof* (Num. 18:11). Similarly slaves bought by money and born in the house,³⁵ and a widow³⁶ who returned to her father’s house.³⁷) and a widow who returns to her *kohen* father’s house may eat the breast and the thigh. As you now eat the breast and the thigh, so shall the law be that you take these portions from all the sacrifices of peace offerings.

**שורף.** מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו כמו ומורק ושוטף:


WAS BURNT. *Soraf* (was burnt) is a *pu’al*.³⁸ is vocalized with a *cholam. Pu’als* are usually vocalized with a *kubbutz*. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *morak*³⁹ is *a pu’al. Morak*, like *soraf*, is vocalized with a *cholam*. (sodden) in *it shall be scoured, and rinsed in water* (Lev. 6:21).

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:17:1)


**במקום הקדש.** פתח אוהל מועד כי כן כתוב ואותה נתן השם לכם כי כאשר תאכלו אתם את החטאת השם ישא את עון העדה או פירושו אתם תשאו את החטאת והטעם כי על ידכם יכופר עונם על כן לכפר:


IN THE PLACE OF THE SANCTUARY. At the opening of the tent of meeting, for this is clearly stated in Scripture.⁴⁰ *And He hath given it you [to bear the iniquity of the congregation* ], for when you eat the sin offering God will bear the iniquity of the congregation.⁴¹ refers to God. The verse is to be interpreted, And He hath given it to you so that God will bear (forgive) the iniquity of the congregation. Or its meaning⁴². is, you will bear the iniquity of the congregation; that is, the sins of the congregation will be forgiven through you.⁴³ to the *kohanim*. Hence Scripture states, *to make atonement*.⁴⁴ explains the meaning of *to bear the iniquity of the congregation; that is, to bear the iniquity* means *to make atonement for*.

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:18:1)


**הן לא הובא את דמה אל הקדש פנימה.** כפר הבא על כל המצות שהוא לכהן ולקהל. ואם ישאל שואל הנה פר חטאת שורף ולא הובא את דמו לפנים בקדש והטעם הפרכת ומזבח הזהב דע שפר חטאת הוא לחטאת על המזבח ושעיר החטאת הוא לעם ובשר החטאת לכהן:


BEHOLD, THE BLOOD OF IT WAS NOT BROUGHT INTO THE SANCTUARY WITHIN. Like the bullock brought by the *kohen* and the congregation for violation of any of the commandments.⁴⁵ Should one ask, “Look, the bullock of the sin offering⁴⁶ was burned⁴⁷ and its blood was not brought into the inner sanctuary,⁴⁸ and the congregation, and yet it was not eaten. The point is, Moses implies that if the blood of a sin offering is not brought into the sanctuary, then the flesh of the sin offering must be eaten. However, the case of the sin offering offered during the seven days of consecration shows that this is not necessarily so. the reference being to the place of the curtain and the golden altar?” Know that the sin offering bullock⁴⁹ was to purify the altar,⁵⁰ consuming its flesh. Hence its flesh was burned. the he-goat sin offering⁵¹ was to atone for the people, and the flesh of the sin offering was for the *kohen*.⁵² eats the sin offering the sins of the people are forgiven. Hence Moses was angry that the flesh was burned and not eaten.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:19:1)


יש מדקדקים שאמרו שהה״א של התימה לא יהיה אחריו דגש עם אחד מהז׳‎ מלכים כי אם עם השוא הנע ימצא והה״א פתוח בעבור שלא יתכן להתחבר שני שואי״ן ובעבור שמצאו יו״ד הייטב דגוש אמרו כי זה הה״א לידיעה כה״א השבה עם נעמי הנמצאו פה העיר ההוללה ואלה ההי״ן במקום אשר הנה כן פירוש הייטב ועוד אמרו כי ואכלתי פועל עבר ואילו היה עתיד היה מלרע כמו ודברתי אל הנביאים ודברתי אתך והטעם כי בני הנשרפים היום הקריבו את חטאתם ואת עולתם כי העגל והאיל בעד אהרן ובניו:


There are grammarians who say that the interrogative *heh* is not followed by a *dagesh* when the letter⁵⁴. is vocalized with one of the seven “kings.”⁵⁵, and *chirik* (*Weiser*). The letter following an interrogative *heh* is found with a *dagesh* only when it has a mobile *sheva* beneath it. The *heh*⁵⁶. is vocalized with a *pattach* because two mobile *shevas* cannot come back to back.⁵⁷ is vocalized with a *chataf pattach*. However, when the interrogative *heh* precedes a letter vocalized by a *sheva na* (a mobile *sheva*), it is vocalized with a *pattach*, for the *chataf pattach* has a *sheva na* within it and two mobile *shevas* cannot follow each other. Now, since they⁵⁸ found that the *yod* of *ha-yitav* (would it have been well pleasing) has a *dagesh* in it, they said that the *heh* of *ha-yitav* is the *heh* which indicates the direct object,⁵⁹ of *ha-yitav* is not an interrogative *heh*. like the *heh* of *ha-shavah* (that came) in *that came back with Naomi* (Ruth 2:6); the *heh* in *ha-nimtze’u foh* (that are found here) (I Chron. 29:17); the *heh* in *ha-hullalah* (the renowned) in *The renowned city* (Ezek. 26:17). These *hehs* are in place of the word *asher* (that). This is the meaning of *ha-yitav* (would it have been well pleasing).⁶⁰ is, that finds favor. They further said that *ve-akhalti* (and if I had eaten) is a verb in the perfect.⁶¹) an amount that finds favor (*ha-yitav*) in the eyes of God. If the word were an imperfect it would have been ultimately accented like the words *vedibbarti* (I have also spoken)⁶² in *I have also spoken unto the prophets* (Hosea 12:11) and *ve-dibbarti* (and I will speak) in *And I will speak with thee* (Ex. 25:22). The meaning of the first half of our verse is, my sons who were burned today offered their sin offering and their burnt offering, for the bull-calf and the ram⁶³ were offered on behalf of Aaron and his sons.⁶⁴

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 10:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/10:19:2)


**ותקראנה אותי כאלה.** פירוש צרות או דאגות על מות הבנים ובעבור זה לא יכולתי לאכול כל חטאת רק אכלתי ממנה מה שייטב בעיני השם והטעם מה שיוציאני לידי חובה. ועל דעת המעתיקים שה״א הייטב לתימה והוא מלה זרה גם על הפירוש שהזכרתי היא זרה כי לא מצאנו בכל המקרא ה״א הדעת עם פועל עתיד. ומלת **ואכלתי חטאת.** כאילו אמר ואילו אכלתי חטאת גם מצאנו מלות לעתיד והן מלעיל פן אורש וגנבתי והטעם כי הייתי אונן ואין אונן אוכל חטאת כטעם לא אכלתי באוני:


AND THERE HAVE BEFALLEN ME SUCH THINGS AS THESE. The reference is to troubles or anxiety because of the death of the sons. It was because of this that I was not able to eat the entire sin offering, but I ate enough of it to find favor in the eyes of God, that is, an amount which is enough to fulfill my obligation.⁶⁵ However, in the opinion of the transmitters of tradition⁶⁶ the *heh* of *ha-yitav* is an interrogative *heh*⁶⁷ is to be rendered, would it have been well pleasing. See *Zevachim* 101a. Also see Rashi. The sages render the second half of our verse, *and if I had eaten* (ve-akhalti) *the sin-offering to-day, would it have been well-pleasing* (ha-yitav) *in the sight of the Lord?* and the word is irregular.⁶⁸ in the letter following the interrogative *heh*. It is also irregular according to the explanation which I mentioned above,⁶⁹ of *ha-yitav* is a definite article. for we do not find in all of Scripture the definite article *heh* prefixed to a verb in the imperfect.⁷⁰ is an imperfect. The meaning of *ve-akhalti chattat* is to be interpreted as, and if I had eaten the sin offering.⁷¹ is a perfect, as it is penultimately accented. According to this interpretation *ve-akhalti* is to be rendered, and if I would have eaten. We also find words in the imperfect that are penultimately accented.⁷² be taken to be an imperfect. According to this interpretation *ve-akhalti* does not have to be rendered, and if I had eaten, but rather, if I would eat. Compare, *ve-ganavti* (and steal)⁷³ is an imperfect. It is penultimately accented. in *lest I be poor, and steal* (Prov. 30:9). The meaning of our clause⁷⁴ (ve-akhalti) *the sin-offering to-day would it have been well-pleasing* (ha-yitav) *in the sight of the Lord?* is, I was mourning, and a mourner does not eat of the sin offering. Its meaning is similar to *I have not eaten thereof*⁷⁵ *in my mourning* (Deut. 26:14).⁷⁶

## Chapter 11


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:1:1)


**וידבר ה׳‎ אל משה ואל אהרן.** בעבור שהוא הכהן המורה המבדיל בין הטמא ובין הטהור:


AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO MOSES AND TO AARON. For Aaron is the *kohen* who teaches and who differentiates between the unclean and the clean.¹

footnotes:
¹ Aaron teaches Israel how to differentiate between the unclean and unclean. Hence God’s word was also directed to Aaron.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:1)


**מפרסת.** שם התואר וכן מקרין מפריס והטעם כל שהוא מתוארת שהיא בעלת פרסה:


PARTETH. *Mafreset* (parteth) is an adjective.² (whatsoever parteth the hoof) as whatsoever has parted hoofs. Similarly *makrin mafris*³ and *mafris* are adjectives. (horned and hoofed) (Ps. 69:32).⁴ It means all animals which are described as having a hoof.

footnotes:
² Even though it has the form of a verb. I.E. renders kol mafreset parsah
³ Makrin
⁴ Translated according to I.E.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:2)


**ושוסעת שסע.** שם התאר והטעם שפרסותיה שסועות:


AND IS WHOLLY CLOVEN-FOOTED. *Shosa’at* (wholly cloven) is an adjective. The meaning of *ve-shosa’at shesa perasot* (and is wholly cloven-footed) is, whose hoofs are cloven.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:3:3)


**מעלת גרה.** מגזרת גרון ומעלת פועל. והזכיר הגמל והשפן והארנבת והחזיר בעבור שיש לכל אחד הסימן האחד ודרך לשון הקדש להזכיר הזכר מכל מין כי הנקבה בכלל הזכר היא והזכיר ארנבת יש אומרים לפי שלא ימצא הזכר מהם ויש אומרים שהזכר ישוב נקבה והפך הדבר והראשון קרוב אלי:


AND CHEWETH THE CUD. *Gerah* (cud) is related to the word *garon* (throat).⁵. The word *ma’alat*⁶ (cheweth) is a verb in the present.⁷ and *shosa’at*. Scripture mentions the camel (v. 4), the rockbadger (v. 5), the hare (v. 6), and the swine (v. 7) because each one has one sign.⁸ It is the style of Scripture to mention the male of each species because the masculine also takes in the feminine.⁹ Scripture mentions the *arnevet* (hare).¹⁰ is feminine and so does not seem to follow the rule stated by I.E. Some say that it does so because the male is not found among them.¹¹ There are others who say that the male turns into a female and vice versa. However, the first interpretation appeals to me.

footnotes:
⁵ The root of which is gimel, resh, resh
⁶ Literally, brings up.
⁷ Unlike mafreset
⁸ They either chew the cud or have split hoofs. These animals are enumerated in order to emphasize the fact that the law requires a clean animal to have both signs.
⁹ The masculine form of a noun at times refers to males and females; that is, the term “man” sometimes refers to males and females. Most of the names of the animals mentioned in verses 4-7 are in the masculine. I.E. points out that the same law applies to females of the species. Otherwise only the male animals listed would be forbidden to be eaten.
¹⁰ Arnevet
¹¹ Some interpret this to mean that the male of the species is rarely seen. However, this opinion seems to be saying that this species has no male.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:8:1)


**ובנבלתם לא תגעו.** והכתוב לא הזכיר הנוגע שיטמא עד הערב רק מדברי קבלה ידענו זה. והנה הנוגע בזדון יש עליו מלקות כי הוא עבר על לא תעשה:


AND THEIR CARCASSES YE SHALL NOT TOUCH. Scripture does not mention that one who touches their carcasses shall be unclean until evening.¹² However, we know this from tradition. Now, the one who deliberately touches their carcass incurs the penalty of flogging, for he violated a negative commandment.¹³ (p. 47) I.E. interprets this verse in accordance with the opinion of the rabbis. He writes: “The one who touches them does not incur the penalty of *karet* or stripes.”

footnotes:
¹² As it does with regard to winged swarming things (v. 24), animals that walk on their paws (v. 27), and other creatures. See verses 31, 32, 39, and 40.
¹³ This is I.E.’s opinion based on a literal reading of the text. However, in the Yesod Mora



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:9:1)


ואם רדפנו אחרי הכתוב לבדו בלי העתקה הנה לא הותר הדג שהוא באגמים כי הכתוב פרט **בימים ובנחלים**:


[9. IN THE SEAS.] If we pursue the literal meaning of the verse without the tradition of the rabbis, then the fish that are found in the lakes (*agammim*) are prohibited, for Scripture specifies *the seas and the rivers*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:9:2)


**סנפיר וקשקשת.** כדברי המתרגם ארמית:


FINS AND SCALES. *Snappir ve-kaskeset* (fins and scales) is to be rendered as the Aramaic translator does.¹⁴ as *tzitzin ve-kalfin*.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Onkelos renders snappir ve-kaskeset



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:10:1)


**שרץ המים.** הם הקטנים שנבראו מן המים ובמים נפש חיה מזכר ונקבה. ואחר שאמד שקץ הם לכם אמר ושקץ יהיו לכם לפרש מה שהוא שקץ והוא מבשרם לא תאכלו והנה הדג יקרא בשר רק מה שאמרו חז״ל בנדרים הוא על מנהג דברי אלה הדורות והוסיף כל אשר אין לו סנפיר וקשקשת להיות כלל לכל מים כי לא הזכיר בתחלה רק הימים והנחלים:


OF ALL THAT SWARM IN THE WATERS. They are the small creatures that were created from the water.¹⁵ The waters contain living creatures that are both male and female.¹⁶. After stating *they are a detestable thing unto you* Scripture goes on to say, *and they shall be a detestable thing unto you* (v. 11) in order to explain in what way they shall be detestable namely, *ye shall not eat of their flesh* (*Ibid*.). Note, fish are called flesh.¹⁷. However, what the sages of blessed memory said with regard to vows¹⁸ 104a, “One who takes a vow not to eat flesh is permitted to eat fish and locusts.” refers to the custom of their generations.¹⁹ Scripture adds, *Whatsoever hath no fins nor scales* to include all waters, for Scripture earlier mentioned only the seas and the rivers.

footnotes:
¹⁵ See Gen. 1:20.
¹⁶ I.E.’s interpretation of and of all living creatures that are in the waters
¹⁷ For our verse states, ye shall not eat of their flesh
¹⁸ The rabbis say that fish are not considered flesh. See Hullin
¹⁹ Post-Biblical generations. In Rabbinic and contemporary times fish are not considered flesh.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:13:1)


**הנשר.** ידוע גם זה עוף שיקרא כן בלשון ישמעאל יש כדמות ראיה שהוא כן בעבור היות שתי הלשונות קרובות:


THE GREAT VULTURE The meaning of *nesher* (the great vulture) is known. This is also the bird which has the same name in the language of the Ishmaelites.²⁰ in Arabic. Hence we have additional proof of the identity of this bird. There is something of a proof that this is the case, because the two languages are related.

footnotes:
²⁰ It is called niser


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:13:2)


**העזניה.** אף על פי שיש דברי יחיד שאיננה ביישוב טעה הגאון שתרגם אותה אלענקא כי זה השם בלשון ישמעאל על דבר שלא נמצא בעולם ולא היה ולא נברא אלא למשל וכן יודו חכמי לשונם אם כן לא יתכן שיאסר השם הדבר שלא היה:


AND THE OSPRAY. Even though there is an individual opinion that it (the osprey) is not found in inhabited places, the Gaon erred in rendering it *al enka*, for this term in the language of the Ishmaelites refers to something that never existed. It refers to something that was not created and never was. It is only employed metaphorically. The Arab grammarians admit this. This being the case, it is not possible that the Torah would prohibit something that never existed.²¹

footnotes:
²¹ Hence the first quoted opinion is superior to that of the Gaon.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:14:1)


**הדאה.** שם כלל לשני מינים והם דאה ודיה כמו ואת הצפור לא בתר עם תור וגוזל והנה צדקו המעתיקים שאמרו כי ראה היא דאה וסרה טענת רבי יונה שאמר איך יתכן לאמר השמר לך מהאריה והליש. ויש אומרים שנשר מן אשורנו ופרס מן הלא פרס לרעב. ועזניה מן עוז והנו״ן נוסף והוא הנקרא עקב״א. ודאה מן כאשר ידאה הנשר ואיה שמנהגה לשבת באיים ידועים:


AND THE KITE. The term *da’ah* (kite) is a general term which takes in two kinds, namely, the *da’ah* (kite) and the *dayyah* (falcon).²². 38 reads: “The term *da’ah* (kite) is a general term which takes in two kinds, namely, the *ra’ah* (kite) and the *dayyah* (falcon).” The latter are mentioned in Deut. 14:13 but omitted in our verse. Thus there is no contradiction between the list of the 20 prohibited birds in our chapter and the 21 in Deut. 14. Also, see I.E. on Deut. 14:11 (Vol. 5, pp. 93,94). Compare, *tzippor* (birds), the word used for a turtle-dove and a young pigeon in *but the birds divided he not* (Gen. 15:10). Now the transmitters of tradition who said that the *ra’ah*²³ (glede) is to be identified with the *da’ah* (kite)²⁴ 63:2. are correct.²⁵ (kite) is a general term and takes in the *ra’ah* The argument of Rabbi Jonah,²⁶, root *resh, alef, heh*. who asked how is it possible to say be on guard because of the *aryeh* (lion) and the *layyish* (lion),²⁷ and the *layyish*, for both words have one meaning. Rabbi Jonah argues that the *ra’ah* is not to be identified with the *da’ah*, for the Torah would not say do not eat the *ra’ah* and the *da’ah* if both referred to the same kind (Krinsky). is turned aside.²⁸ is a general term, and *layyish* is a specific term. Others say that *nesher* (the great vulture) is connected to the word *ashurennu* (I see him) (Num. 23:9);²⁹ thus means the bird who sees great distances. *peres* (bearded vulture) is related to the word *pros* (deal)³⁰ in *Is it not to deal thy bread to the hungry* (Is. 58:7);³¹ thus means the bird that breaks. *ozniyyah* (osprey) is related to the word *oz* (mighty)³² thus means the mighty bird. and its *nun* is superfluous.³³ in *oz*. It is the bird which is called *akba*;³⁴ *da’ah* (kite) is related to the word *yideh* (swoopeth) in *as the vulture swoopeth down* (Deut. 28:49);³⁵ means the swooping bird. *ayyah* (falcon) is so called because it is accustomed to dwell in certain well-known islands (*iyyim*);³⁶ means the island-dwelling bird.

²² Which is not in our verse but is mentioned in Deut. 14:13. Vat. Ebr
²³ Mentioned in Deut. 14:13.
²⁴ Mentioned in our verse. See Chullin
²⁵ For the term da’ah
²⁶ In the Sefer Ha-Shoroshim
²⁷ According to Rabbi Jonah an intelligent person would not say watch out for the aryeh
²⁸ For aryeh
²⁹ Nesher
³⁰ Literally, break.
³¹ Peres
³² Ozniyyah
³³ For there is no nun
³⁴ In Arabic.
³⁵ Da’ah
³⁶ Ayyah

ו**עורב** מלשון ערב בעבור שחרותו:


*orev* (raven) is related to the word *erev* (evening). It is so called because of its darkness.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:1)


**בת היענה.** יש אומרים שהוא מין שלא ימצאו זכרים כמו הארנבת ואין מלת כיענים טענה כי אינם זכרים רק הם נקבות כמו יעלים ורחלים:


AND THE OSTRICH. Some say that the *bat ha-ya’anah* (ostrich)³⁷ is feminine. is a kind among whom males are not found.³⁸ It is like the hare. There is no argument from the word *ka-ye’enim* (like the ostriches)³⁹ is a masculine plural form. The singular of this form is *ya’an*. We thus see that males are found among the ostriches. (Lam. 4:3), for the reference is to females as in the case of *ye’elim* (wild goats) (Ps. 104:18)⁴⁰ is a masculine plural form. However, it is the plural of *ye’elah*, which is feminine. and *rechelim*⁴¹, which is feminine. We thus see that there are instances in which a feminine singular has a plural masculine form. (ewes) (Gen. 32:15).

footnotes:
³⁷ Bat ha-ya’anah
³⁸ Hence Scripture mentions only the feminine.
³⁹ Ye’enim
⁴⁰ Ye’elim
⁴¹ Which is a masculine plural form but is actually the plural of rechelah


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:2)


**ואת התחמס.** מגזרת חמס:


AND THE NIGHT-HAWK. *Tachmas* (night-hawk) is related to the word *chamas* (violence) (Gen. 5:11).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:3)


**ואת השחף.** מוליד השחפת:


THE SEA-MEW. The *shachaf* (sea-mew) gives birth to consumption (*shachefet*) (Lev. 26:16).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:16:4)


**והנץ.** יש לו נוצה רבה והקרוב שהוא הידוע הפורש כנפיו תמיד לפאת דרום כי יבקש מקום חם:


AND THE HAWK. The *netz* (hawk) has many feathers.⁴². It most likely refers to the well-known bird which always spreads its wings to the south,⁴³ (netz) *soar by thy wisdom, and stretch her wings toward the south*? for it seeks a warm place.

⁴² The Hebrew word for feather is notzah
⁴³ See Job 39:26, Doth the hawk



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:1)


**הכוס.** ככוס חרבות ויש אומרים שהוא נכסה מעין אדם על כן ישכון במקום שאין שם יישוב:


AND THE LITTLE OWL. The word *kos* (little owl) is similar to the word *kos* (owl) in *as an owl of the waste places* (Ps. 102:7). Some say that the bird is hidden (*nikhseh*) from the eyes of people. It therefore rests in uninhabited places.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:2)


**השלך.** יש אומרים עוף מנהג תולדתו להשליך ילדיו:


AND THE CORMORANT. Some say that the *shalakh* (cormorant) is so called because its nature is to cast (*le-haslikh*) its offspring down.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:17:3)


**ינשוף.** עוף יעופף בנשף כי לא יראה ביום מפני להט אור השמש:


AND THE GREAT OWL. The *yanshuf* (great owl) is a bird that flies at night (*neshef*), for it cannot see during the day because of the brightness of the sun light.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:18:1)


**התנשמת.** כל רואהו ישום גם זה השם נמצא בשרצים:


AND THE HORNED OWL. Whoever looks at the *tinshemet* (the horned owl) is astonished (*yishom*). The term *tinshemet* is also found among the swarming things (v. 30).⁴⁴ refers to both a bird and a reptile. This is an anomaly which I.E. points out.

⁴⁴ In other words, the word tinshemet

**הקאת.** יש אומרים שיש עוף מנהג תולדתו להקיא מאכלו:


AND THE PELICAN. Some say that the *ka’at* (pelican) is a bird whose nature is to regurgitate (*le-haki*) its food.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:18:3)


**הרחם.** אמר הגאון שהוא בלשון קדר בחלוף חי״ת בכ״ף כי בכתב׳‎ אות אחד להם והאחרים אמרו שהוא עוף מרחם על בניו:


AND THE CARRION-VULTURE. The Gaon⁴⁵ says that the *racham* (carrion-vulture) is the animal which is similarly called in Arabic,⁴⁶. with the *chet* interchanging with a *khaf*, for they are written with the same letter.⁴⁷ and *khaf* are similarly writtten. Others say that *racham* refers to a bird that has pity (*merachem*) upon its offspring.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:1)


**החסידה.** היא הנראית למועדים ידועים בשנה ויש אומרים שהיא מפזרת חסד בדרך רחוקה:


AND THE STORK. Some say the *chasidah* (stork) is the bird which appears at certain well-known times in the year. Others say that it scatters loving kindness (*chesed*).⁴⁸? It is so called because it acts kindly towards its fellow creatures with regard to food.” See also *Chullin* 63a. This is far-fetched.

⁴⁸ See Rashi: “Why is it called “chasidah

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:2)


**האנפה.** שיתנאף מהרה:


AND THE HERON. The *anafah* (heron) grows angry (*yitannef*) quickly.⁴⁹ 63a.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ See Chullin


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:3)


**הדוכיפת.** אמרו הצדוקים שהיא התרנגולת ואלה טפשי עולם כי מי הגיד להם:


AND THE HOOPOE. The Sadducees say that the *dukhifat* (hoopoe) refers to the chicken. However, they are the world’s greatest fools, for who told them this?


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:4)


**העטלף.** עוף קטן יעוף בלילה והוא מלה מרובע (ר״ל בת ד׳‎ אותיות):


AND THE BAT. A small bird that flies at night. The word *atalef* (bat) is a quadriliteral; that is, it comes from a four-letter root.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:19:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:19:5)


ודע כי מלת תשקצו מן העוף. בדקדוק הטעם היא מעט רחוקה ממלת אל תשקצו את נפשותיכם כי הנפשות פעולים ובעוף טעמו שתדעו שהם שקץ ויהיו נחשבים לכם לשקץ וכל שרץ קטן שיעוף פעם ופעם הולך על ארבע שקץ:


Note that the word *teshakketzu* in *teshakketzu min ha-of* (these ye shall have in detestation among the fowls) (v. 13) has a slightly different grammatical meaning from *teshakketzu* in *al teshakketzu et nafshotekhem* (ye shall not make yourselves detestable) (v. 43). In the latter instance the word *nafshotekhem* (yourselves) is the object, while in the case of the fowl (v. 13) its meaning is, you shall know that they are detestable and they shall be considered detestable to you. All winged swarming things that at times fly and at times go upon all fours are detestable.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁰ I.E.’s interpretation of verse 20.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:21:1)


**אשר לא כרעים.** העיקר להיות לו עם וי״ו:


WHICH HAVE JOINTED LEGS. The main reading is the one that spells *lo* with a *vav*.⁵¹. The *ketiv* spells *lo* with a *vav*. With a *vav* the phrase means “which have jointed legs;” without a *vav* it reads, “which does not have jointed legs.” According to the rabbis the meaning of the latter is: which does not have jointed legs now but will grow them later. See *Sifra* on this verse.

footnotes:
⁵¹ The kre


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:21:2)


**לנתר.** אין לו אח והמתרגם אמר לקפצא בהון כמו מקפץ וטעם דולג ואמת תרגם:


TO LEAP. The word *le-natter* has no brother.⁵² The Aramaic translator renders *le-natter ba-hen* by *le-kappatza behen;* since the word *le-kappatza* is similar to *mekappetz* (leaping) (Cant. 2:8),⁵³ means wherewith to leap. its meaning is leaping. He translated correctly.

footnotes:
⁵² The word is not found again in Scripture.
⁵³ Thus le-kappatza ve-hon



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:1)


נקר׳‎ **ארבה.** מלשון ריבוי:


[THE LOCUST.] The word *arbeh* (locust) is connected to the word *ribbuy* (large amount).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:2)


**הסלעם.** מין יעלה בסלעים:


AND THE BALD LOCUST. The *sale’am* (bald locust) refers to a kind of locust which climbs on rocks (*sela’im*).⁵⁴ means a rock. Thus *sela’im* means the “rocker,” i.e., the grasshopper that climbs a rock.

⁵⁴ Sela


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:22:3

**החרגול.** אם היא מרובעת אין לו אח ואם היא שתי מלות כמלת לפלמוני המדבר תהיה תולדתו הפך תולדת הסלעם:


AND THE CRICKET. If the word *chargol* (cricket) is a quadriliteral, then it has no brother.⁵⁵ If it is a combination of two words⁵⁶ (trembling). See I. E. on Ps. 18:46 and *regel* (foot). like the word *palmoni* (certain one) in *unto that certain one who spoke* (Dan 8:13), then the nature of the *chargal* is the reverse of the nature of the *sale’am* (bald locust).⁵⁷ climbs rocks while the *chargal* has “trembling feet” and is not adept at climbing.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:22:4)


**החגב.** ידוע מלשון ישמעאל:


AND THE GRASSHOPPER. Its meaning is known from the language of the Ishmaelite.⁵⁸ in Arabic.

⁵⁸ A grasshopper is called chajaba



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:23:1)


וטעם **אשר לו ארבע רגלים.** וכבר נכתב ההולך על ארבע. שיתכן שיש שרץ יש לו ד׳‎ רגלים ולא ילך בהן רק יעוף לבדו:


[BUT ALL WINGED SWARMING THINGS.] Scripture had previously stated, *that go upon all fours* (v. 20).⁵⁹ (v. 21). Why then state here, *But all winged swarming things which have four feet are…detestable*? The Torah now states, *But all winged swarming things, which have four feet*, because it is possible that there is a swarming creature that has four legs but that does not walk upon them but only flies.⁶⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Scripture had previously stated that go on all four are…detestable
⁶⁰ Verse 20 teaches that a swarming creature that walks on four legs is detestable. Our verse teaches that a swarming creature that has four legs is detestable even if it does not walk upon them. Our verse thus not repeat verse 20.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:1)


**ולאלה תטמאו.** לכל שרץ העוף בעל ד׳‎ רגלים ואת אלה תשקצו מן העוף ודעת אחרים שפי׳‎ אלה לכל הבהמה וכל הולך על כפיו ולפי דעתי שמלת אלה על כל הנזכרים למעלה:


AND BY THESE YE SHALL BECOME UNCLEAN. By all winged swarming things that have four feet; *And these ye shall have in detestation among the fowls*.⁶¹ Others believe that the word *elleh* (these) (v. 24) refers to *among all the beasts* and to *And whatsoever goeth upon its paws* (v. 27).⁶² I believe that the word *elleh* refers to all those mentioned above.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶¹ See verse 13. In other words, verses 24 and 25 refer to that which comes before, that is to unclean fowl and unclean winged swarming things.
⁶² In other words, verses 23 and 24 introduce that which follows. See Rashi and Rashbam.
⁶³ Unclean fowl and unclean winged swarming things.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:2)


**תטמאו.** מבנין התפעל ודגשות הטי״ת להתבלע התי״ו וכן וישמע את הקול מדבר אליו וכבס המטהר:


YE SHALL BECOME UNCLEAN. The word *tittamma’u* (ye shall become unclean) is a *hitpa’el*. The *tet* receives a *dagesh* because the *tav*⁶⁴. is swallowed. The same is the case with the word *middabber* (speaking) in *then he heard the Voice speaking unto him* (Num. 7:89) and the word *mittaher* (cleansed) in *And he that is to be cleansed shall wash* (Lev. 14:8).⁶⁵ whose *tav* is compensated for by a *dagesh*.

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Of the hitpa’el
⁶⁵ Both words are hitpa’els


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:24:3)


**יטמא עד הערב.** אחז הכתוב דרך קצרה כי הוא צריך שירחץ במים:


SHALL BE UNCLEAN UNTIL THE EVEN. Scripture chose the path of brevity, for the unclean must bathe in water.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:25:1)


**והנושא.** חמור מהנוגע על כן יכבס בגדיו אף כי גופו וכן משפט כל בהמה גם פה אחז דרך קצרה וכן הוא ושסע שוסעת וגרה איננו מעלה או גרה מעלה ושסע איננו שוסעת וכבר אמר ובנבלתם לא תגעו טמאים הם לכם:


AND WHOSOEVER BEARETH. Bearing is more serious than touching. Therefore the bearer must wash his garments. How much more so [must he bathe] his body. The same law applies to all beasts.⁶⁶ Scripture also follows the path of brevity here.⁶⁷ The verse is to be understood as follows, or is clovenfooted but cheweth not the cud or cheweth the cud but is not cloven-footed.⁶⁸. However, this contradicts verses 4-7 which teache that an animal with only one kosher sign is unclean. Hence I.E. points out that the above clause is short for: a beast which cheweth the cud and parteth the hoof but is not cloven-footed, or a beast that cheweth not the cud but which parteth the hoof and is cloven-footed. Scripture has previously stated, *and their carcasses ye shall not touch; they are unclean unto you* (v. 8).⁶⁹

footnotes:
⁶⁶ It does not apply only to unclean fowl and unclean winged swarming things.
⁶⁷ As it did in verse 24.
⁶⁸ Our verse seems to imply that only an animal that has two signs of uncleanliness is unclean, for it speaks of a beast which parteth the hoof, but is not cloven-footed, nor cheweth the cud
⁶⁹ Hence Scripture does not mention here that their carcasses are unclean (Weiser).



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:26:1)


וטעם **כל הנוגע בהם.** והם הנבלות הנזכרות למעלה יטמא כמשפט הנזכרים שהוא עד הערב ויש מין ממין הצדוקים שאמרו כי כל הנוגע בהם והם חיים יטמא ואין צורך להשיב על דברי שגעון כי הכתוב לא אסר כי אם בשרם ואמר ובנבלתם לא תגעו:


[EVERY ONE THAT TOUCHETH THEM.] This means everyone who touches any of the above-mentioned carcasses shall become unclean until the evening, for that is the law regarding the previously mentioned carcasses. There is a type of misbeliever who belongs to the Sadducee heresy who says that *every one that toucheth them* means whoever touches them while they are alive shall be unclean. There is no need to respond to insane talk, for Scripture only prohibited their flesh.⁷⁰ (v. 8). Hence the prohibition against touching unclean animals applies only to their carcasses. It states, *and their carcasses ye shall not touch* (v. 8).

footnotes:
⁷⁰ To be eaten. The point is that the law dealing with touching unclean animals only concerns dead unclean animals, for Scripture explicitly states, and their carcasses ye shall not touch



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:27:1)


**וכל הולך על כפיו.** בתחלה המשפט אחד וכן הנושא את נבלת׳‎ וזה הפסוק יישר פירושי במלת ולאלה תטמאו כי שם כתוב וכל הנושא מנבלתם ושניהם בפרשה אחת:


AND WHATSOEVER GOETH UPON ITS PAWS. At first⁷¹ the law is the same.⁷² Similarly *And he that beareth the carcass of them* (v. 28).⁷³ This verse affirms my explanation of the words *And by these ye shall be come unclean* (v. 24),⁷⁴ applies to what comes before, not what follows. See I.E. on verse 24. for Scripture there states, *And whosoever beareth aught of the carcass of them* (v. 25).⁷⁵ *he that beareth the carcass of them*. According to I.E., Scripture is not repeating itself, for verse 25 refers to winged creatures while verse 28 refers to animals that walk on all fours. Both of them are in one section.

footnotes:
⁷¹ Before they died.
⁷² They have the same law as the animals mentioned in verse 26.
⁷³ The law that governs the animals mentioned in verse 28 applies here too.
⁷⁴ According to I.E., And by these ye shall be come unclean
⁷⁵ And verse 28 states, And


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:27:2)


וטעם **טמאים הם לכם.** להכניס כל ישראל אנשים ונשים וטף משפט אחד להם:


The reason Scripture states, *They are unclean unto you*, is that it wants to include all of Israel: men, women, and children.⁷⁶. Hence *unclean unto you* in our verse is not a mere repetition of the same statement. One law applies to all.

footnotes:
⁷⁶ Verse 26 states, Every one that toucheth them shall be unclean

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:29:1)

AND THESE ARE THEY WHICH ARE UNCLEAN UNTO YOU. By touch.⁷⁷ We also are not able to actually know what these eight creatures⁷⁸ refer to. This is also true of the fowl.⁷⁹ We can only know their identity by tradition.

### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:34:1

### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:35:1)


והנה התנור ללחם לאפות והכירים לבשל הבשר יותצו כי טמאים הם וזאת גזרת מלך:


Now, an *oven* (v. 35) which is used to bake bread and a *range* (*Ibid*.) which is used to cook meat shall be broken into pieces,⁸⁹ for they are unclean. This is the decree of the king.⁹⁰ (p. 112), I.E. points out that there are many laws whose meaning only the very intelligent can ascertain. I.E.’s comments here seem to be directed to this group of people.

### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:36:1)


**באר.** בלשון הקדש לשון נקבה והיא הנובעת וכן כהקיר ברר מימיה ובור כרוי או חפור למטה השמים אם כן תהיה הבאר כמו מעיין ואין צורך להזכיר הנחל ויהיה מקוה מים סמוך אל בור כי יש בור אין בו מים ויש אומרים כי הוא חסר וי״ו כמו ראובן שמעון שמש ירח:


A CISTERN. The Hebrew word *be’er* (well) is a feminine. It bubbles forth. Compare, *As a well* (bir)⁹¹ is a variant of *be’er*. Translated according to I.E. *welleth with her waters* (Jer. 6:7). However, a *bor* (cistern) is dug or excavated to store the rain from the sky. If so,⁹² the well is a fountain.⁹³ There is no need to mention rivers.⁹⁴ *Mikveh mayim* (a gathering of water) is connected to *bor* (cistern),⁹⁵ (v. 36). for there are *borot* (cisterns) without water.⁹⁶ follows *bor*. Some say that *mikveh* is missing a *vav*.⁹⁷ should be read as if written, *u-mikveh mayim*. According to this interpretation our verse reads, or a cistern, or a gathering of water. Compare, *Reuben, Simeon* (Ex, 1:2),⁹⁸ (Reuben and Simon). and *shemesh yare’ach* (the sun moon) (Hab. 3:11).⁹⁹ (the sun and the moon).

footnotes:
⁹¹ This is the ketiv. Bir
⁹² If the water in the well bubbles forth.
⁹³ Mentioned in our verse.
⁹⁴ In our verse, for the waters in the rivers have their origin in underground fountains.
⁹⁵ Bor mikveh mayim means a cistern wherein is a gathering of water
⁹⁶ Hence mikveh mayim
⁹⁷ Mikveh mayim
⁹⁸ Which is to be interpreted as if written, re’uven ve-shimon
⁹⁹ Which is to be interpreted as if written, shemesh ve-yare’ach


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:36:2)


**ונגע בנבלתם.** המים הנוגעים בנבלתם יהיו טמאים:


BUT HE WHO TOUCHETH THEIR CARCASS. The water that touches their carcasses will be unclean.¹⁰⁰

¹⁰⁰ Water that is removed from the ground.



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:37:1)


**זרוע.** שם התאר כמו פעול או הם ב׳‎ שמות והעד ונתן להם זרעונים:


SOWING. *Zeru’a* (sowing) is an adjective.¹⁰¹ (seed). It is similar to a *pa’ul*.¹⁰². On the other hand, there might be two nouns for the word seed.¹⁰³ and *zeru’a*. Compare, Rashi: *“Zera zeru’a* (sowing seed) means any kind of seed. *Zeru’a* is a noun.” *And gave them pulse* (zeronim¹⁰⁴ is a variation of the plural of *zeru’a*.) (Dan. 1:16) is proof of this.

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ Modifying zera
¹⁰² It is an adjective vocalized like a verb in the pu’al
¹⁰³ Zera
¹⁰⁴ Zeronim



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:38:1)


**וכי יותן מים.** שישקו את השדה ונפל מנבלתם על הזרע ויש אומרים על המים ובא מלת יותן שהוא לשון יחיד עם מים וכן מי נדה לא זורק עליו ומלת יותן כמו יוקח נא מעט מים מהבנין הכבד הנוסף:


BUT IF WATER BE PUT. If they water their field and aught of their carcasses fall on the seed.¹⁰⁵ (thereon) refers to the seed. This opinion interprets our verse as follows: But if water be put upon the seed, and aught of their carcass fall upon the seed, the seed is unclean. Some say on the water.¹⁰⁶ (thereon) refers to the water. This opinion interprets our verse as follows: But if water be put upon the seed, and aught of their carcass fall upon the water, the seed is unclean. The word *yuttan* (be put), which is singular, governs *mayim* (water).¹⁰⁷ We find a similarity in *because the water* (meh) *of sprinkling was not dashed* (zorak)¹⁰⁸ is singular. It governs *meh*, which is plural. *against him* (Num. 19:13). The word *yuttan* is similar to *yukkach* (be fetched) in *Let now a little water be fetched* (Gen. 18:4). It belongs to the heavy conjugation to which a letter is added.¹⁰⁹ as “the heavy conjugation to which a letter is added.” However, *yuttan* and *yukkach* are *hofals*. We either have a scribal error or I.E. was not exact in his language.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ The seed is unclean. According to this interpretation the word alav
¹⁰⁶ In other words, the word alav
¹⁰⁷ Which is a plural. I.E. points this out because verbs governing nouns in the plural are normally in the plural.
¹⁰⁸ Zorak
¹⁰⁹ I.E. usually refers to the hifil

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:1)


**אל תשקצו את נפשותיכם.** להיות׳‎ מטונפות ומגואלות.


YE SHALL NOT MAKE YOURSELVES DETESTABLE. By being filthy and dirty.¹²⁴
