###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:43:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:2)


**ולא תטמאו בהם** כי ידוע כי הגוף הנאכל ישוב בשר בגוף האוכל:


NEITHER SHALL YE MAKE YOURSELVES UNCLEAN. For it is known that that which is eaten turns into the flesh of the one who ingests it.¹²⁵

footnotes:
¹²⁵ Hence one who eats filthy things turns into a filthy thing himself.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:43:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:43:3)


**ונטמתם בם.** חסר אל״ף כמו מראשית בלי אלף השנה ויש אומרים שהם שני שרשים והעד נטמינו בעיניכם וטעמו כאדם שאין לו דעת:


THAT YE SHOULD BE DEFILED THEREBY. The word *ve-nitmetem* (that ye should be defiled) is missing an *alef*.¹²⁶ is spelled *nun, tet, mem, tav, mem*. It should be spelled *nun, tet, mem, alef, tav, mem*, for the root of *tame* is *tet, mem, alef*. Compare, *reshit*¹²⁷, though it should have been spelled with an *alef*. (beginning) in *from the beginning of the year* (Deut. 11:12). Others say that they come from two roots.¹²⁸, and *tet, mem, heh*. The word *nitmetem* in our verse comes from the latter root. Hence there is no *alef* in it. The word *nitminu* (unclean) in *And reputed unclean in your sight*¹²⁹ (Job 18:3) is proof. It¹³⁰. means that you do not become dumb-witted.¹³¹ means that you do not become dull-witted thereby.

footnotes:
¹²⁶ Nitmetem
¹²⁷ Which is spelled without an alef
¹²⁸ There are two words in Hebrew for unclean: tet, mem, alef
¹²⁹ Translated literally.
¹³⁰ That ye should be defiled thereby
¹³¹ In other words, that ye should be defiled thereby



### Verse 44

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/11:44:1)


**והייתם קדושים כי קדוש אני.** על כן אמרתי לכם ולא תטמאו והוי״ו כפ״א רפה בלשון ישמעאל וכן ויעזוב את עבדיו ורבים כמוהו:


YE SHALL THEREFORE BE HOLY, FOR I AM HOLY. Hence I told you *neither shall ye defile yourselves* (v. 44). The *vav*¹³² (ye shall therefore be). is similar to the unaspirated *fah* in Arabic.¹³³ is not a continuation of the first half of the verse. It is an independent clause. Similarly the *vav* in *va-ya’azov* (left) in *left his servants* (Ex. 9:21).¹³⁴ (left) in Ex. 9:21 is to be rendered as left, not and he left. There are many such instances.¹³⁵ has the meaning of an unaspirated *fah* in Arabic.

¹³⁴ Va-ya’azov
¹³⁵ Where the vav

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 11:45:1

וטעם **כי אני ה׳‎.** כי לא העליתי אתכם מארץ מצרים כי אם להיות לכם לאלהים ואם לא תהיו קדושים לא אהיה לכם לאלהים על כן אם רצונכם שאהיה לכם לאלהים תהיו קדושים:


[FOR I AM THE LORD.] This means I did not take you out of Egypt for any reason but to be your God. If you will not be holy, I will not be a God to you. Hence if you want Me to be your God you must be holy.

## Chapter 12


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:1)


**אשה כי תזריע.** אחר שהשלים תורת הטהור והטמא בנאכלין הזכיר טמא אדם והחל מן האשה היולדת כי הלידה היא תחלה ורבים אמרו שהאשה מזרעת תחלה יולדת זכר על כן וילדה זכר ועל כן דעת חכמי יון שהזרע לאשה וזרע הזכר מקפיא וכל הבן מדם האשה והנה פירוש תזריע תתן זרע כי היא כמו הארץ:


IF A WOMAN BE DELIVERED.¹ (literally, produces a seed). After Scripture concludes the law of clean and unclean food, it deals with human uncleanliness. The Torah starts² with the woman who gives birth, because human life begins at birth. Many say that if the woman produces the seed first,³ then she gives birth to a male.⁴ 60a; *Niddah* 28a. Scripture therefore states, *and bear a man-child*.⁵ The wise men of the Greeks similarly believed that the seed is of the woman. The seed of the male congeals.⁶ All of the child is created out of the blood of the woman.⁷ Note, the meaning of *tazri’a* (be delivered) means will give seed, for a woman⁸ (she) as in *Vat. Ebr*. 38 rather than *hu* (it) as in some printed editions. is like the ground.⁹ (zeru’ehah) *to spring forth* (Is. 62:11). Thus *ishah ki tazri’a* (if a woman be delivered) literally means if a woman gives forth her seed, i.e., the seed planted in her.

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, tazri’a
² Its regulations of human uncleanliness.
³ Before the male.
⁴ However, when the male produces the seed first then the result is a female. See Berakhot
⁵ That is, Scripture therefore says, If a woman produce seed and bear a man-child.
⁶ The Greeks believed that the male seed gives form to the seed of the woman.
⁷ According to the Greek sages the woman provides the matter and the man the form of the embryo. See I.E. on Deut. 13:7 (Vol. 5, pp. 89,90).
⁸ Reading he
⁹ The point is that the field nurtures the seed planted in it. The same is the case with a woman. Compare, And as the garden causeth the things that are sown in it


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:2)


וי״ו **וטמאה** כפ״א רפה בלשון ישמעאל. וסבת טומאתה שבעת ימים עד שובה אל המרובע וכן ימצא בימי החולי כי השנוי יראה עד סוף כל ז׳‎:


The *vav* of *ve-tame’ah* (then shall be unclean) is like the unaspirated *vav* in Arabic.¹⁰ is not to be rendered: and she shall be unclean, but, then she shall be unclean. The reason that she is unclean for seven days is that she must wait until she completes¹¹ a quarter of days.¹² We find a similarity in the days of illness, for a change is seen at the end of the seven days.¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ It does not always indicate the start of a new clause. See I.E. on Gen. 1:2 (Vol. 1, p. 30). In other words, ve-tame’ah
¹¹ Literally, returns to.
¹² The month is divided into quarters. According to the astrologers there is a change in nature and in fate at the end of each quarter. The same is true with each individual.
¹³ In other words, the quarters do not apply only to lunar months but also to events in life.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:3)


**נדת.** שם במשקל בזת והם שניהם מפעלי הכפל:


THE IMPURITY OF. The word *niddat* (impurity of) is a noun.¹⁴ It follows the paradigm of *bizzat* (spoil of).¹⁵ Both words come from a double root.¹⁶ comes from *nun, dalet, dalet. Bizzat* from *bet, zayin, zayin*.

footnotes:
¹⁴ In the construct.
¹⁵ The word is not found in the Bible. It is found in Rabbinic literature (see Rashi, Ex. 15:22).
¹⁶ Niddat


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:2:4)


**דותה.** טעמו כמו חולי כמו מדוה מצרים כי הדם היוצא הוא חולי בבשר האשה:


HER SICKNESS. *Devotah* (her sickness) is a noun in the plural.¹⁷ plus the feminine pronominal suffix. Compare, *madveh* (diseases) in *the evil diseases of Egypt* (Deut. 7:15).¹⁸ (her sickness) and *madveh* (diseases) come from the same root. Thus *devotah* means her illness. Some interpret *devotah* as her flow. See Rashi. The blood that comes out is an illness in the woman’s body.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁷ It’s the plural of davah
¹⁸ Devotah
¹⁹ A woman feels sick when she experiences the loss of blood. See Rashi, “No woman sees a flow of blood…without first experiencing a heaviness in her head and limbs.”



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:1)


**וביום השמיני.** אמרו חז״ל ביום ולא בלילה והנה הנולד חצי שעה קודם שקיעת החמה יהיה נמול לששה ימים וחצי יום כי יום התורה אינו מעת לעת:


AND IN THE EIGHTH DAY. Our wise men, of blessed memory, said during the day, not at night.²⁰ Now a child born a half hour before sunset is circumcised after six and half days,²¹ for the day of the Torah is not measured by twenty-four hours.²²

footnotes:
²⁰ Circumcision is to be performed during the day.
²¹ The end of the day that the child was born, the following six days, and the morning of the eighth day.
²² It does not consist of twenty-four hours, for part of the day is considered a whole day.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:2)


**ימול.** מבנין נפעל כמו לא יכון ויהיה מגזרת מולים היו ויתכן להיותו חסר נו״ן מגזרת ונמלתם כמו איש כי ידור נדר ויחסר המל כמו אביו או בית דין:


SHALL BE CIRCUMCISED. *Yimmol* (shall be circumcised) is a *nifal*.²³. Compare, *yikkon*²⁴. (be established) in *shall not be established* (Ps. 101:7). According to this opinion, *yimmol* is related to the word *mullim*²⁵. (circumcised) in *were circumcised* (Josh. 5:5). It is also possible that *yimmol* is missing a *nun*²⁶ comes from the root *nun, mem. lamed*. and is related to the word *u-nemaltem*²⁷. (and ye shall be circumcised) (Gen. 17:11). *Yimmol* is like the word *yiddor* (voweth)²⁸. in *When a man voweth a vow unto the Lord* (Num. 30:3). Scripture omits the one who performs the circumcision, such as the father or the Beth Din (court).²⁹ comes from the root *nun, mem, lamed*, then the meaning of *yimmol* (shall be circumcised) is, he shall circumcise. In this case the subject is missing, for Scripture omits who “he” is. Hence I.E. points out that “he” refers to the father of the child, the Beth Din, or any authorized person. For similar comments of I.E., see I.E. on Gen. 25:26; 48:1; 50:26 (Vol. 1, pp. 251, 412, 452).

footnotes:
²³ From the root mem, vav, lamed
²⁴ From the root kaf, vav, nun
²⁵ From the root mem, vav, lamed
²⁶ In this case yimmol
²⁷ From the root nun, mem, lamed
²⁸ From the root nun, dalet, resh
²⁹ If yimmol


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:3:3)


**ערלתו.** ידועה כי הוא בערוה ואין כן ערלת לב ושפה ואזן כי כלם סמוכים:


HIS FORESKIN. It is known that the foreskin is on the private part.³⁰ This is not the case regarding the foreskin of the heart,³¹ the lip,³² and the ear,³³ for all of them are connected to another word.³⁴ (foreskin) stands by itself, it refers to the foreskin of the penis. When Scripture uses the term *orlah* metaphorically, it connects it to another word, as in *aral sefatayim* (of uncircumcised lips) (Ex. 6:12).

footnotes:
³⁰ The penis.
³¹ Deut. 10:16; Jer. 4:4.

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 12:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/12:4:1)


ה״א **טהרה.** נח נעלם והי׳‎ ראוי להראות כה״א ויקרא לה נבח. וטעם דמי טהרה. שהוא דם טוהר כנגד דם נדה ואיננו מטמא והשם גזר על הזכר כמספר הזכר אשר תשלם צורתו בבטן והנקבה כפלים וזה דבר ברורה ומנוסה:


[PURIFICATION.] The *heh* of *tohorah*³⁵ should be rendered, her purification. (purification) is unsounded.³⁶ (dot) in it. According to I.E. the *heh* of *tohorah* (purification) is a pronominal suffix, and *tohorah* should be rendered as her purification. Such a *heh* usually has a dot in it. Hence I.E.’s comment. It should have been pronounced. It is like the *heh*³⁷ in it. in *lah* (it)³⁸ in *and called it Nobah* (Num. 32:43). The meaning of *deme tohorah* (the blood of purification) is clean blood.³⁹ as blood of cleanliness. This blood, in contrast to *dam niddah* (menstrual blood), is clean. It does not cause uncleanliness. The forty days that God decreed regarding a male child⁴⁰ corresponds to the time it takes for the form of the male to be completed in the womb.⁴¹ 3:7). It takes double that time for a female.⁴². This is clear and tested.

**בן שנתו.** לולי הקבלה מי היה מפרש לנו בן שנתו דאם הוא בן שנה תמימה לא יותר או פחות או תוספות ויש אומרים שטעם הכבש לעולה שמא עלתה שום מחשבה על רוחה בשעת לדתה מרוב הצער. חטאת שמא דברה בפיה:


OF THE FIRST YEAR. If we did not have tradition, who would explain whether *ben shenato* (of the first year)⁴⁶ means of the first year. See *Mekhilta* 4. means exactly one year, or less than a year, or a year plus?⁴⁷ Some say that the reason a woman who gives birth brings a lamb for a burnt offering is that she may have had some unseemly thought because of her pain when giving birth.⁴⁸ She brings a sin offering because she might have uttered some unseemly thing with her lips.⁴⁹ 31b: “Rabbi Johanan ben Zakkai’s student asked: Why did the Torah say that a woman who gives birth shall bring an offering? He said to them: when she crouches to give birth she swears that she will not have sexual relations with her husband.”

**וידבר ה׳‎ אל משה ואל אהרן.** וטעם אהרן. כי על פיו יהיו כל נגעי אדם מי שיטהר ומי שיטמא:


AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO MOSES AND UNTO AARON. This commandment was directly communicated to Aaron because all human maladies shall be determined according to his pronouncement.¹ Aaron shall declare who is clean and who is unclean.

¹ See Deut. 21:5.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:2:1)


**אדם.** ולא אמר איש איש מבית ישראל להכנים הגרים וכן אדם כי יקריב מכם כי תורה אחת לאזרח ולגר בקרבנות גם כן והכניס הגר שלא יטמא אחר כי הצרעת היא מהחליים הנדבקים מהחולה אל השלם:


WHEN A MAN. Scripture does notread, “Whosoever… of the house of Israel”² because it wants to include strangers.³, Scripture employs the term “man” because the latter includes converts to Judaism. According to Krinsky, I.E. interprets similarly. However, this is far from certain, for elsewhere I.E. refers to any non-Jew living in the Land of Israel as a stranger. See I.E. on Ex. 20:8 (Vol. 2, p. 427). Similarly *When any man of you*⁴. (Lev. 1:2), for there is one law to the native born and to the stranger⁵. regarding offerings. The Torah includes the stranger [in the laws relating to maladies]⁶ so that the stranger does not defile another person, for leprosy is a disease that is passed on from the sick to the healthy.⁷

² I.E. paraphrases Lev. 20:2. His point is that our verse reads “a man,” not an Israelite.
³ According to the Sifra
⁴ Bringeth an offering unto the Lord
⁵ See Lev. 22:18: Whosoever he be of the house of Israel, or of the strangers in Israel
⁶ The stranger, like the Israelite, is quarantined as long as he has the malady.
⁷ This comment seems to indicate that I.E., contrary to Krinsky, refers to a non-Jewish stranger.

THEN HE SHALL BE BROUGHT. By his consent or against his will, for one who sees one of these signs⁸ will force the afflicted [to go to the *kohen* ].

OR UNTO ONE OF HIS SONS THE PRIESTS. This means one of the common priests outside of the temple¹⁰ such as the *kohanim* of Anatot.¹¹. It was the birthplace of the prophet Jeremiah. See Jer. 1:1. [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:2:5)


ומלת **שאת.** כמו שרפה וכן והמשאת החלה וישאם דוד ויתכן שנקרא כן בעבור שהאש תולדתה להנשא למעלה:


[A RISING.] The word *se’et* (a rising) means a burn.¹³ is the name of an illness caused by the burning bile in the body (Nahmanides). Similarly *maset* (fire) in *But when the fire began to rise*¹⁴ (Jud. 20:40) and *va-yisa’em david* (and David burnt them)¹⁵ (II Sam. 5:21). The *se’et* is probably so called because it is the nature of fire to rise upwards.¹⁶, which means to lift up. Hence I.E.’s comment.

A SCAB. The word *sappachat* (scab) is related to *sefachani* (put me)¹⁷ in *Put me, I pray thee* (I Sam. 2:36) *and ve-nispechu* (and they shall cleave) in and *they shall cleave to the house of Jacob* (Is. 14:1). It refers to a disease which attaches itself to one place.

A BRIGHT SPOT. The word *baheret* (bright spot) is related to the word *bahir* (bright) in *which is bright in the skies* (Job 37:21). It is a known malady which produces a mark and a sign.

STAY IN ITS APPEARANCE. *Amad be-enav*²⁵ means stay in its appearance. The term *enav*²⁶ is employed, for its appearance is seen by the eye. ###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:6:1)

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:6:2)


**כהה הנגע.** רבים החכמים אמרו כמו חשך ועדותם ותכהין עיניו מראות ואמרו כי כהות לבנות פירושו כמו לבנה אדמדמת שיהיו בו שניהם ולפי דעתי שמלת כהה הפך פשה ומגזרת ותכהין עיניו והקרוב אליו ולא כהה בם וטעם לא פשה הנגע במקום אחר והנה הם שני סימנים שכהה המקום הנגוע ולא פשה בשלם:


THE PLAGUE BE DIM. Many of the wise men say that the word *kehah* (dim) means dark.²⁸ (the plague be dim) means the affliction is black. They offer proof from *vatikhhena* (were dim) in *and his eyes were dim* (Gen. 27:1).²⁹ They say that the phrase *kehot levanot* (dull white) (v. 39) is similar to *le-vanah adamdamet* (reddish-white) (v. 19)³⁰ (dull white) means black white. in that it contains both colors. However, I believe that the word *kehah* (dim) is the reverse of *pasah* (spread).³¹ It is related to the word *va-tikhhena* (were dim) in *and his eyes were dim* (Gen. 27:1). The word *khihah* (stopped) in *and he diminished*³² *them not*³³ (I Sam. 3:13) is close to it. The meaning of *kahah ha-nega* (the plague be dim) is that the plague did not spread to another place. Note, there are two signs, namely, the size of the affliction was diminished and it did not spread to the healthy part of the body.

²⁹ According to this interpretation the clause should be rendered, and his eyes were dark.
³⁰ In other words, kehot levanot
³¹ It means diminished.
³² Translated according to I.E. See Krinsky.
³³ He did not “cut them down to size.”

THEN THE PRIEST SHALL PRONOUNCE HIM CLEAN.³⁴ The *kohen* shall say that he is clean,³⁵ shall declare him clean. for it was a scab (*mispachat*). However, if it spreads afterwards he is unclean.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:9:1)


**והובא.** האדם כחברו:


THEN HE SHALL BE BROUGHT. The man having the affliction. *Be brought* is to be interpreted like its counterpart.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ In verse 2. It means he shall be brought with or without his consent. See I.E. on verse 2.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:10:1)


**והיא הפכה.** השער השחור ללבן:


AND IT HAVE TURNED. The black hair white.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁷ The illness turned the black hair white.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:10:2)


**ומחית.** פירוש בשר חי והנה הבשר שלא ירגיש איננו כן:


QUICK. The meaning of *michyat* (quick) is live flesh.³⁸ (raw flesh; literally, live flesh) explains *michyat* (quick). Flesh which does not feel is not alive.

footnotes:
³⁸ In other words, basar chai



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:11:1)


**נושנת.** קדמונית:


OLD. Of yore.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:11:2)


מלת **יסגירנו.** יוצאה לשנים פעולים והטעם שיצוה שיסגר:


*Yasgirennu* (shut him up) governs two actions.³⁹. It means he shall command that the afflicted be shut up.

³⁹ I.E.’s term for the hifil



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:13:1)


**וטהר את הנגע.** כי כבר יצא הנגע כולו לחוץ והוא סר ממנו וטעם **וטהר את הנגע.** בדבור שאותו הנגע לא יטמא אחרים:


HE SHALL PRONOUNCE HIM CLEAN THAT HATH THE PLAGUE. For the plague has already surfaced and removed itself from the afflicted. The meaning of *ve-tiher et hanaga*⁴⁰ (he shall pronounce him unclean that hath the plague) is that he shall verbally clean the affliction; that is, he shall declare that this plague will not make others unclean.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Literally, and he shall clean the malady.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:16:1

**או כי ישוב.** שיתכן שישוב:


BUT IF THE RAW FLESH AGAIN BE TURNED.⁴¹ For it is possible that the affliction will return.

⁴¹ Literally, but if the raw flesh return. Hence I.E.’s comment.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:19:1)


**לבנה אדמדמת.** מעורבת משני העינים או קצתם כן:


REDDISH-WHITE. A mixture of the two colors. Or a little of each.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:19:2)


מלת **צרעת** מחלה וכן ושלחתי את הצרעה ואין מלת ושלחתי טענה כי הנה ישלח דברו אני שולח את כל מגפותי ישלח בם חרון אפו:


The word *tzara’at* (leprosy) (v. 20) means a disease. Similarly *ha-tzirah* (the disease)⁴² in *I will send the disease* (Ex. 23:28). The fact that the Torah reads, *and I will send* is no argument against this interpretation,⁴³ means a disease. There are some who argue that the term “sent” (*shalach*) applies only to living things. They translate *ha-tizirah* (the disease) in Ex. 23:28 as the hornet. They render Ex. 23:28 as *I will send the hornet*. for Scripture reads, *He sent His word* (Ps. 107:20); *For I…send all My plagues* (Ex. 9:14); and *He sent forth upon them the fierceness of His anger* (Ps. 78:49).

footnotes:
⁴² Translated according to I.E.
⁴³ That tzirah



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:20:1)


**והנה מראה.** לא יתכן בדקדוק מראה בקמץ גדול תחת האל״ף והה״א במפיק כמו ידה רגלה כי לא נמצא כן בבעלי הה״א הנח הנעלם בסוף המלה ודגשות נו״ן:


AND, BEHOLD, IF THE APPEARANCE THEREOF. It is not grammatically correct to vocalize the *alef* of *mareha* (its appearance) with a long *kamatz* and a *mappik*⁴⁴ of *marah* is a pronominal suffix. We would therefore expect it to have a *mappik*. Hence I.E.’s comment. in the *heh* as in the case of *yadah* (her hand) and *raglah* (her foot),⁴⁵ (her hand) and *raglah* end in *kamatz, mappik heh*. If *marehah* followed their vocalization, the word would read *marah*. for we do not find words whose roots end in a silent *heh* to be so treated.⁴⁶.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ A dot which indicates a pronominal suffix (her or its). The heh
⁴⁵ Yadah
⁴⁶ The root of marehah is resh, alef, heh



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:21:1)


**יראנה** כמו יסגירנה לחסרון הה״א הנוסף וכן מלת ממנה וממנו:


[LOOK ON IT.] The *nun* of *yirennah* (look upon it) has a *dagesh* in it like the *nun* of *yasgirennu* (v. 11) to compensate for the missing added *heh*.⁴⁷. The word should have read *yirenhah. Yasgirennu* should have read *yasgirennhu*. The same is true with *mi-mennah* (from her) and *mi-mennu* (from it).⁴⁸ and *mi-mennu* (from it) should have read *mi-mennhah* and *mi-mennhu*. See I. E. on Ex 1:9 (Vol. 2, pp. 11-14).

footnotes:
⁴⁷ A pronominal suffix heh
⁴⁸ Mi-mennah



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:23:1)


**ואם תחתיה.** אם עמדה במקומה כי יש נגע שיסע ממקום אל מקום:


IN ITS PLACE.⁴⁹ (literally, beneath it). If the affliction stays in its place,⁵⁰ is to be rendered, it its place. for there are afflictions that move from place to place.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ Hebrew, tachtehah
⁵⁰ Tachtehah


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:23:2)


**צרבת.** כמו ונצרב׳‎ בו כל פנים כטעם מוקד וחום ולהבת:


SCAR. The word *tzarevet* (scar) is similar to the word *ve-nitzrevu* (shall be seared) in *and all faces…shall be seared* (Ezek. 21:30). It has the meaning of a burning mass, heat, and a flame.⁵¹ (scar) is a mark caused by the searing of the skin. It does not indicate disease.

footnotes:
⁵¹ A tzarevet



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:24:1)


**או לבנ׳‎.** לבדה:


OR WHITE. Alone.



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:28:1)


**ואם תחתיה תעמוד הבהרת. והיא כהה.** כאשר פירשתי ולא הזכיר כן בפסוק ואם תחתיה תעמוד הבהרת הראשון בעבור שעמדה הבהרת כאשר היתה:


AND IF THE BRIGHT SPOT STAY IN ITS PLACE…BUT BE DIM. The word *kehah* (dim) is to be understood as I explained it earlier (v. 6).⁵² means be diminished. Scripture does not mention *kehah* (dim) the first time, *But if the bright spot stay in its place* (v. 23), because there the bright spot remains as it was.⁵³

footnotes:
⁵² According to I.E. kehah
⁵³ In verse 23 if the affliction remains as it was, it is declared clean. However, the affliction in our verse has to diminish, in addition to not spreading, for the afflicted person to be declared clean (Krinsky).



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:29:1)


וטעם להזכיר **ואיש או אשה.** בעבור הזקן ועוד כי הוא צריך לאמר על הקרח והגבח איש ולא אדם סלל להוציא האשה והנה פירוש (פי׳‎ מ״ח ואיש או אשה ר״ל אחר שלקח לשון איש שאינו כולל לאשה כמו מלת אדם. הוצרך לומר אשה בעבור הראש לא בעבור הזקן כי הזקן שב לאיש ולא לאשה ובקרח ובגבח אמר כמו שאמר ואיש כי ימרט ראשו ולא הזכיר בו אשה כי לא תמצא קרחת וגבחת באשה מפני הלחות שבה כמו שמפרש למטה. ומה שאמר ולא אדם. ר״ל ולא אמר אדם שהוא כלל לאיש ולאשה עד כאן) כי יהיה בו נגע בראש או בזקן לאיש:


[AND WHEN…] Scripture mentions a man or a woman⁵⁴ (man). It now employs *ish ve-ishah* (a man or a woman). Hence I.E.’s comment. because it deals with an affliction in the beard⁵⁵, then we would have thought that the law spoken of in our verse applies only to males. Hence Scripture speaks of a man and a woman. The law regarding the affliction in the head applies to both a man and a woman, while the law of the beard applies only to a man. [and the head]. Also, because the Torah has to use the word *ish* (man), and not the term *adam* (a human being) to exclude women from the law of the bald head and the bald forehead (v. 40).⁵⁶ with regard to the aforementioned. Scripture thus wanted to emphasize that the law regarding afflictions in the hairy head applies to both men and women. Note, our verse means, when a man or a woman has an affliction upon the head or a man upon the beard.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Till now Scripture introduced the afflictions with the term adam
⁵⁵ Our verse deals with an affliction in the head and the beard. Had it read adam
⁵⁶ The law regarding an affliction in a bald head does not apply to a woman, for women are rarely bald. Hence Scripture could not use the term adam
⁵⁷ So Sarim and Krinsky.



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:30:1)


**צהוב.** בלשון ישמעאל קרוב מעין הלובן:


YELLOW. The word *tzahov* (yellow) in Arabic refers to a color that is close to white.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:30:2)


**נתק הוא.** מגזרת כאשר יונתק פתיל הנעורת והטעם על השער:


IT IS A SCALL. The word *netek* (scall) is related to the word *yennatek* (is broken) in *as a string of tow is broken* (Jud. 16:9). The reference is to the hair.⁵⁸ is so called because the hair is torn or removed from the place of the malady.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ A netek



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:33:1)


**והתגלח.** הראש או הזקן ומקום הנתק לא יגלח:


THEN HE SHALL BE SHAVEN. The head or the beard shall be shaven. However, the place of the *netek* (scall) shall not be shaven.



### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:34:1)


**וכבס בגדיו.** ואין צורך לאמר שירחץ במים:


AND HE SHALL WASH HIS CLOTHES. It is unnecessary to say that he shall bathe in water.



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:36:1)


**לא יבקר הכהן.** הטעם יבקש וכן ובקרתם וקרוב מלשון הבדלה וכן לא יבקר בין טוב לרע:


SHALL NOT SEEK. The meaning of *yevakker* is, he shall seek. Similarly *u-vikkartim* (and seek them out) (Ezek. 34:11). It is close in meaning to the word separate.⁵⁹ Similarly *He shall not inquire* (yevakker) *whether it be good or bad* (Lev. 27:33).

footnotes:
⁵⁹ It means to investigate in order to separate.



### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:39:1)


**בהק הוא.** זאת המלה ידועה בלשון חז״ל ואין ריע לו במקרא:


A TETTER. The word *bohak* (tetter) is known in Rabbinic literature.⁶⁰. 1: “When the candles burned brightly (*mavhikim*).” It has no counterpart in Scripture.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ It has the meaning of shine or glisten. See Yershalmi, Pes



### Verse 40

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:40:1)


**ימרט.** כמו ולחיי למורטים בעבור השער שהוא סביב הלחיים:


BE FALLEN OFF. *Yimmaret* (be fallen off) is similar to *moretim* (them that plucked off the hair) in *And my cheeks to them that plucked off the hair* (Is. 50:6).⁶¹ and have a similar meaning. The latter⁶² refers to the hair that is around the cheeks.

footnotes:
⁶¹ Both words come from the root mem, resh, tet
⁶² Unlike the similar word in our verse, which refers to the hair on the head.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:40:2)


**קרח.** מגזרת לא תשימו קרחה וטעם **ראשו.** כלו:


HE IS BALD. *Kere’ach* (bald) is similar to *korchah* (baldness) in *nor make any baldness* (Deut. 14:1). The meaning of *his head* is, his entire head.



### Verse 41

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:41:1)


**גבח.** אין לו חבר חוץ מהפרשה הזאת והוא שם התאר ולפי דעתי שקרח הוא במעלה של הראש ולא הזכיר האשה בעבור הלחה הרבה שיש בה לא יקרח ראשה כי השער הוא כדמות עשב:


FOREHEAD-BALD. The word *gibbe’ach* (forehead-bald) has no neighbor⁶³ is not found elsewhere in Scripture. except in this section. *Gibbe’ach* is an adjective. In my opinion *kere’ach* (bald) refers to the top of the head.⁶⁴ Scripture does not mention a woman⁶⁵ because a woman does not become bald as she contains a lot of moisture,⁶⁶ and hair is similar to grass.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁶³ The word gibbe’ach
⁶⁴ From the top of the head backwards to the base of the skull.
⁶⁵ In introducing this law.
⁶⁶ A woman’s body contains more water than that of a man.
⁶⁷ Grass requires water in order to grow. Similarly, hair requires moisture to grow. Thus a well-watered body such as that of a female will not lose its hair. I.E. speaks from the point of view of medieval science. What he says is rejected by modern biology.



### Verse 43

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:43:1)


**כמראה צרעת עור בשר.** שאר הגוף:


AS THE APPEARANCE OF LEPROSY IN THE SKIN OF THE FLESH. On the rest of the body.⁶⁸

footnotes:
⁶⁸ See Rashi: “As the appearance of leprosy that is stated in the section (treating) of the skin of the flesh (Lev. 13:2).”



### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:1)


**בגדיו יהיו פרומים.** כמו קרועים להכירו ללכת במנהג משונה או טעמו כענין אבל על כן בגדיו יהיו פרומים וראשו פרוע והטעם שיתאבל על רוע מעשיו כי בעבור מעשיו בא לו זה הנגע:


HIS CLOTHES SHALL BE RENT. The word *perumim* means rent. The leper is to be so dressed so that he may be recognized because of his abnormal garb. Or the leper is to behave like a mourner. Scripture therefore states, *his clothes shall be rent, and the hair of his head shall go loose*. The reason for the latter is so that the leper mourn for his evil actions, for this plague came upon him because of his deeds.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:45:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:2)


**ועל שפם.** למעלה מהשפם והמ״ם מהשורש, והעד ולא עשה שפמו:


HIS UPPER LIP. *Safam* refers to the upper lip. The *mem* of *safam* is a root letter. The word *sefamo* (his beard) in *nor trimmed his beard* (II Sam. 19:22)⁶⁹. is proof.⁷⁰ of *safam* were not a root letter, then it would not be present when the word is followed by a pronominal suffix.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ I.E. renders this as nor trimmed his upper lip
⁷⁰ If the mem


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:45:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:3)


**יעטה.** בבגדיו מגזרת עוטה אור והטעם שלא יזיק ברוח פיו:


AND HE SHALL COVER.⁷¹ With his garments. The word *yateh* (he shall cover) is related to the word *oteh* (coverest) in *Who coverest Thyself with light* (Ps. 104:2). He shall cover his upper lip so that he does not harm anyone with the breath of his mouth.

footnotes:
⁷¹ His upper lip.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:45:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:45:4)


**וטמא.** פעמים שיאמר כן תמיד בעברו במסלה שיש שם ישוב שישמרו בני אדם ולא יגעו בו:


AND…UNCLEAN. The word *unclean* is repeated. When the leper passes on a road in an inhabited area he shall continually announce⁷² that he is unclean so that people will be on guard and not touch him.

footnotes:
⁷² The repetition of the word unclean indicates consistency. It does not mean he should announce twice that he is unclean.



### Verse 46

### Verse 49

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:49:1)


**ירקרק.** מגזרת ירק כי העין כמוהו וזה כפול לחסרון וכן שחרחר׳‎ ויש אומרים הפך הדבר:


GREENISH. The word *yerakrak* (greenish) is related to the word *yerek* (green), for the color of the affliction is like it. The word is doubled⁷⁸ and *kof* of *yerek* are doubled in *yerakrak*. to indicate that it is less than full green. The same applies to *shecharchoret* (swarthy) (Cant. 1:6).⁷⁹ (black). *Shecharchoret* means blackish. Others say that the reverse is true.⁸⁰ (greenish) means very green and *shecharchoret* very black.

### Verse 51

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:51:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:51:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:51:1)


**ממארת.** כמו סלון ממאיר ואחר שסלון כמו קוץ יהיה ממאיר כמו מכאיב והמ״ם הב׳‎ במלת ממארת שורש ואינה מגזרת מארה. וטעם שלא הזכיר משי וצמר גפן יתכן שדבר הכתוב על ההוה הנמצא כטעם כי תראה חמור שונאך וכן משפט הסוס והפרד או יתכן שלא יארע הנגע כי אם לצמר ופשתים:


MALIGNANT. The word *mameret* (malignant) is similar to the word *mammir* (pricking) in *a pricking brier* (sillon mamir) (Ezek. 28:24). Now, since the word *sillon* (brier) means a thorn, the word *mamir* means painful.⁸¹ (Ezek. 28:24) as a painful thorn. The second *mem* in *mameret* is a root letter and the word *mameret* is not related to the word *me’erah* (curse).⁸² has only one *mem* in it. It is possible that the reason Scripture does not mention silk and cotton⁸³ is that the Torah speaks of what is contemporarily present.⁸⁴ Compare, *If thou see the ass of him that hateth thee* (Ex. 23:5). The same law applies to a horse and a mule. On the other hand, it is possible that the affliction only strikes wool and flax.

footnotes:
⁸¹ I. E. renders sillon mamir
⁸² For the word me’erah
⁸³ When it lays down the laws dealing with plagues in garments.
⁸⁴ Plagues do not usually strike silk or wool (Weiser).

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:55:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:55:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:55:1)


**הכבס את הנגע.** שם הפעל שלא נקרא שם פועלו מהבנין הכבד הנוסף:


AFTER THAT THE PLAGUE IS WASHED. The word *hukkabbes* (is washed) is an infinitive whose subject is not identified. It is in the *hofal*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:55:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:55:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:55:2)


**פחתת.** אין לה אח במקרא והיא ידועה בלשון חז״ל וטעמו חסרון הוא שאירע בקרחת הבגד או בגבחתו והגאון אמר שפירש בקרחת הפאה האחרת כי בגבחת היא מפאת פני הבגד אם כן יהיה קרח מאחור הראש ויפה פירש:


A FRET. The word *pechetet* (fret) has no brother in Scripture.⁸⁵ It is known in Rabbinic literature.⁸⁶ in Rabbinic literature refers to a cavity, a pit or a defective spot. See *Sotah* 21a; *Betzah* 4:3. *Pechetet* refers to a defect which appears in the *karachat* (bareness…within) or the *gabachat* (bareness…without) of the garments. The Gaon says that *karachat* refers to the underside of the garment, for *gabachat* refers to the face of the garment. If this is the case, then the word *kere’ach* (bald) (v. 40) refers to the back of the head. He explained well.

footnotes:
⁸⁵ The word is not found again in Scripture.
⁸⁶ Pachat



### Verse 57

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:57:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:57:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:57:1)


**ואם תראה עוד.** הפחתת וכן פורחת היא ואחר שאמר תשרפנו באר כי מקום הנגע לבדו ישרף:


AND IF IT APPEAR STILL. The word *tera’eh* (it appear)⁸⁷ refers to the *pechetet* (the fret).⁸⁸ (plague), which is masculine. Similarly *porachat hi* (it is breaking out).⁸⁹ (it) is feminine. It too refers to *pechetet*, not to *nega*. After stating thou shalt burn [it], Scripture goes on to say that the place of the affliction alone shall be burned.⁹⁰ (thou shalt burn that wherein the plague is with fire) as thou shalt burn it (*tisrefennu*), i.e., the place where the malady is. In other words, “it” refers to the place on the garment, not to the entire garment.

### Verse 59

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 13:59:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:59:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/13:59:1)


**זאת תורת נגע צרעת בגד הצמר.** ארבע סמוכים ויש במקרא חמש גבורי חיל מלאכת עבודת בית ה, וכלם סמוכים אל השם שהוא סומך לכל הנופלים:


THIS IS THE LAW OF THE PLAGUE OF LEPROSY IN A GARMENT OF WOOL. Our verse has four words in the construct.⁹⁴ (this is the law of the plague of leprosy). Elsewhere in Scripture we find five words in the construct.⁹⁵ Compare, *Gibore chayil melechet avodat bet haElohim* (very able men for the work of the service of the house of God) (I Chron. 9:13). They all depend (*semukhim*)⁹⁶ on God, who supports (*somekh*) all who fall.⁹⁷ literally means connected or dependent. Hence I.E.’s homily.

## Chapter 14


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:1:1)


אמר **וידבר ה׳‎.** כי יחל תורת המטהר ואמר ר׳‎ יונה הספרדי המדקדק כי נרפא נגע הצרעת מן הצרוע הוא הפוך והיה ראוי להיותו נרפא הצרוע מנגע הצרעת ולמה נהפוך דברי אלהים חיים בעבור חוסר דעתנו והלא שם כתוב כי נרפא הנתק טהור הוא ועוד כי נרפא הנגע:


[AND THE LORD SPOKE.] Scripture states *and the Lord spoke*¹ in Lev. 13:1 would appear to apply to our chapter as well. Therefore the clause *And the Lord spoke unto Moses* in Lev. 14:1 seems unnecessary. Hence I.E. points out that our portion begins with *And the Lord spoke* because it contains a new commandment, *viz*., the laws of the cleansing of the leper. In other words, our chapter is not a mere continuation of what came before it. because it begins the laws of the one who is cleansed. Rabbi Jonah the Spaniard, the grammarian, says that *and, behold, if the plague of leprosy be healed in the leper* (v. 3) is inverted. It should have read “and, behold, if the leper be healed from the plague of leprosy.”²

footnotes:
¹ Our chapter continues the theme of the preceding chapter. Thus the statement And the Lord spoke unto Moses
² In other words, our verse should be interpreted as if written, if the leper be healed from the plague of leprosy, for according to Rabbi Jonah it is the person not the plague who is healed.

[HE SHALL BE BROUGHT.] *He shall be brought* is to be interpreted like its counterparts,⁴, by his consent or against his will.” for it is possible that after the plague of leprosy is removed the afflicted person will not want to bring what he is obligated to.⁵

TO TAKE FOR HIM THAT IS TO BE CLEANSED. The *kohen* shall take of his own.⁹ shall command someone to take the items enumerated in our verse from the *kohen’s* property. I.E.’s point is that *mittaher* is a *hitpa’el*. in *he that boasteth himself* (Prov. 25:14).

**ועץ ארז ואזוב.** הוא הגדול וקטן במיני הצמחים והעד מדברי חכמת שלמה ואין צורך לחפש על האזוב כי הוא ידוע בקבלה. והנה המצורע והבית המנוגע וטומאת המת קרובים והנה גם הם כדמות פסח מצרים:


AND CEDAR-WOOD…AND HYSSOP. These are the biggest and smallest among the plants. This is proven by Scripture’s description of Solomon’s wisdom.¹⁵ There is no reason to search for the meaning of the word *ezov* (hyssop), for it is known by tradition.¹⁶ 14:6. **והזה.** מהדם המעורב אחר טבול גם הצפור החיה והנזכרים:


AND HE SHALL SPRINKLE. From the blood mixed with water after immersing the living bird and the other items mentioned.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ The cedar-wood, the scarlet, and the hyssop.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:7:2)


**על פני השדה.** מקום שאין שם יישוב שלא תדבק הצרעת. וההזיה בשלשתן או באזוב לבדו כטומאת המת:


INTO THE OPEN FIELD. An uninhabited place so that the leprosy does not infect anyone. The sprinkling was done with all three.²⁷ On the other hand, it might only have been done with the hyssop as in the case of one who has been made unclean by a corpse.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁷ The cedar-wood, the scarlet, and the hyssop.
²⁸ See Num. 19:18.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:8:1)


**וכבס. וגלח את כל שערו.** כלל:


AND…SHALL WASH HIS CLOTHES AND SHAVE OFF ALL HIS HAIR. A general statement.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ The first half of our verse does not describe what the leper is to undergo on the day that he is sprinkled with the hyssop, but rather describes in general terms what the leper must undergo seven days later in order to be cleansed. The details are given in the next verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:8:2)


**ורחץ במים וטהר.** בערב כמשפט ואחר כן שב לפרש כי זה יהיה אחר ימים שבעה וכן היה משפט שבעה של מרים ופרט כי יגלח את כל שערו ביום השביעי וביאר כל שערו את ראשו ואת זקנו ואת גבות עיניו וזאת המלה קרובה מגזרת גב:


AND BATHE HIMSELF IN WATER, AND HE SHALL BE CLEAN. In the evening, as is the law.³⁰. See also Lev. 11:32. After stating this,³¹. Scripture backtracks and explains that this³² occurs after seven days.³³ describes what happens before the bathing and shaving. The same law applied to the seven days of Miriam.³⁴ Scripture details³⁵ that he shall shave all his hair on the seventh day. Scripture then goes on to explain that the meaning of *all his hair* is, the hair on his head, his beard, and his eyebrows. The word *gabbot* (eyebrows) is close in meaning to the word *gav* (high place).³⁶

footnotes:
³⁰ See Lev. 17:15: And bathe himself in water, and be unclean until the even; then shall he be clean
³¹ And…shall wash his clothes and shave off all his hair, and bathe himself in water, and he shall be clean
³² The washing and shaving.
³³ In other words, But shall dwell outside his tent seven days
³⁴ Miriam, who was struck with leprosy, had to dwell outside of the camp for seven days. See Num. 12:14.
³⁵ In verse 9.
³⁶ See Ezek. 16:24.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:9:1)


**ואת כל שערו.** שער הרגלים ויש אומרים אף זרועיו ושוקיו והחזה:


EVEN ALL HIS HAIR. The hair of the feet. Some say the hair of his arms, thighs, and chest.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:10:1)


**שני כבשים וכבשה אחת.** בעבור היות נגע הצרעת מוסר על מעשה הלשון על כן יקריב כבש אחד לעולה כמשפט כל העולה על הרוח ואחד אשם כמשפט כל אשם ונקבה לחטאת כמשפט כל חטאת ושלשה עשרונים כמשפט עשרון לכבש:


TWO HE-LAMBS…AND ONE EWE-LAMB. The leper shall bring a he-lamb for a burnt offering as is the law regarding all who have evil thoughts,³⁷ because the plague of leprosy is a punishment for deeds of the tongue.³⁸ 15b. Another he-lamb is brought as a guilt offering as is the law with all guilt offerings.³⁹ A ewe-lamb is offered for a sin offering as is the law with all sin offerings.⁴⁰ Also three tenth parts of an *ephah* as is the law, for a tenth part of an *ephah* is brought with a lamb.⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁷ See I.E. on Lev. 1:4.
³⁸ See Num. 12:1-13. Also see Arakhin
³⁹ See Lev. 5:18.
⁴⁰ See Lev. 4:32.
⁴¹ See verse 21.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:10:2)


**ולוג אחד.** מדה ואין לו אח וטעם אותם הכבשים והכבשה גם זה האשם גם זאת החטאת לכהן חוץ מהאמורים:


AND ONE LOG. A measure.⁴² refers to a measure. The term *log* has no brother.⁴³ [11. AND THOSE THINGS.] This refers to the he-lambs and the ewe-lamb. Also, this guilt offering⁴⁴ and also the sin offering,⁴⁵ with the exception of the parts offered on the altar,⁴⁶ belong to the *kohen*.⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴² The word log
⁴³ The word is not found again in Scripture.
⁴⁴ Like other guilt offerings.
⁴⁵ Like other sin offerings.
⁴⁶ Which are burned on the altar.
⁴⁷ The guilt offering and the sin offering brought by the leper are part of a cleansing ritual. One might think that they are not to be treated as all other guilt and sin offerings. Hence I.E.’s comments.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:14:1)


**על תנוך.** טעמו ידוע ממקומו והנה המטהר מהצרעת שהוא בגוף כמו הכהן שימלא ידו כי העון כמו הצרעת בנפש והבהן מקום חבור והוא עקר כל המעשים והימנות בעבור כח הצד הימין והתנוך זכר לשמוע אשר צוה:


UPON THE TIP. The meaning of *tenuch* (tip) is understood from its context.⁴⁸ Now, the one who is cleansed from leprosy, which is an affliction in the body, is similar to the *kohen* who is installed,⁴⁹ for sin is like a leprosy in the soul.⁵⁰ who is installed is cleansed from leprosy of the soul, i.e., from sin. The thumb is the place of connection.⁵¹ It is the main component of all acts.⁵² The right thumb was selected because of the power of the right side. The tip of the ear is a reminder to listen to what God has commanded.⁵³

footnotes:
⁴⁸ It refers to a part of the ear.
⁴⁹ Part of the installation ritual entailed the placing of the blood of a sacrifice on the tip of the right ear, upon their thumbs and the great toe of their foot.
⁵⁰ The kohen
⁵¹ I.E. believed that the sinews of the fingers are connected to the thumb. See Krinsky.
⁵² The thumb is necessary for all sophisticated acts of the hand.
⁵³ The placing of the blood of the guilt offering on the tip of the ear serves as a reminder that one should listen to God’s commandments.



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:15:1)


**על כף הכהן השמאלית.** היא כף הכהן המטהר רק כן דרך צחות בלשון הקדש והעד הפסוק הבא אחריו:


INTO THE PALM OF HIS OWN LEFT HAND.⁵⁴ *The palm* in *kaf ha-kohen* (the palm of his own)⁵⁵ refers to the palm of the *kohen* who cleanses. Scripture reads thus⁵⁶ because such style is considered elegant in the Hebrew language.⁵⁷ The verse that follows is proof of this.⁵⁸.

footnotes:
⁵⁴ Literally, into the palm of the priest’s left hand.
⁵⁵ Literally, the palm of the priest.
⁵⁶ Into the palm of the priest’s left hand, rather than into his left hand.
⁵⁷ It is considered poetic to employ a noun where we would expect a pronoun.
⁵⁸ That our verse speaks of one and the same kohen

**לפני ה׳‎.** אל פתח אהל מועד:


BEFORE THE LORD. Towards the entrance of the tent of meeting.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:17:1)


**על דם האשם.** שהוא תנוך ובהונות:


UPON THE BLOOD OF THE GUILT-OFFERING. Which is upon the tip, the thumb, and the great toe.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:19:1)


**ועשה הכהן את החטאת.** היא הכבשה כתורת כל חטאת:


AND THE PRIEST SHALL OFFER THE SIN-OFFERING. The ewe-lamb as is the law with all sin offerings.⁵⁹

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Which required a female offering. See Lev. 4:32.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:20:1)


**והעלה. את העולה.** הוא הכבש ומנחתו שהוא עשרון ויש אומרים שכל המנחה יקריב לעולה ובתחלה האשם שהוא החמור:


AND THE PRIEST SHALL OFFER THE BURNT-OFFERING. The he-lamb and its meal offering, which consists of a tenth of an *ephah*.⁶⁰ Some say that the *kohen* shall offer the entire meal offering⁶¹ mentioned in verse 10. with the burnt offering.⁶² He shall offer the guilt offering first⁶³ because it is more severe.⁶⁴

footnotes:
⁶⁰ According to this interpretation the three tenth parts of an ephah mentioned in verse 10 were divided among the three sacrifices offered by the leper.
⁶¹ All three tenth parts of an ephah
⁶² For Scripture does not mention the term meal offering with regard to the sin offering and guilt offering.
⁶³ Before the sin and burnt offerings.
⁶⁴ It indicates that the one who offers it is guilty of a crime. See I.E. on Lev. 5:19.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:20:2)


**וטהר.** אז יהיה טהור ככל הטהורים:


AND HE SHALL BE CLEAN. Then⁶⁵ he shall be clean like all who are clean.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ Not before.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:21:1)


**דל.** יש בגוף כמו דלות ורעות אשרי משכיל אל דל ככה דל בן המלך ויש דל שאין לו ממון:


AND IF HE BE POOR. The term *dal* (poor) is found with regard to the body.⁶⁶ Compare, *poor* (dal) *and very ill-favored* (Gen. 41:19); *Happy is he that considereth the poor*⁶⁷ (dal) (Ps. 41:2); *son of the king, art thou thus becoming leaner* (dal) (II Sam. 13:4). The term *dal* is also found with regard to one who has no money.⁶⁸

footnotes:
⁶⁶ It is used to describe the state of a person or an animal.
⁶⁷ That is, the poor in body, the sick.
⁶⁸ In other words, it means poor.



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:33:1)


**וידבר ה׳‎ אל משה ואל אהרן.** החל לבאר צרעת הבית:


AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO MOSES AND UNTO AARON. Scripture begins explaining the laws dealing with leprosy in a house.⁶⁹.

footnotes:

### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:34:1)


וטעם **כי תבאו אל ארץ כנען.** כי זה נוהג בארץ לבדה בעבור גודל מעלת הארץ כי המקדש בתוכם והכבוד בתוך המקדש:


[WHEN YE ARE COME INTO THE LAND OF CANAAN.] Scripture reads thus⁷⁰ because this law applies only in the Land of Israel because of the great status of the land; for the temple was among them, and the Glory was in the midst of the temple.

footnotes:
⁷⁰ When ye are come into the land of Canaan…

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:34:2)

AND I PUT. The event⁷¹ is contingent on God’s putting.⁷²

⁷² The house plagues are a result of divine providence. They do not come by accident.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:35:1

**ובא אשר לו הבית.** מצוה שיבא אל הכהן:


THEN HE THAT OWNETH THE HOUSE SHALL COME. There is a divine command that he⁷³ come to the *kohen*.

⁷³ The owner of the house.



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:36:1)


**ופנו את הבית.** בעל הבית וכל אנשי ביתו. וטעם לשון רבים לפנותו מהרה:


THAT THEY EMPTY THE HOUSE. The master of the house and all men of his household.⁷⁴. Hence I.E.’s comment. Scripture employs the plural⁷⁵. for it is to be emptied quickly.⁷⁶

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Shall empty the house. Scripture reads, that they empty
⁷⁵ That they empty

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:36:2)


**בטרם יבא הכהן.** כי עוד יסגיר הבית בעבור הספק וטרם בואו ספקו:


BEFORE THE PRIEST GO IN. For the *kohen* will close up the house because of its uncertain status.⁷⁷ The house shall be emptied before its status is declared to be in doubt.

footnotes:

### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:37:1)


**שקערורות.** אין אחות למלה הזאת ולא נדע אם היא מרובעת כפולה האות האחרון או מחמשת אותיות ופירושיה לפי טעם המקום יש אומרים כדמות סימנין או חוטין ויש אומרים שהיא מלה מורכבת מטעם ותשקע האש. ורורות מגזרת רר בשרו וזה רחוק:


HOLLOW STREAKS. The word *sheka’arurot* (hollow streaks) has no sister.⁷⁸ is feminine. We do not know if it is a quadrilateral⁷⁹. with the last letter⁸⁰. doubled or if it is a pentaliteral.⁸¹. The meaning of *sheka’arurot* is to be ascertained from its context. Some say that it refers to marks or streaks. Others say that it is a compound word. It is akin to *va-tishka* (sank down)⁸² in *and the fire sank down* (Num. 11:2) and to *rorot*, which is related to the word *rar* (run)⁸³ in *run with his issue* (Lev. 15:3).⁸⁴ is made up of the words *shaka* (sank down) and *rorot* (run). According to this interpretation *sheka’arurot* means running cracks in the skin. However, this is far-fetched.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ The word is not found again in Scripture. I.E. uses the term sister because sheka’arurot
⁷⁹ From the root shin, kof, ayin, resh
⁸⁰ The resh
⁸¹ From the root shin, kof, ayin, resh, tav
⁸² Translated literally.
⁸³ That is, flow.
⁸⁴ That is, sheka’arurot

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:37:2)


**ומראיהן.** ומראה כל אחד מהם שפל:


AND THE APPEARANCE THEREOF.⁸⁵ (literally, and their appearance). Hence I.E.’s comment. The appearance of each one of them⁸⁶ be lower [than the wall].

footnotes:
⁸⁵ Hebrew, u-marehen
⁸⁶ The plagues. The verse opens with the singular (the plague). It goes on to speak of “their appearance.” According to I.E. such a combination of the singular and the plural indicates each one of the plural references.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:40:1

**וחלצו את האבנים.** יסירו וכן חלצני ה׳‎ מאדם רע:


THAT THEY TAKE OUT THE STONES. *Ve-chilletzu* means *that they take out*. Compare, *challetzeni* (deliver me) in *Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man* (Ps. 140:2).

**אל מקום טמא.** שלא יקחם משם אדם שיהיו ניכרים שהם טמאים:


INTO AN UNCLEAN PLACE. So that no one remove the stones from there.⁸⁷ So that it be recognized that they⁸⁸ are unclean.⁸⁹

### Verse 41

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:1)


**ואת הבית יקציע.** מגזרת מקצעות וכן מר ואהלות קציעות וטעמו פצולות ויש אומרים שיפצלו המקצוע ואין זה נכון כי כבר פשה נגע בבית רק יקציע כל הבית:


AND HE SHALL CAUSE THE HOUSE TO BE SCRAPED. The word *yaktzi’a* (he shall cause…to be scraped) is related to the word *maktzu’ot* (planes) (Is. 44:13) and the word *ketzi’ot* (cassia) in *Myrrh, and aloes, and cassia* (Ps. 45:9). The meaning of *ketz’ot* (cassia) is cuttings.⁹⁰ he shall cut. Some say the corner⁹¹ (corner). shall be peeled.⁹² is, they shall peel the corner. This interpretation connects the word *yaktzi’a* to the word *miktzo’a* (corner). See Ezek. 46:21. However, this is incorrect, for the plague had already spread in the house.⁹³ Actually, he must scrape the whole house.

footnotes:
⁹⁰ He renders yaktzi’a
⁹¹ Hebrew, miktzo’a
⁹² In other words, the meaning of yaktzi’a
⁹³ Why then peel only the corners?

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:2)


וטעם **מבית.** מבפנים:


The meaning of *mi-bayit*⁹⁴ literally means from the house. Hence I.E.’s comment. means within.

footnotes:
⁹⁴ Mi-bayit


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:41:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:41:3)


**הקצו.** מגזרת קצה כי קצתו ההו׳‎ למעלה הסירו לבדו:


The word *hiktzu* (they scrape off) is related to the word *katzeh* (extremity), for they only removed the upper extremity.⁹⁵

footnotes:
⁹⁵ The top layer.



### Verse 42

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:1)


**וטח.** הפך יקציע והוא מגזרת טחים אותו טפל:


AND SHALL PLASTER THE HOUSE. The word *tach* (plaster) is the reverse of *yaktzi’a* (he shall cause…to be scraped) (v. 41).⁹⁶ It is related to *tachim* (daub) in *they daub it with whited plaster* (Ezek. 13:10).

footnotes:
⁹⁶ In other words, he shall plaster the place that he peeled (Krinsky).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:42:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:2)


ומלת **ולקחו והביאו.** רבים במהרה:


AFTER THE HOUSE HATH BEEN SCRAPED. After the stones have first been taken out and the house has been scraped. Scripture employs the plural forms *ve-lakechu* (and they shall take) and *ve-hevi’u* (and put them) to indicate haste.⁹⁹

footnotes:
⁹⁹ See I.E. on verse 36 and the notes thereto.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:42:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:42:3)


**ועפר אחר יקח.** בעל הבית שירצה לדור בו:


AND HE SHALL TAKE OTHER MORTAR.⁹⁷ The owner of the house who wants to dwell in the house.

footnotes:
⁹⁷ In most editions of I.E. this comment has been placed after verse 43.



### Verse 43

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:43:1)


ואם הנגע ישוב ובא הכהן **אחר חלץ.** אחר שחלץ ואחרי הקצות את הבית בתחלה:


If the plague returns (v. 43), then the *kohen* shall come (v. 44).⁹⁸

footnotes:
⁹⁸ And declare the house unclean.



### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:45:1)


**ונתץ את הבית.** בצווי. וכן והוציא והקרוב שסגור יהיה שבעת ימים רק הכתוב אחז דרך קצרה והעד והבא אל הבית כל ימי הסגיר אותו בראשונה ובשנייה:


AND HE SHALL BREAK DOWN THE HOUSE. By command.¹⁰⁰ himself does not physically break down the house. Similarly *and he shall carry them forth*.¹⁰¹ It is most likely that the house was shut up for seven days.¹⁰² However, Scripture employed an abridged style.¹⁰³ He *that goeth into the house all the while that it is shut up* (v. 46) which applies to the first and second times is proof of this.¹⁰⁴

footnotes:
¹⁰⁰ The kohen
¹⁰¹ Its meaning is, by command.
¹⁰² As when the plague is first seen in the house. See verse 38.
¹⁰³ It omits the law that the house is to be closed up for seven days.
¹⁰⁴ That the plague is seen in the house.



### Verse 46

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:46:1)


**יטמא עד הערב.** אחר שירחץ בשרו כמשפט:


SHALL BE UNCLEAN UNTIL THE EVEN. After he bathes his body as the law requires.¹⁰⁵

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ See Lev. 15:5,6.



### Verse 47

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:47:1)


**והשוכב.** חמור על כן יכבס בגדיו ורחץ במים וטמא עד הערב ולא הזכירו הכתוב כי כל שוכב ואוכל הוא בא בבית:


AND HE THAT LIETH. This¹⁰⁶ is very severe uncleanliness. He that lies in the house must therefore wash his garments¹⁰⁷ and bathe and be unclean until evening. Scripture does not mention that he be unclean until evening, for whoever lies and eats in the house, first comes into the house.¹⁰⁸

footnotes:
¹⁰⁶ Eating and lying in the house.
¹⁰⁷ Wherein the one who comes into the house does not.
¹⁰⁸ And Scripture already stated in verse 46 that whoever comes into the house is unclean unto evening.

**ולקח.** בחבירו:


AND HE SHALL TAKE. This is to be interpreted as its counterpart.¹⁰⁹

¹⁰⁹ Verse 4.



### Verse 57

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 14:57:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:57:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/14:57:1)


**ביום הטמא.** ביום שיהיה האדם או הבגד או הבית טמא או טהור:


WHEN IT IS UNCLEAN. The day when the man or the garment or the house will be unclean or clean.



## Chapter 15


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:1:1)


**וידבר יי אל משה ואל אהרן.** כי הכהנים יפרישו בין זבה לנדה כאשר אחז"ל בין דם לדם ועתה יחל לבאר הטמאים מדבר נסתר כי הצרעת נגע גלוי:


AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO MOSES AND TO AARON. For the *kohanim* distinguish between a woman who has an issue (*zavah*) and one who menstruates (*niddah*).¹ Compare the Rabbinic interpretation on *between blood and blood*² then the *kohen* shall decide the aforementioned. The Rabbis interpret *between blood and blood* as meaning between “between menstrual blood and blood of an issue and blood of childbirth (*Sifre*, on Deut. 17:8).” (Deut. 17:8). Scripture now begins to deal with those who become unclean from a hidden source,³ for until now it dealt with leprosy which is a visible plague.

footnotes:
¹ Hence this commandment was also communicated to Aaron.
² Deuteronomy 17:8-9 states that if there be a dispute regarding blood and blood
³ Such as an issue.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:1)


**זב.** כמו זבת חלב כדמות טפות נוטפות:


AN ISSUE. The word *zav* (issue) is similar to the word *zavat* (flowing) in *flowing with milk* (Ex. 44:13). *Zav* refers to dripping drops.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ I.E. interprets Ex. 44:13 as “dripping with milk.”


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:2)


**מבשרו.** כנוי לערות הזכר:


OUT OF HIS FLESH. A euphemism for the male’s private part.⁵ is a euphemism for the penis.

footnotes:
⁵ Flesh


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:2:3)


**זובו.** שם:


HIS ISSUE. *Zovo* (his issue) is a noun.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ With the pronominal suffix.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:1)


**וזאת תהיה טומאתו.** הטעם כי הזוב הוא על ב׳‎ דרכים:


AND THIS SHALL BE HIS UNCLEANLINESS. This means the issue takes two forms.⁷ (v. 3).

footnotes:
⁷ His flesh will run with his issue or be stopped with his issue


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:2)


**רר בשרו.** מגזרת ויורד רירו על זקנו:


HIS FLESH RUN WITH HIS ISSUE. The word *rar* (run) is related to the word *riro* (his spittle) in, *and let his spittle fall down upon his beard* (I Sam. 21:14).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:3:3)


**או החתים.** כמו נסגר מגזרת כי חתום הוא והטעם שהתחבר הזוב ונקרש ולא יצא הקרי בשכבו עם האשה:


OR BE STOPPED. The word *hechtim* means stopped. It is related to the word *chatum* (sealed) in *that is sealed* (Is. 29:11). It means that the issue pools and congeals so that semen is not ejaculated when the afflicted sleeps with a women.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:4:1)


וגזרה אחת למשכב ולמושב וכן הנוגע באחד מהם משפט אחד לו:


The same law applies to what the one who has an issue lies or sits on.⁸ Similarly, there is one law regarding the one who touches what the afflicted lies on or sits on.⁹

footnotes:
⁸ They have to be immersed in water and are unclean till the evening.
⁹ He has to immerse himself and his clothes and remains unclean until evening.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:7:1)


**והנוגע בבשר הזב.** באיזה אבר שיהיה:


ANDS HE THAT TOUCHETH THE FLESH OF HIM THAT HATH THE ISSUE. Any part without exception.¹⁰ refers to the penis here it refers to any organ. Thus the meaning of *and he that toucheth the flesh of him that hath the issue* means, and he that touches any part of him that has the issue.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Unlike earlier (v. 2 and v. 3) where the word flesh



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:8:1)


**ירק.** ידוע מפעלי הכפל והעד דגשות קו״ף רוקי הנה גם רוקו יזיק כי הזוב מהחליים הנדבקים ואין מנהג האדם שירוק בפני חברו רק איננו בכוונה כי הזב זרק רוקו ונפל על הטהור:


SPIT. The meaning of *yarok* (spit) is known. It comes from a double root.¹¹. The fact that the *kof* of *rukki* (my spittle) (Job 7:19) has a *dagesh* is proof of this.¹² compensates for the missing root *kof*. Note, the spit of the one who suffers an issue is harmful,¹³ for the issue is one of the infectious diseases. It is not the practice of men to spit in the face of other people.¹⁴ (*ve-khi*) *he that hath an issue spit upon him that is clean* could conceivably be rendered, and when (*ve-khi*) he that hath an issue spit upon him that is clean. “And when” implies that this is the normal course of events. Hence I.E.’s comment. What happened occurred unintentionally. The one suffering from an issue expectorated his spittle and it fell on a clean person.¹⁵ is to be rendered as “and if” that is and if by accident.

footnotes:
¹¹ Its root is resh, kof, kof
¹² For the dagesh
¹³ Hence it is a source of uncleanliness.
¹⁴ And if
¹⁵ In other words, ve-khi



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:9:1)


**וכל המרכב.** שהוא מקבל טומאה כדברי המעתיקים:


AND WHAT SADDLE. Which, as the transmitters of tradition explained, becomes unclean.¹⁶ (saddle) refers to equipment set aside for riding. See *Torat Kohanim, Metzora* 3:9. Also see Rashi, “…The hold in front of the saddle…becomes unclean as a part of something for riding on (*mercav*)…and the saddle…is unclean as being something to sit upon (*moshav*)” (Rosenbaum and Silverman translation).

footnotes:
¹⁶ According to the Rabbis the term mercav



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:10:1)


**בכל אשר יהיה תחתיו.** תחת המרכב יטמא טומאה קלה וטומאה הנושא אותם יותר חמורה:


ANY THING THAT IS UNDER HIM. Under the saddle shall become lightly unclean.¹⁷ However, the uncleanliness of those who carry them¹⁸ is more strict.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁷ It does not require immersion of garments (Weiser).
¹⁸ The objects earlier mentioned, i.e., that which the one having an issue lies upon, sits upon, or rides upon.
¹⁹ It requires the immersion of garments.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:11:1)


**וידיו לא שטף במים.** היה נראה לנו כי כל מאכל והדומה לו שיגע בו הזב והנגיעה היא בידים וידיו שטופות איננו טמא בעבור שלא נגע במקום הזוב רק כאשר ראינו כל אבותינו פירשו וידיו כגופו קבלנו דבריהם ועל דרך הפשט כי כל טהור שיגע בו הזב וידיו שטופות הוא יטמא ולא בגדיו ואם לא היו שטופות יטמאו בגדיו וזה כמו הנוגע בכל אשר יהיה תחתיו:


WITHOUT HAVING RINSED HIS HANDS IN WATER. It would have appeared to us²⁰ that any food or anything similar, that the one having an issue touched by hand,²¹ means touched by hand. and the hands were washed, is not unclean, for the afflicted did not touch the place of the issue.²² However, since we saw that our fathers²³ explained that the hands are like the body²⁴ means without immersing his body. See *Niddah* 66b; *Kiddushin* 25a. we accept their words. According to the plain meaning of our verse if the one experiencing an issue touches someone with hands that have been washed the one touched is unclean but his garments are not unclean. However, if the one suffering the issue did not wash his hands then the garments are unclean.²⁵ there is no difference whether the one having an issue touched a person with hands that have or have not been washed. This law is similar²⁶ to the one regarding he who touches anything which is under the saddle.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁰ From a plain reading of the text.
²¹ In others words, toucheth
²² The hands most certainly did not have any issue on them, hence his hands do not defile. However, if the hands were not washed they may have come into contact with his issue, hence they defile.
²³ The Rabbis of the Talmud.
²⁴ According to the Rabbis the term hands in our verse does not only refer to the hands. It refers to the whole body. According tao the Rabbis without having rinsed his hands with water
²⁵ According to halakhah
²⁶ In that at times it entails a light uncleanliness and at times a strict uncleanliness.
²⁷ One who touches that which is under the saddle becomes lightly unclean. However, one carries these objects becomes more strictly unclean. See I.E. on verse 10.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:13:1)


**לטהרתו.** מיום טהרתו:


FOR HIS CLEANSING.²⁸, literally, to his cleansing. From the day of his cleansing.²⁹ of *le-taharrato* is not to be rendered to, but from. Hence *le-tahorrato* is to be interpreted, from his cleaning.

footnotes:
²⁸ Hebrew, le-taharrato
²⁹ The lamed

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:14:1)


והקרבן עולה וחטאת בעבור כי הזוב מוסר על עון:


The sacrifice consists of a burnt-offering³⁰ and a sin-offering,³¹ for the issue is a punishment for sin.

³⁰ To atone for evil thoughts. See I.E. on Lev. 1:4. The reference might be to thoughts of forbidden sexual practices. See next note.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:16:1)

GO OUT. Involuntarily.³²

³² For verse 18 speaks of voluntary emission of semen.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:16:2)


**שכבת זרע.** כמו שכבת הטל מגזרת מי ישכיב:


FLOW OF SEED. *Shikhvat* (flow) is similar to *shikhvat* (layer) in *the layer of dew* (Ex. 16:14).³³ It is related to the word *yashkiv* (pour out) in *who can pour out* (Job 38:37).

³³ Which I.E. renders a descent or a pouring down of dew. See I.E. on Ex. 16:14 (Vol. 2, p. 320).



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:17:1)


היה נראה לנו שצריך רחיצ׳‎ הבגד או העור אם היה בו זרע לח או יבש והאמת כדברי הקבלה:


It would appear to us³⁴ that the garment or the skin requires washing if it has wet semen or dry semen on it.³⁵ However, the truth lies with the words of tradition.³⁶ 7:1 states, “Semen [coveys] uncleanliness when wet but not when dry.”

footnotes:
³⁴ From the plain meaning of the text.
³⁵ If it come into contact with wet or dry semen, for the verse does not differentiate among them.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:19:1)


והשלים דבר הזכר ועתה יחל בנקבה:


Scripture³⁷ concludes the laws of the male.³⁸ It then begins with the female.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁷ In verse 18.
³⁸ Who has a normal or abnormal discharge from his penis.
³⁹ Who has a normal or abnormal discharge of blood.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:19:2)


**בבשרה.** ערות האשה וטעו המפרשים בבעל קרי את כל בשרו האבר לבד והעד כל:


IN HER FLESH. In the women’s private part. Those who explain *then he shall bathe all his flesh* (v. 16) which is written concerning one who ejaculates as referring to the penis⁴⁷ err. The word *all* is proof.⁴⁸ in our verse does not only apply to the penis.

footnotes:
⁴⁷ They take the word flesh to mean penis. See I.E. on verse 2.
⁴⁸ That they err, for “all” takes in the entire body. Thus flesh



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:23:1)


ופירוש **ואם על המשכב הוא** שב אל הכלי אשר תשב עליו והנה הכלי על המשכב או כלי על כלי על כן הטומאה קלה ולא הזכיר לאשה מרכב כי (בהמ׳‎) (ס״א בהווה) דבר הכתוב והמשפט אחד:


The meaning of *And if he be on the bed* (v. 23) refers back to the object⁴⁰ (hu) *be on the bed* as, And if it be on the bed, for the word *hu* may mean he or it. that she sits upon (v. 22). Now the object⁴¹ is on the bed or the object⁴² is on another object.⁴³ The uncleanliness is therefore light.⁴⁴ Scripture does not mention a saddle with regards to a women for Scripture speaks of what is prevalent.⁴⁵ (cattle). According to this version our text reads, for Scripture speaks of [an] animal. The latter is probably to be interpreted, Scripture speaks of one who usually rides on an animal, that is, males, and not females. The law is the same.⁴⁶

⁴¹ The object which a person touches.

⁴³ Upon which the women sits.
⁴⁴ The one who touches it does not have to immerse his garments.
⁴⁵ Most of riding was done by men. Some editions read behamah
