### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:24:1)


**ואם שכב איש אותה.** בלא זדון רק שוכב עמה בשוגג והנה באה נדתה והיתה עמו וזה טעם עליו כי כרת יש על השוכב עם נדה בזדון וכן הוא משפט הזבה ואם לא נזכר:


AND IF ANY MAN LIE WITH HER. Without any sinful intent.⁴⁹ On the contrary, he erred in sleeping with her.⁵⁰ Behold her menstrual flow came while he was with her. This is the meaning of *be upon him*, for the one who sleeps with a menstruating women incurs the penalty of *karet*.⁵¹ The same law applies to a women who has an issue,⁵² even though it is not stated.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ He has no intention in engaging in coitus with a menstruating women.
⁵⁰ He was not aware that her menstrual flow would commence while he slept with her.
⁵¹ See Lev. 20:18. Had the man had an evil intention when he had intercourse with the menstruating women then Scripture would have mentioned that he committed a major sin. Scripture appears to state, as a matter of fact, that a man who sleeps with a menstruating woman is unclean. One may take this to imply that one is permitted to sleep with a menstruating woman but is unclean if he does so. Hence I.E.’s comments.
⁵² The same law applies to one who engages in coitus with a women who has an issue.

**וכל המשכב אשר ישכב עליו יטמא.** כי הוא טמא טומאת שבעה וגם יטמא אחרים ואם לא נכתב:


AND EVERY BED WHEREON HE LIETH SHALL BE UNCLEAN. For he is unclean for seven days. He also defiles others. The latter is so even though it is not stated.

### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 15:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:31:1)


**והזרתם.** יש מפרשים בו והזרתם בחסרון הה״א וזה לא יתכן כי הה״א איננו מאותיות המשך כי לא יהיה נח נעלם כי אם בסוף המלה רק פירושו כמו ונזרו מקדשי בני ישראל כעין והרחקתם וממנו נזיר שהרחיק נפשו מתאות העולם ומשקלו והפלתם את הארץ כי הנו״ן מובלע בדגש:


THUS SHALL YE SEPARATE. Some say that *ve-hizzartem* (thus shall ye separate) is missing a *heh*⁵⁵ comes from the root *zayin, heh, resh*. and is to be interpreted *ve-hizzhartem* (and you shall warn). This cannot be so, for the *heh* is not vowel letter.⁵⁶ It is only silent at the end of a word.⁵⁷ On the contrary, *ve-hizzartem* is related to the word *ve-yinnazeru* (that they separate)⁵⁸. in *that they separate themselves from the holy things of the children of Israel* (Lev. 22:2). The meaning of that *ve-hizzartem* is, and you shall distance.⁵⁹. The word *nazir* (nazirite) is derived from the same root. A *nazir* is one who has distanced himself from the lusts of the world. *Ve-hizzartem* is vocalized like the word *ve-hippaltem* (and you shall divide)⁶⁰. in, *and you shall divide the land* (Ezek. 45:1), for the *nun*⁶¹ and *ve-hippaltem*. is swallowed in the *dagesh*.⁶² of *ve-hizzartem* and the *peh of ve-hippaltem*.

⁵⁵ According to this interpretation ve-hizzartem
⁵⁶ A vowel letter is occasionally dropped, for it is not sounded.
⁵⁷ It serves as a vowel letter at the end of the word. However, when it comes in the middle of a word it is sounded. Hence it is not dropped.
⁵⁸ It comes from the root nun, zayin, resh
⁵⁹ Or thus shall ye separate
⁶⁰ From the root nun, peh, lamed
⁶¹ The root nuns of ve-hizzartem
⁶² In the zayin

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/15:31:2)


**בטמאתם.** בעבור טומאתם ופירושו בטמאם את משכני:


IN THEIR UNCLEANLINESS. *Be-tumatam* (in their uncleanliness) is to be rendered because of their uncleanliness.⁶³ of *be-tumatam* has the meaning of “because.” Its meaning is, *when they defile My tabernacle*.⁶⁴ explains *because of their uncleanliness*. The point is that Scripture does not prohibit being “unclean.” It only does so when one enters the tabernacle in a state of impurity.

## Chapter 16


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:1:1)


**אחרי מות.** אחרי שהזהיר את ישראל שלא ימותו אמר השם למשה שיזהיר גם לאהרן שלא ימות כאשר מתו בניו וזאת הפרשה לאות כי בני אהרן הכניסו הקטורת לפנים מהפרכת:


AFTER THE DEATH. After God warned Israel not to incur the death penalty¹ He also told Moses to caution Aaron not to die as his sons died.² This section is proof that the sons of Aaron brought the incense within the veil.³

footnotes:
¹ In Lev. 15:31.
² See Lev. 10:1,2.
³ Into the Holy of Holies. The veil separated the Holy of Holies and the rest of the tabernacle. Our chapter relates that after Aaron’s sons died, God told Moses to caution Aaron against entering the Holy of Holies so as not to die as his sons did. The implication of the latter is that Aaron’s sons died because they entered the Holy of Holies.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:1:2)


ומלת **בקרבתם** שם הפעל וה״א לקרבה אל המלאכה שבה תי״ו כמנהג ה״א הנקבה וטעה רבי ישועה שאמר שהוא שם במשקל חכמתם:


[WHEN THEY DREW NEAR.] The word *be-korvatam* (when they drew near) is an infinitive.⁴ Compare, *le-korvah* (to come unto) in *to come unto the work* (Ex. 36:2).⁵ is made up of the infinitive *korvah* plus the pronominal suffix. The *heh* in *le-korvah* has been turned into a *tav* in *be-korvatam* as is the practice with the feminine *heh*.⁶ is connected to a pronominal suffix, the *heh* changes into a *tav*. Rabbi Joshua,⁷ who says that *be-korvatam* is a noun following the paradigm of *chokhmatam* (their wisdom) (Jer. 49:7),⁸ is made up of the noun *korvah* plus a pronominal suffix. errs.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:2:1

**אל הקדש.** כנגד אהל מועד:


INTO THE HOLY PLACE. In contrast to the tent of meeting.⁹ here refers to the place that is considered holy with regard to the tent of meeting, i.e., it is holier than the tent of meeting.

footnotes:
⁹ The tent of meeting is also a holy place. Hence I.E. points out that the holy


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:2:2)


**כי בענן.** הטעם שלא יכנס כי אם בקטרת שיעשה ענן ולא יראה הכבוד פן ימות. והנה טעמו לא אהיה נראה אליו כי אם בענן ויש אומרים כי פירוש כי בענן בעבור כי אני דר בענן על הכפורת כטעם ה׳‎ אמר לשכון בערפל:


FOR I APPEAR IN THE CLOUD.¹⁰, which I.E. renders: for only with a cloud. It means Aaron shall only enter with incense. He shall produce smoke so that God’s glory will not be seen, and as a result he dies.¹¹,: for only with a cloud may I be seen over the ark-cover. Note, the meaning of *ki be-anan era’eh* (for I appear in the cloud upon the ark-cover) is, I will not appear to him except through the agency of a cloud.¹² means, I may only be seen through the smoke of the incense. There are those who say that the meaning of *ki be-anan era’eh al ha-kapporet* (for I appear in the cloud) is, because I dwell in a cloud upon the ark-covering.¹³ (v. 2). *For I appear in the cloud* is similar in meaning to *The Lord hath said that He would dwell in the thick darkness* (I Kings 8:12).

footnotes:
¹⁰ Hebrew, ki be-anan
¹¹ This interpretation renders ki be-anan era’eh al ha-kapporet
¹² Aaron is not permitted to look directly at God’s glory. He must screen God’s glory with the smoke of the incense. According to this interpretation ki be-anan era’eh al ha-kapporet
¹³ Hence Aaron shall come not at all times into the holy place

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:3:1)


**בפר בן בקר.** אין פירושו שיביא הפר אל הקדש רק שיתן משלו בתחלה פר חטאת לכפר עליו ועל הכהנים ויש אומרים גם הלוים בעבור שמצאו ואת שם אהרן תכתוב על מטה לוי והוא רחוק רק הלוים יחשבו עם ישראל:


WITH A YOUNG BULLOCK. Its meaning is not that Aaron should bring the bullock into the holy place¹⁴ might be taken to mean that Aaron should bring the bullock into the Holy of Holies. Hence I.E.’s comment. but rather that he first bring a bullock of his own¹⁵ for a sin offering to atone for himself and the *kohanim*. Others say also for the Levites,¹⁶ for they found, *And thou shalt write Aaron’s name upon the rod of Levi* (Num. 17:18).¹⁷ form one unit. However, this is farfetched. On the contrary, the Levites are considered among the Israelites.

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:4:1)


**כתנת בד קדש.** ולא הזכיר האפוד והחשן ומעיל האפוד כי כבר הזכיר ונשמע קולו בבואו אל הקדש ומזה הכתוב למדו אנשי בית שני ששמשו בלא אורים ותומים גם נביאים היו שם:


THE HOLY LINEN TUNIC. Scripture does not mention the ephod,¹⁸ the breastplate,¹⁹ and the robe of the ephod,²⁰ for it earlier mentioned, *and the sound thereof*²¹. *shall be heard when he*²². *goeth in unto the holy place* (Ex. 28:35).²³ must wear the robe when he enters the Holy of Holies. Now, the *ephod* and the breastplate were attached to the robe. Hence there was no need for Scripture to repeat once again that Aaron shall wear these garments when he enters the Holy of Holies. Rashbam on Ex. 28:35 explains similarly. It should be noted that this interpretation is contrary to the accepted *halakhic* opinion which rules that the *kohen gadol* does not enter the Holy of Holies wearing the *ephod*, its band, the breastplate, and the robe. See *Rosh Ha-Shanah* 26a. Also see Rashi, “This teaches us that he must not officiate in the interior (the Holy of Holies) robed in the eight garments which were the insignia of the High Priest…in which he performed the service outside…but in the four garments…like an ordinary priest” (Rosenbaum and Silbermann). The men of the second temple who served without the *Urim* and the *Thummim*²⁴ learned from this verse [that their practice was proper].²⁵ and the *Thummim* in our verse, it appears that the wearing of the *Urim* and the *Thummim* is not essential for the service to be valid. There were also prophets there.²⁶ may officiate without the *Urim* and the *Thumim* if the latter are not available.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:8:1)


**גרלות.** ידועים מדברי קבלת אבותינו. אמר הגאון כי עזאזל שם הר ונקרא כן בעבור שהוא תקיף כי טעם זכר השם כטעם הררי אל יקותיאל והגאון הלוי תפשו כי האל״ף במלת עזאזל הוא בין זי״ן לזי״ן ויש אומרים שהוא הר סמוך אל הר סיני והשם צוה להוליך השעיר ולהעלותו שם עד שישחט ואחר כן היו מוליכים אותו בבית המקדש להר אחר והעד שאמר ויעש אהרן וכן בחג שבועות לפי דעתי כאשר אפרש במקומו ויש אומרים כי עבודת בית שני לא היתה כראשון כי היה הכהן מחוסר בגדים ואין כפרת וזה המפרש אמר כי השעיר ישלח במדבר כי כן כתוב ושלח את השעיר במדבר וטעמו כטעם צפור המטהר מהצרעת שהוא על פני השדה מקום שאין שם ישוב והעד אל ארץ גזרה ואנחנו השיבונו כי השעיר ישלחו במדבר כאשר הוא כתוב וירדוף אחריו עד שיברח ויעל אל הצור על כן אמרו חז״ל ודוחפו ואמר רב שמואל אף על פי שכתוב בשעיר החטאת שהוא לשם גם השעיר המשתלח הוא לשם ואין צריך כי המשלח איננו קרבן כי לא ישחט ואם יכולת להבין הסוד שהוא אחר מלת עזאזל תדע סודו וסוד שמו כי יש לו חברים במקרא ואני אגלה לך קצת הסוד ברמז בהיותך בן שלשים ושלש תדענו:


LOTS. We know what they are from the words of our sages.³¹ 36a. The Gaon³² says that Azazel is the name of a mountain. It is so called because of its might.³³ 63a. Azazel is a combination of *az* (strong) and *el* (tall). Azazel thus means mighty and tall mountain (Krinsky). God’s name³⁴ is mentioned in Azazel as it is in *ke-harere el* (the mighty mountains) (Ps. 36:7) and in *yakti’el* (Joktheel) (II Kings 14:7). The Gaon, the Levite,³⁵ caught him in error. He pointed out that the *alef* of Azazel is placed between one *zayin* and the other *zayin*.³⁶ in it, for the word is spelled *ayin, zayin, alef, zayin lamed*. There are those who say that Azazel is the name of a mountain close to Mount Sinai. God commanded that the goat be led and brought up there until it slipped down the mountain. Later on in the time of the temple, they led it to a different mountain. “And Aaron did” (v. 34) is proof of this.³⁷. I.E.’s point is that Scripture goes out of the way to state that Aaron did as God commanded him in the wilderness, for later on when Israel entered Israel some parts of the Yom Kippur ritual were performed a bit differently. The sending of the goat to Mount Azazel is an example. The same, in my opinion, as I will explain in its place,³⁸ is true of the festival of Shavu’ot.³⁹ There are those who say that the service⁴⁰ during the second temple was unlike that of the first, for the *kohen*⁴¹ lacked the garments⁴²., the ephod, its band, the breastplate, and the robe. and there was no ark-cover. This commentator says that the goat was sent into the wilderness, for Scripture states, *and he shall let go the goat in the wilderness* (v. 22).⁴³ The latter is similar in meaning to the bird of the one who is cleansed from leprosy⁴⁴ which is sent into the open field, an uninhabited place. *A land which is cut off*⁴⁵ (v. 22) is proof of this.⁴⁶ We answered him that they sent the goat into the wilderness as it is written.⁴⁷ However, the goat was pursued⁴⁸ until it fled and climbed up a rock. The rabbis therefore said, “and he⁴⁹ pushes it down.”⁵⁰ 67a. The point is that since a person accompanied the goat it was possible for him to push the goat off the mountain. Rabbi Samuel⁵¹ says that even though it is written that the goat offered as a sin offering is to be offered to God, the goat that was sent was also offered to God. There is no need for this interpretation, for the goat sent into the wilderness did not serve as an offering since it was not slaughtered. If you are able to understand the secret that follows Azazel⁵² (for Azazel into the wilderness). According to I.E. the word *ha-midbarah* is the key to understanding the meaning of Azazel. then you will know its secret and the secret of its name, for it has comrades in Scripture.⁵³ (goat) and *azal* (to go). There are many such compound names in Scripture. Compare, Emmanuel (God is with us). According to I. E. the meaning of *la-azazel ha-midbarah* is, for a goat that goes into the wilderness. I will reveal a bit of its secret to you in a hint. When you are thirty-three, you will understand it.⁵⁴ (satyrs). According to I.E. the *se’irim* were demons that had the shape of goats. Now it is not clear what the purpose was of sending the goat to demons who were shaped like goats. According to Nahmanides the goat was a gift to Satan so that he would not interfere with Israel’s sacrifice. However, it is doubtful that I.E. had this in mind. Weiser suggests that the goat was sent to a place of demons so that the sins which it (literally or symbolically) bore would not contaminate people, for demons dwell in a place which is uninhabited.

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:10:1)


**יעמד חי.** מהבנין הכבד הנוסף שלא נזכר שם פועלו והיה היו״ד ראוי להיותו מאוסף בשור״ק רק נרחב בעבור אות הגרון וכן יחרם כל רכושו:


SHALL BE SET ALIVE. The word *yo’omad* (shall be set) is a *hofal*.⁵⁶ The *yod*⁵⁷ should have been vocalized with a *shuruk*.⁵⁸, for that is the way words in the imperfect are conjugated in the *hofal*. Compare, *yuktzar, yurad* However, it was enlarged⁵⁹) to a major vowel (a *kamatz*). because of the guttural.⁶⁰, for the root of *yo’omad* is *ayin, mem, dalet*. The word *yochoram* (should be forfeited) in *all his substance should be forfeited* (Ez. 10:8) is similar.⁶¹ rather than with a *kubbutz* because of its guttural.

⁶¹ It too is vocalized with a kamatz

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:14:1)


היה נראה לנו כי על פני הכפרת ולפני הכפרת כמשמעו והמעתיקים אמרו כי על פני הכפרת הוא על בין הבדים ולפני הכפורת ידוע מדבריהם והוא אמת:


It would have appeared to us that *upon the ark-cover* (v. 14); *and before the arkcover* (*Ibid*.) are to be taken literally. However, the transmitters of tradition said that *upon the ark-cover* means between the staves.⁶⁸ stands between the staves of the ark and sprinkles from there. Furthermore, according to the rabbis the *kohen* does not actually sprinkle blood on the ark-cover but upward towards the ark-cover. See *Yoma* 53b. The meaning of *and before the ark-cover* is known from their words.⁶⁹ means downward in front of the ark-cover. The blood fell on the ground. See *Yoma* 53b. What they said is true.

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:21:1)


**ונתן אותם.** אחר שיסורו מישראל כאילו נתונים הם אל ראש השעיר והולכים אל מקום שלא יזכרו עוד והכתוב דבר להבין הכל:


AND HE SHALL PUT THEM. After the sins are removed from Israel they are, as it were, placed on the head of the goat. The sins will go to a place were they will never again be remembered. Scripture speaks in a way that all will understand.⁷⁸

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:21:2)


**עתי.** מזומן לעתו או מנהגו ללכת בכל עת בכפורים והיו״ד להתיחס לעת כמו פנימי והמפרשים אותו חכם איננו נכון בעיני וחז״ל אמרו שהנושא הוא כהן ודבריהם אמת:


APPOINTED. *Itti* (appointed) means prepared for his time.⁷⁹ Or *itti* refers to an individual whose custom it was to always lead the goat on the Day of Atonement. The *yod*⁸⁰. relates to the word *et* (time).⁸¹ *Itti* is similar to *penimi* (internal).⁸² is the *yod* of relation. The *yod* suffixed to a word is often a first person pronominal suffix, hence I.E.’s comment. The interpretation of those who explain *itti* to mean wise⁸³ (ittim) (Est. 1:13). According to this interpretation a “timely” man is a man who is aware of what is currently happening, i.e., a wise man. does not appear correct to me.⁸⁴ Our sages, of blessed memory, said that the “bearer”⁸⁵ was a *kohen*.⁸⁶ 66a. Their words are true.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:29:2)


**בחדש השביעי בעשור לחדש תענו את נפשותיכם.** אחר שהכתוב אומר ותתענג בדשן נפשכם ידענו כי הענוי הפך התענוג והוא הצום ועוד ונפש נענה תשביע שטעמו כמו ותפק לרעב נפשך כי דרך דברי הנביאים להכפל ואחר שיש לנו קבלה אין צורך לחפש ואין עניתי בצום נפשי ראיה בעבור זכר צום והכלל כל עינוי שימצא במקרא דבק עם נפש הוא הצום:


IN THE SEVENTH MONTH, ON THE TENTH DAY OF THE MONTH, YE SHALL AFFLICT YOUR SOULS. Since Scripture writes, *And let your soul delight itself in fatness* (Is. 55:2), we know that affliction is the reverse of delight and that affliction of the soul refers to fasting.¹⁰⁵ Furthermore, Scripture states, *And satisfy the afflicted soul* (Is. 58:10), the meaning of which is like *And if thou draw out thy soul to the hungry* (*Ibid*.), for it is the style of the prophets to repeat themselves.¹⁰⁶ is another way of saying *the afflicted soul*. We thus see that affliction of the soul refers to a lack of food. Hence it refers to fasting. Now since we have tradition¹⁰⁷ there is no reason to go on searching.¹⁰⁸ *I afflicted my soul with fasting* (Ps. 35:13) is no proof¹⁰⁹ because the word fast is mentioned.¹¹⁰ The general rule is, whenever we find in Scripture the word affliction connected to soul, the reference is to a fast.¹¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ When a person eats, the soul is said to “delight itself.” Thus when Scripture speaks of the soul being afflicted it means the reverse; i.e., the reference is to a fast.
¹⁰⁶ The hungry
¹⁰⁷ That affliction of the soul refers to fasting.
¹⁰⁸ For proof that our verse speaks of fasting.
¹⁰⁹ That afflicting the soul means fasting, for one may argue that since Scripture goes on to explain that the psalmist afflicted his soul by fasting, in cases where the “affliction of the soul” is not qualified, the reference is not to fasting.
¹¹⁰ In the verse.
¹¹¹ Whenever the Torah speaks of the affliction of the soul the reference is to fasting.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:29:3)


**האזרח והגר הגר בתוככם.** לא נעזבנו לעשות מלאכה ולא נכריתנו על הצום:


THE HOME-BORN, OR THE STRANGER THAT SOJOURNETH AMONG YOU. We are not permitted to let him¹¹² do any work,¹¹³ but we do not force him¹¹⁴ as in *Vat. Ebr*. 38 instead of *nachritennu* (we shall cut him off). to fast.¹¹⁵

footnotes:
¹¹² The stranger.
¹¹³ On Yom Kippur.
¹¹⁴ Reading nachrichennu
¹¹⁵ For Scripture does not mention the stranger when it speaks of fasting. It only mentions the stranger when it speaks of not doing work on Yom Kippur.



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:30:1)


**יכפר עליכם.** הכהן על כן אמרו המפרשים על השעיר המשתלח כי יעמד חי לכפר עליו כי העמדתו היא כפרה:


SHALL ATONEMENT BE MADE FOR YOU. By the *kohen*. The commentators therefore say regarding the goat which is sent into the wilderness, concerning which it is written, *shall be set alive…to make atonement over him* (v. 10), that the atonement becomes effective by the placing of the goat before the Lord.¹¹⁶.

footnotes:
¹¹⁶ In other words, atonement is not affected by driving the goat into the wilderness, for the latter is not done by the kohen gadol


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:30:2)


**מכל חטאתיכם.** כאשר פירשתי ששם חטאת שם כלל על כן תמצא על השגגה ועל הזדון:


FROM ALL YOUR SINS. This is to be understood in accordance with my earlier explanation that the noun *chattat* (sin) is a general term. It is therefore found with reference to both accidental and deliberate sin.



### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:31:1)


**שבת שבתון.** יש אומרים שביתה לנפש ולגוף ואחרים אמרו שהוא שביתת השביתה שאין למעלה ממנה ושניהם שמות פעם יבא זה קודם זה והם שוים ואינו רחוק:


IT IS A SABBATH OF SOLEMN REST. Some say that *shabbat shabbaton* (a sabbath of solemn rest) means rest for the soul and rest for the body.¹¹⁷ is repeated in the phrase. Others say that *shabbat shabbaton* means a rest above which there is no other rest.¹¹⁸ Both words¹¹⁹ are nouns. At times the other word comes first.¹²⁰. However, Ex. 16:23 reads *shabbaton shabbat*. They have one meaning,¹²¹ and *shabbaton* have one meaning. This interpretation is not farfetched.

footnotes:
¹¹⁷ For the word shabbat
¹¹⁸ It means a total rest.
¹¹⁹ In the phrase.
¹²⁰ Our verse reads: shabbat shabbaton
¹²¹ Shabbat


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:31:2)


**חקת עולם.** להתענות:


IT IS A STATUTE FOR EVER. To fast.



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:32:1)


**וכפר הכהן.** שב עתה לפרש על כל מה שיכפר:


AND THE PRIEST…SHALL MAKE ATONEMENT. Scripture now goes on to explain what the *kohen* shall make atonement for.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 16:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/16:32:2)


**אשר ימשח אותו.** המושח בשמן המשחה אחר שמלא את ידו:


WHO SHALL BE ANOINTED. By the anointer¹²²) and who shall be consecrated…shall make atonement. Now the priest who anoints the *kohen gadol* does not make atonement. Neither is the priest who anoints consecrated to wear the high priestly vestments. Furthermore, the text omits with what the anointed shall do the anointing. Hence I.E. points out that *yimshach oto* is short for *yimshach ha-moshech oto*, which means whom the anointer shall anoint. Thus according to I.E. the first part of our verse should be rendered: And the priest whom the anointer shall anoint with the anointing oil. with the anointing oil¹²³ was anointed. Hence I.E. points out that *asher yimshach oto* is short for *yimshach oto be-shemen ha-mishchah*. after he has been consecrated.¹²⁴.

footnotes:
¹²² Our verse literally reads: And the priest who shall anoint him (yimshach oto
¹²³ Scripture does not say with what the kohen gadol
¹²⁴ I.E.’s interpretation of and who shall be consecrated

AND HE SHALL PUT ON. He¹²⁵. is the one who shall put on these holy garments, for another person may not wear them.

AND SHALL MAKE ATONEMENT FOR THE PRIESTS AND FOR ALL THE PEOPLE OF THE ASSEMBLY. The Levites are included among the latter, for the Levites are not called *kohanim*. On the contrary, every *kohen* is a Levite¹²⁶ but every Levite is not a *kohen*.¹²⁷.

## Chapter 17


### Verse 2

**דבר אל אהרן ואל בניו.** כי הם השוחטים לישראל:


SPEAK UNTO AARON, AND UNTO HIS SONS. For they are the ones who slaughter¹ for Israel.

footnotes:
¹ The sacrifices.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:3:1)


אילו היה כתוב במחנה מותר לשחטו מחוץ למחנה וזו המצוה תלויה גם בבית המקדש רק בכל מקום שהוא קרוב לירושלים והקרוב ידענו מדברי קבלה כי אם היה המקום רחוק יזבח ויאכל בשר כי כן כתוב ורבים אמרו כי הבשר אסור בגלות ויפרשו בשר ויין לא בא אל פי בשר דג ולא דברו נכונה ואיני רוצה להאריך להשיב עליהם:


If the Torah had read, *in the camp* (v. 3),². then it would have been permissible to slaughter it³ outside the camp.⁴. Also, this⁵ commandment⁶ is dependent upon the Holy Temple.⁷ However, this law applies only to any place that is close to Jerusalem.⁸ and Rashi. We know how close from the words of tradition,⁹ 57b. for if the place is far, one is permitted to slaughter flesh.¹⁰ The latter is so, for Scripture states it.¹¹ Many say that it is forbidden to eat flesh in the exile.¹² They explain *neither came flesh nor wine in my mouth* (Dan. 10:3)¹³ as referring to the flesh of fish.¹⁴ as referring to the flesh of fish, for they maintain that one cannot eat meat outside of the Land of Israel. They did not speak correctly. I do not want to dwell on this and answer them.

footnotes:
² That is, if verse 3 had only stated, in the camp
³ Any one of the animals mentioned in our verse.
⁴ Hence verse 3 reads, in the camp, or…without the camp
⁵ The law codified in verses 3 and 4.
⁶ Like many others which precede it.
⁷ It is in effect only when a central sanctuary for the bringing of sacrifices exists.
⁸ Even when the temple stood, the law applied only to places which were close to Jerusalem. I.E. is interpreting according to the plain meaning of the text. According to the rabbis, our verse speaks of sacrifices; i.e., sacrifices are not to be offered in any place except for the tabernacle or the temple. See Sifra
⁹ See Kiddishin
¹⁰ He is permitted to slaughter an animal for the purpose of eating its flesh without presenting it for an offering.
¹¹ In Deut. 12:21.
¹² This was a Karaite opinion.
¹³ Daniel, who lived in Babylon, tells us that he refrained from meat and wine while he fasted. This indicates that he ate meat when he did not fast.
¹⁴ The Karaites explain the word “flesh” in neither came flesh…in my mouth



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:5:1)


**למען אשר יביאו.** זה הטעם על המצוה הזאת ופירוש על פני השדה ולא יזבחו עוד את זבחיהם:


TO THE END THAT THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL MAY BRING. This is the reason for this commandment. *And they shall no more sacrifice their sacrifices unto the satyrs* (v. 7) explains *in the open field*.¹⁵ whom they believe to dwell there.

footnotes:
¹⁵ The reason Israel shall not sacrifice in the open field is so that they shall no more sacrifice their sacrifices unto the satyrs



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:7:1)


**לשעירים.** הם השדים ונקראו כן בעבור שישתער הגוף הרואה אותם והקרוב בעבור שיראו אותם המשוגעים כדמות שעירים ומלת עוד תורה שכן היו ישראל עושין במצרים:


UNTO THE SATYRS. *Se’irim* (satyrs) are the demons. They are so called because the body of one who sees them “storms.”¹⁶, spelled with a *sin*, to the word *sa’arah* (storm), spelled with a *samekh*. However, it appears that they are so called because the insane see them in the form of goats (*se’irim*). The word “more” teaches that the Israelites did so in Egypt.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The person who sees them is agitated. This opinion connects se’irim


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:7:2)


**אשר הם זונים.** כי כל מי שמבקש אותם ומאמין בהם הוא זונה מתחת אלהיו שיחשוב כי יש מי שייטיב או ירע חוץ מהשם הנכבד והנורא. ובזו הפרשה לא הזכיר הגר כי המצוה על ישראל בזבחים ובעולות והזכיר הגר שלא יעזבו ישראל שיזבח הגר לעבודת כוכבים בארץ ישראל וכן משפט כל דם שהוא אסור בעבור שהוא הנפש כי הבשר בדם והאמת כי הנפש שבה יחיה האדם הוא בדם הלב:


AFTER WHOM THEY GO ASTRAY. For whoever seeks after them and believes in them strays from under his God, for he thinks that there is someone aside from God the glorious and awe inspiring who does good or bad. This section¹⁷ does not mention the stranger, for the commandment regarding sacrifices and burnt offerings falls on Israel. Scripture mentions the stranger¹⁸ in order to teach that Israel shall not permit a stranger to sacrifice to idols in the Land of Israel. The law regarding *all manner of blood* (v. 10) is similar.¹⁹ The eating of blood is prohibited²⁰ because it is the very life, for the flesh lives by the blood.²¹ The truth of the matter is that the life by which a person lives is in the blood which is in the heart.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Which prohibits the slaughter of meat except as an offering.
¹⁸ In verse 8, which prohibits offering a sacrifice outside of the sanctuary.
¹⁹ It is the same case with the law regarding the prohibition of eating all manner of blood.
²⁰ To the Israelite and to the stranger.
²¹ The blood is offered on the altar to atone for the life of the sinner, life for life. It is therefore not to be eaten.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:11:1)


**בנפש יכפר.** בנפש שיש בו יכפר. והטעם נפש תחת נפש ויש מפרשים כמו על נפשותיכם ואין לו טעם אחר שאמר לכפר על נפשותיכם:


THAT MAKETH ATONEMENT BY REASON OF THE LIFE.²². The blood makes atonement by the life which is in it.²³ (makes atonement by reason of the life) as makes atonement with the life which is in it. The reason for the latter is life for life.²⁴ There are others who explain *ba-nefesh yekhapper* (that maketh atonement by reason of the life) as, to make atonement for your souls.²⁵ (by reason of the life) as for the soul, the reference being for the soul of the sinner. However, the latter makes no sense, for what reason is there for Scripture to say this after stating *to make atonement for your souls*?²⁶

footnotes:
²² Hebrew, ba-nefesh yekhapper
²³ This interpretation renders ba-nefesh yekhapper
²⁴ The life of the animal is given in place of the life of the sinner.
²⁵ They render ba-nefesh
²⁶ What reason is there for Scripture to repeat itself?



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:13:1)


**אשר יצוד ציד חיה.** כאיל וצבי:


THAT TAKETH IN HUNTING ANY BEAST OR FOWL THAT MAY BE EATEN. Such as a deer or a gazelle.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:13:2)


**או עוף אשר יאכל.** גם מזה הכתוב נלמוד שלא נעזוב הגר שיאכל בארצנו חזיר או סוס או עוף דורס שהוא טמא ויתכן בעבור שצוה שלא יאכל דם ולא יראה דם נשפך חוץ מהמזבח השם צוה לכסות כל דם שאינו קרב לגבי המזבח שלא יחשוב הרואה בראותו והוא דם איל וצבי או עוף כי זבח נזבח לעבודת כוכבים ובפרשת ראה אנכי אפרש סוד זה הפסוק:


OR FOWL THAT MAY BE EATEN. We also learn from this verse that we should not allow the stranger to eat in our land the flesh of a pig or a horse, or any bird of prey which is unclean. It is possible that God commanded that all blood which is not offered on the altar²⁷ (chayyah) *or fowl that may be eaten*. be covered so that the one who sees the blood of the deer, stag, or fowl not think that it comes from a sacrifice that was offered to an idol, for God had commanded that no blood be eaten and that no blood from God’s altar be spilled outside of the altar.²⁸ I will explain the secret of this verse in the Torah portion opening with the words *Behold, I set* (Deut. 11:26).²⁹

footnotes:
²⁷ That is, the blood of any beast
²⁸ Hence a person who sees blood pooled on the ground will assume that it is from blood offered to idols.
²⁹ See I.E. on Deut. 12:25. I.E. there explains that eating the blood of an animal will harm one’s soul as well as have a bad effect on one’s children.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:14:1)


**דמו בנפשו.** הוא דבק עם הנפש כי ידוע שהגידים היוצאים מפאת שמאל הלב מחולקים בחצי לדם ולרוח כדמות שמן זית עם האור:


THE BLOOD THEREOF IS ALL ONE WITH THE LIFE THEREOF. The blood is coupled with the life thereof,³⁰ as the blood thereof is with the life thereof. for it is known³¹ that the threads³² that go out from the left side of the heart are divided, half for blood and half for the breath, like the olive oil and the light thereof.³³

³⁰ I.E. renders damo be-nafsho
³¹ That was the belief of physicians in I.E.’s time.

THEREFORE I SAID UNTO THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. The meaning of *va-omar* (therefore I said) is, and I have previously said.³⁴ The reference is to the section now mentioned.³⁵

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:15:1)


ובעבור שהזכיר חיה שאיננה קרבה וכן עוף הזכיר **כל נפש אשר תאכל נבלה** שאיננה קרבה גם היא גם הזכירה בעבור הציד אם בזדון הוא עובר בלאו וילקה ואם בשגגה יביא חטאת. ואם טען טוען והלא הכתוב אמר לגר אשר בשעריך תתננה ואכלה התשובה כי זה גר תושב וכן כתוב והגר הגר בתוככם לא נעזבנו שיאכל נבלה רק הנכרי שיבוא בשערינו נתן אותה לו ויאכל אותה לחוץ:


Since Scripture mentions the beast (*chayyah*) which is not offered as a sacrifice, and similarly the fowl,³⁶ it goes on to speak of *every soul that eateth that which dieth of itself* (v. 15), which is also not offered as a sacrifice. Scripture also mentions the latter because of its reference to an animal which is taken in hunting.³⁷ (dying of itself) or *terefah* (tom), for they are taken by bow and arrow or traps. If one does so deliberately³⁸. he violates a negative precept³⁹ and is flogged.⁴⁰ also is unclean. Hence I.E. points out that he is not only unclean but he violates the law which prohibits the eating of an animal that dies of itself or is torn. If he does so accidentally, he brings a sin offering. If one should argue,⁴¹ does not Scripture state, *thou mayest give it*⁴² *unto the stranger that is within thy gates, that he may eat it* (Deut. 14:21)? The answer is that this⁴³ speaks of a stranger who dwells in the Land of Israel. Scripture similarly reads, *or the stranger that sojourneth among you* (Lev. 16:29). We shall not allow him to eat anything that dies of itself. However, we may give it⁴⁴ to the stranger that comes into our gates⁴⁵ and he will eat it outside.⁴⁶

footnotes:
³⁶ In verse 13.
³⁷ In verse 13. Animals taken in hunting are very likely to fall into the category of nevelah
³⁸ If he deliberately eats of an animal which dieth of itself, or that which is torn of beasts
³⁹ See Ex. 22:30; Deut. 14:21.
⁴⁰ Verse 15 tells us that one who eateth that which dieth of itself
⁴¹ With I.E.’s earlier statement that verse 13 teaches that Israel is not permitted to allow the stranger to eat the flesh of a pig or a horse in the Land of Israel.
⁴² The flesh of an animal that dies of itself and, by extension, the flesh of all prohibited animals.
⁴³ Verse 13.
⁴⁴ Forbidden meat.
⁴⁵ The stranger who does not live in the Land of Israel but visits it. This is what Deut. 14:21 deals with.
⁴⁶ Outside the city gates.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 17:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/17:16:1)


**ונשא עונו.** תמיד והוא יסלח עונו בעונש שיביא השם עליו ובעבור שהזכיר דברי השעירים שהיו עושים במצרים נסמכה זו הפרשה והעריות על מעשה ארץ כנען כי כן מפורש בסוף:


THEN HE SHALL BEAR HIS INIQUITY. Permanently. He⁴⁷ will be forgiven for his sin by the punishment which God shall bring upon him. This section⁴⁸ (v. 13). follows because Scripture mentioned⁴⁹ the topic of the satyrs which Israel sacrificed to in Egypt. The prohibited sexual practices⁵⁰ deal with those of the land of Canaan,⁵¹ refers to offering to the satyrs. for Scripture so states at the end.⁵²

וטעם **אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם.** על זה התנאי אהיה אלהיכם:


[I AM THE LORD YOUR GOD.] The meaning of this verse is, on this condition¹ I will be your God.

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:3:1

**ובחקתיהם לא תלכו.** שלא ירגיל אדם ללכת בדרך הזה עד שיהיה לו חק:


NEITHER SHALL YE WALK IN THEIR STATUTES.². A person should not habituate himself to walk in this way³ to the point where it becomes a statute for him.⁴

footnotes:
² Hebrew, chukkotehem
³ The way of the Canaanites. I.E. interprets statutes as referring to the practices of the Canaanites because verses 27 and 28 make it clear that the practices enumerated in our chapter refer to Canaanite mores.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:3:2)


ו**מעשה ארץ מצרים.** על המשפטים והראיה את משפטי תעשו הם הכתובים על ספר הברית המפורש בפרשת אלה המשפטים:


However, *after the doings of the land of Egypt* refers to their ordinances.⁵ *Mine ordinances* (mishpatai)⁶ *shall ye do* (v. 4) is proof of this. The latter refers to the ordinances recorded in the Book of the Covenant,⁷ which is set forth in the Torah portion that opens with *Now these are the ordinances* (mishpatim) (Ex. 21:1).⁸

footnotes:
⁵ Their laws.
⁶ Not those of the Egyptians.

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:4:1

**אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם.** אז אהיה אלהיכם:


I AM THE LORD YOUR GOD. I will then be your God.⁹

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:5:1)


וטעם **ושמרתם את חקתי ואת משפטי.** לבאר שהם חיים לעושיהם בשני עולמות כי המבין סודם חי העולם יחיינו ולא ימות לעולם על כן כתוב אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם כאשר פירשתיו:


The reason Scripture states, *Ye shall therefore keep My statutes, and Mine ordinances* (v. 5) is to make it clear that they are a source of life in both worlds¹⁰ to those who observe them. God will give eternal life to the person who understands their secret. He will never die.¹¹ Scripture, as I have explained, therefore reads, *I am the Lord your God*.

footnotes:
¹⁰ This world and the world to come.
¹¹ His soul shall live on after the death of the body.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:1)


**איש איש.** כל איש ואיש ובעבור שלא הזכיר מבית ישראל לא נעזוב הגר שיעשה אחת מהתועבות הנזכרות בארצנו שלא יטמא את הארץ:


NONE OF YOU. *Ish ish*¹² (none of you) means each and every man. Since Scripture does not mention the house of Israel,¹³ we learn that we are not permitted to allow the stranger to practice in our country any of the abominations mentioned, so that he does not defile the land.

footnotes:
¹² Literally, man, man.
¹³ In our verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:2)


**אל כל שאר בשרו.** שם כלל לכל העריות ואחר כן פרט. ורב אהרן הכהן אמר שפירושו להוציא שכבת זרע לבטלה:


ANY THAT IS NEAR OF KIN TO HIM. *She’er*¹⁴ (near) is a general term for all those with whom one is prohibited to engage in sexual relations. Scripture then goes into detail.¹⁵ Rabbi Aaron Ha-Kohen¹⁶ says that the reference is to the emission of semen in vain.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁴ Hebrew, flesh, that is, one’s flesh, i.e., one’s near relative.
¹⁵ And lists the people with whom one may not have sexual intercourse.
¹⁶ A Gaon in Pumpaditha, author of a commentary on the Torah. Rabbi Hai Gaon was his student.
¹⁷ According to Rabbi Aaron Ha-Kohen our verse does not speak of sexual intercourse but deals with approaching a near relative and uncovering her nakedness for the purpose of obtaining sexual pleasure (ejaculation).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:3)


**לא תקרבו.** כנוי לשכיבה וכן ואקרב אל הנביאה וקרוב ממלת אל תגשו אל אשה:


NONE OF YOU SHALL APPROACH. Approach is a euphemism for lying with.¹⁸ Similarly *And I approached the prophetess* (Is. 8:3).¹⁹ Our verse is close to *come not near a woman* (Ex. 19:15).²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁸ Engaging in sexual intercourse.
¹⁹ The meaning of which is, I had intercourse with the prophetess.
²⁰ The meaning of which is, do not have intercourse with a woman.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:4)


**ערוה.** דבר מגונה נחשף צריך להתכסות:


NAKEDNESS. *Ervah* (nakedness) refers to something unseemly which is exposed and has to be covered.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:6:5)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎.** שהשם אוהב הנבדל לעבדו ולשמוע דברו והר סיני לעד גם הראשון והוא סוד האדם ובעבור שיצר לב האדם כבהמות לא יתכן לאסור כל הנקבות והנה אסר כל הנמצאות עמו בכל עת ובפרשה כי תצא מחנה אגלה לך סוד סתום וחתום והנה כל המתגאל ירחק משם השם המקובל על כן הזכיר אני ה׳‎:


The meaning of *I am the Lord* is that God loves the one who separates himself to serve God and to obey his word. What happened at Mount Sinai is proof.²¹ The first man is proof.²² This is the secret of man.²³ 10; 12. Since the inclination of the heart of man is like that of beasts, it was not possible to prohibit all women.²⁴ Note, Scripture prohibited all women that are found around a person at all times.²⁵ I will reveal to you a hidden and sealed secret in the Torah portion *When thou goest forth in camp against thine enemies* (Deut. 23:10).²⁶ 3:64, “The female relatives whom a man may not marry…as a rule…(being) constantly together with him in his house; they would easily listen to him, and do what he desires; they are near at hand, and he would have no difficulty in procuring them” (Friedlander translation, p. 376). Observe, whoever defiles himself is distanced from God, from receiving God.²⁷ Scripture therefore mentions, *I am the Lord*.

footnotes:
²¹ Before the revelation Israel had to separate themselves from sexual relations and sanctify themselves. See Ex. 19:15.
²² According to I.E., Adam experienced sexual desire only after eating from the tree of knowledge. See I.E. on Gen. 3:6 (Vol. 1, p. 67). He was expelled from the presence of the Lord because of his lust.
²³ Man is to separate himself from the pleasures of this world and serve God. See the introduction to Vol. 4. Also, see Yesod Mora
²⁴ Since people have sexual desire, the Torah did not prohibit it. It limited it.
²⁵ If Scripture had permitted close relatives, then a person would indulge in sexual intercourse at all times.
²⁶ See I.E. on Deut. 23:10. So too Maimonides, Guide
²⁷ God can only be received when a person is in a state of purity. God turns away from those who are defiled. See Deut. 23:15.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:7:1)


**ערות אביך.** והחל מהאב שהוא קודם הבן ואמר שאסור הוא כל מי שהיא ערות אב ואם והנה היא האם בראש ואחר כך ערות אשת אב ואיננה אם ואחר כך האחות שהיא בת אביו או בת אם:


THE NAKEDNESS OF THY FATHER. Scripture begins²⁸ with the father, who precedes the son.²⁹ Scripture states that anyone who is closely related to his father or mother³⁰ is prohibited. Note, the nakedness of the mother is the one that is mentioned first.³¹ The nakedness of the father’s wife, who is not the mother, follows. Then comes the sister, who is the daughter of his father or the daughter of his mother.

footnotes:
²⁸ The list of those with whom one is not permitted to engage in sexual intercourse.
²⁹ Scripture begins the list of those with whom one is not allowed to engage in sexual intercourse with those related to one’s closest male relative.
³⁰ Literally, of the nakedness of the father or the nakedness of the mother.
³¹ For she is the most closely related female.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:9:1)


**מולדת בית** שנולדה על משפט בית ישראל אחר אירוסין וקדושין:


BORN AT HOME. Who was born in accordance with the laws of the house of Israel following the first and second stage of marriage.³² and *nusu’in*. During the first stage the bride still lives with her parents. In the second step she moves into her husbands house. Today the two stages are combined.

footnotes:
³² A Jewish marriage is effectuated in two stages, erusin


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:9:2)


**מולדת חוץ** חוץ מהמנהג ויש אומרים כי פירוש מולדת כמו יולדו על ברכי יוסף שגדלה עמך בבית או היא במקום אחר ומדינה אחרת וגדלה בחוץ מבית האב עמך ואחר כך הזכיר בת בנך:


BORN ABROAD. Unconventionally.³³ Some say that *born at home* is similar to *were born upon Joseph’s knees* (Gen. 50:23).³⁴ means were brought up in Joseph’s house. It means she grew up with you in the same house. It is possible that *born abroad* means she is in a different place and a different city and grew up away from you, outside of your father’s house. Scripture next mentions *thy son’s daughter* (v. 10).³⁵

³³ Out of wedlock.
³⁴ According to I.E., were brought up on Joseph’s knees
³⁵ After mentioning those closest to one’s father, his wives, and his daughters, it mentions those close to one’s son.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:11:1)


**ערות בת אביך.** יש אומרים שהיא האחות מאב ואם ואנחנו ידענו כי השוכב עמה עובר על שני לאוין ויש אומרים כי זה הפסוק לחזוק והצדוקים אמרו שאיננה בת אם ויפרשו מולדת אביך שגדלה אביך והטעם שלקח האב אשה והיתה לה בת קטנה וכן יפרשו על תמר וראייתם כי לא ימנעני ממך רק לא היתה מולדת דוד ואחר שהמעתיקים אמרו כי בת אשת אב מותרת אין צורך להשיב על הבודאים מלבם ויתכן להיות בת אשת אביך בת אנוסה:


THE NAKEDNESS OF THY FATHER’S WIFE’S DAUGHTER. There are those who say that this refers to a sister from a father and mother.³⁶ We know³⁷ that the one who sleeps with a sister from a father and mother violates two negative laws.³⁸ There are those who say that our verse was written for emphasis.³⁹ The Sadducees⁴⁰ say that *thy father’s wife’s daughter* does not refer to the daughter of one’s mother. They explain that *begotten of thy father* means whom your father raised. Our verse deals with an instance in which the father took a woman who had a small daughter. They say the same thing regarding Tamar.⁴¹ Their proof is, *for he will not withhold me from thee* (II Sam. 13:13).⁴² She thus was not begotten by David. Now since the transmitters of tradition said that the daughter of your father’s wife is permitted, we have no need to answer those who invent things in their hearts. It is possible that “the daughter of thy father’s wife” refers to a daughter⁴³ resulting from a rape.⁴⁴

³⁶ While verse 9 speaks of a sister from one’s father or one’s mother. Hence our verse is not redundant.

³⁹ It repeats what is written in verse 9 to emphasize that one is not to sleep with one’s sister.
⁴⁰ The Karaites.
⁴¹ Tamar was the daughter of David. She was raped by her brother Amnon. See II Sam. 13:1-22. According to the Karaites, Tamar was not actually David’s daughter. She was his stepdaughter. When David married her mother she was already grown. According to the Karaites, Scripture only prohibits a stepsister that grew up in one’s home. Hence it was permitted for Amnon to take her in marriage,
⁴² If she were his real sister, she would not have said this to Amnon.
⁴³ Of one’s father.
⁴⁴ While verse 9 speak of a sister born in wedlock. Hence our verse is not redundant.

ומלת **שאר** ובשר קרובים בטעם:


The word *she’er* (kin⁴⁵) and *basar* (kin)⁴⁶ are close in meaning.

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:14:1)


**דודתך היא.** חשובה היא כדודתך וכן הנה חנמאל דודי והנה מה יעשו הסומכים במצות על התורה לבדה כי לא אסר הכתוב אשת אחי האם ולא אם האב ולא אם האם על כן אנחנו צריכים לקבלה ויתכן שלא הזכיר אשת אחי האם כי נלמוד מאשת אחי האב והזכירה בפרשת קדושים גם הנזכרות שלא הזכירם הכתוב דבר על ההוה:


SHE IS THINE AUNT. She is considered as your aunt.⁴⁷ Similarly “Behold Hanamel, my uncle.”⁴⁸. 38 reads: *in the presence of Hanamel mine uncle* (Jer. 32:6). Be that as it may, I.E.’s point is that Hanamel was not Jeremiah’s uncle. He was the son of Jeremiah’s uncle. Nevertheless, Scripture refers to him as his uncle. Now what will those who rely on the Torah alone for the commandments⁴⁹ do? For Scripture does not prohibit the wife of the brother of one’s mother, and not the mother of one’s father, or the mother of one’s mother.⁵⁰ We therefore need tradition. It is possible that the reason that Scripture did not mention that the wife of one’s mother’s brother is prohibited is that we learn this from the prohibition of the wife of the father’s brother. The prohibition of the wife of one’s brother is mentioned in the Torah (Lev. 19:3) portion *Ye shall be holy*. Also, the reason that those whom I mentioned⁵¹ are not listed in Scripture is that the Torah speaks of that which is prevalent.⁵²

⁴⁹ The Karaites.
⁵⁰ They are prohibited by Rabbinic dictate. However, the Karaites, who do not accept Rabbinic tradition, have no basis for prohibiting these women.
⁵¹ The mother of one’s father and the mother of one’s mother.
⁵² Sexual intercourse with grandparents is extremely rare.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:16:1)


**ערות אשת אחיך.** בפרשת כי ישבו אחים:


THOU SHALT NOT UNCOVER THE NAKEDNESS OF THY BROTHER’S WIFE. This is explained in the Torah portion *If brethren dwell together* (Deut. 25:5).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:16:2)


והאומר למה לא הזכיר הכתוב הבת והיא מפורשת ערות אשה ובתה בתו או שאיננה בתו כי אחר ששכב עם האם נאסרה הבת:


To the one who asks why didn’t Scripture mention a daughter, [I reply] that the daughter is stated in *Thou shalt not uncover the nakedness of a woman and her daughter* (Lev. 18:17), whether or not it is his daughter, for once he has slept with the mother the daughter is prohibited.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:17:1)


**שארה הנה.** הם שני שמות שאר עם שארה. היה האל״ף ראוי לנוח כלמ״ד שלמה לולי שהיא מהגרון:


THEY ARE NEAR KINSWOMEN. *She’er* (kin) and *sha’arah* (kinswomen) are two nouns. If the *alef* of *sha’arah* were not a guttural, it would be quiescent like the *lamed* of *salmah* (raiment) (Ex. 22:8).⁵³ of *sha’arah* were not a guttural, it would have been vocalized with a *sheva na*, rather than with a *chataf pattach*.

footnotes:
⁵³ I.E.’s point is, if the alef



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:18:1)


**לצרור.** מגזרת וכעסתה צרתה והנה לא הזכיר בפרשת קדושים עונש השוכב עם שתי אחיות כאשר לא הזכיר עונש השוכב עם בת בנו ועם בת בתו וטעם המפרש כי רחל ולאה לא היו אחיות וראייתו כי את כל התועבות האל וזו איננה ראיה גמורה והשואל איך יעניש השם על דבר שלא הזהיר יש משיבים כי בני נח נצטוו על העריות האלה ואחרים אמרו אף על פי שהכתוב אמר כל התועבות האל בשם כלל אין הפירוש על כולם כי אם על רובם ודעתי תדענה בפרשת וילך משה:


TO BE A RIVAL. *Li-tzeror* (to be a rival) is related to the word *tzaratah* (her rival) in *And her rival vexed her* (I Sam. 1:6). Note, Scripture does not mention in the Torah portion *Ye shall be holy* (Lev. 19:2)⁵⁴ lists those with whom one is not permitted to have sexual intercourse and the punishment for violating these prohibitions. the punishment for sleeping with two sisters, and it does not mention the punishment for sleeping with the daughter of one’s son and with the daughter of one’s daughter.⁵⁵ The one who says that Rachel and Leah⁵⁶ were not sisters⁵⁷ errs. His proof is, *for all these abominations…* (Lev. 18:27).⁵⁸ However, this is not a rigorous proof. There are those who answer the one who asks, how could God punish for something He did not warn against,⁵⁹ by saying the sons of Noah were warned about these sexual offenses. Others say⁶⁰ that even though Scripture makes a general statement, *for all these abominations…*, the reference is not to all these abominations but to most of them.⁶¹ You will learn my opinion⁶² in the Torah portion *And Moses went* (Deut. 31:1).⁶³

⁵⁶ Two sisters whom Jacob married.
⁵⁷ Hence Jacob was permitted to marry them.
⁵⁸ Marrying two sisters is thus an abomination. Jacob would not commit an abominable act.
⁵⁹ Verses 27 and 28 tells us that the Canaanites were punished for practicing the sexual acts that the Torah prohibits. Hence the question arose from the anonymous commentator quoted by I.E.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:19:1)


והשוכב עם הנדה חשוב מהעריות:


One who sleeps with a menstruating woman is considered to have slept with a woman whose nakedness one is prohibited from uncovering.⁶⁴ (illegitimate).

footnotes:
⁶⁴ Even though the prohibition to have intercourse with her is limited to the time of her menses and the child born from such an act is not considered a mamzer



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:20:1)


**ואל אשת עמיתך.** להוציא השבויה ויש אומרים כי כבר היתה אסורה ואשר גברה תאות יצרם על דעת לבם תעו בפירוש לזרע וידענו כי המשגל נחלק לג׳‎ חלקים האחד לפריה ורביה בלא תאוה והשני להקל מלאות הגוף והשלישי לתאוה הנמשלת לתאות הבהמה ואחר שאמר הכתוב לזרע שטעמו אפילו לזרע הנה היא אסורה אסור חמור ומזרעך לא תתן הבא אחריו לעד. והוסיף לטמאה בה כי לא ינקה כל הנוגע בה והוא טמא לעולם ובפרשה הזה לא אסר הנכרית כלל ואין טענה מהשבויה כי היא מתייהדת:


WITH THY NEIGHBOR’S WIFE. This excludes the captive woman.⁶⁵ There are those who say that she⁶⁶ was previously prohibited. Those whose sexual craving⁶⁷ overcame the intelligence of their heart erred in explaining the meaning of *le-zara*⁶⁸ (carnally).⁶⁹ We know that sexual intercourse is divided into three groups. One, to be fruitful and multiply⁷⁰ and not to satisfy lust. Two, to relieve the fullness of the body.⁷¹ 3:2, “He will…not engage in sexual intercourse whenever he lusts, but only when he knows that he has to ejaculate for reasons of health.” Three, for the purpose of lust, which is similar to the lust of an animal.⁷² Now since Scripture says *le-zara* (carnally), the meaning of which is even for seed,⁷³ a married woman is prohibited by a very severe prohibition.⁷⁴ *And thou shalt not give any of thy seed to set them apart to Molech* (v. 21) is proof.⁷⁵ (for seed) means for the purpose of children, for the word seed in verse 21 definitely applies to children. Scripture adds to *defile thyself with her* to let us know that the one who touches her will not be cleansed; he will be defiled forever. This verse⁷⁶ does not at all prohibit a non-Jewish woman.⁷⁷. 38 reads: This verse (verse 21) prohibits all non-Jewish women. There is no argument from the captive woman,⁷⁸ for she converts to Judaism.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ See Deut. 21:10-14.
⁶⁶ Our verse does not deal with a non-Jewish woman, for a non-Jewish woman is prohibited to an Israelite whether she is married or not. However, a Jewish woman is prohibited to an Israelite only if she is married. Thus “thy neighbor’s wife” does not stand in contrast to a married captive woman but in contrast to an unmarried Jewish woman (Filwarg). For other interpretations see Rabbi Samuel Zarza, Krinsky, and Weiser.
⁶⁷ Literally, lust of their inclination.
⁶⁸ Literally, to give seed.
⁶⁹ These commentators read our verse as follows: You shall not lie with your neighbor’s wife for the purpose of seed that is to impregnate her.
⁷⁰ For reproduction.
⁷¹ For release of sexual tension. So too Maimonides, M.T. Hilkhot De’ot
⁷² For pure sexual pleasure.
⁷³ I.E. renders our verse: You shall not lie with your neighbor’s wife even for the purpose of producing seed (children), that is, to impregnate her.
⁷⁴ For a married woman is prohibited even if the adultery is not committed for the purpose of sexual lust.
⁷⁵ That le-zara
⁷⁶ Verse 21.
⁷⁷ According to this interpretation verse 21 prohibits intercourse with a non-Jewish woman, who will offer her children to Molech (Weiser). See I.E. on Lev. 21:2. Other non-Jewish women are not included in this verse. Vat. Ebr
⁷⁸ That one may marry non-Jewish women, for the captive woman is not Jewish and yet an Israelite may marry her.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:21:1)


**למלך.** שם צלם ודרשו חז״ל שהוא שם כלל כל מי שימליכנו עליו ויתכן שהוא תועבת בני עמון:


TO MOLECH. Molech is the name of an image. The rabbis interpreted Molech as a general term for all⁷⁹ whom a person accepts as a king over him.⁸⁰ (king). It is possible that Molech is the abomination of the Ammonites.⁸¹

footnotes:
⁷⁹ Any idol.
⁸⁰ For the word Molech is connected to the word melekh
⁸¹ God of the Ammonites.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:21:2)


ומלת **להעביר.** כמו לשרוף כי זאת היתה עבודתו ויש אומרים כי יעביר הבן על האש ויש מי שיחיה ויש מי שימות ואחרים פירשו בעבור שאין בפסוק זכר אש להעביר מתורת השם לדת המולך וחללת השם שהוא נקרא עליך שהוא אלהיך בתתך מזרע קודש למולך. והגאון אמר כי הנאוף על שמנה מעלות והקשה שבכלם הבהמה שהיא ממין אחר ואחריה הזכר שכל המין אסור ואחריה ערות אשת אב וחביריה שהן אסורו בכל עת ולא אאריך כי לא יועילו אלו הדברים כי הכתוב אמר על כלם ונכרתו הנפשות העושות:


The word *le-ha’avir* (to set apart) is to burn, for that was the way Molech was worshipped. Some say that they used to pass⁸² (to set apart) literally means to pass over. their sons over the fire. Some lived and some died. In view of the fact that the verse does not mention fire, some explained *le-ha’avir lamolekh* (to set apart to Molech) as meaning to cause to pass over from the Torah of God to another religion.⁸³ You will defile the Lord who is known as your God⁸⁴ when you give from the holy seed to Molech. The Gaon says that fornication has eight levels.⁸⁵ The most severe is intercourse with a beast of another species; afterwards comes the male, the entire gender of which is prohibited; afterwards comes the nakedness of the father’s wife and her companions,⁸⁶ which are prohibited at all times. I will not dwell on this, for what purpose is there to these words since Scripture says, concerning all of them, *even the souls that do them shall be cut off* (v. 29).

footnotes:
⁸² The word le-ha’avir
⁸³ Our verse literally reads: You shall not give any of your seed to cause them to pass over to Molech.
⁸⁴ Literally, the Lord who is called over you that He is your God.
⁸⁵ See I.E. on Ex. 20:13 (Vol. 2, p. 430).
⁸⁶ Other women who are similarly prohibited.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:22:1)


**ואת זכר.** אחר שמצאנו הן שכבתי אמש את אבי הנה אזהרה לשוכב ולנשכב ויאמר רב חננאל ז״ל כי יש מי שיחדש בגופו כצורת בשר אשה וזה לא יתכן בתולדה ויש אומרים אנדרוגינוס וכל הצער הזה בעבור היות משכבי אשה לשון רבים ודברי יחיד שני משכבים והנכון בעיני כי המצוה כפשוטה גם אנשי התושיה חייבו מיתה עליה והכתוב אחז דרך כבוד לאמר כמו לא תקרב ואחר שהזכר נברא לעשות והנקבה להעשות הזהיר הכתוב שלא תהפוך דברי השם ויש משכב להוציא הזרע על כן מלת משכבי לשון רבים. ואין ראוי להאריך:


WITH MANKIND. Since we find, *Behold, I lay yester-night with my father* (Gen. 19:34), we see that the prohibition⁸⁷. applies to the one who lay and the one who is lain with.⁸⁸. Rabbi Hannanel, of blessed memory, says that there are those in whose body a female genitalia is newly formed.⁸⁹ However, this is impossible according to the laws of nature.⁹⁰ There are those who say that the reference is to an androgynous person. All this pain was caused by the fact that Scripture employs the plural *mishkeve ishah* (as with womankind).⁹¹ is “intercourses of a woman.” Intercourses is plural. It implies more than one type of intercourse, i.e., anal and vaginal intercourse. There is an individual opinion⁹². 55a. that *mishkeve ishah* refers to two types of intercourse.⁹³ I believe that the precept is to be taken literally.⁹⁴ The men of sound wisdom⁹⁵ also applied the death penalty to those who performed this act.⁹⁶ 83b. Scripture employed respectable language in saying [thou shalt not lie].⁹⁷ is a euphemism for intercourse. Compare, *thou shalt not approach* (v. 14).⁹⁸ is a euphemism for intercourse. Now, since the male was created to make⁹⁹ and the female to be made,¹⁰⁰ Scripture notes that one should not reverse the words of God. There is a type of laying whose aim is to emit semen.¹⁰¹ literally means layings of. I.E.’s point is that *mishkeve* here refers to laying down for the purpose of pouring seed; i.e., it is a euphemism for intercourse. Hence Scripture employs the term *mishkeve* which is a plural.¹⁰² (Krinsky). It is unfit to dwell at length on this.

⁸⁷ Thou shalt not lie
⁸⁸ For Lot’s daughter was lain with and yet she said, Behold, I lay yesternight with my father
⁸⁹ According to Rabbi Hannanel our verse speaks of anal intercourse and vaginal intercourse with a man who has an artificial vagina.
⁹⁰ Scripture would not prohibit something which does not normally exist.
⁹¹ The literal meaning of mishkeve ishah
⁹² In the Talmud. See San
⁹³ Vaginal and anal. This interpretation explains our verse as follows: You shall not sleep with a man by employing one of the two types of intercourse that one employs when having intercourse with a woman. In other words, do not engage in anal intercourse with a male.
⁹⁴ The reference in our verse is to anal intercourse.
⁹⁵ The rabbis.
⁹⁶ See Yevamot
⁹⁷ Lie
⁹⁸ Approach
⁹⁹ To penetrate the woman and thus impregnate her.
¹⁰⁰ To be penetrated and impregnated.
¹⁰¹ The term mishkeve
¹⁰² Both partners in the homosexual act emit semen. Hence the plural mishkeve


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:22:2)


והזכיר **תועבה היא.** כי הוא דבר נתעב לנפש קדושה אפי׳‎ לו בתולדה:


Scripture states *it is abomination*, for it is an abomination to a holy soul even by the laws of nature.¹⁰³

¹⁰³ It is not only prohibited by the Torah, it is unnatural.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:1)


ומלת **שכבתך.** כבר פירשתיה ויתכן להיותו שכבתך משכיבה כי עם השכיבה יקרה קרי וכמו שאמר באשת איש לטמאה בה כן אמר בבהמה:


I have already explained the word *shekhovtekha* (lie carnally).¹⁰⁴ It is possible that the word *shekhovtekha*¹⁰⁵ is derived from *shekhivah* (lying), for the emission of semen comes along with lying.¹⁰⁶ As Scripture states to defile *thyself with her* (v. 20) with regard to a married woman, it similarly says so with regard to a beast.

¹⁰⁴ See I.E. on verse 20.
¹⁰⁵ Literally, your laying.
¹⁰⁶ Hence laying is a euphemism for ejaculation.

**ובכל בהמה.** נקבה:


AND…ANY BEAST. A female beast.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:3)


**ולפני בהמה.** זכר:


BEFORE A BEAST. A male beast.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:23:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:4)


**לרבעה.** שב אל הבהמה כי האשה היא הנרבעת וזאת המלה מגזרת אדבעה:


TO LIE DOWN THERETO.¹⁰⁷ (to lie her down) i.e., to have intercourse with her. *Le-rivah* reverts to the beast, for the woman is the one who is penetrated.¹⁰⁸ literally means to lie her down, i.e., to have intercourse with her. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. The word *rivah* (lie down) is related to the word *arba’ah* (four).¹⁰⁹

¹⁰⁷ Hebrew, le-rivah

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:23:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:23:5)


**תבל הוא.** אנשי הדקדוק אמרו שזאת המלה מהכפל כמו תמס יהלוך והתי״ו נוסף ואמרו כי טעמה כמו השחתה ויש אומרים כי גם מבול ממנו וקרוב מגזרת בלל ה׳‎ שפת כל הארץ וזה הכלל אסר הכתוב כל מין שיוכל האדם להתייחד עמם אשת איש ונכריה ואשר לא יוליד כי מין לא יוליד כי אם ממינו וכמינו:


IT IS PERVERSION. The grammarians say that the word *tevel* (perversion) comes from a double root.¹¹⁰. Compare, *temes* (melteth)¹¹¹. in *which melteth and passeth away* (Ps. 58:9). The *tav* of *tevel* is superfluous.¹¹² They say that *tevel* means destruction.¹¹³ There are those who say that the word *mabbul* (flood) is related to it.¹¹⁴ means destruction. However, the word *mabbul* is most likely related to the word *balal* (confound) in *the Lord did there confound the language of all the earth* (Gen. 11:9). This is the general rule: Scripture prohibits all kinds of women with which it is possible for a person to be alone;¹¹⁵ a married woman, a non-Jewish woman,¹¹⁶. 38 quoted in note 77. and those with whom intercourse cannot result in children,¹¹⁷ for a kind cannot reproduce except with its own kind and with those like its kind.

¹¹¹ Whose root is mem, samekh, samekh
¹¹² It has no translatable meaning. It is a form letter.
¹¹³ The seed is destroyed, for conception cannot result.
¹¹⁴ According to this interpretation mabbul
¹¹⁵ Close relatives. See I.E. on verse 6.
¹¹⁶ This comment seems to satisfy the reading in Vat. Ebr
¹¹⁷ Bestiality.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:24:1)


**אל תטמאו.** דגשות הטי״ת להתבלע תי״ו התפעל ולא נו״ן בנין נפעל והעד דגשות הטי״ת להתבלע תי״ו התפעל ולא נו״ן בנין נפעל והעד דגשות המ״ם כמשפט והנה הזכיר כל כלם שהם מטמאים הנפש:


DEFILE NOT YE YOURSELVES. The *tet* of *tittamme’u* (defile ye yourselves) has a *dagesh* to compensate for the swallowed¹¹⁸ *tav* of the *hitpa’el*.¹¹⁹ is a *hitpa’el*. The *dagesh* does not compensate for the *nun* of the *nifal*.¹²⁰ is not a *nifal* imperfect. The *dagesh* in the *mem* of *tittamme’u*, which is there according to the rules of grammar, is proof of this.¹²¹ is not placed in the middle root letter in the *nifal* imperfect It is in the *hitpa’el*. Observe, Scripture notes that each one of these acts defiles the soul.

¹¹⁸ Missing.
¹¹⁹ Tittamme’u
¹²⁰ In other words, tittamme’u
¹²¹ A dagesh

**משלח מפניכם.** טעמו מהארץ הטהורה מתים או מגורשים:


WHICH I CAST OUT FROM BEFORE YOU. It means, from the Holy Land, dead or expelled.¹²²

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:25:1)


**ותקיא הארץ.** כי מה שיקיא האדם הוא נתעב בעיניו ואליו לא ישוב:


AND THE LAND VOMITED OUT HER INHABITANTS. Whatever a person regurgitates is despised in his eyes, and he will not take it back.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:26:1)


**ושמרתם את חקתי.** אלה שאמרתי לכם אלה תהיינה אסורות:


YE THEREFORE SHALL KEEP MY STATUTES. The very ones that I enumerated¹²³ shall be prohibited to you.

footnotes:
¹²³ In this chapter.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:26:2)


**ואת משפטי.** שתעשו המשפט שאצוה על העובר על אחת מהן וטעם ולא תעשו מכל התועבות האלה שנית להכניס הגר כי זאת המצוה היא שוה לאזרח ולגר בעבור שהוא דר בארץ ישראל ואם יש לך לב תוכל להבין כי בימי יעקב שלקח שתי אחיות בחרן ואחריו עמרם שלקח דודתו במצרים לא נטמאו בהם:


AND MINE ORDINANCES. You shall execute the judgment which I will command, upon the one who violates any of these. The reason that *and shall not do any of these abominations* is written a second time¹²⁴ is to include the stranger, for this precept¹²⁵. applies equally to the native and to the stranger who dwells in the Land of Israel. If you have any intelligence¹²⁶ you can understand why in past days Jacob took two sisters in Haran and later Amram took his aunt in Egypt, and they were not defiled through them.¹²⁷

footnotes:
¹²⁴ God had already told Israel not to follow Canaanite practices in verse 3. Why then repeat the same idea in our verse?
¹²⁵ Not do any of these abominations
¹²⁶ Literally, a heart.
¹²⁷ See I.E. on Deut. 31:16 and the notes thereto (Vol. 4, p. 227).



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:27:1)


וטעם **כי את כל התועבות האל.** שתדעו זה גם אתם גם בניכם שתשמרו אותם ותהיה הארץ טהורה ולא תקיא הארץ אתכם:


The meaning of *for all these abominations* ¹²⁸. (Lev. 18:27) is, you and your children shall know this so that you shall keep these precepts, and the land will be pure and the land will not regurgitate you.

¹²⁸ For all these abomination have the men of the land done…and the land…vomited out the nation that was before you


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:27:2)


**האל** עם האלה שוה כמו ההם ההמה ואין זאת דעת הגאון:


THESE. *Ha-el* (these) and *ha-elleh* (these) (v. 29) have one meaning. Compare, *ha-hem* (those) and *ha-hemah* (those). However, this is not the opinion of the Gaon.¹²⁹

¹²⁹ See I.E. on Gen. 19:8 (Vol. 1, p. 204).



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:28:1)


**כאשר קאה.** פועל עבר מגזרת שתו ושכרו וקיו כי אותיות אהו״י מתחלפים ויש ארץ לשון זכר נעתם ארץ ולא נשא אותם הארץ או חסר מלת היא כאילו הוא כאשר היא קאה כמו באה עם הצאן:


AS IT VOMITED OUT. *Ka’ah* (vomited out) is a verb in the perfect. It is related to the word *keyo* (spew)¹³⁰. in *Drink ye, and be drunken, and spew* (Jer. 25:27), for the letters *alef, heh, vav, yod* interchange.¹³¹ of *ka’ah* is in place of a *yod*. The word *eretz* is found in the masculine.¹³² is masculine. Hence the noun *eretz*, which it governs, has to be masculine. Compare, *netam aretz* (the land burnt up)¹³³ is masculine. Hence the noun *aretz*, which it governs, is masculine. (Is. 9:18), and *ve-lo nasa otam ha-aretz* (and the land was not able to bear them)¹³⁴ is masculine. Hence the noun *eretz*, which it governs, is masculine. (Gen. 13:6).¹³⁵ is usually feminine. Hence I.E.’s comment. On the other hand, the word *hiy* (it)¹³⁶ might be missing.¹³⁷ is a feminine participle from the root *kof, alef, heh*, and *eretz* is feminine. Our verse should be interpreted as if it read, *ka’asher hiy ka’ah* (as it vomited out). In this case, *ka’ah* is similar to *ba’ah* (cometh) in *cometh with the sheep* (Gen. 29:6).¹³⁸ is a feminine participle from the root *bet, alef, heh*.

footnotes:
¹³⁰ From the root kof, yod, heh
¹³¹ The alef
¹³² Ka’ah
¹³³ Netam
¹³⁴ Nasa
¹³⁵ Eretz
¹³⁶ Literally, she.
¹³⁷ In this case, ka’ah
¹³⁸ For ba’ah

EVEN THE SOULS THAT DO THEM SHALL BE CUT OFF. If they do so in public you shall put them to death. If they do so in secret then I will cut them off.
