**ושמרתם את משמרתי.** זאת המשמרת והיא לבלתי עשות:


THEREFORE SHALL YE KEEP MY CHARGE. This charge. The charge being not to do [any of these abominable practices].


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:30:2)


וטעם **אשר נעשו לפניהם.** שלא יאמר אדם הואיל ועשו כן הקדמונים אעשה כן גם אני:


The meaning of *which were done before you* is, a person shall not say, since the earlier ones did so, I will also do so.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 18:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/18:30:3)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם.** דבק עם משמרתי שלא ישאל חסר לב למה זאת המשמרת כי אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם עשו כל אשר אצוה אתכם:


*I am the Lord your God* is connected to *My charge*.¹³⁹, which precedes it. The thrust of our verse is, *Therefore shall ye keep My charge*, for *I am the Lord your God*. One who lacks intelligence¹⁴⁰ shall not ask,¹⁴¹ “Why this charge?”¹⁴² For I am the Lord your God. Do all that I command you.

footnotes:
¹³⁹ Not to that ye defile not yourselves therein
¹⁴⁰ Literally, an intelligent heart.
¹⁴¹ An intelligent person would not ask this question, for the prohibitions are based on reason.
¹⁴² What reason is there for this charge?



## Chapter 19


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:2:1)


טעם **אל כל עדת בני ישראל.** להכניס את הגרים בעבור שהם נזהרים על העריות כישראלים:


[UNTO ALL THE CONGREGATION OF THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL.] The reason that Scripture states *unto all the congregation of the children of Israel* is to exclude strangers¹ 38. *Mikra’ot Gedolot* reads, *The reason why Scripture states unto all the congregation of the children of Israel is to include the strangers*. The reading in *Vat. Ebr.* 38 appears correct, for *unto all the congregation of the children of Israel* seems to imply that what follows only applies to the children of Israel. Furthermore, in his comments on Lev. 20:1, I.E. explicitly says that our chapter applies only to the children of Israel. By strangers I.E. means non-Jews living in Israel. See I.E. on Ex. 20:9 (Vol. 2, p. 427). since they, like the Israelites, are commanded to abstain from prohibited sexual relations.

footnotes:
¹ So Vat. Ebr.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:2:2)


וטעם להזכיר זאת הפרשה אחרי העריות שלא יחשבו כי בעבור שמירת העריות לבדם יעמדו בארץ אמר להם שגם יש מצות אחרות אם לא תשמרום תגורשו מן הארץ והם עשרת הדברים הנזכרים כי הנה הזהיר על עבודת כוכבים אחר שאמר כי קדוש אני ה׳‎ כי הוא כנגד הדבור הראשון על דעת רבים ולא תשבעו בשמי הוא דבור לא תשא ואת שבתתי תשמרו זכור את יום השבת גם איש אמו ואביו תיראו הוא דבור כבד את אביך ואת אמך גם לא תעמוד על דם רעך דבור לא תרצח. ודבר השפחה הנחרפת אזהרת לא תנאף עם שהיא חפשית. גם לא תגנובו ולא תכחשו ולא תשקרו ולא תעשוק את רעך כנגד השלשה הנשארים:


The reason this chapter follows the one dealing with prohibited sexual relations is that the Israelites should not imagine that they would remain in the land by observing only the laws dealing with the prohibited sexual relations. God informs them that there are other commandments. If they do not observe them, they will be exiled from the land. The aforementioned are the ten statements.² God warns against idol worship after stating, *I the Lord…am holy* which, according to many commentators, is parallel to the first statement of the Decalogue.³ (Ex. 20:7). *And ye shall not swear by my name falsely* (v. 12) is parallel to *Thou shalt not take the name of the Lord thy God in vain* (Ex. 20:7).

footnotes:
² In our chapter. Our chapter, as I.E. goes on to explain, contains ten precepts which parallel the Decalogue.
³ For I the Lord…am holy



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:3:1)


וכבר אמרתי לך בפרשת ואלה המשפטים שכל מצוה ומצוה עומדת בפני עצמה לכן יש כדמות סמך להדביק הפסוקים. והנה פרשת קדושים תחלתו איש אמו ואביו תיראו והנה אמר כי יאריכון ימי המכבד אם כן יקצרון ימי המקלה על כן אמר תיראו וטעם להזכיר האם קודם האב כי הקטן איננו מכיר בתחלה כי אם אמו ואחר כן אביו ואחר כן שבת כי מצוה על הקטן לשמור את השבת ואין ככה שאר המועדים ואחר כן יכיר אלהיו ששבת ביום השביעי ואחריו אל תפנו אל האלילים ומורא האבות ידוע מהקבלה. ואמר איש פעם אחת דרך קצרה. ואמר תיראו לשון רבים כי חייבים הרואים ללמדו ולהכריחו וכן בשמירת שבת:


AND YE SHALL KEEP MY SABBATHS. This parallels *Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy* (Ex. 20:8). *Ye shall fear every man his mother, and his father* is similar to *Honor thy father and thy mother* (Ex. 20:12). *Neither shalt thou stand idly by the blood of thy neighbor* (v. 16) is likewise the same as the statement *Thou shalt not murder* (Ex. 20:13). The law regarding the bondmaid designated for a man (v. 20) is parallel to *Thou shalt not commit adultery* (Ex. 20:13 with a free woman. Similarly, *Ye shall not steal; neither shall ye deal falsely, nor lie one to another* (v. 11); *Thou shalt not oppress thy neighbor* (v. 13) are parallel to the three remaining statements.⁴ is parallel to *Thou shalt not steal* (Ex. 20:13). *Neither shall ye deal falsely, nor lie one to another* is parallel to *Thou shalt not bear false witness* (*Ibid*.). *Thou shalt not oppress thy neighbor* is parallel to *Thou shalt not covet* (Ex. 20:14). I have previously told you in my comments on the Torah portion *Ve-Elleh Ha-Mishpatim* (now these are the ordinances) (Ex. 21:1) that each and every precept stands by itself.⁵ However,⁶ (literally, therefore). However, I.E. occasionally uses the term *lakhen* in the sense of however (Filwarg). It is also possible that we should read *akeh* rather than *lakhen*. there is some support for connecting the verses.⁷ Note, the section that opens with the words *Ye shall be holy* (v. 2) begins with *Ye shall fear every man his mother, and his father* (v. 3). Now Scripture tells us that the years of the one who honors his parents will be long (Ex. 20:12). This implies that the days of one who disrespects his parents will be shortened. Scripture therefore says, *Ye shall fear*.⁸. The implication is that one who disrespects his parents will suffer premature death. According to I.E. the meaning of *Ye shall fear every man his mother, and father* is, be afraid of disrespecting your parents, for if you dishonor them you will suffer dire consequences. Our verse is thus parallel to Ex. 20:12. The reason Scripture mentions the mother before the father is that a child first recognizes only its mother.⁹ Then it recognizes its father. The Torah next mentions the Sabbath because, unlike all the other festivals, a child is commanded to keep the Sabbath.¹⁰ The child shall then recognize his God who rested on the seventh day.¹¹. I.E. speaks of a progression. The child learns to recognize its mother, then its father, and then God. Scripture next mentions, *Turn ye not unto the idols* (v. 4). We know from tradition what Scripture means by fearing parents.¹² fearing one’s father refers to sitting in his place, speaking in his turn, and contradicting him. See *Sifra* on this verse. Our verse employs the word *ish* (every man) once¹³. See Lev. 17:3,10,13; Lev. 15:2. because it wants to be brief. Scripture employs the plural *tira’u* (ye shall fear)¹⁴ is in the singular. Thus the verb governing it should be in the singular. Hence I.E.’s comment. because those who observe the child have to teach him and force him to fear his parents. It is the same case regarding Sabbath observance.¹⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Of the Decalogue. Ye shall not steal
⁵ Each and every precept is an independent statement. It is not connected to what comes before nor to what follows. See I.E. on Ex. 21:2 (Vol. 2, p. 448).
⁶ Hebrew, lakhen
⁷ If we try, we can find a reason that the commandments follow each other.
⁸ Ex. 20:12 reads, Honor thy father and thy mother, that thy days may be long
⁹ According to I.E. our verse is directed at a child. Hence it mentions the mother first. When the Bible mentions the father first (Ex. 20:12) it is directed at an adult.
¹⁰ According to I.E. a father is obligated to prevent his child from doing work on the Sabbath. See I.E. on Ex. 20:8 (Vol. 2, p. 426).
¹¹ By resting on the Sabbath the child will come to know that there is a God in the world. Hence our verse concludes with I am the Lord your God
¹² According to the Sifra
¹³ Elsewhere the Torah uses the term ish ish
¹⁴ Ish
¹⁵ Those who observe the child violating the Sabbath have to correct him.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:3:2)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם.** שתעשו כמוני ששבתתי מכל מלאכה:


The meaning of *I am the Lord your God* is, act as I did, for I rested from all work on the Sabbath.

**האלילים.** הם הפסילים ונקראו כן כי הם דבר שקר כמו רופאי אליל כלכם ויתכן להיות מגזרת אל והטעם שאיננו יש:


UNTO THE IDOLS.¹⁷. The reference is to statues. They are called *elilim* because they are false objects. Compare the word *elil* (no value) in *Ye are physicians of no value* (Job 13:4). It is possibly related to the word *al* (not). *Elilim* means things that do not exist.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:4:3

**ואלהי מסכה.** לקבל כח העליונים כי אין צורך לאלוה אחר עמי על כן כתוב אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם ובלשון רבים תפנו גם תעשו כי הרואה ולא יגלה הדבר הוא משתתף עמו:


NOR…MOLTEN GODS. Made to receive power from those who are on high, for there is no need for another god along with Me.¹⁸ Scripture therefore states,¹⁹ *I am the Lord your God*. Scripture employs the plurals *tifnu* (turn ye) and *ta’asu* (make), for the one who sees idol worship and does not reveal the matter is a partner of the idolater.

¹⁹ At the end of our verse.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:5:1)


**וכי תזבחו זבח שלמים.** דבק עם הכתוב למעלה בעבור שלא יזבחו לשדים לא אלוה כי אם לשם לבדו. וטעם לשון רבים תזבחו כי על הרוב דבר הכתוב שיתחברו אנשים על זבח אחד:


AND WHEN YE OFFER A SACRIFICE OF PEACE-OFFERINGS. This is connected to what is written above.²⁰ It means that one shall not sacrifice to the demons, no-gods,²¹ but only unto God. Scripture employs the plural *tizbechu* (ye offer), for Scripture is speaking about what is most prevalent, for people to join together to offer a sacrifice.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:8:1)


**ואכליו.** כל אחד מאוכליו וכן וצדיקים ככפיר יבטח:


BUT EVERY ONE THAT EATETH IT. The meaning of *okhelav* is every one that eats of it.²³ is a plural. It literally means they that eat of it. Our verse thus literally reads, they that eat of it shall bear his iniquity. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. Similarly *But the righteous are secure as a young lion*²⁴ (Prov. 28:1).²⁵

²³ Okhelav

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:8:2)


**כי את קדש ה׳‎ חלל.** אחר שהקריב האימורים לגבוה הנה כל הבשר קדש. וחלל מגזרת חול שאין לו טעם כדמות חלל. ובפרשה הזאת באר עונש העון ונכרתה הנפש ההוא:


BECAUSE HE HATH PROFANED THE HOLY THING OF THE LORD. After he offered the parts designated to God by Scripture, all the flesh²⁶ is holy. *Chillel* (profaned) is related to the word *chol* (profane). It is something that has no significance.²⁷ It is like an empty space (*challel*). In this section²⁸ Scripture explains the punishment,²⁹ namely, *and that soul shall be cut off*.

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:1)


וטעם **ובקצרכם.** אחר זבח שלמים כאשר נתת לשם האימורים כן תתנו מקציר ארצכם לכבוד השם לעני ולגר. ודקדוק בקצרכם זר והוא שם הפעל מן הקל:


[AND WHEN YE REAP THE HARVEST.] This follows the law dealing with the peace offering, for as you gave to God the parts designated in Scripture, so shall you, out of respect for God, give what Scripture designates from the harvest of your land³⁰ for the poor and the stranger. The word *u-ve-kutzrekhem* (and when ye reap the harvest) is grammatically irregular.³¹ rather than with a *kubbutz*. In other words, *u-ve-kutzrekhem* should have read *u-ve-kotzrekhem*. It is an infinitive³² in the *kal*.

footnotes:
³⁰ In verses 9 and 10.
³¹ The word should have been vocalized with a kamatz katan
³² With a pronominal suffix.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:2)


**פאת שדך.** שיעזוב פאה בשדה:


THE CORNER OF THY FIELD. The Israelite shall leave a corner in the field.³³ means you shall leave a corner of the field.

footnotes:
³³ In other words, thou shalt not wholly reap the corner of thy field


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:9:3)


**ולקט.** ידוע:


THE GLEANING. The meaning of *leket* (gleaning) is known.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:10:1)


**תעולל.** תכרית העוללות הם הקטנים כמו עולל גם יקראו יונקות וזאת המלה כמו ושרשך מארץ חיים שטעמו יכרית השרש וכן מסעף פארה יכרות הסעיף:


GLEAN. *Te’olel* (glean) means to cut off the *olelot*, that is the young grapes. Compare, *olel* (young child). Very young children are also called *yonekot* (sucklings).³⁴ (young children and the sucklings). This word³⁵ means a young child or a young plant. *Te’olel* thus appears to mean to plant a young shoot. However, it has the opposite meaning. It means to harvest a young plant. Thus a word coming from a root meaning a young plant can, in the *pi’el*, have the opposite meaning, i.e., to destroy a young plant. is similar to *shereshkha* (root thee out)³⁶ comes from the stem meaning root. Thus the word might be taken to mean to root. However, it has the opposite meaning in the *pi’el*. in *And root thee out of the land of the living* (Ps. 52:7), the meaning of which is, to cut the root. Similarly the meaning *of mesa’ef*³⁷ comes from the same root as *se’if* (a branch). However, in the *p’iel* it means to cut the branches. *purah* (Is. 10:33) is to lop the boughs.

footnotes:
³⁴ See Lam. 2:11, olel ve-yonek
³⁵ Olel
³⁶ Shereshkha
³⁷ Mesa’ef


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:10:2)


**ופרט.** ידוע בדברי קבלה מגזרת הפורטים על פי הנבל:


THE FALLEN FRUIT. The meaning of *peret* (fallen fruit) is known in the words of tradition.³⁸ is often used in Rabbinic tradition and there has the meaning of that which is singled out. See *Sotah* 37b; *Chagigah* 6a. Thus *peret* means the single grapes that fall off the vine. See *Pe’ah* 6:5, *Two grapes are peret; three are not peret*. It is related to the word *poretim* (thrum)³⁹ means those that pluck on each one of the strings of the psaltery. in *That thrum on the psaltery* (Amos 6:5).

footnotes:
³⁸ The word perat
³⁹ According to I.E. ha-poretim…al ha-navel

**לעני.** ישראל:


FOR THE POOR. Of Israel.

AND FOR THE STRANGER. Who lives among you.⁴⁰

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:11:1)


וטעם **לא תגנבו.** אחר כך. כי כן צויתיך שתתן משלך אל העניים לכבוד השם אף שתקח מה שהוא לאחרים:


[YE SHALL NOT STEAL.] *Ye shall not steal* follows,⁴¹ for Scripture, as it were, says I have commanded that you give from your property to the poor out of respect for God.⁴² (v. 10) which concludes the laws of leaving part of the produce to the poor. You shall certainly not take what belongs to others.

footnotes:
⁴¹ The laws of leaving part of the produce to the poor.
⁴² I.E.’s interpretation of I am the Lord your God


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:11:2)


וטעם **תגנובו.** כי הרואה ומחריש גם הוא גנב:


Scripture employs the plural *Ye shall not steal*, for the one who sees and is silent is also a thief.

**תכחשו.** בפקדון מופקד אצלך והיודע ואיננו מעיד גם הוא מכחש:


NEITHER SHALL YE DEAL FALSELY. This applies to something which has been deposited with you. The one who knows and does not testify also deals falsely.⁴³

NOR LIE. Seek money from one who does not owe you anything.⁴⁴

### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:12:1)


וטעם **ולא תשבעו בשמי לשקר.** אחר לא תגנובו כי החשוד בגנבה ופקדון ישבע. וטעם **תשבעו.** להכניס המשביע:


[AND YE SHALL NOT SWEAR BY MY NAME FALSELY.] This follows *Ye shall not steal*, for the one who is suspected of theft and dealing falsely with regard to a deposit swears.⁴⁵ Scripture employs the plural *ye shall not swear* because it wants to include the one who causes an oath to be taken.⁴⁶

⁴⁵ See Lev. 5:20-25.
⁴⁶ One is not permitted to concoct a story and force someone to take an unnecessary oath (Krinsky).

וטעם **וחללת.** שהנשבע לשקר מכחש השם כאשר פירשתיו:


Scripture reads, *so that thou profane the name of thy God* because, as I have noted,⁴⁷ the one who swears falsely denies God.

### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:1)


**לא תעשק את רעך.** בסתר:


THOU SHALT NOT OPPRESS THY NEIGHBOR. In secret.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:2)


**ולא תגזול.** בגלוי בחזקה:


NOR ROB HIM. Openly by the use of force.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:13:3)


**פעלת.** כמו שכירות וכן ופעולתו לפניו ויתכן היותו דרך קצרה והטעם שכר פעולתו ורבים פירשוהו על שכיר יום שיאמרו לו גם תעשה בבקר ובבקר אתן לך שכר שני ימים והמעתיקים אמרו כי הוא שכיר יום כי שכיר לילה לא תבוא עליו השמש.


THE WAGES. *Pe’ulat* means the wages of.⁴⁸ literally means labor of. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *Behold, His reward* (u-fe’ulato) *is with Him* (Is. 40:10). It is possible that Scripture is elliptical and that *pe’ulat* is short for *sekhar pe’ulato* (the wages of his labor). Many say that Scripture speaks of a person hired for the day.⁴⁹ One is not permitted to tell him to work the next morning and in the morning say I will pay you for two days’ work. The transmitters of tradition⁵⁰ 61a. say that our verse⁵¹ refers to someone hired for a day,⁵² for the sun is not permitted to set on a worker⁵³ who is hired for the night.⁵⁴

footnotes:
⁴⁸ The word pe’ulat
⁴⁹ According to this opinion a person hired for the day must be paid as soon as the day is over.
⁵⁰ See Bava Metzi’a
⁵¹ Which states that a worker must be paid before the morning.
⁵² According to the rabbis a person hired for the day must be paid before the arrival of the next morning.
⁵³ An unpaid worker.
⁵⁴ That is, the verse in Deut. 19:24 which states that a worker’s wages are to be paid before the sun sets speaks of a worker hired for the night; that is, the employer has the entire following day to pay him.

וכן **לא תקלל חרש** בעבור שיש לך כח. וכן ולפני עור:


[THOU SHALT NOT CURSE THE DEAF.] Because you have the power to do so.⁵⁵ You shall likewise not put a stumbling block before the blind.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:16:1)


**רכיל.** כמו רכולתך מכל אבקת רוכל והטעם המלשין כי הרוכל מעתיק יקנה מזה וימכור לזה והרכיל יגלה לזה מה ששמע מזה:


A TALEBEARER. The word *rakhil* (talebearer) is similar to *rekhulltekh* (thy merchandise) (Ezek. 26:12) and *rokhel* (merchant) in *With all powders of the merchant* (Cant. 3:6). *Rakhil* means a slanderer, for the merchant moves goods. He buys from one person and sells to another. The talebearer reveals to one person what he heard from another person.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:16:2)


**לא תעמד על דם רעך.** שלא יתחבר עם אנשי דמים וידוע כי כמה נרצחו ונהרגו בעבור המלשינות ודואג האדומי לעד:


NEITHER SHALT THOU STAND IDLY BY THE BLOOD OF THY NEIGHBOR. A person should not join bloodthirsty men.⁵⁸ It is known that many people have been murdered and killed because of slander.⁵⁹ follows the law prohibiting tale-bearing. Doeg the Edomite⁶⁰ of Nob, fed David. This resulted in the destruction of the city of Nob. See I Sam. 22:19. is proof of this.

⁵⁸ Literally, men of blood, persons who resort to violence to gain their end. See Prov. 29:10.
⁵⁹ Hence Neither shalt thou stand idly by the blood of thy neighbor
⁶⁰ Doeg told King Saul that Achimelech, the kohen

וטעם **אני ה׳‎.** רואה מה שאתה עושה בסתר:


The meaning of *I am the Lord* is, I see what you do in secret.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:17:1)


**לא תשנא את אחיך.** הפך ואהבת לרעך והנה אלה המצות כולם נטועות בלב ובהשמרם ישבו בארץ כי על שנאת חנם חרב בית שני:


THOU SHALT NOT HATE THY BROTHER IN THY HEART. This is the reverse of *but thou shalt love thy neighbor* (v. 18). Now all of these commandments are implanted in the heart.⁶¹, Chapter 5. Israel will dwell in the land when they observe them, for the second temple was destroyed because of hatred of one’s neighbor for no reason.⁶² 10:9.

⁶¹ They are rational laws. See I.E. on Ex. 20:1 (Vol. 1, pp. 407,408); Yesod Mora
⁶² Yoma


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:17:2)


**הוכח תוכיח.** שמא תחשדהו בדבר ולא היה כן וזה טעם ולא תשא עליו חטא כי עונש יהיה לך בעבורו:


THOU SHALT SURELY REBUKE THY NEIGHBOR. Perhaps you suspected him of something which was not so.⁶³ The meaning of *and not bear sin because of him* is that you be punished because of him.⁶⁴

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:1)


**לא תקם ולא תטר.** מפורש בדברי חז״ל:


THOU SHALT NOT TAKE VENGEANCE, NOR BEAR ANY GRUDGE. This is explained in the words of our rabbis of blessed memory.⁶⁵ 23a.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ See Rashi on this verse. “If one says to another, Lend me your sickle, and he replies, No. The next day the latter says to the former, Lend me your hatchet, and he replies, I am not going to lend it to you, just as you refused to lend me your sickle—this is taking vengeance…If one says to another, Lend me your sickle, and he replies, No! and the next day the latter says to the former, Lend me your hatchet, and he replies, Here it is; I am not like you, because you would not lend me—this is called bearing a grudge.” See also Yoma


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:2)


**ואהבת לרעך.** על דעת רבים שהלמ״ד נוסף כלמ״ד לאבנר ועל דעתי שהוא כמשמעו שיאהב הטוב לחברו כמו לנפשו:


BUT THOU SHALT LOVE THY NEIGHBOR. Many are of the opinion that the *lamed* of *le-re’akha*⁶⁶ literally means to thy neighbor. (thy neighbor) is superfluous.⁶⁷ literally, then our verse reads, but thou shalt love to thy neighbor as thyself. This does not appears to make sense. Hence this interpretation means that we should ignore the *lamed* prefixed to *re’akha* and interpret our verse as reading, but thou shalt love thy neighbor as thyself. It is like the *lamed* of *le-avner* (Abner) (II Sam. 3:30).⁶⁸ is to be interpreted as if written *avner*. I believe that *le-re’akha* is to be taken literally.⁶⁹ of *le-re’akha* (thy neighbor) is not superfluous. Its meaning is that one should love that which is good for one’s neighbor as he does for himself.⁷⁰ of *le-re’akha* as meaning “for.” He interprets our verse as follows: You shall love for your neighbor what you love for yourself (Krinsky). Weiser claims that I.E. interprets the *lamed* of *le-re’akha* as meaning “of.” He interprets our verse as follows: You shall love of your neighbor’s welfare as you love your own welfare.

footnotes:
⁶⁶ Le-re’akha
⁶⁷ If we take le-re’akha
⁶⁸ Le-avner
⁶⁹ That is, the lamed
⁷⁰ I. E. interprets the lamed


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:18:3)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎.** כי אני אלוה אחד בראתי אתכם:


The meaning of *I am the Lord* is, I the Lord alone created all of you.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:1)


וטעם להזכיר אחר אלה המצות **בהמתך לא תרביע כלאים.** להזהיר אחר היותך קדוש שלא תעשה חמס לבן אדם כמוך גם לא תעשה לבהמה לשנות מעשה השם על כן כתוב את חקתי תשמרו לשמור כל מין שלא יתערב מין עם מין:


[THOU SHALT NOT LET THY CATTLE GENDER WITH A DIVERSE KIND.] The reason Scripture states *Thou shalt not let thy cattle gender with a diverse kind* is to warn us. Since you are holy, in that you do not act violently towards your fellow human being, you must also not do anything to an animal which entails changing God’s work. Scripture therefore reads, *Ye shall keep My statutes*; that is, each species is to be preserved. A kind is not to interbreed with another kind.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:2)


**כלאים.** שני מינים ועוד אפרש מלת כלאים. וטעם השדה והבגד להיות לזכרון כי יש מצות רבות לזכר כחג המצות וסוכות וציצית ושופר ומזוזה ותפילין. ופה ארמוז לך סוד דע כי השלם שלם מאד על כן כתוב באברהם וישמור משמרתי מצותי חקותי ותורותי:


WITH A DIVERSE KIND. *Kilayim* (a diverse kind) means two kinds. I will yet explain the meaning of *kilayim*.⁷¹ The reason for the law regarding a field and a garment is that they serve as reminders,⁷² for many commandments serve as reminders. Examples of the latter are: the festival of unleavened bread,⁷³ Sukkot,⁷⁴ serve as a reminder of booths in which the Israelites dwelled during their sojourn in the desert (Lev. 23:43). *tzitzit*,⁷⁵ serve as a reminder of God’s commandments (Num. 15:39). *shofar*,⁷⁶ is to serve as a reminder, among them, God’s revelation, to repent. *mezuzah*,⁷⁷ and *tefilin*.⁷⁸ serves a reminder of the Exodus (Ex. 13:9) and of our obligation to study and to teach Torah to our children (Deut: 6:4-9; 13-21). See *Yesod Mora*, Chapter 5. I will hint to you here at a secret. Know that “the complete is very complete.”⁷⁹ Scripture therefore says with regard to Abraham,⁸⁰ *and kept My charge, My commandments, My statutes, and My laws* (Gen. 26:5).⁸¹

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:19:3)


**שעטנז.** המעתיקים אמרו שהיא שלש מלות או מחומשת ופירושה מעורב והוא שם לבדו במקרא:


OF TWO KINDS. The transmitters of tradition said that the word *shatnez* (two kinds) is made up of three words.⁸² is a combination of *shu’a* (hackled), *tavuy* (spun), and *nuz* (twined). It may come from a five-letter root.⁸³. The meaning of *shatnez* is “mixed.”⁸⁴ It is a noun which stands alone in Scripture.⁸⁵

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:1)


**והוא שפחה.** אמרו המכחישים כי איננה יהודית והנכון על דרך הפשט שהיא הנזכרת בפרשת וכי ימכור איש את בתו לאמה והיא ישראלית והטעם שהוא יעדה שיקחנה אדוניה או בנו ואיננה מאורסה וזה טעם כי לא חפשה איננה חפשית עד שתהיה מאורסה כמשפט הבנות:


A BONDMAID, DESIGNATED FOR A MAN. The deniers⁸⁶ say that the bondmaid is not a Jewess. However, the correct interpretation according to the plain meaning of the text is that the bondmaid is the one mentioned in *And if a man sell his daughter to be a maid-servant* (Ex. 21:7) and that she is an Israelite. Our verse deals with an instance in which the bondmaid was designated for marriage to her master or his son.⁸⁷ However, she had not undergone the first stage of marriage.⁸⁸. During the period of *erusin*, which lasted for a year, the bride remained with her family. In the second stage, called *nesu’im*, the bride went to live with her husband. The meaning of *nor was freedom given her* is that she is not free until she undergoes the first stage of marriage as is the practice of young ladies.

footnotes:
⁸⁶ The Karaites.
⁸⁷ See Ex. 21:8,9.
⁸⁸ In ancient times marriage was effectuated in two stages. The first stage was called erusin


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:2)


ומלת **נחרפת.** על דעת רבים יעודה ולפי דעתי שהמלה מגזרת חרפה בעבור היותה שפחה והיא בתולה ברשות אחר ואינה מאורשה:


Many believe that *necherefet* means designated. However, I believe that *necherefet* is related to the word *cherpah* (reproach) because she is an unmarried virgin maidservant under the control of a stranger.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:3)


**והפדה.** שם הפעל מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו מהכבד הנוסף והטעם שלא פדאה אביה או אחד ממשפחתה קודם היותה בוגרת:


REDEEMED. *Hofdeh* (redeemed) is an infinitive in the *hofal*. The meaning of *and not at all redeemed* is that her father or any other family member did not redeem her before she became a young woman.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:20:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:4)


**או חפשה.** השורק מקום קמץ חטף כי האחד חופש והטעם שהיה המנהג לכתוב לה גט חופש אם מכרה האב עד זמן קצוץ והמעתיקים אמרו שחציה חפשית וחציה אמה והוא האמת:


FREEDOM. The *shuruk*⁸⁹. in *chufshah* (freedom) is in place of a furtive *kamatz*, for the individual form of the word is *chofesh*.⁹⁰ are vocalized with a *kamatz katan* when declined. Compare, *kotel, kotlah*; *chodesh, chodshah*. Thus *chufshah* should have read *chofshah*, for the word *chufshah* (freedom) is the word *chofesh* plus the pronominal suffix. Scripture states, *nor was freedom given her*, because it was traditional to write a writ of manumission⁹¹ if her father sold her for a set time. The transmitters of tradition said that our verse speaks of a woman who was half free and half servant.⁹² 2:5. “What manner of bondwoman [does Scripture speak of in Lev. 19:20]? She that is half bondwoman and half freedwoman.” What they said is true.

⁸⁹ The kubbutz
⁹⁰ Nouns that are vocalized cholam, segol
⁹¹ When she was released from bondage.
⁹² See Keritot


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:20:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:20:5)


**בקרת תהיה.** יש אומרים שפירושו כמו פילגש ואמרו כי כן בנות מלכים ביקרותיך ולא פרשו נכון כי ביקרותיך טעמו השפחות היקרות ונעלם היו״ד בעבור האות המשרת בחיר״ק והיה בחיר״ק בעבור התחברות שני שואי״ן נעים האחד תחת האות המשרת והשני תחת האות הראשון של השרש בלשון נקבות ואין כח בלשון להזכיר ככה וכן משפט כל הלשון ואין מלה זרה חוץ ממלת ורבים מישני אדמת עפר יקיצו על קריאת בן נפתלי. ויש אומרים בקורת מגזרת לא יבקר הכהן והטעם שיחפש הדבר והמעתיקים אמרו כי יש עליו מלקות ברצועה של בקר וזה אמת מדברי קבלה והמלה כדמות אסמכתא. וטעם להזכיר זאת הפרשה כי הוא עושק אדני השפחה על כן יביא איל אשם ועוד על דרך דרש שזאת השכיבה כמו כלאים חפשי עם שפחה:


THERE SHALL BE AN INQUISITION. Some say that *bikkoret* (inquisition) means a concubine.⁹³ (there shall be an inquisition) as she shall be a concubine; that is, she shall not be considered a fully married woman. They say that the same applies to the word *biykkrotekha* (among thy favorites) in *Kings’ daughters are among thy favorites* (Ps. 45:10).⁹⁴ However, they did not explain it correctly, for the meaning of *biykkrotekha* is, among the precious handmaidens.⁹⁵ is the word *yikkrotekha* (your precious ones), i.e., your precious maidens plus the *bet* prefix. There is no connection between *bikkoret* and *biykkrotekha*, for the former comes from the root *bet, kof, resh* and the latter from the root *yod, kof, resh*. The *yod*⁹⁶. is missing⁹⁷ because the *bet*, which serves as a preposition, is vocalized with a *chirik*.⁹⁸ of *yikkrotekha* is swallowed up by the *chirik*. It is vocalized with a *chirik* because of the confluence of two mobile *shevas*,⁹⁹ is a combination of the prefix *be* plus the word *yekkrotekha*. In such cases the prefix is vocalized with a *chirik* and the *yod* is not sounded. the first beneath the prefix¹⁰⁰. and the second beneath the first root letter of the word *yikkrotekha*, which is in the feminine.¹⁰¹. The tongue does not have the power to enunciate two mobile *shevas* in succession.¹⁰² back to back. The same is true for the entire language.¹⁰³. It applies to all cases where we have two furtive *shevas* in succession. There is no exception to this except for the word *mi-yeshene* (that sleep) in *And many of them that sleep in the dust* (Dan. 12:2),¹⁰⁴ should have read *miyyshene*. according to the reading of Ben Naphtali.¹⁰⁵ There are those who say that *bikkoret* is related to the word *yevakker* (seek) in *shall not seek* (Lev. 13:36). The meaning of *bikkoret*¹⁰⁶ is that the matter shall be searched out. The transmitters of tradition say he¹⁰⁷ 11a it is the bondwoman who is lashed. Hence some emend *alav* to *aleha*. incurs the penalty of being lashed with a strap made from the skin of cattle (*bakar*).¹⁰⁸ 3:12. This interpretation renders *bikkoret* as cattled, i.e., lashed with a strap made of cattle hide. In our texts of Rabbinic literature there is no such interpretation. Radak quotes this interpretation in the name of Rabbi Saadiah Gaon. See Weiser. This is true by way of tradition.¹⁰⁹ The word *bikkoret* is used as a support for the tradition.¹¹⁰ is not “lashed by cattle skin.” This section is placed here because the one who sleeps with the bondwoman oppresses the master of the bondwoman.¹¹¹ He therefore brings a ram as a guilt offering.¹¹² Furthermore, this type of intercourse can be interpreted midrashically as mixing diverse kinds, namely, a free man and a bondwoman.¹¹³

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:22:1)


**ונסלח לו.** אפרשנו עוד:


AND HE SHALL BE FORGIVEN.¹¹⁴. I will explain this later.¹¹⁵ is that punishment shall be delayed until the person fully repents. See Volume 4, p. 110. The reason Scripture now discusses forbidden fruit is that Scripture had previously mentioned the seed of the field¹¹⁶ and the seed of the woman,¹¹⁷ who is likened to the ground.¹¹⁸ Scripture therefore¹¹⁹ now mentions that which is planted. It is known that fruit which grows before three years have passed is of no value and is harmful to the body. It is like all fish that have no fins and scales that are injurious to the body,0 and like all flesh of birds of prey and unclean animals that harm the wise soul. The intelligent will understand this.¹²⁰

### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:23:1)


וטעם להזכיר ערלת הפרי. בעבור שהזכיר זרע השדה וזרע האשה שהיא כארץ הזכיר גם הנטוע וידוע כי הפרי הבא עד שלש שנים אין בו תועלת ומזיק כאשר יזיק לגוף כל דג שאין לו סנפיר וקשקשת ויזיק לנפש החכמה בשר כל עוף דורס והבהמות הטמאות והמשכיל יבין:


[THEN YE SHALL COUNT THE FRUIT THEREOF AS FORBIDDEN.] The meaning of *ve-areltem et orlato* (then ye shall count as forbidden) is that the fruit is counted as a foreskin¹²¹ as, and you shall consider its fruit as foreskin. *Orlah* means a foreskin. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. which is harmful and serves no purpose, such as the “foreskin” of the lips,¹²² the ear,¹²³ and the foreskin of the penis.

וטעם **וערלתם ערלתו.** שאותו הפרי הוא חשוב כערלה שהיא מזקת ולא תועיל כערלת שפה ואוזן וערלת בשר:


The meaning of *ve-areltem* (then ye shall count) is, you shall consider it as a foreskin. It is also beautifully explained in the Aramaic translation.¹²⁴, you shall surely distance.

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:25:1)


וטעם **להוסיף לכם תבואתו.** דבק עם קדש הלולים ואם הוא רחוק וכן שלם ישלם ואם אין לו דבוק עם חמשה בקר ישלם תחת השור ורבים כמוהם:


[THAT IT MAY YIELD UNTO YOU MORE RICHLY THE INCREASE THEREOF.] *That it may yield unto you more richly the increase thereof is* connected to *shall be holy, for giving praise* (v. 24) even though it is some distance from it.¹²⁸ is connected to *shall be holy, for giving praise* even though it does not immediately follow it. *If he have nothing, then he shall be sold for his theft* (Ex. 22:2) is similarly connected to *he shall pay five oxen for an ox* (Ex. 21:37). There are many other similar instances.

### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:26:1)


**לא תאכלו על הדם.** דבוק עם הכתוב למעלה כי הזהיר על עץ נטוע שלא יאכלו מפריו עד השנה החמישית גם כן כל בשר טהור לא יאכל עד שיזרוק דמו על מזבח השם אם היה קרוב אל מקום הקדש והעד הנאמן דברי שאול כי הארון היה עמו כי כן כתוב הנה אוכלים על הדם כאילו אוכלים זבחים לשעירים כאשר פירשתי שהיה כן מנהגם במצרים לזבוח בשם השדים כי אחר שלא נזרק הדם על המזבח לשם השם הנה הדבר ברור על כן דבק עמו לא תנחשו כי במצרים היו אוכלים על הדם וזונים אחרי השדים ובארץ כנען מנחשים ומעוננים כי כן כתוב גם זה כמעשה ארץ מצרים וכמעשה ארץ כנען. ומלת מנחש מנסה כמו נחשתי ויברכני ה׳‎ כי נחוש בצורות ובמקלות ובמעשים ובתנועות ובימים ושעות:


YE SHALL NOT EAT WITH THE BLOOD. This is connected¹²⁹ to what is written above it.¹³⁰ Scripture warned against eating from the fruit of any planted tree until the fifth year. It similarly also prohibited one, if he is in the proximity of the sanctuary, to eat from any permitted flesh unless its blood first be sprinkled on the altar of God. The account of King Saul is a faithful witness to this interpretation, for the ark of the Lord was with Saul, and Scripture explicitly states, “Behold, the people are eating with the blood.”¹³¹. As I explained,¹³² it was as if the people were sacrificing to the demons, for this was their custom in Egypt. There they were accustomed to sacrifice in the name of the demons. Now this is clear,¹³³ is clear (Krinsky). since the blood was not poured on the altar in the worship of God. *Neither shall ye practice divination* is connected to *ye shall not eat with the blood* because they used to eat with the blood and go astray after the demons in Egypt, and they used to practice divination and soothsaying in Canaan. The aforementioned is clearly stated in Scripture.¹³⁴ This too was like the *doings of the land of Egypt;*…and…*the doings of the land of Canaan* (Lev. 18:3). The word *menachesh* means divines. Compare, *I have observed the signs* (nichashti), *And the Lord hath blessed me* (Gen. 30:27). Divinations take many forms. There are types of divination that employs images,¹³⁵ sticks,¹³⁶ actions,¹³⁷ movements,¹³⁸ days,¹³⁹ and hours.¹⁴⁰

footnotes:
¹²⁹ That is, related.
¹³⁰ To the law which precedes it.
¹³¹ See I Sam. 14:33. The exact reading of the latter is, Behold, the people sin against the Lord, in that they eat with the blood
¹³² See I.E. on Lev. 17:7.
¹³³ That the animal was sacrificed to the demons (Weiser). Or I.E.’s interpretation of Ye shall not eat with the blood
¹³⁴ See Deut. 18:10-14.
¹³⁵ Making predictions based on the images drawn on cards (Krinsky).
¹³⁶ Such as making predictions based on the way sticks fall.
¹³⁷ Making predictions based on whom a person meets or what happens to something a person owns, i.e., a black cat crossing one’s path or a mirror breaking.
¹³⁸ Bodily movements.
¹³⁹ Lucky days.
¹⁴⁰ Lucky hours.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:26:2)


**תעוננו.** יש אומרים מלשון ענה שיחשוב בלבו דבר ויטה אזניו לשמוע מה יענה המדבר ולא אבה הדקדוק ויש אומרים מעונן לשון ועונתה לא יגרע והנכון בעיני מגזרת ענן כי ידוע כי יש מי שיעונן שיסתכל בעננים ובדמותם ובתנועתם ויתכן להיות תעוננו מפעלי הכפל כמו תסובבו כי מצאנו ועוננים כפלשתים כי יתכן היותו מבנין הכפול והעד ומעונן ומנחש:


SOOTHSAYING. Some say that the word *te’onennu* (soothsaying) is related to the word *anah* (answer). The soothsayer imagines something in his heart and inclines his ear to hear what the speaker will answer.¹⁴¹ means “and do not practice answering.” However, the rule of grammar rejects this interpretation.¹⁴² (answer) comes from the root *ayin, nun, heh*. Verbs from this root do not double their middle stem. There are those who say that *te’onennu* comes from the word *onah* (time). It is similar to the word *onatah* (her conjugal rights)¹⁴³ in *and her conjugal rights, shall he not diminish* (Ex. 21:10).¹⁴⁴ is, and do not set times; that is, do not divine by setting aside lucky days. However, it appears to me that *te’onennu* is related to the word *anan* (cloud), for it is known that the one who practices divination looks at the clouds and observes their forms and their movements. *Te’onennu* probably comes from a verb with a doubled root.¹⁴⁵. It is like the word *tesovevu* (you will go round about),¹⁴⁶. for we find, *And with soothsayers* (onenim) *like the Philistines* (Is. 2:6).¹⁴⁷ is a *kal*. There is no question that *onenim* comes from the root *ayin, nun, nun*. [I note this] for it is possible that *te’onennu* is a doubled form.¹⁴⁸. A verb which has a *vav* as it middle letter has its last stem letter doubled in the *pi’el*. In other words, the root of *te’onennu* is *ayin, vav, nun*. The last stem letter is doubled in the *pi’el*. Compare, *te-komem* from *kof, vav, mem*. *Me’onen* (one that uses divination) *u-menachesh* (Deut. 18:9) is proof.

footnotes:
¹⁴¹ The practitioner of this type of divination has a question in mind. He does not reveal the question to the person whom he is speaking to. However, he divines an answer to his question from what the person with whom he is conversing says. According to this interpretation ve-lo te’onennu
¹⁴² Anah
¹⁴³ The time set aside for conjugal rights.
¹⁴⁴ The meaning of ve-lo te’onennu
¹⁴⁵ Its root is ayin, nun, nun
¹⁴⁶ Which comes from the root samekh, bet, bet
¹⁴⁷ Onenim
¹⁴⁸ A word from the root ayin, vav, nun



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:27:1)


וטעם להזכיר **לא תקפו פאת ראשכם.** כמעשה הגוים להיות מובדלים מהם ואחר ששער הראש והזקן לתפארת נברא אין ראוי להשחיתו ויש אומרים כי זה הפסוק דבק עם ושרט לנפש כי יש מי שישחית פאת ראשו גם פאת זקן בעבור המת:


[YE SHALL NOT ROUND THE CORNERS OF YOUR HEADS.] Scripture states, *Ye shall not round the corners of your heads* as the non-Jewish nations do, so that you will be separated from them. Now since the hair on the head and the beard serve to glorify a person it is unfit to destroy them. Some say that this verse is connected to *Ye shall not make any cuttings in your flesh for the dead* (v. 28), for there are people who destroy the hair on the corner of their heads and also on the corner of their beards for the dead.



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:1)


**ושרט.** גם הפאה בראש ובזקן ידועים מדברי קבלה:


CUTTINGS. We know the meaning of the corners of the head and of the beard from the words of tradition.¹⁴⁹ 32b.

footnotes:
¹⁴⁹ See Kiddushin


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:2)


ומלת **לנפש.** הגוף המת וכן מתורגם והוא האמת ולא נדגש הנו״ן להקל על הלשון:


The word *le-nefesh* means for a dead body. It is also so rendered in the Aramaic translation.¹⁵⁰. This is its true meaning. The *nun*¹⁵¹. does not receive a *dagesh*¹⁵², or *kamatz*. If it is vocalized with *a pattach*, the letter which follows it receives a *dagesh*. I.E. explains that Scripture chose to vocalize *la-nefesh* with a *kamatz* to simplify enunciation (Weiser). However, it should be noted that if the *lamed* of *la-nefesh* was vocalized with a *sheva*, then the *nun* would also not receive a *dagesh*. Thus I.E.’s interpretation does not explain why the *lamed* of *la-nefesh* was not vocalized with a *sheva*. It is possible that in I.E.’s version of the Torah *la-nefesh* was vocalized with a *pattach* and that I.E. is explaining why, contrary to the laws of grammar, there is no *dagesh* in the *nun* of *la-nefesh*. in order to simplify enunciation.

footnotes:
¹⁵⁰ Onkelos renders la-nefesh, al mit
¹⁵¹ Of la-nefesh
¹⁵² A preposition prefixed to a stressed syllable of a noun can be vocalized with a sheva, pattach


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:28:3)


**וכתבת קעקע.** יש אומרים שהוא דבק עם ושרט לנפש כי יש מי שירשום גופו בצורה הידועה באש על המת ויש עוד היום רושמים בנערותם בפניהם להיות נכרים. ומלת **קעקע.** כפולה כמו רוקע הארץ וצאצאיה והוא מגזרת והוקע אותם ועל דעת המתרגם גם היא מלה זרה גם הוא הנכון:


IMPRINT ANY MARKS. Some say that this is connected to *Ye shall not make any cuttings in your flesh*, for there are people who, employing fire, mark their bodies with a recognizable form for the dead.¹⁵³ Even today there are those who, while young, mark their faces so that they [may] be recognized.¹⁵⁴ The word *ka’aka* (marks) is doubled.¹⁵⁵ and *ayin* root letters are doubled. Compare, *He that spread forth* the *earth and that which cometh out of it* (ve-tze’etza’ehah)¹⁵⁶ and *alef* of the root *yod, tzadi, alef* are doubled in *ve-tze’etza’ehah* (Is. 42:5). *Ka’aka* is related to the word *ve-hoka* (and hang)¹⁵⁷. in *and hang them up* (Num. 25:4).¹⁵⁸ ] is a writing dug into the flesh. *Ka’aka* is related to *ve-hoka* in *and hang them up* (Num. 25:4)…” They were hanged upon poles which were driven into the ground. According to the Aramaic translator, it is also an irregular word. He is also correct [in translating *ka’aka* as engraved].

footnotes:
¹⁵³ They brand an image of the deceased into their flesh (Weiser).
¹⁵⁴ This is still practiced among certain Arab tribes (Weiser).
¹⁵⁵ The kof
¹⁵⁶ The tzadi
¹⁵⁷ From the root resh, kof, ayin
¹⁵⁸ So too Rashi. “[ka’aka



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:29:1)


וטעם להזכיר **אל תחלל את בתך.** בעבור שרט לנפש שלא תתגלה לעיני הכל כי קול שבאשה ערוה ואף כי שרט:


[PROFANE NOT THY DAUGHTER.] Scripture now mentions *profane not thy daughter* because it earlier stated *Ye shall not make any cuttings in your flesh* (v. 28). The import of our verse is, your daughter shall not be displayed before the public.¹⁵⁹ Now the voice of a woman is like her nakedness.¹⁶⁰ 24a. A cutting is certainly so.¹⁶¹

footnotes:
¹⁵⁹ Your daughter shall not make markings upon her flesh for the dead because she may expose the place of the mark to show that she cut her flesh as a sign of mourning.
¹⁶⁰ Berachot
¹⁶¹ If it is prohibited to hear a women’s voice, it is certainly prohibited to look upon any part of her body that is exposed.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:29:2)


**ולא תזנה הארץ.** הטעם זנות אנשי הארץ כמו ארץ כי תחטא לי:


LEST THE LAND FALL INTO HARLOTRY. The reference is to the harlotry of the people of the land.¹⁶² is short for lest the people of the land. Compare, *when a land sinneth against Me* (Ezek. 14:13).¹⁶³

footnotes:
¹⁶² In other words, lest the land
¹⁶³ The meaning of which is, when the people of the land.



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:1)


וטעם להזכיר **את שבתתי תשמרו.** בעבור המת שאין אבל בשבת:


[YE SHALL KEEP MY SABBATHS.] Scripture now says, *Ye shall keep My sabbaths*, because it earlier¹⁶⁴ dealt with the dead, for there is no mourning on the Sabbath.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁴ In verse 28.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:2)


וטעם **ומקדשי תיראו.** על הכהן הגדול שלא יהיה כשאר האבלים וכן אזהרה על כל אנשי המקדש:


*And reverence My sanctuary* is to be understood as referring to the *kohen gadol;* he shall not act as other mourners do.¹⁶⁵ is not to observe mourning customs. See Lev. 21:10-12. There is a similar prohibition upon all the people of the sanctuary.¹⁶⁶ applies to all those who serve in the sanctuary.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁵ The kohen gadol
¹⁶⁶ And reverence My sanctuary


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:30:3)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎.** שאם לא ייראו ממקדשו ויתנו לו כבוד יענישם:


The meaning of *I am the Lord* is, God will punish you if you do not revere the sanctuary and respect it. Scripture similarly next mentions *the ghosts* and *the familiar spirits* (v. 31) because of the dead.¹⁶⁷ It similarly writes in Isaiah *on behalf of the living unto the dead* (Is. 8:19).¹⁶⁸; i.e., instead of seeking ghosts and familiar spirits and the dead concerning the welfare and future of the living, a people should inquire of God.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁷ Scripture earlier spoke of the dead. It now tells us that we are not to inquire of the dead by employing a ghost or a familiar spirit.
¹⁶⁸ The full verse reads: And when they say unto you: Seek unto the ghosts and the familiar spirits, that chirp and that mutter; should not a people seek unto their God? On behalf of the living unto the dead



### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:1)


וטעם להזכיר האובות וידעונים. בעבור המת וכן כתוב בישעיה בעד החיים אל המתים. **האבת.** מגזרת וכאובות חדשים כי הם עיקר זאת האומנות:


THE GHOSTS. *Ovot* (ghosts) is related to the word *ovot* (wineskins) in *Like new wine-skins* (Job 32:19), for wineskins are the main instruments in this “art.”¹⁶⁹

footnotes:
¹⁶⁹ The practitioners of this “art” use wineskins to make ghost-like sounds.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:2)


**והידענים.** מגזרת דעת שיבקשו לדעת העתידות וריקי מוח אמרו לולי שהאובות אמת גם כן דרך הכשוף לא אסרם הכתוב ואני אומר הפך דבריהם כי הכתוב לא אסר האמת רק השקר והעד האלילים הפסילים ולולי שאין רצוני להאריך הייתי מבאר דבר בעלת אוב בראיות גמורות:


NOR UNTO FAMILIAR SPIRITS. *Yidonim* (familiar spirits) is related to the word *da’at* (knowledge). Those who turn to *yidonim* seek to know the future. The empty heads say if there were no truth in the *ovot* (ghosts) and in various magical practices, then Scripture would not have prohibited them. However, I say the reverse. Scripture would not have permitted that which was true. It only prohibited that which is false. *The idols* (elilim)¹⁷⁰ means something which does not exist. See I.E. on verse 4. and *the images* (v. 4) are proof of this. Were it not that I do not want to go on at length, I would have explained the incident of the woman who was an expert in divining by ghosts.¹⁷¹ I would have brought unquestionable proof to my explanation.

footnotes:
¹⁷⁰ According to I.E. elilim
¹⁷¹ The witch of En-dor. See I Sam 28:15. According to I.E the witch did not actually bring up the prophet Samuel. She employed trickery. See I.E. on Ex. 20:3 (Vol. 2, p. 418, and the notes thereto).

וטעם **אל תפנו.** ליודע האומנות:


The meaning of *Turn ye not* is, do not turn to the one who knows the art.¹⁷²

¹⁷² Of communicating with the dead.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:4)


וטעם **אל תבקשו.** לשאול כשאול:


The meaning of *seek them not out* is, do not inquire of them as king Saul did.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:31:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:31:5)


וטעם **אני ה׳‎ אלהיכם.** שלא תבקשו כי אם השם לבדו והנה הזכיר לטמאה כי נפש הפונה והמבקש טמאה היא כי איננה דבקה בשם:


The meaning of *I am the Lord your God* is, you shall not seek except for the Lord alone. Note, Scripture reads, *to be defiled by them*, because the soul of the one who inquires of a ghost or a familiar spirit is defiled, for it does not cleave to the Lord.



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:32:1)

[THOU SHALT RISE UP BEFORE THE HOARY HEAD.] Scripture mentions the hoary head because it earlier mentioned the dead¹⁷³ and the aged are close to death, for the bodies of the old are considered dead.¹⁷⁴ Note, our verse refers to all the old and to every hoary head.¹⁷⁵ 32b. Also see Rashi.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:33:1

**וכי יגור אתך גר.** הזכירו אחר הזקן והטעם כאשר הזהרתיך לכבד הזקן הישראלי בעבור שאין לו כח כך אזהירך על הגר שכחך גדול מכחו או בעבור שאין לו כח שהוא בארצך ברשותך:


AND IF A STRANGER SOUJORN WITH THEE. Scripture mentions the stranger after the old man. It does so to, as it were, to say, as I warned you to respect the old Israelite because he has no strength, I warn you not to wrong the foreigner because you are stronger than he is, or who has no power because he is in your country and subject to you.¹⁷⁷

### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 19:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/19:35:1)


וטעם **לא תעשו עול.** בעבור הגר כטעם ושפטתם צדק בין איש ובין אחיו ובין גרו וכן במדה שלא תעשה מרמה במדת האמות גם האמות משתנות ויתכן להיות פירוש כמשפט הידוע בארץ:


[YE SHALL DO NO UNRIGHTEOUSNESS.] Scripture states, *Ye shall do no unrighteousness* because it earlier dealt with the stranger. JUST BALANCES, JUST WEIGHT. *Avne tzedek*¹⁸¹ means just weights.

A JUST EPHAH. For that which is dry.
