### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:4:1)


ואחר כן הזכיר המועדים ואמר בשבת אלה הם מועדי ובמועדים אמר במועדם והטעם באיזה יום שיהיה מהשבוע והנה אני נותן לך כלל בדברי המועדים כי בזמן שבית המקדש קיים היו המועדים נמסרים לבית דין הלא תראה כי אמר על פסח חזקיהו ויועץ המלך ואחר שקבע ראש חודש ניסן עבר השנה ובזה תפשוהו חכמינו ז״ל כי היה ראוי שלא יעבר ניסן בניסן והמעתיקים אמרו כי בית דין היה מסתכל בדברים רבים בקביעות השנה ואמרו על רבי עקיבא שקבע שתי שנים מעוברות שנה אחר שנה לפי צורך השעה ואין בכל המקרא ראיה איך היו ישראל קובעים החדשים והמועדים ומה שאמר הגאון כי על חשבון העיבור היו נסמכים איננו אמת כי במשנה גם בתלמוד ראיות שהיה פסח בבד״ו גם שני מעשיות שם ועבור השנים קרוב מתוספת שנת החמה על הלבנה ואיננו על דרך הקדמונים ותחלת החשבון וי״ד פי׳‎ שים עם לבהר״ד ד״ח תתע״ו יבא המולד וי״ד כי שנה נוספה להחל מחשבון בהר״ד בעבור חמשה הימים שהיו קודם בריאת האדם ויום בשנה חשוב שנה וזה לא יזיק על כן לא תוכל לקחת תוספת שנה נוספה כי לא היתה והנה העבור אחר שנה וחצי שנה והעבור בנוי על המהלך התיכון על כן אמרו פעמים שבא בארוכה ובקצרה ואין הקביעות רודף אחר ראית הלבנה בירושלים או בקצה מזרח או במערב כלל והעד מקום המחברת התיכונה כי הנה היא על ירושלים ועוד שראינו פעמים רבות הלבנה בחדש ניסן בליל שני וכן היתה נראית בכל העולם וקביעות החדש היה ליל שלישי וכן אירע לשלשה חדשים קודם ניסן וזה נתקן בשנת גטר״ד בעבור הדחיה גם פעמים היה הקביעות בתשרי יום חמישי ולא נראית הלבנה בליל שבת והיה האויר זך וזה יקרה בכל שנה שהמולד קרוב מחצי היום והיתה הלבנה בחצי גלגל הגבוה גם אין הקביעות בנוי על עת התחברות המאורות אפילו במהלך האמצעי כי הנה בטור״ד גם גטר״ד יוכיחו ולולי שלא אאריך הייתי מפרש סוד העיבור וסוד ההלכה החמורה שהוא נולד קודם חצות והכלל שחז״ל העתיקו לנו שנסמוך על העיבור בגלות וכן קבלנו מפי נביאים ולא נוכל לעשות דבר אחר טוב ממנו וזה ששמו ב׳‎ ימים בגלות והוא על דבר ספק והמתענים יום הכפורים שני ימים מה יועילם בעבור הדחיות כי בשנת גטר״ד אם על המולד היה ראוי שיתענה בשמונה לקביעתנו וכי למה נעוות אנחנו חשבון שנתנו בעבור שנה אחרת וכן יקרה להם עם הראיה:


Scripture then mentions the festivals (v. 4). The Torah says, *even these are My appointed seasons*, with regard to the Sabbath and *in their appointed season* with regard to the festivals. The meaning of *in their appointed season* is, in any day of the week in which they fall. Note, I will now lay down a general rule with regard to the festivals. During the time of the Temple the festivals were in the hands of the Beth Din. Do you not see that Scripture says, *For the king had taken counsel* (II Chron. 30:2), with regard to the Passover proclaimed by Hezekiah.³ After Hezekiah established the new moon of Nisan he proclaimed a leap year.⁴ Our sages, of blessed memory, criticized him for this, for he should not have added a Nisan on Nisan.⁵ 10b. The transmitters of tradition said that the Beth Din took many things into consideration in estabhshing the year.⁶ 11a,b. The Bet Din had to consider many things in deciding whether or not to make a year a leap year. They said, regarding Rabbi Akiba, that he established two leap years back to back according to the needs of the hour.⁷ 12a. However, the text there speaks of three years. Hence Krinsky suggests emending I.E. to read three years. *Vat. Ebr.* 38 omits the word two. It reads, “He established leap years back to back.” There is no proof in Scripture as to how Israel established the months and the festivals. The Gaon’s statement to the effect that they relied on the intercalational calculation⁸ is not true, for there are proofs in the Mishnah and also in the Talmud that Passover fell on a Monday, Wednesday, and Friday.⁹ There are also two stories in the Talmud [which show that they did not rely on a fixed calendar].¹⁰ 2:9 records a controversy between Rabban Gamliel and Rabbi Joshua as to when Yom Kippur fell in a certain year. If the rabbis of the Mishnah had used a fixed calendar there would be no place for this difference of opinion. The Mishnah (*Ibid.* 2:8) also reports an instance of two sets of witnesses contradicting themselves as to the position of the new moon. The latter shows that the month was established by actually seeing the new moon and not by calculation. See Weiser and Krinsky. Leap years¹¹ come close to compensating for the additional days which a solar year has over a lunar year.¹² The latter does not follow the way of the ancients.¹³ The calculation¹⁴, or *molad baharad*, which is the Hebrew abbreviation for five hours and 204 parts of an hour of the second day of the week. How do we arrive at this calculation? Ibn Ezra explains that we do so by counting back a year from the conjunction which occurred on the day that Adam was born. It should be noted that that year, except for the five days preceding the birth of Adam, did not exist in reality. It was a theoretical year. starts with the end of 14 hours of the sixth day;¹⁵ that is, add four days, eight hours, and 876 parts¹⁶, 6:4,5. to five hours and 204 parts of an hour of the second day of the week.¹⁷ Then the birth of the new moon will occur at the end of 14 hours of the sixth day.¹⁸ A year is added,¹⁹ for we start counting from five hours and 204 parts of an hour of the second day of the week²⁰ because of the five days which preceded the creation of Adam, for a day into a year is considered a year.²¹ This presents no problem.²² You cannot add an additional year,²³ for it did not yet exist. Note, an additional month was added to the year after a year and a half.²⁴ (or *yetizrah*, creation), the year in which Adam was born (*molad adam*), and a half of the following year. The intercalation is based on the average²⁵, p. 123). Also see Maimonides, *M.T., Laws of the Sanctification of the New Moon*, 6:1. The length of a junction varies. Its average time is 29 days, 12 hours, 44 minutes, and 3 and 1/3 seconds. The average movement (or conjunction) stands in contrast to the true conjunction of the sun and moon. movement of the moon.²⁶ The sages therefore said that sometimes the birth of the moon comes at longer intervals; at times it comes at shorter intervals.²⁷ 25a. The establishment of the month²⁸ is not tied to the sighting of the moon in Jerusalem,²⁹ or in the far east,³⁰ by stating that “the earth is round like a sphere. The water of the Great Ocean covers half of it, which is uninhabitable, the other half is dry and habitable” (W.M. Feldman, *Rabbinical Mathematics and Astronomy*, p. 104). or in the west [of the world].³¹ The place of the average conjunction of the moon³² is proof of this,³³ for look, it is reckoned when occurring over Jerusalem.³⁴ Furthermore, we have seen the moon many times on the second night of the month of Nisan. It was similarly seen in the entire world. However, the month was established on the third night [of the week].³⁵ The same thing happened in the three months preceding Nisan. This occurs when the birth of the new moon of Tishri occurs at nine hours and 204 parts of an hour of the third day [of the week].³⁶ It was enacted³⁷ because of the postponement.³⁸. “There are five separate occasions which necessitate the postponement of New Year’s day by one or even two days,” Feldman, *op. cit*, p. 191. For these *dechiyot*, see Maimonides, *M.T., Laws of the Sanctification of the New Moon*, Chap. 7. Also see Feldman, pp. 191-194. There are also times when the month of Tishri was established on the fifth day [of the week] and the moon was not seen on the night of the Sabbath. Now, the air was then clear. This occurs every year when the birth of the new moon³⁹ is close to half of the day⁴⁰ (*yod chet* = 18), i.e., the 18th hour. “If the *molad* of Tishri occurs at or past noon (i.e., the 18th hour of any day. Noon is the 18th hour of a day which consists of 12 hours of daylight and 12 hours of night. Such a day begins at 6:00 P.M. New Year (Rosh Ha-Shanah) is postponed to the following day. Such a *molad* is called an Old *Molad* (*molad zaken*) because on the day of the new month, the moon is already old” (Feldman, pp. 191,192). and the moon is in half of its sphere’s apogee. The establishment⁴¹ is also not dependent on the time of the conjunction⁴² of the sun and moon. It is not even dependent on their mean conjunction, for note, the conjunction⁴³ of the sun and moon at 15 hours and 599 parts⁴⁴ of the hour on the second day⁴⁵ (*bet, tet-vav, tav-kof, peh-tet*), which stands for two days, 15 hours, 599 parts. and similarly the conjunction at nine hours and 204 parts of the third day⁴⁶ (*gimel-tet, resh-dalet*), which stands for three days, nine hours, 204 parts. is proof of this.⁴⁷ If I did not want to go on at length I would explain the secret of the calculation of the calendar and the secret of the very difficult law, i.e., the rule regarding a moon born before midday.⁴⁸ 20b. The general principle is: our wise men, of blessed memory, transmitted to us the rule that we are to rely on the intercalated calendar in exile. We received the same from the mouth of the prophets. We cannot do anything better than it. The sages ruled that the festivals be observed for two days in the Diaspora because of uncertainty.⁴⁹ Those who fast for two days in observance of Yom Kippur⁵⁰ fast in vain⁵¹, pp. 11,12. due to the “postponements.” For in a year when the birth of the new moon⁵² occurs at nine hours and 204 parts of the hour on the third day of the week, they should fast on what is the eighth day of the month according to our calculation if they rely on the conjunction.⁵³ Why⁵⁴ manipulate the date of the start of one year⁵⁵ because of another year?⁵⁶ 7:4. Those who fast for two days believe that our festivals should be based on astronomical reality. If that is the case, then all the rules of establishing the calendar should be discarded. This would happen to them⁵⁷ if the month is established by seeing the new moon.⁵⁸

³ Hezekiah put off the observance of Passover for a month. According to I.E., Hezekiah’s actions were based on a ruling of the Bet Din. See Vol. 1, pp. 9,10.
⁴ And proclaimed that Passover be observed in the next month.
⁵ See Berakhot
⁶ See Sanhedrin
⁷ San.
⁸ The rules governing the establishment of the fixed Jewish calendar.
⁹ Which cannot occur under the rules of the fixed Jewish calendar.
¹⁰ The Mishnah, Rosh Ha-Shanah
¹¹ Of which there are seven in an 19 year cycle.
¹² The solar year is about ten days longer than the lunar year. The extra month added during the leap year comes very close to closing this gap.
¹³ Who established the new moon visually, not by calculation.
¹⁴ Of the fixed calendar. What follows is a bit complicated. Hence a few words are in order. The Jewish months begin with the conjunction of the sun and moon. The calculation starts with the very first conjunction. That conjunction theoretically occurred at the end of five hours and 204 parts of an hour of the second day of the week (the rabbis divided an hour into 1,080 parts). The latter is known as molad tohu
¹⁵ Adam was born on Friday. That day was the first day of the month of Tishri. According to the rabbis the moon was then in conjunction with the sun at the end of 14 hours of the day.
¹⁶ The days between the start of one simple lunar year to the next lunar year. See Maimonides, M.T., Laws of the Sanctification of the New Moon
¹⁷ There were five days preceding the birth of Adam. Those days belonged to the previous year. In that year the conjunction of the sun and the moon (had they existed) would have taken place at five hours plus 204 parts of on hour of the second day. The calculation is as follows. A lunar month consists of 29 days, 12 hours, 44 minutes, and 3 1/3 seconds. A year consists of 12 months. If we work backwards from the birth of Adam, who was born on a Friday, a day which we mark as the New Year, then the previous New Year which came 12 months earlier had to fall on a Monday. For 12 times 29 1/2 days and 44 minutes comes to 50 weeks plus four days and some five hours.
¹⁸ Adam was born on a Friday. The day of Adam’s birth started a new year.
¹⁹ To the year which started with the birth of Adam.
²⁰ Of the “year” which preceded the birth of Adam.
²¹ The five days preceding the birth of Adam count as part of a separate year.
²² To the calculation of the calendar.
²³ To the year preceding the birth of Adam and declare the year in which Adam was born a leap year. I.E.’s point is that our calendar is accurate. It is based on a 19-year cycle. The 3rd, 6th, 8th, 11th, 14th, 17th, and 19th year of the cycle are leap years. In those years we add a second month of Adar. According to current calculations the first leap year occurred a year after Adam was born (the third year from creation). If we begin the calendar two years before Adam was born, our calendar would have to be accordingly readjusted. However, we cannot do so, for there was nothing in existence two years before Adam’s birth. Hence the calendar must begin a year before the birth of Adam, not before.
²⁴ A real year and a half following the birth of Adam, for that was the “third” year from creation. The three years are calculated as follows. The “year” of tohu
²⁵ “The moment that the moon would have the same longitude as the sun, if both moved uniformly” (W.M. Feldman, Rabbinical Mathematics and Astronomy
²⁶ The average conjunction of the moon and sun.
²⁷ Rosh Ha-Shanah
²⁸ In the post-calendar period.
²⁹ In the post-calendar period the month is established by calculation, not by the actual sighting of the new moon.
³⁰ Literally, edge of the east. During the Middle Ages, before the Western Hemisphere was discovered, the Jews, in common with others, believed half of the globe to be covered by water (the Great Ocean) and the other half to be dry and habitable. Thus R. Abraham bar Chiya of the 11th century begins his work on the Jewish Calendar
³¹ That is, to the actual sighting of the new moon at the edge of the East or the West of the world.
³² Which determines the start of the new year.
³³ That the new moon is not contingent on the actual sighting of the new moon at the edge of the East or the West of the world.
³⁴ The new moon begins when it is calculated that the moon would be in conjunction with the sun over Jerusalem if both moved uniformly.
³⁵ If the new moon were based on sighting the moon, then the month would be established when the moon was actually seen.
³⁶ On an ordinary year, that is, not a leap year.
³⁷ By those who established the calendar.
³⁸ Hebrew, dechiyot
³⁹ In the month of Tishri.
⁴⁰ Another example of a postponement. This postponement is called dechiyat yach
⁴¹ Of the new month.
⁴² The true conjunction of the sun and the moon.
⁴³ The mean conjunction.
⁴⁴ The printed editions read 204 parts. However, the commentaries point out that this is a scribal error.
⁴⁵ This is known as dechiyat betutkapat
⁴⁶ This postponement is called dechiyat gatrad
⁴⁷ That the new moon is not always contingent on the mean conjunction.
⁴⁸ See Rosh Ha-Shanah
⁴⁹ Whether the Bet Din in Jerusalem declared the new month on the 30th or 31st day from the start of the previous month. This was in the time before the fixed calendar.
⁵⁰ Because they want to start the month with the day that the moon is calculated to be in conjunction with the sun. They also keep the Rabbinic calendar.
⁵¹ They fast an extra day in vain. I.E. will point out that one should follow Rabbinic law with regard to the calendar. Thus if the rabbis say that one does not have to fast for two days to ensure that one fasts on the true Yom Kippur, it is not necessary to do so. See I.E., Sefer Ha-Ibbur
⁵² Of Tishri.
⁵³ For in this instance the first day of the month falls two days after the birth of the new moon. The point is that those who fast for two days do so because they want to be certain that they observed Yom Kippur on the real astronomical day. However, in a year when the birth of the new moon of the month of Tishri in an ordinary year occurs at nine hours and 204 parts of the third day, the new month should begin on Tuesday, not Thursday as in the intercalated calendar. However, since these people want to be certain that they celebrate Yom Kippur ten days from the start of the “real” New Year they should fast on what is the 8th day of the month according to the intercalated calendar.
⁵⁴ According to those who fast two days on Yom Kippur because of doubt.
⁵⁵ Literally, why should we pervert the calculation of our year?
⁵⁶ The start of the new year is postponed when the birth of the new moon in the month of Tishri occurs at nine hours and 204 parts of an hour on Tuesday so that there not be a difference of six days between that year and the next. See Maimonides, Hilchot Kiddush Ha-Chodesh
⁵⁷ They would have to fast on what is the eighth day of the month according to our calculation in a year when the birth of the new moon occurs at nine hours and 204 parts of the third day.
⁵⁸ If they established the month by the actual sighting of the new moon and not only by calculating the birth of the new moon.

AT DUSK. I have previously explained this.⁵⁹

**אשה לה׳‎.** בפרשת פינחס:


AN OFFERING MADE BY FIRE. These are described in the Torah portion *Pinechas*.⁶⁰.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Num. 28:19. Our verse mentions that an offering made by fire is to be made on Passover. Hence I.E. points out that Torah lists the various types of offerings to be made on Passover in Numbers. The offerings are described in the Torah portion Pinechas



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:10:1)


וטעם להזכיר **כי תבאו אל הארץ.** בעבור שישמרו כל השבתות במדבר ופסח בהר סיני כי זו הפרשה סמוכה להקמת המשכן:


Scripture reads, *When ye are come into the land* (v. 9),⁶¹ so that Israel would observe all the Sabbaths in the wilderness and the Passover⁶² was erected on the first day of Nisan. Israel celebrated the Passover soon after. at Mount Sinai.⁶³ This section was given close to the [time of the] erection of the tabernacle.⁶⁴

footnotes:
⁶¹ After stating the laws of the Sabbath and Passover.
⁶² Israel offered only one Passover sacrifice in the wilderness. They did this when they camped around Mount Sinai. See I.E. on Ex. 29:42 (Vol. 2, pp. 620,621). This celebration took place close to the time of the erection of the tabernacle, for the mishkan
⁶³ And in the merit of observing the Sabbath and Passover enter the Land of Israel, for after leaving the wilderness of Sinai, Israel was supposed to enter the Promised Land (Weiser).
⁶⁴ The revelation of the laws in the Book of Leviticus commenced with the completion of the tabernacle. See Ex. 40:34-38; Lev. 1:1.

וטעם **דבר אל בני ישראל.** שיתחברו ואחר כן ואמרת אליהם:


[SPEAK UNTO THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL.] This means speak unto the children of Israel and tell them to gather. Afterwards,⁶⁵ *say unto them…*⁶⁶

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:11:1)


**ממחרת השבת.** אמרו חז״ל ממחרת יום טוב והמכחישים אמרו שהוא כמשמעו והמאמינים הביאו ראיות משנת השמטה והיובל והצום הגדול ויום תרועה שכתוב בו שבתון וכן שבתון נאמר בא׳‎ של סוכות ובשמיני ואמרו כי שבע שבתות שבועות וכמוהו באי השבת עם יוצאי השבת והם שנים פירושים בפסוק אחד והנה שלשים עיירים וראייתם תמימות וחכם ברומי הביא ראיה ממחרת הפסח מצות וקלוי ולא ידע כי בנפשו הוא כי הפסח ביום ארבעה עשר וממחרתו יום חמשה עשר וכן כתוב ויסעו מרעמסס בחדש הראשון ולאכול קלי אסור עד עבור תנופת העומר והגאון אמר ששני פסחים הם פסח ה׳‎ ופסח ישראל ופסח ה׳‎ בליל חמשה עשר והנה ממחרת הפסח ביהושע יום ט״ז ויהיה פירוש ממחרת הפסח הכתוב בתורה ממחרת זבח הפסח ולא אמר כלום כי לא נקרא החג פסח רק בעבור שפסח השם על הבתים וממחרתו הוא לבקר יום ט״ו וכן כתוב כל היום ההוא וכל הלילה וכל יום המחרת. ועוד אמר הגאון שמצות וקלוי היה מהישן וזה איננו רחוק כי הכתוב אמר על תנופת העומר שתהיה מהחל חרמש בקמה ואם תנופת העומר היתה יום חמשה עשר מתי קצרו ועשו מצות ועוד כי אין קציר כי הוא ראשית קציר שעורים גם יש כדמות ראיה על פירושו שאמר מעבור הארץ ויתכן שזה השם נקרא על התבואה שיש להשנה שעברה. וטעם מעבור הארץ ארץ סיחון ועוג והעד שאמר אחר כן מתבואת ארץ כנען ועוד כי קלוי איננו כמו קלי והכתוב אמר ולחם וקלי וכרמל לא תאכלו ואין פסוק קלוי באש טענה כי הוא תאר המעשה כי אביב קלוי באש יקרא קלוי ועוד יש להשיב מאין נדע כי הניף הכהן בעבר הירדן תנופת העומר כי הכתוב אמר אשר אני נותן לכם ועוד לא נתנה להם ולא בא הכתוב להזכיר מצות וקלוי רק בעבור ששבת המן וכן טעם כל הפסוק ועוד אמר הגאון אם השבת כמשמעו מאיזה שבת נחל לספור וישועה השיב כי שמנה עשר ימים הם שיש בהם קרבן ונקראו מועדים מלבד השבתות ואחר שיש קרבן ביום תנופת העומר לאות ולעד כי התנופה באחד מימות מועד האביב והנה שכח קרבנות ראשי חדשים שלא נזכרו ועוד כי הכתוב אמר כאלה תעשו ליום שבעת ימים והיה ראוי שיאמר מלבד תנופת העומר גם זאת איננה טענה עלינו כי אנחנו נסמוך במצות על הקבלה והנה ביום שבועות לא הזכיר בפרשת פנחס מלבד כבשי השלמים ולמעלה פר בן בקר אחד ואילים שנים ובפנחס פרים שנים ואיל אחד גם יוכל המאמין להשיב כי בדרך נבואה ידע היו׳‎ שיהיה בו תנופת העומר שיום השבת יהיה הקכיעות והעד מערך הלחם שהיה בשבת והזכיר הכתוב איך יחשבו בשנה הראשונה ואז הקריבו שלמים והעולה הנזכרת ואחר כן הנזכר׳‎ בפנחס ואחר כך אמר בשבועותיכם שבעה שבועות ולא אמר שבתות ואין ראיה כי תחלת השבוע יום ראשון וטמאה שבועים יוכיח והנה ארמוז לך סוד אחד שכל המועדים תלוים ביום ידוע מהחדש ולא נאמר בחג השבועות (מספר) (ס״א יום מועד) בעבור הספירה שהיא מצוה וחז״ל העתיקו כי בחג שבועות היה מתן תורה ועליו נאמר כי חג ה׳‎ לנו:


ON THE MORROW AFTER THE SABBATH. The sages said that on *the morrow after the sabbath* means on the morrow after the festival.⁶⁷ Those who contradict the sages⁶⁸ say that the word Sabbath is be taken literally.⁶⁹ was brought on the first Sunday following the start of Passover. Those who believe in the Rabbinic tradition offer proof from the Sabbatical year,⁷⁰ the Jubilee, the great fast,⁷¹ and the day of *shofar* sounding,⁷² for the word *shabbaton* (a solemn rest) is employed with regard to them.⁷³. Thus the word *shabbat* does not refer only to the Sabbath. Scripture similarly employs *shabbaton* with regard to Sukkot⁷⁴ and Shemini Atzeret.⁷⁵. They also say that the meaning of *sheva shabbatot* (v. 15) is seven weeks.⁷⁶ does not always mean the Sabbath. The same is true of *ba’eh shabbat im yotze’eh shabbat* (those that were to come in on the sabbath, with those that were to go out on the sabbath) (II Kings 11:9).⁷⁷ The same word⁷⁸. is employed with two meanings in one verse.⁷⁹ is used it refers to the Sabbath; the second time, to a week. We thus see that *shabbat* does not necessarily refer to the Sabbath. Note, we find a similarity in *sheloshim ayarim* (thirty ass colts) (Jud. 10:4).⁸⁰ (ayarim), *and they had thirty cities* (*ayarim*). The word *ayarim* appears twice in this verse. The first time it means ass colts; the second time, cities. They⁸¹ offer proof from the word *complete* (v. 15).⁸². The latter can only mean seven complete weeks, for it makes no sense to render it seven complete Sabbaths. A wise man in Rome⁸³ offered proof⁸⁴ means on the morrow after the first day of festival, i.e., on the 16th of Nisan. from on *the morrow after the passover, unleavened cakes and parched corn* (Josh. 5:11).⁸⁵. This scholar believed that the term *“passover”* in Josh. 5:11 refers to the 15th of Nisan. Hence *the morrow after the passover* refers to the 16th. This scholar also assumed that the *unleavened cakes and parched corn* came from the new produce. Now, since eating of new produce is forbidden till the *omer* is offered, and Scripture tells us, *And they did eat of the produce of the land*, we must assume that the eating of the new grain was preceded by the offering of the *omer*. We thus see that the *omer* is brought on the 16th of Nisan. Furthermore, Josh. 5:11 shows that *on the morrow after the sabbath* means on the morrow following the first day of the festival. He did not know that it cost him his life,⁸⁶. The “proof” offered by this scholar does not prove that which he intended. In fact, the Karaites or Sadducees can, as we shall see below, use his interpretation for their own purposes. for the Passover falls on the 14th⁸⁷ and the morrow following it is on the 15th.⁸⁸). Thus if we interpret Joshua 5:11 as alluding to the bringing of the *omer*, then we have to assume that the *omer* was brought on the 15th of Nisan, which we today call the first day of Passover. This is not in keeping with Rabbinic tradition. On the contrary, it supports the Karaites, for they believe that the *morrow after the sabbath* in Leviticus 23:16 refers to the first Sunday following the start of Passover. In other words, the Karaites say that in the year that Israel celebrated the first Passover in Canaan, the 15th of Nisan fell on a Sunday and Israel therefore offered the *omer* on that day. Scripture similarly reads, *And they journeyed from Rameses in the first month* (Num. 33:3).⁸⁹. We thus clearly see that *on the morrow after the passover* refers to the 15th of Nisan. The eating of parched corn is prohibited until the waving of the *omer*.⁹⁰ The Gaon⁹¹ as follows: And they did eat of the produce of the land on the morrow after the Passover of the Lord, i.e., the morrow following the 15th day. says that there are two Passovers, a Passover of the Lord and a Passover of Israel. The Passover of God is on the night of the 15th.⁹² Now, the *day after the Passover* in Joshua refers to the 16th day.⁹³ It follows from this that the interpretation of *on the morrow after the passover* (Num. 33:3), is on the morrow following the sacrifice of the Passover.⁹⁴ What he said has no value. The festival⁹⁵ is called Passover only because God skipped over the houses.⁹⁶). Hence the term Passover in Josh. 5:11 cannot refer to the 15th of Nisan. Its morrow is the morning of the 15th.⁹⁷ Scripture similarly writes, *all that day, and all the night, and all the next day* (yom ha-mochorat)⁹⁸ (morrow) refers to the following morning. (Num. 11:32). The Gaon further states that the unleavened cakes and the parched corn were from the old grain.⁹⁹). This is not far-fetched, for Scripture says that the waving of the *omer* should begin *from the time the sickle is first put to the standing corn* (Deut. 16:9). If the *omer* was waved on the 15th¹⁰⁰ when did they harvest and make unleavened bread?¹⁰¹ was waved on the 15th of Nisan when Israel entered the Land of Israel as the Karaites maintain, when did they harvest the grain to make the unleavened bread? Harvesting is prohibited on a festival. Furthermore, there was no harvest,¹⁰² for the *omer* is brought from the first of the barley harvest.¹⁰³ was waved. There is also what appears to be proof of the Gaon’s opinion,¹⁰⁴ for Scripture states, *of the produce* (meavur) *of the land* (Josh. 5:11). It is possible that the term *avur* refers to the produce of the year that has passed.¹⁰⁵ means passed. Hence this interpretation. The meaning of *the produce of the land* is the produce of the land of Sihon and Og.¹⁰⁶ The fact that Scripture afterwards states, *of the fruit of the land of Canaan* (Josh. 5:12), is proof.¹⁰⁷. This implies that Josh. 5:11 refers to grain that was not from the land of Canaan. Furthermore, *kaluy* (parched corn) (Josh. 5:11) is not the same as *kali* (parched com) (Lev. 23:14).¹⁰⁸ (Lev.23:14) refers to parched corn from the new produce. *Kaluy* (Josh. 5:11) refers to any parched corn. Now, Scripture says, *And ye shall eat neither bread nor parched corn* (kali), *nor fresh ears* (v. 14). The verse which reads *kaluy ba-esh* (parched with fire) (Lev. 2:14)¹⁰⁹). cannot serve as an argument to the contrary,¹¹⁰ refers to the new grain. for it describes the act¹¹¹ since corn in the ear parched in fire is called *kaluy*.¹¹². Hence that word is used. However, its use does not prove that the reference is only to new grain. We can also ask:¹¹³ From where do we know that the *kohen* waved the *omer* on the other side of the Jordan,¹¹⁴ as the Karaites maintain. for the verse states, *which I give unto you* (v. 10)?¹¹⁵ applies only after Israel has taken possession of the land. Thus Josh. 5:11 cannot allude to the waving of the *omer*. However, the land was not yet given to them.¹¹⁶. Scripture mentions matzot and corn only because the manna ceased. This is the meaning of the entire verse.¹¹⁷, to inform us that they waved the *omer* and then ate matzot and grain from the new produce. It rather tells us, *And they did eat of the produce of the land on the morrow after the passover, unleavened cakes and parched corn*, because the manna ceased. The Gaon¹¹⁸ further says: If the word Sabbath¹¹⁹. is to be taken literally¹²⁰ then from which Sabbath shall we start to count? Joshua¹²¹ answers that there are 18 days aside from the Sabbath whereon sacrifices are offered. These days are called *mo’adim* (festivals). Now, since there is a sacrifice¹²² on the day that the *omer* is offered¹²³ it is a sign and a witness that the waving takes place on one of the days of the spring festival.¹²⁴ is waved on the first day of the Passover holiday that follows a Sabbath. Note, he¹²⁵ forgot the sacrifices that are offered on the new moon, which are not mentioned.¹²⁶ is waved. In other words, the fact that the Torah does not specify the day of the *omer* waving as a festival does not mean that it falls on the festival of Passover. Hence if we reject the Rabbinic tradition and take the word Sabbath literally, we do not know from which Sabbath to start the count of seven weeks. Furthermore, Scripture states, *After this manner ye shall offer daily*,¹²⁷ *for seven days* (Num. 28:24). Scripture should have stated, aside from the offering of the *omer*.¹²⁸. This¹²⁹.” too¹³⁰ is no argument against us, for we rely on tradition when it comes to the commandments. Now, Scripture does not state “aside from the he-lambs brought for a peace offering,” when it lists in the Torah portion *Pinechas* the offerings to be brought on the festival of Shavu’ot.¹³¹, which according to verse 19 were to be offered on Shavu’ot. It states above,¹³² *and one young bullock, and two rams* (v. 18). However, *Pinechas* reads, *two young bullocks, one ram, seven he-lambs of the first year* (Num. 28:27).¹³³ are to be brought as burnt offerings on Shavu’ot. However, Num. 28:27 states that *two young bullocks*, are to be brought as burnt offerings on Shavu’ot. See further for I.E.’s explanation of the discrepancy. The believer can also answer¹³⁴. that Moses prophetically knew the day that the *omer* would be waved.¹³⁵ would take place on the Sabbath. Our verse alludes to that historic event. He knew that the first day of the month¹³⁶ would be established on the Sabbath.¹³⁷. The arrangement of the bread which took place on the Sabbath is proof of this.¹³⁸ Scripture notes¹³⁹ how they calculated the first year.¹⁴⁰ They then¹⁴¹ brought the peace offerings and the burnt offerings which are mentioned [in our Torah portion] and then¹⁴² the ones mentioned in the Torah portion *Pinechas*.¹⁴³ Scripture then goes on to say,¹⁴⁴ in all the other years. *in your feast of weeks* (Num. 28:26) and *seven weeks* (Deut. 16:9); it does not state Sabbaths.¹⁴⁵ There is no proof that the beginning of the week¹⁴⁶ and conclude with shavu’ot. starts with the first day.¹⁴⁷ and conclude with shavu’ot starts on a Sunday as the Karaites maintain. In fact, *then she*¹⁴⁸ *shall be unclean two weeks* (Lev. 12:5) shows that this is not the case.¹⁴⁹ is counted. It does not start on a Sunday. Look, I will hint at a secret.¹⁵⁰. It does not date when the counting is to begin. See Judah Ha-Levi, *The Book of Kuzari* 3:41, “…The Synhedrion and all the sages found that this period [of 50 days] was fixed with the intention to create an interval of fifty days between ‘the first fruits of harvest of the barley and the harvest of wheat,’ and to observe ‘seven weeks,’ which are seven complete Sabbaths.’ The first day of the week [*the morrow after the Sabbath* ] is only mentioned for argument’s sake in the following manner: should the day of putting ‘the sickle to the corn’ be on a Sunday, you count till Sunday…The date of putting of the sickle from which we count is left for us to fix” (*The Book of Kuzari*, translated by H. Hirschfeld, N.Y., 1946, p. 153). All the festivals are contingent on a specific day of the month. Scripture does not give a date for the festival of Shavu’ot¹⁵¹ because of the counting, which is a commandment. The rabbis, of blessed memory, transmitted the tradition that the Torah was given on the festival of Shavu’ot and that it is concerning this day that Scripture states, *for we must hold a feast unto the Lord* (Ex. 10:9).

והמכחישים אמרו כי בן שנה איננו כבן שנתו כי בן שנתו עוד אין לו שנה ובן שנה שיש לו שנה תמימה והנה לא קראו בחנוכת המזבח כבש אחד בן שנתו לעולה ובפרשה אחרונה כבשים בני שנה שנים עשר:


Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition¹⁵⁶ say that *ben shanah* (of the first year) (Ex. 12:5) does not have the same meaning as *ben shenato*¹⁵⁷ (of the first year), for *ben shanato* refers to a lamb under a year and *ben shanah* refers to a lamb of a complete year. Note, they did not read the section dealing with the dedication of the altar. The latter reads, *one he-lamb of the first year* (ben shenato), for a burnt offering (Num. 7:15). However, in the final section¹⁵⁸ it reads, *the he-lambs of the first year* (bene shanah) *twelve* (Num. 7:87).¹⁵⁹

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:15:1)


לולי הקבלה היה נראה כי ספירת הימים כשנות היובל והמכחישים אמרו כי טעם תמימות שלא תעלה במספר השבת הראשונה שיחל ממחרת׳‎:


If it were not for tradition¹⁶² and Shavu’ot. it would have appeared that the counting of the days is similar to the counting of the years¹⁶³. Some editions read *bi-shenot*. According to this reading, “the counting of days only applies in the days of the Jubilee.” In other words, the *omer* is not counted when the Jubilee is not observed. of the Jubilee.¹⁶⁴ and the 50th day declared as the festival of Shavu’ot. Those who deny the Rabbinic tradition say that the meaning of *complete* is that the first Sabbath from the morrow on which the count begins is not to be considered in the count.¹⁶⁵ is waved is not to be considered in the count.

footnotes:
¹⁶² That requires us to count the days between the waving of the omer
¹⁶³ Hebrew, ki-shenot
¹⁶⁴ Forty-nine years (seven weeks of years) are counted and the 50th year declared as the year of Jubilee. Similarly, seven weeks would be counted from the offering of the omer
¹⁶⁵ In other words, the Sabbath preceding the day on which the omer



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:16:1)


**תספרו חמשים יום.** כי כן מספר התורה וכן וביום השמיני בכל מקום:


SHALL YE NUMBER FIFTY DAYS. For this is the way the Torah counts.¹⁶⁶ Similarly, in all cases of the eighth day.¹⁶⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶⁶ The 50th day is in the count; i.e., Shavu’ot does not fall on the 49th day, but on the 50th day. In other words, when the Torah gives a count of days it does not mean up to the last day of the count, it means including the last day mentioned.
¹⁶⁷ When the Torah tell us to count 50 days and bring an offering, it does not mean to count 50 full days and bring the offering on the 51st day. Similarly, with circumcision, which the Torah requires to be performed on the eighth day from birth. One does not wait till the eighth day is concluded and then perform the rite of circumcision. The ritual is to be performed on the eighth day (Motot). According to Filwarg, I.E.’s point is that the Torah tells us to count seven weeks and to offer the sacrifice on the 50th day. Similarly, when Scripture requires seven days to pass (as in the case of one experiencing an issue, or a Nazirite who defiled himself) and then to offer a sacrifice, it means count seven days and offer the sacrifice on the eighth day.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:1)


מלת **תביאו.** זרה בעבור דגשות האל״ף כי לא נדע לו טעם:


[YE SHALL BRING OUT.] The word *tavi’u* (ye shall bring out) is irregular in that its *alef* has a *dagesh* in it. We do not know the reason for this.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:2)


**ממושבתיכם.** צריכים אנו לקבלה מאיזה מקום יוקח ומתי יבוקש:


OF YOUR DWELLINGS. We need tradition to teach us from which place it was brought¹⁶⁸ and when it is to be brought.¹⁶⁹

footnotes:
¹⁶⁸ Whether the two loaves must brought only from grain of the Land of Israel or if it can be brought from grain grown outside of the Land of Israel (Krinsky).
¹⁶⁹ The date that the two loaves are to be waved.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:17:3)


**לחם תנופה.** לכהן עם כבשי הלשמים:


TWO WAVE-LOAVES. For the *kohen* along with the peace offering lambs.¹⁷⁰.

footnotes:
¹⁷⁰ The two wave-loaves and the peace offering lambs are to be eaten by the kohen



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:18:1)


ויתכן ששנה הכתוב בשנה הראשונה בעבור שהיא תחלת ההתחלה ויש אומרים כי יקרב פר ואילים שנים או שני פרים ואיל אחד כרצון הכהן ועוד לא ראינו מצוה כזאת ובפנחס אפרש האמת:


It is possible that Scripture changed the sacrifices in the first year¹⁷¹ because it was the beginning of the beginning.¹⁷² Some say that the *kohen* shall offer¹⁷³ either a bullock and two rams¹⁷⁴ or two bullocks and one ram.¹⁷⁵ It depends on the will of the *kohen*.¹⁷⁶ We do not find such a commandment again.¹⁷⁷ I will give its true explanation in the Torah portion *Pinechas*.¹⁷⁸

footnotes:
¹⁷¹ In the first year of Israel’s entering the Land of Israel. There is a discrepancy between our Torah portion and Num. 28:27. Our chapter states that one bullock and two rams are to be offered on Shavu’ot. Num. 28:27 states that two bullocks and one ram are to be offered. According to I.E. our Torah portion refers to the order of sacrifices to be offered by Israel after first entering the Land of Israel, and Numbers, to the years that followed.
¹⁷² It was the first Shavu’ot in Israel’s first year in the Land of Israel.
¹⁷³ On Shavu’ot.
¹⁷⁴ In accordance with Lev. 23:18.
¹⁷⁵ In accordance with Num. 28:27.
¹⁷⁶ In other words, Lev. 23:18 and Num. 28:27 do not contradict. They are options.
¹⁷⁷ Where the Torah offers an option as to how a commandment should be observed.
¹⁷⁸ According to I.E. one bullock and two rams were offered along with the two loaves on Shavu’ot, while two bullocks and one ram were offered on Shavu’ot as burnt offerings. Thus there is no discrepancy between Leviticus and Numbers. See I.E. on Num. 28:26 and the notes thereto (Vol. 4, p. 235), “The two wave-loaves were the main offerings. The seven lambs, one young bullock, two rams (Lev. 23:18)…were brought along with it. The burnt offerings (two bullocks and one ram) in our chapter (Num. 28:26) refer to the obligatory sacrifice of the festival day.”



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:19:1)


ואחר ששחיטת השלמים ביום חג שבועות והוא יום מקרא קדש הנה מותר לשחוט בכל מועדים הפך דברי הצדוקים:


Now, since the slaughtering of the peace offerings took place on the festival of Shavu’ot and it is a day of holy assembly, it is thus permissible to slaughter¹⁷⁹ on all the festivals. The latter is contrary to the opinion of the Sadducees.¹⁸⁰

footnotes:
¹⁷⁹ An animal for meat.
¹⁸⁰ I.E.’s term for the Karaites. The Karaites claim that it is prohibited to slaughter an animal on a festival.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:22:1)


וטעם להזכיר **ובקצרכם את קציר ארצכם.** פעם שנית בעבור כי חג שבועות בכורי קציר חטים הזהיר שלא תשכח מה שצויתיך לעשות בימים ההם:


[AND WHEN YE REAP.] The reason Scripture mentions *And when ye reap the harvest of your land…* a second time¹⁸¹ is that Shavu’ot is the festival of the first-fruits of the wheat harvest (Ex. 34:22). Scripture cautions: Do not forget what I commanded you¹⁸² to do at this time.

footnotes:
¹⁸¹ The same is stated in Lev. 19:9.
¹⁸² In Lev. 19:9.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:23:1)


וטעם להזכיר **וידבר ה׳‎** עם יום התרועה בעבור היותו מועד בפני עצמו כי חג שבועות תלוי מיום תנופת העומר וכן הזכיר עם הצום גדול ועם חג הסוכות:


[SPOKE.] The reason the statement *And the Lord spoke* precedes the law dealing with the day of the sounding of the *shofar*¹⁸³ is that the latter is a festival unto itself.¹⁸⁴. The preceding festival of Shavu’ot does not have this introduction. Hence I.E.’s comments. However, the festival of Shavu’ot is connected to the day the *omer* is waved.¹⁸⁵. *And the Lord spoke* is similarly written in connection with the great fast¹⁸⁶ and the festival of Sukkot.

footnotes:
¹⁸³ Which it does not do with regard to the festival of Shavu’ot.
¹⁸⁴ The laws dealing with the festival of Rosh Ha-Shanah opens with And the Lord spoke unto Moses saying
¹⁸⁵ Hence it does not open with And the Lord spoke unto Moses saying
¹⁸⁶ Yom Kippur.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:24:1)


**בחדש השביעי.** כי תחלת החשבון מניסן כי בו יצאנו ממצרים ועוד כי אביב השעורים תחלה ואחר כן בכורי קציר חטים ואחר כך באספך את מעשיך ואלה המועדים תלוים להיותם בימים הנזכרים:


IN THE SEVENTH MONTH. We begin the count of months from Nisan,¹⁸⁷ for we then left Egypt. Furthermore, the festival of the new ears of barley¹⁸⁸. comes first,¹⁸⁹ next come *the first-fruits of wheat harvest* (Ex. 34:22),¹⁹⁰ and then the time when you gather in the produce of your work (Deut. 16:13).¹⁹¹ These festivals are contingent upon the days that are mentioned.¹⁹²

footnotes:
¹⁸⁷ See Ex. 12:2.
¹⁸⁸ The omer
¹⁸⁹ Scripture lists it first. Or it is first in the agricultural year.
¹⁹⁰ Shavu’ot.
¹⁹¹ Sukkot.
¹⁹² In our Torah portion.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:24:2)


ואחר שאמר לתקוע בחצוצרות בכל חדש הנה **זכרון תרועה** גם יום תרועה מצוה לתקוע בשופר וכן ביום הכפורים ואני הנה ארמוז לך סודות ושים לבך אולי תבינם אמרו המעתיקים שיום ראש השנה יום הדין וטעם התרועה זכר למלכיות השם ואחר שהעיקר כל ר״ח היה ראש חדש ניסן נכבד כי בו הוקם המשכן ובעתיד אמר יחזקאל בראשון באחד לחדש וכן תעשה בשבעה בעבור המרובע והנה חמשה עשר לנכח ויום שביעי גם מרובע ופסח שני יתחלפו הגדול והקטן על כן יום ראש השנה גדול מכולם אף על פי שהוא יום דין אסור להתענות בו ועזרא יוכיח ויום הכפורים הקטן במחברת והנה סכות כחג פסח רק שמיני ואין שביעי ומזה יתברר לך הסוד וסוד השבת:


Scripture commands that trumpets are to be sounded on all new moons.¹⁹³ It follows from the aforementioned that *zikhron tru’ah* (a memorial proclaimed with the blast of horns) and *yom tru’ah* (a day of blowing the horn) refer to the sounding of a *shofar*.¹⁹⁴, or Scripture would have explicitly stated that trumpets be sounded on Rosh Ha-Shanah. Similarly, on Yom Kippur.¹⁹⁵ be sounded on Yom Kippur of the Jubilee year. See Lev. 25:9. I will hint at some secrets to you. Pay attention. Perhaps you will understand them. The transmitters of tradition said that Rosh Ha-Shanah is a day of judgment and that the sounding of the *shofar* proclaims the kingdom of God.¹⁹⁶ 16a. Now, since the new moon¹⁹⁷ is the most important [day of the month], the first month of Nisan¹⁹⁸ is the most glorious month because the tabernacle was erected then. Ezekiel in speaking of the future says, *In the first month, in the first day of the month* (Ezek. 45:18). Similarly, *And so thou shalt do on the seventh day of the month* (Ezek. 40:20) because of the quarter.¹⁹⁹ Note,²⁰⁰ the 15th day of the month²⁰¹ corresponds to the time when one half of the month faces the other half.²⁰² The seventh day²⁰³ is also a quarter.²⁰⁴ On the second Passover²⁰⁵ the great²⁰⁶ and the small²⁰⁷ interchange.²⁰⁸ The day of Rosh Ha-Shanah is therefore greater²⁰⁹ than all of them. Now, even though it is the Day of Judgment it is prohibited to fast on it. The Book of Ezra shows that this is the case.²¹⁰ On Yom Kippur the small light²¹¹ is in the conjunction.²¹² Note, Sukkot is like Passover.²¹³ However, its eighth day, not its seventh, is a festival. From this, the secret²¹⁴ and the secret of the Sabbath²¹⁵ will become clear to you.

footnotes:
¹⁹³ Num. 10:10.
¹⁹⁴ Not to trumpets, for otherwise Scripture would have referred to the new moon as a yom tru’ah
¹⁹⁵ The Torah commands that a shofar tru’ah
¹⁹⁶ See Rosh Ha-Shanah
¹⁹⁷ The first day of the month.
¹⁹⁸ The first month. The point is just as the first day of the month is special, so is the first month.
¹⁹⁹ The seventh day of the month marks a quarter of the month. That day has astrological significance.
²⁰⁰ I.E. points out that the days on which the festivals fall have astronomical significance.
²⁰¹ When Passover falls.
²⁰² The 15th day of the month marks half a month.
²⁰³ Of Passover.
²⁰⁴ Of the month.
²⁰⁵ Those who for certain stated reasons could not observe it in the first month slaughtered the paschal lamb on the 14th day of the second month and ate it on the eve of the 15th. See Num. 8:6-12.
²⁰⁶ The true Passover.
²⁰⁷ Those who could not observe the first Passover offer the Passover sacrifices on this day. I.E’s point is that the second Passover, like the first, is observed in the middle of the month which he believed had astronomical significance.
²⁰⁸ The substitute or second Passover is then observed (Weiser).
²⁰⁹ It falls at the time of the new moon, it falls on the first day of the first quarter of the month, and it is the day on which the world was created. That day is thus more significant than the other days mentioned (Weiser).
²¹⁰ Ezra told the people to eat and enjoy themselves on Rosh Ha-Shanah. See Neh. 8:2-10. I.E. considered Ezra and Nehemiah as one book.
²¹¹ The moon.
²¹² In a propitious heavenly conjunction. Some interpret I.E. as referring to the end of the first third of the month. If we divide the month into three parts then the moon is most powerful on the 10th day of the month, for on the 20th it is waning and at the 30th it is invisible. Others say that the moon and sun are then in Saturn’s “house,” which is good for the moon and unfavorable for the sun.
²¹³ Both fall in the middle of the month.
²¹⁴ Of why the festivals fall on certain days.
²¹⁵ Why the Sabbath falls on the seventh day. According to I.E. the Sabbath falls on the seventh day because the seventh day has astrological significance. See I.E. on Lev. 13:2 and Gen. 8:5 (Vol. 1, p. 114).



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:27:1)


וטעם **אך בעשור.** בעבור שיום מקרא קדש יום שמחה וכן כתוב בספר עזרא לכו אכלו משמנים ושתו ממתקים:


[HOWBEIT.] Scripture reads, *Howbeit on the tenth day*, because a day of solemn assembly (v. 24) is a joyful day.²¹⁶ is a joyful day. The Book of Ezra similarly reads, *Go your way, eat the fat, and drink the sweet* (Neh. 8:10).²¹⁷), Yom Kippur does not have these characteristics. I.E. considered Ezra and Nehemia one book.

²¹⁶ This verse refers to Rosh Ha-Shanah. We thus see that a day of holy convocation
²¹⁷ This verse occurred on Rosh Ha-Shanah. However (Howbeit

### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:28:1)

TO MAKE ATONEMENT FOR YOU. Its meaning is, it is a day of atonement for you alone.²¹⁹

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:29:1)


**כי כל הנפש אשר לא תעונה.** זה מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו וזה אות כי מי שנדע שאינו שומר זו המצוה כאשר נשמרנו נכריחנו להתענות וטעם שלא יעשו מלאכה. שלא תהיינה הנפשות מתעסקות חוץ מבקשת כפור העונות:


FOR WHATSOEVER SOUL IT SHALL BE THAT SHALL NOT BE AFFLICTED. *Te’unneh* (be afflicted) is a *pu’al*. This²²⁰ (be afflicted). is a sign that if we know of a person who does not observe this precept as we observe it, then we shall force that individual to fast.²²¹ implies that one is forced to fast. The reason Scripture says that *no* manner of work is to be done on Yom Kippur is so that people will not be engaged in anything but seeking atonement for sins.

footnotes:
²²⁰ The use of the pu’al te’unneh
²²¹ Be afflicted



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:30:1)


**והאבדתי.** יש הפרש בינו ובין ונכרתה ולא אוכל לפרש:


THAT SOUL WILL I DESTROY. There is a difference between “destroy” and “cut off” (v. 29). I cannot explain the difference.²²²

footnotes:
²²² I am not permitted to explain. According to I.E. “cut off” means death either by execution or by the hand of God (see I.E. on Lev. 18:29). In Ps. 1:6 he explains that “destruction” refers to the obliteration of the soul following death. “I cannot explain” might mean that I.E. cannot explain how the soul is destroyed.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:31:1)

### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:36:1)


**שבעת ימים תקריבו אשה.** ואם איננו שוה כאשה חג הפסח:


SEVEN DAYS YE SHALL BRING AN OFFERING MADE BY FIRE. Even though it is not the same as the *offering made by fire* that is brought on Passover.²²⁵. However, there is a difference in the sacrifices offered on Passover and Sukkot. Thus *an offering made by fire* does not refer to a specific sacrifice.

footnotes:
²²⁵ The Torah tells us that on Passover and Sukkot the Jews are to bring an offering made by fire


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:36:2)


**עצרת הוא.** יש אומרים שטעמו קהלה כטעם עצרת בוגדים והטעם התחברות כל ישראל לשלש רגלים ולא דברו נכונה כי הנה כתוב בפסח וביום השביעי עצרת וכתיב ופנית בבקר והלכת לאהליך והקרוב שהוא כמו נעצר לפני ה׳‎ שיהיה בטל מכל עסקי העולם ופירוש עצרת הוא כל מלאכת עבודה לא תעשו וכן כתוב בעצרת מועד הפסח:


IT IS A DAY OF SOLEMN ASSEMBLY. Some say that *atzeret* (solemn assembly) means an assembly as in *azteret bogedim* (an assembly of treacherous men) (Jer. 9:1). *Azteret* refers to the gathering of all of Israel on the three pilgrimage festivals. However, they did not speak correctly, for look, Scripture states with regard to Passover, *and on the seventh day shall be a solemn assembly* (atzeret) (Deut. 16:8). However, it also states *and thou shalt turn in the morning*,²²⁶ *and go unto thy tents* (Deut. 16:7).²²⁷. It appears that *atzeret* is similar to *netzar* (detained)²²⁸. in *detained before the Lord* (I Sam. 21:8). *Atzeret* means that the Israelite shall not be involved in any worldly affairs.²²⁹ The meaning of *atzeret* is, *ye shall do no manner of servile work*.²³⁰ explains the term *atzeret*. Scripture writes similarly with regard to the *atzeret* of the festival of Passover.²³¹…thou shall do no work therein.”

footnotes:
²²⁶ On the second day of Passsover. See I.E. on Deut. 16:7 (Vol. 5, p. 112).
²²⁷ There was thus no gathering of all Israel on the seventh day of Passover. Yet the Torah refers to this day as an atzeret
²²⁸ Or held back. From the root ayin, tzadi, resh
²²⁹ The Israelite shall hold himself back from doing work.
²³⁰ Ye shall do no manner of servile work
²³¹ Deut. 16:8 reads: “And on the seventh day [of Passover] shall be an azeret



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:37:1)


**אלה מועדי ה׳‎.** שאתם חייבים להקריב בהם אשה ופירוש כי בכולם יש בהם עולה ומנחה זבח ונסכים כי עד עתה לא הזכיר רק אשה לבדו ויתכן להיותו עולה לבדה או מנחה לבדה:


THESE ARE THE APPOINTED SEASONS OF THE LORD. Whereon you are obligated to bring an offering made by fire. Scripture explains²³² that on all of these festivals a burnt offering, a meal offering, a sacrifice,²³³ and libations are to be brought, for until now Scripture only mentioned an offering made by fire, and it is possible that an offering made by fire refers only to a burnt offering or only to a meal offering.²³⁴

footnotes:
²³² That is, Scripture now explains.
²³³ The festival offerings.
²³⁴ Hence our verse explains that the term burnt offering refers to an offering made by fire.

AND BESIDE YOUR GIFTS. On the three pilgrimage festivals.²³⁵ Similarly, And the vows of the congregation and of the individual and their freewill offerings.²³⁶

### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:1)


וטעם **אך.** בעבור שהזכיר בפרשה שהיא קודם זאת ענוי הנפש אסר להתענות בחג הסכות כי בו כתובות ושמחת ושמחתם והיית אך שמח:


[HOWBEIT.] Scripture reads *howbeit* because it mentioned the affliction of the soul in the preceding section. The Torah prohibited fasting on the festival of Sukkot, for Scripture reads with regard to this festival, *And thou shalt rejoice* (Deut. 16:14)…*and thou shalt be altogether joyful* (Deut. 16:15).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:2)


**באספכם את תבואת הארץ.** שדות וכרמים:


WHEN YE HAVE GATHERED IN THE FRUITS OF THE LAND. From the fields and vineyards.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:39:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:3)


**תחגו.** מפעלי הכפל וטעם על הזבחים:


YE SHALL KEEP THE FEAST. *Tachoggu* (ye shall keep the feast) comes from a double root.²³⁷. The reference is to the sacrifices.²³⁸

footnotes:
²³⁷ Its root is chet, gimel, gimel
²³⁸ The peace offerings.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:39:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:39:4)


**ביום הראשון שבתון.** שם ויחסר יהיה לכם ואילו הי׳‎ שם התאר היה ה״א תחת בי״ת:


ON THE FIRST DAY SHALL BE A SOLEMN REST.²³⁹ *Shabbaton* (a solemn rest) is a noun. The words “shall be unto you” are missing.²⁴⁰ should be read as if written: On the first day shall be a solemn rest unto you. If *shabbaton* were an adjective, the word *yom* (day) would have a *heh* rather than a *bet* prefixed to it.²⁴¹ (on the first day…a solemn rest). If *shabbaton* were an adjective, then our verse would read *ha-yom ha-rishon shabbaton* (the first day a solemn rest).

²³⁹ Literally, On the first day a solemn rest.
²⁴⁰ In other words, On the first day a solemn rest
²⁴¹ Our verse reads, be yom ha-rishon shabbaton



### Verse 40

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:40:1)


**ולקחתם לכם.** אנחנו נאמין בדברי המעתיקים כי לא יכחישו הכתוב אף על פי שמצאנו ויקחו להם איש שה לבית אבות. גם הם העתיקו כי פרי עץ הדר הוא אתרוג ובאמת כי אין פרי עץ יותר הדר ממנו ודרשו בו הדר באילנו בדרך אסמכתא כאשר פירשתי בפסוק לעם נכרי והצדוקים אמרו כי מאלה תעשו סוכות והביאו ראיה מספר עזרא ואלה עורי לב הלא יראו כי אין בספר עזרא ערבי נחל ולא פרי עץ כלל רק עלי חמשה מינים ואין זכר לעלי הדס ועלי עץ עבות טענה על קדמונינו וכן מין הדס אין אילנו גבוה והנה הם שני מינים גבוה ונמוך והגולה מארץ קדר לארץ אדום אם יש לו עינים ידע סוד המצוה זאת:


AND YE SHALL TAKE. We shall believe in the words of the transmitters,²⁴² for they do not contradict Scripture.²⁴³ The latter is so even though we find, *they shall take to them every man a lamb* (Ex. 12:3).²⁴⁴ on Ex. 12:3. They²⁴⁵ also transmitted to us the tradition that *the fruit of goodly trees* (v. 40) refers to the etrog (citron). In truth, there is no fruit of the tree more beautiful than the etrog. The rabbis interpreted the phrase *etz hadar* (goodly trees) to mean [a fruit] which dwells on its tree.²⁴⁶ 33a) connects the word *hadar* (goodly) to the word *dar* (dwells). It interprets our verse as meaning a fruit which dwells ((or remains) on its tree from year to year. They used the verse as a support for their tradition. I have explained the latter²⁴⁷ in my comments on *to sell her unto a foreign people* (Ex. 21:8).²⁴⁸ The Sadducees²⁴⁹ say that the *sukkah* shall be built from these plants.²⁵⁰ They brought proof from the Book of Ezra.²⁵¹ out of olive branches, branches of wild olive, myrtle branches, palm branches, and branches of thick trees. However, they are blind of heart. Do they not see that the Book of Ezra²⁵² does not at all mention willows of the book and fruit of the tree?²⁵³ It only mentions the leaves of five kinds of branches.²⁵⁴ The mention there of myrtle branches and branches of thick trees is no argument against our ancients.²⁵⁵ does not disprove the Rabbinic tradition that these plants are to be ritually taken in the hand on sukkot. Similarly the kind called myrtle does not grow on a tall tree.²⁵⁶ There are thus two kinds,²⁵⁷ (ale etz avot) (Neh. 8:15) and *boughs of thick trees* (anaf etz avot) are not the exact same plant. The first-mentioned plant grows on a tall tree and the latter on a low tree. There is thus no proof from Neh. 8:15 that myrtles are to be employed in building the *sukkah*. one growing on a tall tree and one on a low tree. The person who wanders from the land of Kedar²⁵⁸ to the land of Edom²⁵⁹ will know the secret of this commandment²⁶⁰ if he has eyes.²⁶¹ 2:2. So Weiser. For other interpretations see Motot, Krinsky, and Filwarg.

²⁴² The transmitters of tradition, the Talmudic sages.
²⁴³ The rabbis of the Talmud teach that the four types of plants mentioned in our verse are to be actually taken by hand.
²⁴⁴ Here the word “take” does not mean one has to ritually take the animal in one’s hand. Or here the word take means buy. See Mekhilta
²⁴⁵ The transmitters of tradition.
²⁴⁶ It doesn’t fall off the tree. The Talmud (Sukkah
²⁴⁷ The use of Biblical verses as supports.
²⁴⁸ See I.E. on Ex. 21:8 (Vol. 2, pp. 458,459).
²⁴⁹ The Karaites.
²⁵⁰ The plants mentioned in our verse.
²⁵¹ Neh. 8:15 tells us that the Israelites made sukkot
²⁵² That is, Neh. 8:15.
²⁵³ That is, fruit of a goodly tree.
²⁵⁴ Olive branches, branches of wild olive, myrtle branches, palm branches, and branches of thick trees.
²⁵⁵ The fact that myrtle branches and branches of thick trees were used in the days of Ezra and Nehemiah to build a sukkah
²⁵⁶ The Karaites claim the “boughs of thick trees” refers to a plant that grows on tall trees. According to the rabbis the “boughs of thick trees” refers to the myrtle. The latter does not grow on a tall tree.
²⁵⁷ Branches of thick trees
²⁵⁸ A dry land. Kedar was one of the sons of Ishmael (Gen. 25:13). Kedar in I.E. usually refers to Moslems. The descendants of Ishmael dwelt between Egypt and Assyria. The latter area is semi-dry.
²⁵⁹ The Land of the Christians, i.e., Europe.
²⁶⁰ The commandment of taking the four kinds mentioned in verse 40.
²⁶¹ The land of Israel, which is located in the Middle East, is a semi-dry land and required special prayers for rain. The “four kinds” serve to induce rainfall. See Ta’anit



### Verse 43

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:43:1)


**כי בסכות.** שהיו עושים אחר שעברו ים סוף סוכות ואף כי במדבר סיני שעמדו שם קרוב משנה וכן מנהג כל המחנות והנה גם זה המועד זכר ליציאת מצרים ואם ישאל שואל למה בתשרי זאת המצוה יש להשיב כי ענן ה׳‎ היה על המחנה יומם והשמש לא יכם ומימות תשרי החלו לעשות סוכות בעבור הקור:


THAT…IN BOOTHS. The Israelites made booths after they crossed the Sea of Reeds. They certainly did so in the wilderness of Sinai, where they dwelt close to a year. This is the manner of all the camps. This festival too²⁶² is thus in memory of the Exodus²⁶³ from Egypt.²⁶⁴ Should someone ask why this commandment is to be observed in the month of Tishri,²⁶⁵ then one can answer: God’s cloud was over the camp during the day²⁶⁶ and the sun did not strike them.²⁶⁷ However, they started to make *sukkot* from the days of Tishri onwards because of the cold.²⁶⁸

footnotes:
²⁶² Like Passover.
²⁶³ As noted in verse 43.
²⁶⁴ Even though it is not observed in the month of Nisan.
²⁶⁵ The month Israel left Egypt.
²⁶⁶ When they left Egypt.
²⁶⁷ They thus had no need of booths.
²⁶⁸ I.E.’s point is that Israel had no need of booths for shelter until the cold weather set in. The latter occurred in Tishri. Hence booths are constructed in Tishri.



### Verse 44

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 23:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/23:44:1)


**אל בני ישראל.** ולא כל בני ישראל כי לא יוכל לדבר עם כולם וכן כתוב דבר אל בני ישראל ואין טענה מפסוק אל כל עדת בני ישראל כי כל ישראל לא יקרא עדה:


UNTO THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. Not to all of the children of Israel, for Moses could not talk to all of them.²⁶⁹ Scripture similarly reads, *Speak unto the children of Israel*. (verses 2, 10, 24, 34). The verse *unto all the congregation of the children of Israel* (Lev. 19:2) is no argument to the contrary,²⁷⁰ for all of Israel is not called a congregation.²⁷¹) refers to the heads of the people who gathered (*no’adu*) to hear the words of Moses.

וטעם להזכיר פרשת **שמן זית זך** בעבור שהזכיר אשה כל מועד והוא צריך להזכיר הלחם והמנורה כנגד השלחן:


[PURE OLIVE OIL.] The reason Scripture now mentions the section¹ dealing with the pure olive oil is that the Torah earlier recorded the fire offering of each festival² and now has to mention the show bread.³ The menorah stood opposite the table.⁴

### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:4:1)


והוסיף בפרשה הזאת **על המנורה הטהורה** היא הידועה (עטו) [צ״ל זהב] כלה מעשה בצלאל ולא אחרת ואשר עשוה מברזל בשעת הצורך על פי הנביאים עשוה:


[UPON THE PURE CANDLESTICK.] Scripture in our section adds *upon the pure candlestick*.⁵. The latter refers to the known candlestick,⁶ which was constructed by Betzalel⁷ and was completely made of gold.⁸. It does not refer to any other candlestick. [As to the menorah], which was made of iron,⁹ 43a. it was done so in time of need¹⁰ by the word of the prophets.¹¹

AND THOU SHALT TAKE FINE FLOUR. By command.¹² Similarly, *and bake…thereof*.¹³ by command. *Ve-afita* (and shalt bake) is ultimately accented. It is irregular.¹⁴ and have a conversive *vav* prefixed to them maintain their penultimate accent. However, *ve-afita* (and shalt bake) is ultimately accented. Hence it is irregular.

IN TWO ROWS. Corresponding to the number of the tribes.¹⁵ This is similar to the secret of the ephod and the breastplate.¹⁶ The¹⁷ two tenth parts (v. 5) corresponded to the arrangement of the two rows.¹⁸

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:10:2)

WHOSE FATHER WAS AN EGYPTIAN. Who converted to Judaism.²¹

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:10:3)


**ואיש הישראלי.** כמו לאיש העשיר את יום השביעי ולא נדע למה נסמכה זאת הפרשה אולי דבר המקלל דברים אשר לא כן בעבור הלחם והשמן והקרבנות:


AND A MAN OF ISRAEL. *Ve-ish ha-yisra’eli* (and a man of Israel) is similar to *le-ish he-ashir* (unto the rich man) (II Sam. 12:4) and to *et yom ha-shevi’iy* (the seventh day) (Gen. 2:3).²² prefixed to it, the noun which it modifies also has a *heh* prefixed to it. I.E. points out that there are instances in Scripture where the above is not followed, viz., our verse, II Sam. 12:4, and Gen 2:3. See I.E. on Gen 1:31 (Vol. 1, p. 47). We do not know why this section²³ was connected to that which comes before it. Perhaps the blasphemer spoke improperly with regard to the bread, the oil, and the sacrifices.

²² When an adjective has a heh
²³ Verses 10-12.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:11:1)


**ויקב.** יש אומרים שפירושו ויפרש כמו אשר פי ה׳‎ יקבנו אשר נקבו בשמות ויש אומרים שהוא כמו מה אקוב והראשון קרוב לפי דעתי:


BLASPHEMED. Some say that the meaning of *va-yikkov* (blasphemed) is enunciated. Compare, *Which the mouth of the Lord shall mark out* (yikkavennu) (Is. 62:2) and *that are pointed out* (nikkevu) *by name* (Num. 1:17). Others say that *va-yikkov* is similar to *ekkov* (I curse) in *How shall I curse* (Num. 23:8). However, I believe that the first interpretation is correct.²⁴ (and blasphemed the Name and cursed). If *va-yikkov* means “and cursed,” then our verse is repetitious. Hence it appears that our verse should be interpreted as, and enunciated the Name of God and cursed it.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:16:1)


**כל העדה.** הם גדולי הארץ שהם גדולי ישראל ויש אומרים כי יקלל אלהיו בסתר והנכון שמלת אלהים שם התאר והמלאכים יקראו אלהים וכן הדיינים ומי יוכל לדעת מה יש בלב המקלל אך אם יפרש השם הנכבד שאינו שם התאר ולא יתערב עם שם כי הוא לבדו והנה הטעם בעת שיקלל אם יפרש השם יומת בנקבו שם כאשר עשה בן המצרי ולמען כבוד השם לא נזכר. ואמר **כגר כאזרח. יומת.** ויתכן שהכו אלה הנצים זה את זה על כן נכתבה זאת הפרשה וכבר הזכירה רק הוסיף כגר כאזרח יהיה והחל כי יכה כל נפש אדם בזדון ולא במלחמה מגר או אזרח:


ALL THE CONGREGATION. The reference is to the magnates of the land who are the great ones of Israel.²⁷ Some say that *Whosoever curseth his God* (v. 15) means whosoever curseth his God in secret.²⁸ The correct interpretation is as follows. The term *Elohim* (god) is an adjective. The angels are called elohim.²⁹ So are the judges.³⁰. Who can know what is in the mind of the one who curses? However, if he enunciates the glorious name of God, which stands by itself and is not an adjective and is not intertwined with any noun, [he shall be stoned]. Its meaning is,³¹. when one curses and enunciates the Name he shall be put to death if he enunciates the Name as the son of the Egyptian did.³² The Name³³ is not mentioned³⁴ out of respect for God. Scripture reads, *as well the stranger, as the home-born…shall be put to death*. It is possible that the ones who quarreled (v. 10) struck each other. This section³⁵ was therefore written.³⁶ It³⁷ had previously been mentioned. However, our chapter adds, *as well for the stranger, as for the home-born* (v. 22).[17. AND HE THAT SMITETH.] Scripture begins³⁸ with *and he that smiteth any man mortally*.³⁹ He is not participating in war and deliberately smites any man, be that man a stranger or a native

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:19:1)


**כן יעשה לו.** ואמר שמשון כן עשיתי להם:


SO SHALL IT BE DONE TO HIM. Samson said, *so have I done unto them* (Jud. 15:11).⁴². The latter is not to be taken literally, for Samson did not repay his enemies exactly tit for tat. We thus see that *as he hath done, so shall it be done to him* is not necessarily to be taken literally. It rather means punishment is to be inflicted.

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:20:1)


והגאון הביא ראיות משקול הדעת כי לא יתכן להיות שבר תחת שבר כמשמעו כי הראשון בא בלא כוונה ואיך יתכן לעשות שבר כמוהו ואם הוא במקום מסוכן הנה ימות וכן בעין ומי שסר שלישית אור עינו איך יוכל לעשות כן בעין החובל על כן דברי הקבלה אמת כי פירוש כל אלה שיש עליו כופר וראוי להוציא עינו אם לא יפדנו ואם טענו עלינו אם היה החובל עני ותשובתינו כי על הרוב דבר הכתוב ושמא יעשיר העני גם זאת התשובה על הטוענים אם היה חובל העין עור **כן ינתן בו.** יש בי״ת תחת על כמו אשר אני רוכב בה ורבים כן או פירושו כן ינתן בו אם לא יפדה:


The Gaon⁴³ brought proof from logic that *breach for breach* (v. 20) cannot be taken literally. The first wound was made unintentionally. How can we justify making a similar wound?⁴⁴ If the wound is in a vital area, the person will die. The same applies to the eye. How are we to do the same to one who injured his neighbor by causing him to suffer the loss of a third of the sight of an eye?⁴⁵ The words of tradition are thus true.⁴⁶ is not to be taken literally. It rather means that he shall make commensurate compensation. Scripture is to be understood as follows. Ransom money is to be paid in all of these cases. It is fitting to remove his eye if he does not ransom it.⁴⁷ Should one argue against us and say, what happens if the injurer is poor? Then we answer, Scripture speaks about the majority of cases.⁴⁸ It is possible for the poor to become rich.⁴⁹ This also answers those who argue, what shall we do in instances when the injurer of the eye is blind?⁵⁰ cannot be taken literally, for what shall we do in instances when the injurer is blind? I.E. here counters that argument by saying that Scripture speaks of what is usual, and in cases where a blind person blinds someone the penalty of an eye for an eye is not applicable. SO SHALL IT BE RENDERED UNTO HIM.⁵¹ (literally in him). Hence I.E.’s comment. There are instances where the *bet* has the meaning of on.⁵² means unto him. I.E. renders our clause, “so shall it be rendered upon him,” i.e., a penalty shall be placed upon him. Compare, *the beast that I rode upon* (bah) (Neh. 2:12). There are many such instances. On the other hand, its meaning might be, so shall it be rendered unto him if he does not pay ransom.⁵³ means unto him.

It should be noted that Rabbi Saadiah Gaon held that the Torah does not contain laws that contradict reason.

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 24:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/24:21:1)


וטעם **ומכה בהמה.** פעם אחרת להזכיר הגר כי נתינת המום הוא בישראל כי כן כתוב בעמיתו רק מכה אדם ובהמה **משפט אחד להם כגר כאזרח** וכבר הודעתיך כי בהתחבר שני כפי״ן הוא דרך קצרה גם הכתוב אחז דרך קצרה שלא הזכיר עם מכה אדם ומת כי ידוע הוא כי המכה לא יומת עד מות המוכה. ופירוש אחר הזכיר בתחלה נפש והטעם שהכהו במקום מסוכן ואחר כן אמר כלל מי שימית יומת בין שיכה במקום מסוכן או שאינו מסוכן על כן לא הזכיר נפש:


[AND HE THAT KILLETH A BEAST.] Scripture repeats *And he that killeth a beast…*⁵⁴ because it wants to mention the stranger, for when Scripture speaks of maiming (v. 19) it speaks of an Israelite, for the Torah clearly says,⁵⁵ *his neighbor*. However, there is one law for both the stranger and the native regarding he that kills a person and he that kills a beast.I have previously informed you that Scripture is being brief when two *cafs* come together.⁵⁶ prefix follow each other. Our verse reads *ka-ger ka-ezrach* (as well for the stranger, as for the home-born). According to I.E. *ka-ger ka-ezrach* is short for *ha-ger ke-ezrach, ve-ha’ezrach ka-ger* (the stranger is like the home-born and the home-born is like the stranger). See I.E. on Gen. 44:18 (Vol. 1, p. 396). Scripture also chooses to employ brevity when it does not add “and he dies” to *u-makkeh adam*⁵⁷ (and he that killeth a man), for it is known that the culprit is not executed unless the one who is struck dies. Another interpretation for the repetition⁵⁸ is that Scripture earlier mentions “mortally” (v. 17).⁵⁹. The latter refers to a blow struck in a vital area. Scripture then lays down⁶⁰ the following general law: A person who kills shall be put to death whether the blow was struck in a vital area or in a non-vital area. The word *nefesh* (mortal) is thus not mentioned.⁶¹

## Chapter 25


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 25:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:1:1)


**בהר סיני.** אין מוקדם ומאוחר בתורה וזו הפרשה קודם ויקרא וכל הפרשיות שהם אחריו כי הדבור בהר סיני ועתה כרת הברית הכתובה בפרשת ואלה המשפטים והזכירה במקום הזה לחבר תנאי הארץ וכאשר אמר על העריות כי בעבור׳‎ תקיא הארץ אותם כן אמר בפרשת אם בחקתי על שבתות הארץ והזכיר בתחלה פי׳‎ השבתות:


IN MOUNT SINAI. There is no chronological order in the Torah.¹, Jerusalem, 1978, pp. 18-21. This section² precedes, *And the Lord called* (Lev. 1:1) and all the sections that follow it, for the words recorded in our section were told to Moses on Mount Sinai.³ Moses now⁴ made the covenant recorded in the Torah portion *Now these are the ordinances* (Ex. 21:1).⁵ (Lev. 24:3-46) is the same covenant that is recorded in Ex. 24:4-8. See Nachmanides, who explains I.E. in detail. Moses inserted this portion⁶ at this point to place in proximity the conditions under which the Israelites could dwell in the land.⁷ He said that the land would spit out the Israelites if they violated the laws of prohibited sexual relations.⁸ He said a similar thing in the Torah portion *If ye walk in My statutes* (Lev. 26:3) with regard to the Sabbaths of the land. Moses first mentions the Sabbaths.⁹

⁶ The laws dealing with the Sabbatical year and that which follows.
⁷ Lev. 18:28 and 20:22 tell us that Israel will dwell in the Land of Israel on condition that they refrain from perverse sexual practices. Lev. 26:34-43 tells us that Israel will dwell in the land only if they observe the Sabbatical year. Our section which deals with the Sabbatical year was inserted here to place these two conditions in the same portion.

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 25:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:2:1)


**ושבתה הארץ שבת לה׳‎.** מצוה על ישראלי שלא יעזוב גר לזרוע שנת השבת כאשר לא נעזבנו לעשות מלאכה בשבת כי הוא ברשותנו:


THEN SHALL THE LAND KEEP A SABBATH UNTO THE LORD. There is a commandment upon the Israelite not to allow a stranger to sow in the Sabbatical year.¹⁰ This is similar to the commandment that we not allow a stranger to do work on the Sabbath.¹¹ Scripture commands this, for the stranger is in our power.¹²

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 25:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:5:1)


**ספיח.** ידוע שהוא מגזרת ספחני נא גם נזירך ידוע שהוא מגזרת נזיר:


THAT WHICH GROWETH OF ITSELF. It is known that the word *safi’ach* (that which groweth of itself) is connected to the word *sefacheni* (put me)¹⁶ in *Put me, I pray thee* (I Sam. 3:36).¹⁷ (that which groweth of itself) refers to produce which fell to the ground at the time of the harvest and attached itself to the ground (Weiser). It is also known that the word *nezirekha* (undressed vine) is related to the word *nazir* (nazirite).¹⁸ distances himself from a vineyard, for he is not permitted to eat of its fruit. An undressed vine is referred to by the term *nazir* because the owner of the vineyard distanced himself from the vine and did not dress it (Weiser).

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/25:6:3)


וטעם **לך.** שיש רשות לבעל האדמה לאכול הספיחים:


The meaning of *lekha* (for thee) is that the owner of the field is permitted to eat of that which grows of itself.²⁰ (for thee) is a singular. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
²⁰ Lekha
