### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:1)


ולא יהיה לכם מקום לזעוק ולהתפלל להושע מהרעב כי אשמיד במותיכם מקום הזבחים:


You will not have a place to cry out and pray to be saved from the famine, for I will destroy your high places, the place of the sacrifices.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:2)


**חמניכם.** מגזר׳‎ חמה בתים העשויי׳‎ להשתחו׳‎ לשמש והנו״ן נוסף כנו״ן נשים רחמניות:


YOUR SUN-PILLARS. *Chammnekhem* is related to the word *chammah* (sun).¹⁰⁷. *Chammnekhem* refers to structures built for the purpose of bowing down to the sun. The *nun* of *chammnekhem* is superfluous.¹⁰⁸ It is like the *nun* of *rachamaniyyot*¹⁰⁹. (compassion) in *women full of compassion* (Lam. 4:10).

footnotes:
¹⁰⁷ It comes from the root chet, mem, mem
¹⁰⁸ It is not a root letter and does not serve any grammatical purposes.
¹⁰⁹ From the root resh, chet, mem


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:3)


**פגריכם.** גופותיכם כמו פגר מובס. **גלוליכם.** שם גנאי לעבודת כוכבים מגזר׳‎ כאשר יבער הגלל והטעם כי בהתחברכם לבתי עבוד׳‎ כוכבים שם תהרגו וישחיתו אויביכם צורת גלוליכם ואני לא אושיעכם:


YOUR CARCASSES. *Pigrekhem* means your carcasses. Compare, *As a carcass* (peger) *trodden under foot* (Is. 14:19). YOUR IDOLS. *Gillulekhem* (your idols) is a disparaging term for idols. *Gillulekhem* is related to the word *galal* (dung) in *sweepeth away dung* (I Kings 14:10).¹¹⁰ literally means your dung. The meaning of our verse is that when Israel joins themselves to temples dedicated to idol worship they will be killed there and their enemies will destroy the images of their idols. I (God) will not help them.

footnotes:
¹¹⁰ Gillulekhem


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:30:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:30:4)


**וגעלה נפשי אתכם.** שתסור השכינה:


AND MY SOUL SHALL ABHOR YOU. The divine presence shall be removed.



### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:31:1)


אז תחרבנה **עריכם** ונשמו המקדשים שלכם והטעם בתחלה היו מקדשי:


and *your cities* will then be destroyed and your sanctuaries desolated. *Your sanctuaries* (v. 31) refers to the temples, which were at first My (God’s) sanctuaries.¹¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹¹ Hence they are referred to as your sanctuaries rather than My sanctuaries.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:31:2)


**ולא אריח בריח.** והטעם כי כבוד השם מלא שמים וארץ וכתוב על נח וידח ה׳‎ את ריח הניחוח והטעם שלא אקבל עולה אחרי כן מכם:


AND I WILL NOT SMELL THE SAVOR OF YOUR SWEET ODORS. Scripture states this for the heaven and earth are full of God’s glory.¹¹² Scripture writes with regard to Noah, *And the Lord smelled the sweet savor* (Gen. 8:3).¹¹³ Its meaning is,¹¹⁴ I will not accept a burnt offering from you after this.¹¹⁵

footnotes:
¹¹² Thus Israel may want to offer sacrifices to God after the destruction of their holy place.
¹¹³ The meaning of which is, God accepted Noah’s sacrifice.
¹¹⁴ The meaning of our verse.
¹¹⁵ After I destroy your holy places.



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:32:1)


ואחר שאמר **עריכם** ו**מקדשיכם** אמר את הארץ כל ארץ ישראל:


After stating *your cities* and *your sanctuaries*, Scripture mentions the land. The reference is to all of the Land of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:32:2)


**ושממו עליה.** כל כך תהיה שממה שגם האויבים הדרים ישומו עליה הפך משוש לכל הארץ:


SHALL BE ASTONISHED AT IT. There will be so much destruction that the enemies who dwell there will also be astonished. Our verse is in contrast to *The joy of the whole earth* (Lam. 2:15).



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:1)


**אזרה.** מבנין הכבד הדגוש ולא די שתזרו עד אשר אריק אחריכם חרב:


WILL I SCATTER. *Ezareh* (will I scatter) is a *pi’el*. Not only will you be scattered but *I will also draw out the sword after you*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:2)


וטעם **אריק.** מנדנה:


The meaning of *draw out* is draw out from its sheath.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:33:3)


**והיתה ארצכם.** דבק עם הפסוק הבא אחריו והטעם כאשר תהיה ארצכם שממה:


AND YOUR LAND. This is connected to the verse which follows it. Its meaning is, when your land shall be desolate it will then be paid for its Sabbaths.¹¹⁶

footnotes:
¹¹⁶ See next verse.



### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:1)


**אז תרצה הארץ** וזאת המלה כמו תשלי׳‎ וכן עד ירצה כשכיר יומו כי נרצה עונה:


The word *tirtzeh* means pay. Compare, *Till he shall accomplish*¹¹⁷ (yirtzeh), *as a hireling, his day* (Job 14:6); *That her guilt is paid off* (nirtzah) (Is. 40:2).

footnotes:
¹¹⁷ Or pay, i.e., pay with his labor.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:2)


**שבתותיה.** שמיטות ויובלים וכן כתוב עד רצתה הארץ את שבתותיה ושם פירשתיו:


HER SABBATHS. Sabbatical years and Jubilees. It is similarly written, *until the land had been paid her sabbaths* (II Chron. 36:21). I have explained it there.¹¹⁸

footnotes:
¹¹⁸ We do not have I.E.’s commentary on Chronicles. Radak quotes from it in his commentary on II Chron. 30:18.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:34:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:3)


**השמה.** שם ומשקלי השמות משתנים:


DESOLATE. *Hashammah* (desolate) is a noun. The forms of the nouns change.¹¹⁹ (Ex.23:19); *shamah* (Jer. 51:37).

footnotes:
¹¹⁹ There are other forms for the word desolate: shemamah


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:34:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:34:4)


וטעם **כל ימי השמה ואתם בארץ אויביכם.** שתהיה שממה מכם אז תשבת ותמצא מנוחה למלא׳‎ שבתותיה:


The meaning of *as long as it lieth desolate, and ye are in your enemies’ land* is that the land will be desolate, for you will not inhabit it. Then the land will not be worked and it will find rest and complete its Sabbaths.



### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:35:1)


וטעם **כל ימי השמה תשבות.** מספר השנים שתשבות וביחזקאל מפורש:


[35. AS LONG AS IT LIETH DESOLATE.] The meaning of *as long as it lieth desolate* is, the number of years that it shall not be worked. This is explained in Ezekiel.¹²⁰

footnotes:
¹²⁰ The prophet Ezekiel was commanded to lie on his left side for 390 days and on his right side for 40 days. Each of these days represented a year that the Israelites sinned in not observing the Sabbatical years. See Ezek. 4:4-6. Also see Rashi on our verse and on Ezekiel.



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:36:1)


**הנשארים בכם.** בעבור שאמר והריקותי אחריכם חרב:


AND AS FOR THEM THAT ARE LEFT OF YOU. Scripture says this because it previously stated, *and I will draw out the sword after you* (v. 33).¹²¹ (v. 36) refers to those who survive the sword’s destruction.

footnotes:
¹²¹ And as for them that are left of you


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:36:2)


**מורך** מפעלי הכפל וכן במועל ידיה׳‎ מגזרת ורך הלבב ויפחדו עד שירדפם קול עלה נדף והוא שם התאר מבנין נפעל ונו״ן השרש מובלע:


A FAINTNESS. *Morekh* (faintness) comes from a double root.¹²². Similarly, the word *mo’al* (lifting up) in *with the lifting up of their hands* (Neh. 8:6).¹²³. The word *morekh* is related to the word *rakh* (faint) in *and faint-hearted* (Deut. 20:8). They will be so scared that the sound of a driven leaf shall chase them. *Niddaf* (driven) is an adjective in the *nifal*. The root *nun* is swallowed.¹²⁴ is *nun, dalet, peh*. The *nun* in *niddaf* is the root of the *nifal* form.

footnotes:
¹²² It comes from the root resh, kaf, kaf
¹²³ From the root ayin, lamed, lamed
¹²⁴ The root of niddaf



### Verse 37

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:37:1)


**איש באחיו.** שהוא אוהבו:


ONE UPON ANOTHER. Whom he loves.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:37:2)


ת**קומה.** מבנין כבד נוסף מנחי עי״ן וי״ו בעבור התי״ו וכן תרומה:


TO STAND. *Tekumah* (stand) is a *hifil*.¹²⁵ form. It comes from a root whose middle root letter is a *vav*.¹²⁶. The aforementioned is so because of the *tav*.¹²⁷ in *tekumah* indicates that *tekumah* is a noun in the *hifil* form. It is the same case with the word *terumah* (offering).¹²⁸ form.

footnotes:
¹²⁵ That is, a noun in the hifil
¹²⁶ Its root is kof, vav, mem
¹²⁷ The tav
¹²⁸ It is noun in the hifil



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:38:1)


**ואכלה אתכם.** מנהג כל הגולים למקום אחר בהשתנות האויר עליהם והמים ימותו רובם:


SHALL EAT YOU UP. It is the norm for most of those exiled to other places to die because of the effect that the change in the air and the water has upon them.



### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:1)


**והנשארים.** מהנשארים:


AND THEY THAT ARE LEFT. And from those that are left.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:39:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:2)


**ימקו בעונם.** על משקל יסבו ושניהם מפעלי הכפל מגזרת המק בשרו מק יהיה:


[SHALL PINE AWAY.] *Yimmakku* (shall pine away) follows the form of *yissabbu* (turned) in *they turned not* (Ezek. 1:9). Both words come from a double root.¹²⁹ comes from the root *mem, kof, kof. Yissabbu* comes from the root *samekh, bet, bet*. *Yimmakku* is related to the word *hamak* (consume away) in *Their flesh shall consume away* (Zech. 14:12) and *mak* (rottenness) in *there shall be rottenness* (Is. 3:24).

footnotes:
¹²⁹ Yimmakku


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:39:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:39:3)


**ואף בעונות אבותם אתם ימקו.** כטעם אבותינו חטאו ובמגלת איכה פירשתיו:


AND ALSO IN THE INIQUITIES OF THEIR FATHERS SHALL THEY PINE AWAY WITH THEM. Its meaning is like that of *Our fathers have sinned* (Lam. 5:7). I explained this in my commentary on Lamentations.¹³⁰

footnotes:
¹³⁰ I.E. there explains Lam. 5:7 as meaning our sins were joined to the sins of our fathers and the combinations of our and our father’s sins reached the stage where punishment was due. However, when the bill came due our fathers were no longer alive and they thus escaped punishment.



### Verse 40

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:1)


**והתודו.** מבנין התפעל:


AND THEY SHALL CONFESS. *Ve-hitvaddu* (and they shall confess) is a *hitpa’el*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:2)


**במעלם אשר מעלו בי.** אבותם:


IN THEIR TREACHERY WHICH THEY COMMITTED AGAINST ME. Which their fathers committed against Me.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:40:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:40:3)


**ואף אשר הלכו עמי.** אלה בניהם בקרי:


AND ALSO THAT THEY HAVE WALKED CONTRARY UNTO ME. These, their children, have walked contrary unto Me.



### Verse 41

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:41:1)


**אף אני.** כן עשיתי עמהם ויסרתים להביאם אל ארץ אויביהם עד שיכנע לבבם הערל ועוד אפרשנו:


I ALSO. Did the same thing to them.¹³¹ (God) *also will walk contrary unto them*. I punished them by bringing them into the land of their enemies until their uncircumcised heart will be humbled. I will explain it¹³² (also) in the phrase *af ani* (I also). The word *af* appears unnecessary. Hence I.E.’s comment. later.

footnotes:
¹³¹ I
¹³² The use of the term af


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:41:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:41:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:41:2)


ואז ירצו את **עונם.** ישלימו ויפרעו:


THEY THEN BE PAID THE PUNISHMENT OF THEIR INIQUITY. *Yirtzu* (be paid) means they will compensate and pay.



### Verse 42

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:42:1)


**וזכרתי את בריתי.** מושך עצמו ואחר עמו וכן הוא וזכרתי את בריתי את ברית יעקב כמו והנבואה עודד הנביא שהוא כמו והנבואה נבואת עודד הנביא ראשיכם שבטיכם שהוא ראשיכם ראשי שבטיכם ויאמר הגאון כי טעם להזכיר בתחלה יעקב בעבור היות שנותיו כלם בברית:


THEN WILL I REMEMBER MY COVENANT. *My covenant* is to be read as if written twice. *Then will I remember My covenant with Jacob*¹³³ is to be interpreted as if written, Then will I remember My covenant, My covenant with Jacob. Compare, *even the prophesy, Oded the prophet*¹³⁴ (II Chron. 15:8), which is to be read as if written, even the prophesy, the prophesy of Oded the prophet; *your heads, your tribes* (Deut. 29:9), which is to be read as if written, your heads, the heads of your tribes. The Gaon says that the reason Scripture mentions Jacob first¹³⁵ is that all of his years were in the covenant.¹³⁶

footnotes:
¹³³ Our verse literally reads, “Then will I remember My covenant Jacob.” Hence I.E.’s comment.
¹³⁴ Translated literally.
¹³⁵ Scripture usually lists the patriarchs in order. It starts with Abraham and concludes with Jacob. However, here it starts with Jacob. Hence I.E.’s comment.
¹³⁶ The covenant that God made with Abraham. See Gen. 17:1-14. Jacob was born after God entered into the covenant with Abraham. Thus Jacob spent his entire life under the covenant. This was not the case with Abraham. Isaac also was born under the covenant. However, Scripture did not start with him, for one cannot start a list from the middle.



### Verse 43

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:43:1)


**והארץ תעזב מהם.** טעמו אזכור גם הארץ שפרעה את השבתות ונעזבה מהם וגם אזכור שהם הרצו את עונם:


FOR THE LAND SHALL LIE FORSAKEN WITHOUT THEM. This means I will also remember the land that paid off its Sabbaths and was forsaken without them. I will also remember that they paid for their sin.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:43:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:43:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:43:2)


**יען וביען.** האחד כנגד משפטי מאסו והשני ובחקותי געלה נפשם והבי״ת נוסף כבי״ת בראשונה:


BECAUSE, EVEN BECAUSE. The first *because* corresponds to *they rejected Mine ordinances*. The second one corresponds to *and their soul abhorred My statutes*. The *bet* of *ve-ya’an* (even because) is superfluous. It is like the *bet* of *ba-rishonah* (in the beginning) (Gen. 13:4).¹³⁷ of *ba-rishonah* is superfluous. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 21).

footnotes:
¹³⁷ According to I.E. the bet



### Verse 44

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:1)


**ואף גם זאת.** לשון צחות והאחד יספיק וכל המקרא כך וכן הרק אך במשה המבלי אין קברים:


AND YET FOR ALL THAT. *Ve-af gam zot* (and yet for all that) is an elegant phrase.¹³⁸ literally means, and even, even this. Scripture could have read *ve-af zot* or *ve-gam zot*. One word would have sufficed. We find this in all of Scripture.¹³⁹ Compare, *ha-rak akh be-mosheh* (hath indeed…only with Moses) (Num. 12:2);¹⁴⁰ or *ha-akh be-mosheh*. *ha-mibbeli en kevarim*¹⁴¹ or *ha-in kevarim*. (because there were no graves) (Ex. 14:11).

footnotes:
¹³⁸ It is poetic. Ve-af gam zot
¹³⁹ Scripture employs similar phrases in many other places.
¹⁴⁰ Scripture could have read ha-rak be-mosheh
¹⁴¹ Scripture could have read ha-mibbeli kevarim


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:44:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:2)


**לא מאסתים לכלותם.** רק ליסרם עד שיכנע לבם:


I WILL NOT REJECT THEM…TO DESTROY THEM UTTERLY. But I will punish them until their hearts are humbled.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:44:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:3)


**געלתים.** סרתי מהם או בחלה נפשי מהם:


NEITHER WILL I ABHOR THEM. *Ge’altim* (will I abhor them) means I did not turn away from them.¹⁴² Or, it means I did not abhor them.

footnotes:
¹⁴² I did not treat them as an abhorrent thing.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:44:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:44:4)


**להפר בריתי.** שנשבעתי להם ואף אם הם הפרו את בריתי כי כן כתוב. אני לא אפר בריתי אתם כי אלהים אני:


TO BREAK MY COVENANT. Which I made with them by oath. Even though, as Scripture states, they broke My covenant, I will not break My covenant with them, for I am the Lord.



### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:45:1

**וזכרתי להם.** לעולם:


BUT I WILL FOR THEIR SAKES REMEMBER. Forever.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:45:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:45:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:45:2)


**ברית ראשונים.** זאת הברית הכרותה בסיני וקרא זה הדור ראשונים כנגד הבנים שיגלו בארץ אויביהם ויש אומרים על אבות העולם. וטעם **אשר הוצאתי אתם.** אותם שנשבעתי לאבותם להוציא בניהם מעבדות והראשון נכון בעיני:


THE COVENANT OF THEIR ANCESTORS.¹⁴³ (literally, the first). The reference is to the covenant made at Sinai. Scripture refers to this generation as “the first,”¹⁴⁴ in contrast to their descendants who will be exiled in the land of their enemies. Some say that the reference is to the “patriarchs of the world,”¹⁴⁵ and the meaning of *whom I brought forth*¹⁴⁶ is those to whom I swore that I would free their children from slavery. The first interpretation appears to me to be the correct one.

### Verse 46

**אלה החקים והמשפטים.** הם הכתובים בפרשת וישמע ואלה המשפטים ופרשת בהר סיני:


THESE ARE THE STATUTES AND ORDINANCES. These statutes and ordinances are recorded in the Torah portion *Now Jethro…heard* (Ex. 18:1); *Now these are the ordinances* (Ex. 21:1), and *In mount Sinai* (Lev. 25:1).


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 26:46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/26:46:2)


**אשר נתן ה׳‎ בינו ובין בני ישראל.** רמז לברית הר סיני כי אחר שנעשה המשכן והיה הכבוד באהל מועד לא עלה משה בהר סיני גם הזכיר הפרשה של הערכין בעבור שנאמרה בהר סיני כי כן כתוב באחרונה כי תחלת ספר וידבר ה׳‎ אל משה במדבר סיני באהל מועד בספר ויקרא:


WHICH THE LORD MADE BETWEEN HIM AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. This alludes to the covenant made at Sinai, for after the tabernacle was erected and the glory was present in the tent of meeting, Moses did not ascend Mount Sinai. Scripture also mentions the section of valuations here because it was related to Moses at Mount Sinai. The latter is clearly stated at the end of the portion.¹⁴⁷ For¹⁴⁸ the beginning of the Book of Numbers, *And the Lord spoke unto Moses in the wilderness of Sinai, in the tent of meeting* (Num. 1:1), is similar¹⁴⁹ rather than *be-sefer*. See Filwarg and Krinsky. to the opening of the Book of Leviticus.¹⁵⁰

## Chapter 27


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:2:1)


**יפליא.** יפריש ויבאר:


SHALL CLEARLY UTTER. *Yafli* (clearly utter) means to explain¹ as in *Vat. Ebr.* 38, not *yafrish* (set aside) as in the printed editions. and to clarify.

footnotes:
¹ Reading yefaresh


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:2:2)


**בערכך נפשות.** הטעם שידור נדר לאמר אם השם יעשה לי כך אפדה נפשי כדי ערכי או ערך בני או ערך בהמה וכ״ף בערכך על דעת כל המדקדקים נוסף והטעם בערך ויש אומרים כי הכ״ף לנכח הכהן ויפרש כערכך הכהן כערכך אתה והה״א יקרא לו ה״א הקריא׳‎ ואמר על הערכך שהיא מלה זרה כמו בתוך האהלי ולפי דעתי שהוא האהל שלי וכן הערכך:


A VOW OF PERSONS.², literally, lives. Its meaning is, he will take a vow saying: If God will do the following for me then I will redeem my life in accordance with its value, or the value of my son’s life, or the value of the life of a beast. The *kaf*³ suffix, which has the meaning of your (thy). of *be-erkekha* (according to thy evaluation) is, according to all of the grammarians,⁴) as if written *be-erekh*. Our verse literally reads: When a man shall clearly utter a vow of lives according to your valuation (*be-erkekha*) of lives to the Lord. The word “your” does not seem to belong in the verse. Hence these commentaries interpret the verse as follows: When a man shall clearly utter a vow of lives according to the valuation (*be-erkekh*) of lives to the Lord. superfluous. However, there is one who says that the *kaf* is directed to the *kohen*.⁵ refers to the *kohen*. This commentator explains our verse as follows: When a man shall clearly utter a vow of lives in accordance to what you the *kohen* shall value lives to the Lord. He explains *ke-erkekha ha-kohen* (v. 12) as, as thou the priest valuest it.⁶ as if written *ke-erkekh ha-kohen* because they believe that the *kaf* suffix of *be-erkekha* (according to thy evaluation) is superfluous, for if the *kaf* of *be-erkekha* refers to the *kohen*, then the word *ha-kohen* is redundant. He refers to the *heh* of *ha-kohen* as a vocative *heh*.⁷ of *be-erkekha* refers to the *kohen*, then the word *ha-kohen* is redundant. Hence the interpretation that *ha-kohen* does not mean the *kohen* but *O kohen*. This interpretation renders *ke-erkekha ha-kohen* as, *as thou O priest valuest it*. He says that the word, *he-erkekha* (of thy valuation) (v. 23), is an irregular word.⁸ literally reads, the valuation yours. Thus the *heh* in *he-erkekha* is irregular. The *heh* in *he-erkekha* does not present a problem for the first commentaries quoted by I.E., for they claim that the *kaf* of *he-erkekha* is superfluous and the word is to be interpreted as if written *ke-erekh. He-erkekha* is similarly to be read as if written *he-erekh*. It is like the word *ha-oholi* (of my tent)⁹ (of my tent) is irregular, for it is a noun with a suffix with the definite article prefixed to it. in *the midst of my tent* (Josh. 7:21). However, in my opinion the meaning of *ha-oholi* is, the tent, mine. Similarly *he-erkekha* (of thy valuation) (v. 23).¹⁰ is not irregular, for the *heh* serves to emphasize the point made by the word’s suffix (Weiser). However, I.E.’s interpretation presents a problem, for it contradicts the rule that the definite article is not placed before a word with a suffix. See Filwarg.

⁷ If the kaf
⁸ The definite article is not placed before a word with a suffix, for he-erkekha
⁹ Ha-oholi
¹⁰ It means, the valuation-yours. According to I.E. ha-oholi

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:3:1)


**מבן עשרים שנה.** החל משהוא נכנס בפקודים:


FROM TWENTY YEARS OLD. Starting when one reaches the age of those who are counted [to go out to war].¹²

footnotes:
¹² See Ex. 30:14; Num. 1:3.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:3:2)


**בן ששים.** זקן ודעת רבים כי זו גזרת מלך כי מבן חדש עד חמש שנים יתן חמשה שקלים אלו עבר יום אחד נוסף על חודש יתן חמשה שקלים ולפי דעת הגאון שיתן בכל שנה שקל עד חמש שנים שלימות כי אמרו רבים שהם ארבע כחלקי השנים ואחר כן תשע עשרה שלמות ואחר כן תשע וחמשים לשוב אל המקום שהחל ממנו וחמשה עשר להשלמת חמשת ושבעים כנגד הד׳‎ החלקים ולפי האמת שכל אלה שלמות או יום אחד בשנה חשוב שנה ולא נסמוך רק על דברי הקבלה כי מה צורך לחלוק מבן חדש עד חמש ומחמש עד עשרים אחר שיתן לכל שנה שקל ויש להשיב על דבר החדש והנה מי שהוא פחות מחדש ימים אין לו ערך ואחר ששים שב לאחור. ועוד מה יעשה אחר הששים שנה אם יתן כך תמיד וכל אלה שבושים והכלל כי הוא גזרת הכתוב כי אילו היה על דרך תוספת הזכר על הנקבה הנה אחר ששים יוסיף השלישית והנה מחמש עד עשרים יוסיף החצי ומחדש ועד חמש ומעשרים ועד ששים יוסיף על החצי עשיריתו:


SIXTY YEARS OLD. Old age. According to many, it is the decree of the King¹³ that from a month until five years he shall give five *shekalim;*¹⁴. that is, if the child is a day past a month, the one who vowed shall give five *shekalim* (v. 6).¹⁵. However, according to the Gaon,¹⁶ he shall give a *shekel* per year¹⁷ until five full years have passed.¹⁸, for a child of two years two *shekalim*, and so on. Many¹⁹ say that these are four valuations²⁰ corresponding to four divisions of life.²¹ Next²² comes nineteen complete years.²³ Then comes²⁴ fifty-nine years²⁵ when one is valued at the amount he started with.²⁶ The fifteen²⁷ are for the completion of seventy-five years corresponding to the four parts.²⁸ However, in truth, they are all complete years.²⁹, it means after 19 years have been completed, i.e., until the age of 20. I.E. says that the meaning of *even unto twenty years old* is, until 20 years have been completed. So too with regard to *even unto sixty years old*. Or,³⁰ is, after a day into the twentieth year. So too with regard to *even unto sixty years old*. I.E.’s point is that it is not clear whether *even unto twenty* means even unto the completion of 20 or from the start of the age of 20. one day in the year is considered a year.³¹ We will only rely on the words of tradition.³² and *even unto sixty years old* and how many *shekalim* are to be given from the age of one month until the age of 20. According to the *Mishnah Arakhin* 4:14, the years mentioned in Scripture are complete years. This means that the thirtieth day is considered less than 30 days; the fifth year is considered under the age of five. The twentieth year is considered under the age of 20; the sixtieth year is considered under the age of 60. Also according to the Mishnah five *shekalim* are given from a month onward through the age of five, and 15 *shekalim* from the age of five complete years through the age of 20. For what reason is there to differentiate between a month until five, and from five until twenty since he gives a *shekel* for each year?³³ per year from one month on. If so, there is no reason to differentiate between a month and five years and between five and 20 years since a child of one year gives one *shekel*, a child of two, 2 *shekalim*, and so on till the age of 20. Scripture should have simply said that a *shekel* per year is given from one month until the age of 20. We can refute the latter from the law regarding a child of a month.³⁴ is given per year for the first 20 years. However, a child of a month is less than a year old. Why then should a *shekel* be given for him? Note, one who is less than a month old has no value.³⁵ After the age of sixty one goes backwards.³⁶ Furthermore, what should one do after sixty? Shall he always give the same?³⁷ per year until he reaches the age of 75 or is he valued at 15 *shekalim* beyond the age of 75 for life? These are all errors.³⁸ The general rule is that it is a Scriptural decree.³⁹ If the amount to be given in valuation was based on the additional *shekalim* above which the male is valued over the female, then⁴⁰ note that after the age of sixty a third is added.⁴¹, and a male at 15 *shekalim*. From five to twenty add a half, and from the age of a month until five and from twenty until sixty one adds to⁴² the half⁴³ its tenth.⁴⁴ and a male at 5 *shekalim*. The female is valued at half of the male, i.e, 2.5 *shekalim* plus half a *shekel*. Half a *shekel* is a tenth of five *shekalim*. A female is valued at 30 *shekalim* from 20 to 60 years of age and a male at 50 *shekalim*. The female is valued at half of the male, i.e., 25 *shekalim* plus five *shekalim*. Five *shekalim* are a tenth of 50 *shekalim*.

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:8:1)


**והעריך.** שיושם הנערך במערכה אחת עם כך וכך וכמוהו אין ערוך אליך ומלת העריך מהבנין הכבד הנוסף וכן יעריכנו הכהן להפריש בינו ובין יערכנו לפני ה׳‎:


AND THE PRIEST SHALL VALUE HIM. He shall be assessed in one evaluation based on every object that he owns.⁴⁵ The word *arokh* (compared) in *none to be compared unto Thee* (Ps. 40:6) is similar to *he’erikh* (value him).⁴⁶ *He’erikh* is a *hifil*. Similarly *ya’arikhennu* (value him) in *shall the priest value him*?⁴⁷ means value him. *He’erikh* is to be distinguished from *ya’arkhennu* (he shall set it in order) in *he shall set it in order before the Lord* (Lev. 24:8).⁴⁸

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:8:2)


**והעמידו הכהן.** כאילו אמר והעמידו הכהן לפני הכהן כאשר פירשתי ויצק על כף הכהן השמאלית ויהיה דרך צחות או יהיה והעמידו שיעמיד הנודר נפשו:


[THEN HE SHALL BE SET.⁴⁹ (literally, and he shall set him). ] The *kohen*.⁵⁰ shall set him. Our verse⁵¹ [literally, and he (the *kohen*) shall set him before the *kohen* ]. should be interpreted as if written; and the *kohen* shall set him before the *kohen*.⁵² It is similar to my interpretation of *and pour it into the palm of his own left hand* (Lev. 14:15).⁵³ shall take of the log and pour it into the palm of the *kohen’s* left hand. According to I.E. parts one and two of the verse refer to the same *kohen*. See I. E. on Lev. 14:15. *Ve-he’emido li-fene ha-kohen* (then he shall be set before the priest) is lyrical.⁵⁴) shall set him before the *kohen* is poetic. On the other hand, *then he shall be set* might mean that the one who vows shall set him.⁵⁵ (literally, and he shall set him) refers to the one who vows. In this case *ve-he’emido li-fene ha-kohen* is to be rendered: and he (the one who utters the vow) shall set himself before the *kohen*.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:16:1)


**ואם משדה אחזתו.** שיירש עם ישראל:


AND IF…HIS POSSESSION. Which he inherited with the nation of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:16:2)


**חמר שעורים.** וכתוב כי עשרת הבתים חומר ושם כתוב האיפ׳‎ והבת והנה זרע איפת שעורים בחמשה שקלים וזו גזרת המלך כדמי העבד:


A HOMER OF BARLEY. Scripture writes, *for ten baths are a homer* (Ezek. 45:14). Scripture there writes, *The ephah and the bath* (Ezek. 45:11).⁵⁷ are equal. Note, the sowing of an ephah of barley is valued at five *shekalim*.⁵⁸, for an *ephah* is a tenth of a *homer*. This is a decree of the King regarding the monetary value of a slave.⁵⁹. (Ex. 21:32). It makes no difference whether the slave in reality is worth more or less than the latter. Similarly with a field. A field is arbitrarily valued at 50 *shekels* per *the sowing of a homer of barley*, whether the field is worth more or less.

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:18:1)


**ונגרע מערכך.** מה שהלך אחר היובל:


AND AN ABATEMENT SHALL BE MADE FROM THY VALUATION. The time that passed since the Jubilee.⁶¹

footnotes:
⁶¹ The years that passed shall be deducted from the valuation.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:19:1)


וי״ו **ואם גאול יגאל**, כפ״א רפה בלשון ישמעאל והוא דבק בטעם הפסוקים שהם למעלה כי הערך עיקר אם יגאל השדה ואם לא יגאל:


[AND IF HE…WILL INDEED REDEEM.] The *vav* of *ve-im ga’al yigal* (and if he…will indeed redeem) is like the Arabic *fa*.⁶² It⁶³ is connected in meaning to the verses that are above⁶⁴ for the valuation is the main thing.⁶⁵ Its⁶⁶ meaning is, if he redeems the field or if he does not redeem it.⁶⁷ (ve) *if he…will indeed redeem it*. Verse 20 reads, *And* (ve) *if he…will not redeem the field*. According to I.E. verses 19 and 20 continue the thought of the earlier verses. Hence the *vav* is not to be translated, and our verses should be interpreted: If he…will indeed redeem it… Or if he…will not redeem the field.

footnotes:
⁶² It does not introduce a new subject. See I.E. on Lev. 7:15 and the notes thereto.
⁶³ What follows.
⁶⁴ Which speaks of a field’s valuation.
⁶⁵ When one dedicates the value of his field.
⁶⁶ The meaning of the two verses that follow.
⁶⁷ Verse 19 reads, And



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:20:1)


**ואם מכר.** טעמו או מכר, ולא יוכל בעל שדה אחוזה שיגאלנו בעבור שמכרו לה׳‎:


OR IF HE HAVE SOLD. *Ve-im makhar* means, or if he have sold.⁶⁸ of *ve-im* (or if) is not to be rendered as and. The owner of the field cannot redeem the field because he sold it. It is to be holy to God.⁶⁹

footnotes:
⁶⁸ Not “and if he hath sold”. In other words, the vav
⁶⁹ After the year of the Jubilee.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:21:1)


**בצאתו ביבל.** הזכיר הכתוב על דרך כל שדה כי ביובל יצא:


WHEN IT GOETH OUT IN THE JUBILEE. Scripture employs the same language⁷⁰ (goeth out), with regard to this field, which does not revert to its owner but becomes the property of the Lord, as it does with regard to a field which goes back to its owner. as it does with regard to all fields,⁷¹ for it⁷² goes out⁷³ in the Jubilee.

footnotes:
⁷⁰ Scripture employs the same term, yatza
⁷¹ Which return to their owners in the year of the jubilee.
⁷² The field, which becomes the Lord’s.
⁷³ From the one who bought it.


###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:21:2)


**כשדה החרם.** שידור נדר להחרימו גם הוא לכהן והעריך לכהן כי הוא מעריך ואין צורך להיותו כהן גדול:


AS A FIELD DEVOTED. It belongs to the *kohen* as a field does which one takes a vow to devote.⁷⁴ The amount of the valuation⁷⁵ assesses a dedicated object to be worth belongs to the *kohen* who estimated its value. is the *kohen*’s, for he assesses it. He does not have to be a *kohen gadol*.

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Verse 28.
⁷⁵ The amount of money which the kohen



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:23:1)


**מכסת.** כמו מכסת נפשות:


THY VALUATION. *Mikhsat* (the worth of) is similar to *mikhsat* (number of) in *according to the number of* (Ex. 12:4).⁷⁶ means a portion.” That is, according to the portions which each one will eat of the Passover lamb. I.E. renders our verse, the portion (i.e., the number of years left until the Jubilee) of your valuation.

footnotes:
⁷⁶ See I.E. on Ex. 12:4 (Vol. 2, p. 219): “Mikhsat



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:24:1)


**לאשר קנהו מאתו.** הוא המוכר והוא מפורש לאשר לו אחזת הארץ:


OF WHOM IT WAS BOUGHT. The reference is to the seller.⁷⁷ This is explained by *to whom the possession of the land belongeth*.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ The original seller.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:26:1)


**יבכר לה׳‎.** נודע שהוא לשם וזאת המלה מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו ופירושו האומר אתן הבכור לשם:


WHICH IS BORN AS A FIRSTLING TO THE LORD. Known to be the Lord’s. *Yevukkar* (which is born as a firstling) is a *pu’al. No man shall sanctify it* by saying, “I will give the first-born to the Lord.”⁷⁸ This is its meaning.⁷⁹.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ For it already is the Lord’s from birth.
⁷⁹ The meaning of no man shall sanctify it



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:27:1)


**ואם בבהמה הטמאה ופדה בערכך.** הבכור עם התוספות חמישית בעבור שהקדישו גם יתן שה כי כן כתוב אם לא יקדישנו ונמכר. ופטר חמור. בערך גם השה יתן ורבים אומרים לא כן:


AND IF IT BE OF AN UNCLEAN BEAST. The first-born,⁸⁰ along with the addition of a fifth of its value because he sanctified it.⁸¹ He shall also give a lamb,⁸² for Scripture so writes.⁸³ (Ex. 13:13). According to I.E. the same law that governs the first-born of an ass applies to all first-born of beasts, which are dedicated to God (Weiser). If he does not sanctify the first-born,⁸⁴ it may be sold.⁸⁵ The first-born of an ass shall be exchanged in accordance with its valuation.⁸⁶ He shall similarly give the sheep.⁸⁷. Many say that it is not so.⁸⁸ 102a. Also see Rashi and Ramban.

footnotes:
⁸⁰ According to I.E. the unclean beast spoken of in our verse is a first-born animal.
⁸¹ The unclean first-born of the beast.
⁸² In addition to giving the valuation of the first-born plus a fifth of its value.
⁸³ And every firstling of an ass thou shalt redeem with a lamb
⁸⁴ The first-born of an unclean beast.
⁸⁵ Like any other unclean beast, for unlike the first-born of an ass it does not require redemption.
⁸⁶ If he dedicates the first-born of an ass.
⁸⁷ In addition to its valuation. Normally a first-born ass is redeemed by a sheep. However, if one dedicates the first-born ass he redeems it by giving its value in money. This is in addition to the sheep which he has to give. According to I.E. when the first-born of an ass is redeemed by a lamb, the value of the lamb given in exchange for the ass must be commensurate with the value of the ass. The value of the animals is established by the kohen
⁸⁸ They say that our verses do not speak of a first-born animal. See Menachot



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:28:1

**מכל אשר לו.** שהוא ברשותו:


OF ALL THAT HE HATH. Which is in his control.⁸⁹

**מאדם.** כמו והחרמתי את עריהם:


OF MAN. Compare, *I will devote* (ve-hacharamti)⁹⁰ (devoted) in our verse. *their cities* (Num. 21:2).⁹¹). So does Num. 21:2. The devoted cities spoken of in Numbers did not belong to the Israelites. Similarly the object in our verse. Hence I.E.’s interpretation.

⁹⁰ Translated in keeping with the meaning of the word cherem

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:29:1)


**יומת.** ומלת **יחרם** מהבנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו כמו יעמד חי:


[BE PUT TO DEATH.] The word *yochoram* (shall be devoted) is a *hofal*. Compare, *yo’omad* (shall be set)⁹² is a *hofal*. in *shall be set alive* (Lev. 16:10).

footnotes:
⁹² Yo’omad



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:32:1)


**תחת השבט.** שבט הרועה והנה הנבחר הוא הא׳‎ והעשירי שוה כמו זה רק העשירי יגאלנו ואין כן הבכור והנה יתן הבכור והעשירי בבהמה ובזרע הארץ שהיא התבואה הבכורים והמעשר:


UNDER THE ROD. The shepherd’s rod.⁹³ Now the number one⁹⁴ is the chosen one. The tenth is the same as the first.⁹⁵ However, he may redeem the tenth.⁹⁶ This is not the case with the first-born.⁹⁷ Note, the Israelite shall give the first-born and the tenth of the beast, and of the seed of the ground, namely, of the produce, the first fruits⁹⁸. and the tithe.⁹⁹.

footnotes:
⁹³ All animals that are under the supervision of a shepherd (Filwarg).
⁹⁴ The first-born.
⁹⁵ It too is holy.
⁹⁶ The tithe (v. 31) referenced is probably the second tithe (Weiser). See also Rashi and the Rashbam. The reference cannot be to the tenth of the cattle and flock, for Scripture says that these cannot be redeemed (v. 33).
⁹⁷ See Num. 18:17.
⁹⁸ All these are given to the kohen
⁹⁹ The tithe is given to the Levite. The Levite then gives a tithe from the tithe given him to the kohen



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:33:1)


**יבקר.** כמו יבדיל וכבר פירשתי שהמלה מגזרת בקר:


HE SHALL NOT INQUIRE. *Yavakker* (he shall…inquire) means he shall separate. I have previously explained that the word *yavakker* is related to the word *boker* (morning).¹⁰⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰⁰ See I.E. on Lev. 13:36.



### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Leviticus 27:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Leviticus/r/27:34:1)


**בהר סיני.** פירשתיו בפרשה הזאת ומי שיש לו לב להבין סוד העולם אז ידע סוד הבכור והעשירי והנה אברהם נתן מעשר גם כן יעקב אבינו עליו השלום ועוד אגלה קצת הסוד בזכרי מעשר שני אם יעזרני אחד ואין לו שני:


IN MOUNT SINAI. I have explained this earlier in this Torah portion.¹⁰¹ form one unit. Hence I.E. refers to them as one Torah portion (Weiser). The person who has the intelligence to understand the secret of the world will understand the secret of the first-born and the tenth.¹⁰² Now Abraham gave a tithe.¹⁰³ Our father Jacob, unto him the peace, did the same.¹⁰⁴ If the One who has no second will help me, then I will reveal part of the secret when I mention the second tithe.¹⁰⁵ The Book of Leviticus is completed.

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ See I.E. on Lev. 25:1. Be-Har and Be-Chukkotai
¹⁰² See I.E. on Ex. 3:15 (Vol. 2, pp. 69-71). I.E. there notes that “the number one is the…foundation of all numbers.” He goes on to say that there are ten spheres surrounding the earth: “The tenth sphere is holy because its power is manifest throughout the throne of glory.”
¹⁰³ Gen. 14:20.
¹⁰⁴ Gen. 28:22.
¹⁰⁵ See I.E. on Deut. 14:22 (Vol. 5, p. 95): “If you start from the top, then one is the first number. If you start from below, then, behold, ten is the first number. This is the secret of the first-born and the tithe.”
